Hirsch weight-filtered log crystalline complex and Hirsch weight-filtered log crystalline dga of a proper SNCL scheme in characteristic p>0
Yukiyoshi Nakkajima

Abstract




Abstract.—

For a
p
-adic PD-formal family
(S,{\cal I},\gamma)
of log points,
let
S_{0}
be an exact closed log subscheme of
S
defined by
{\cal I}
.
For a proper simple normal crossing log scheme
X
over
S_{0}
,
we construct a theory of the derived PD-Hirsch extension
of the modification of the log crystalline complex of
X
over
the underlying PD-formal scheme

(\overset{\circ}{S},{\cal I},\gamma)
of
(S,{\cal I},\gamma)
.
By using this theory,
we construct a filtered complex
(H_{\rm zar}(X/S),P)

in order to overcome obstacles arising from the incompatibility of
the
p
-adic Steenbrink weight-filtered complex defined in

[
Nak4
]
(cf.
[
M
]
) with the cup product of the log crystalline complex of
X
over

(S,{\cal I},\gamma)
. We also construct a filtered dga
(H_{{\rm zar},{\rm TW}}(X/S),P)

whose underlying filtered complex is isomorphic to
(H_{\rm zar}(X/S),P)\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}
.
When
(S,{\cal I},\gamma)
is the canonical lift with the canonical PD-structure
of the log point of a perfect field
\kappa
of characteristic
p>0
over the
formal spectrum of the Witt ring of
\kappa
,
we prove that
(H_{\rm zar}(X/S),P)
and
(H_{{\rm zar},{\rm TW}}(X/S),P)

are canonically isomorphic to Kim and Hain’s filtered complex and
their filtered dga in
[
KH
]
, respectively.

As applications of this theory, we show fundamental properties of
the weight filtration on the log crystalline cohomology sheaf of
X
over
(S,{\cal I},\gamma)

when the underlying scheme
\overset{\circ}{X}
of
X
is projective over
the underlying scheme
\overset{\circ}{S}_{0}
of
S_{0}
.
When
\overset{\circ}{S}_{0}
is connected,
we prove that the
p
-adic variational filtered log hard Lefschetz conjecture
with respect to the weight filtration conjectured in
[
Nak4
]
is true
when there exists a log fiber
X\times_{S}s
for an exact closed point
s
of
S

which is the log special fiber of a
projective strict semistable family over a complete discrete valuation ring
of any characteristic. Using this result,
we show the existence of the bilinear form on the primitive part of
the log isocrystalline cohomology sheaf which is compatible with the weight filtration.

-degeneration of the

1
Introduction

In this book, for a formal family of log points in characteristic
p>0
,
we define the log crystalline analogue
of the log de Rham complex of the Kato-Nakayama space(=the real blow up)

T^{\log}
(
[
KtN
]
) of a log analytic family
T
of log points.
We use the PD-Hirsch extension to define it.
We show that this very simple complex has an important application
for the cup product of the log crystalline cohomology sheaf of
a proper SNCL(=simple normal crossing log) scheme over a family of log points
in characteristic
p>0
.

First let us recall a classical result (
[
SGA 7-II
]
,
[
St1
]
,
[
GN
]
).

Let

\overset{\circ}{\Delta}:=\{\tau\in{\mathbb{C}}~|~|\tau|<1\}

be the open unit disk over
{\mathbb{C}}
.
Let
\overset{\circ}{\cal X}

be an analytic (not necessarily strict) semistable family over
\overset{\circ}{\Delta}
.
Let
\overset{\circ}{f}\colon\overset{\circ}{\cal X}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{\Delta}
be the structural morphism.
Endow
\overset{\circ}{\cal X}
with the canonical log structure obtained by the special fiber
of
\overset{\circ}{\cal X}/\overset{\circ}{\Delta}

and let
{\cal X}
be the resulting log analytic space.
(See
[
K1
]
and
[
KtN
]
for the definitions of log schemes and log analytic spaces.)
In particular, we obtain
\Delta
.
Let
f\colon{\cal X}\longrightarrow\Delta
be the structural morphism.
Let
\overset{\circ}{O}
be the set of the origin of
\overset{\circ}{\Delta}
.
Let
O:=(\overset{\circ}{O},{\mathbb{N}}\oplus{\mathbb{C}}^{*}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{C}})

be the log point of
\overset{\circ}{O}
, where the morphism

{\mathbb{N}}\oplus{\mathbb{C}}^{*}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{C}}
is
given by
(n,a)\longmapsto 0^{n}a

(n\in{\mathbb{N}},a\in{\mathbb{C}}^{*})
, where
0^{n}=0\in{\mathbb{C}}

for
n\not=0
and
0^{0}:=1\in{\mathbb{C}}
.
Let
X
be the log special fiber of
{\cal X}/{\Delta}
over
O
.
Set
\Delta^{*}:={\Delta}\setminus O

and let
j_{\Delta}\colon\Delta^{*}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\Delta

be the natural inclusion.
Let
{\mathfrak{H}}\longrightarrow\Delta^{*}
be the universal cover of
\Delta^{*}
.
Let us consider the following diagram whose all squares are cartesian:

(1.0.1)

\begin{CD}X@>{i}>{}>{\cal X}@<{j}<{}<{\mathfrak{X}}@<{}<{}<\overline{\mathfrak{X}}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{f}V{}V@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
O@>{\subset}>{}>\Delta @<{j_{\Delta}}<{}<\Delta^{*}@<{}<{}<{\mathfrak{H}}.\end{CD}

Let
\overline{j}\colon\overline{\mathfrak{X}}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{X}}\longrightarrow{\cal X}
be the composite morphism.
Let
R\Psi({\mathbb{C}}):=i^{-1}R\overline{j}_{*}({\mathbb{C}}_{\overline{\mathfrak{X}}})
be
the nearby cycle sheaf
of the constant sheaf
{\mathbb{C}}_{\overline{\mathfrak{X}}}
on
\overline{\mathfrak{X}}

and let
\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal X}/{\mathbb{C}}}

be the log de Rham complex of
{\cal X}
over
{\mathbb{C}}

(not the classical de Rham complex of
\overset{\circ}{\cal X}
over
{\mathbb{C}}
).
Then
R\Psi({\mathbb{C}})
is isomorphic to
the complex
i^{-1}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal X}/{\mathbb{C}}}[\log\tau])

(
[
SGA 7-II
]
,
[
St1
]
,
[
GN
]
),
where

(1.0.2)

\displaystyle\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal X}/{\mathbb{C}}}[\log\tau]:={\mathbb{C}}[\log\tau]\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal X}/{\mathbb{C}}}

is considered
as a subdga of
\overline{j}_{*}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{\mathfrak{X}}/{\mathbb{C}}})
.
Furthermore, as proved in
[
Nak6
]
,
it is not difficult to prove that
the following natural morphism

(1.0.3)

\displaystyle i^{-1}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal X}/{\mathbb{C}}}[\log\tau])\longrightarrow\Omega^{\bullet}_{X/{\mathbb{C}}}[\log\tau]:={\mathbb{C}}[\log\tau]\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X/{\mathbb{C}}}

is a quasi-isomorphism.
Here
\Omega^{\bullet}_{X/{\mathbb{C}}}
is
the log de Rham complex of
X
over
{\mathbb{C}}

and
\Omega^{\bullet}_{X/{\mathbb{C}}}[\log\tau]
is
the complex defined by the following
{\mathbb{C}}
-linear differential

\displaystyle d((\log\tau)^{n}\otimes\omega):=n(\log\tau)^{n-1}\otimes d\log\tau\wedge\omega+(\log\tau)^{n}\otimes d\omega\quad(\omega\in\Omega^{i}_{X/{\mathbb{C}}}).

The complex
\Omega^{\bullet}_{X/{\mathbb{C}}}[\log\tau]

is, what is called, the Hirsch extension of

\Omega^{\bullet}_{X/{\mathbb{C}}}

by a vector space
{\mathbb{C}}\log\tau
over
{\mathbb{C}}
of rank 1
with respect to the following composite morphism

(1.0.4)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{C}}\log\tau\longrightarrow{\rm Ker}(\Omega^{1}_{{\cal X}/{\mathbb{C}}}\longrightarrow\Omega^{2}_{{\cal X}/{\mathbb{C}}})\longrightarrow{\rm Ker}(\Omega^{1}_{X/{\mathbb{C}}}\longrightarrow\Omega^{2}_{X/{\mathbb{C}}}),

where the first morphism is defined by

\log\tau\longmapsto d\log\tau
.
The definition of the Hirsch extension of a dga(=differential graded algebra) is as follows.

Let
A
be a commutative ring with unit element
and let
(B^{\bullet},d)
be a dga over
A
.
The Hirsch extension of
B^{\bullet}
by
a free
A
-module
M
is, by definition,
a natural inclusion

\displaystyle B^{\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\rm Sym}_{A}(M)\otimes_{A}B^{\bullet},

where
M
is placed at degree 0
with an
A
-linear map
\varphi\colon M\longrightarrow{\rm Ker}(d\colon B^{1}\longrightarrow B^{2})

and
{\rm Sym}_{A}(M)
is a symmetric algebra over
A
generated by
M
;

{\rm Sym}_{A}(M)\otimes_{A}B^{\bullet}
becomes a dga over
A
by

d\colon B^{\bullet}\longrightarrow B^{\bullet+1}

and
d_{M}(x):=\varphi(x)

(x\in M)
.
(See
[
Su
]
for a more general notion of the Hirsch extension.)

The complex
\Omega^{\bullet}_{X/{\mathbb{C}}}[\log\tau]

has a close relation with the log de Rham complex
of the Kato-Nakayama space
T^{\log}
of
an fs log analytic space
T
over
{\mathbb{C}}
.
Indeed,
let
\epsilon_{T}\colon T^{\log}\longrightarrow T

be the natural morphism of topological spaces defined in
[
KtN
]
.
Then
\Omega^{\bullet}_{O^{\log}/{\mathbb{C}},x}=({\mathbb{C}}[\log\tau]\longrightarrow{\mathbb{C}}[\log\tau]d\log\tau)

for any point
x\in O^{\log}={\mathbb{S}}^{1}

and
\Omega^{\bullet}_{O^{\log}/{\mathbb{C}},x}
is the Hirsch extension of

\Gamma(O,\Omega^{\bullet}_{O/{\mathbb{C}}})=({\mathbb{C}}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{C}}d\log\tau)

by
{\mathbb{C}}\log\tau
with respect to the morphism (
1.0.4
) in the case
X=O
.
In
[
FN
]
Fujisawa and Nakayama have proved that

(1.0.5)

\displaystyle\Omega^{\bullet}_{X/{\mathbb{C}}}[\log\tau]=R(\epsilon_{X}\pi_{X})_{*}\pi^{-1}_{X}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{X^{\log}/{\mathbb{C}}}),

where
\pi_{X}
is the natural morphism

X^{\log}\times_{O^{\log}}{\mathbb{R}}=X^{\log}\times_{{\mathbb{S}}^{1}}{\mathbb{R}}\longrightarrow X^{\log}
.
Though we give up defining the
p
-adic analogue of
X^{\log}
and

X^{\log}\times_{O^{\log}}{\mathbb{R}}
, we define the
p
-adic analogue of

\Omega^{\bullet}_{X/{\mathbb{C}}}[\log\tau]
instead.
This is a case in which it is possible to define an appropriate sheaf of dga’s
even when it is difficult to define an appropriate space.

In this book, in order to give applications for the (integral) log crystalline cohomology sheaf
of a proper SNCL scheme in characteristic
p>0
,
we define the (derived) Hirsch extension obtained
by the PD-polynomial algebra
\Gamma_{A}(M)

generated by
M
over
A
(
[
BO1
, Appendix A]
) for a certain complex
instead of the Hirsch extension of a dga obtained by
{\rm Sym}_{A}(M)
.
We call this extension the
(
derived
)
PD-Hirsch extension
of the complex.
An example of a PD-Hirsch extension has first appeared in an article
by Kim and Hain (
[
KH
]
), which will be explained later.

To explain our motivation for studying the (derived) PD-Hirsch extension,
let us raise a fundamental problem on
the log crystalline cohomology sheaf of
a proper SNCL scheme
over a family of log points in characteristic
p>0
.

For a log (formal) scheme
Y
, denote by
\overset{\circ}{Y}
the underlying
(formal) scheme of
Y
.
Let
S
be a
p
-adic formal family of log points defined in
[
Nak4
]
;
locally on
S
,
S
is isomorphic to a log
p
-adic formal scheme

(\overset{\circ}{S},{\mathbb{N}}\oplus{\cal O}_{S}^{*}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{S})
,
where the morphism
{\mathbb{N}}\oplus{\cal O}_{S}^{*}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{S}

is defined by the morphism

(n,a)\longmapsto 0^{n}a

(n\in{\mathbb{N}},a\in{\cal O}_{S}^{*})
, where
0^{n}=0\in{\cal O}_{S}

for
n\not=0
and
0^{0}:=1\in{\cal O}_{S}
.
Let
(S,{\cal I},\gamma)
be a
p
-adic formal PD-family of log points
(
S
is a
p
-adic formal family of log points and

{\cal I}
is a quasi-coherent
p
-adic PD-ideal sheaf of
{\cal O}_{S}

with PD-structure
\gamma
).
Let
S_{0}
be an exact closed log subscheme of
S
defined by
{\cal I}
.
Let
X/S_{0}
be a proper SNCL scheme with structural morphism

f\colon X\longrightarrow S_{0}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}S
.
(In §
2
below we recall the definition of the SNCL scheme.)
Let
\{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
be
the set of smooth components of
X/S_{0}
defined in
[
Nak4
]
.
(When
\overset{\circ}{S}_{0}
is the spectrum of a field of characteristic
p>0
,

\{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
can be taken as
the set of the irreducible components of
\overset{\circ}{X}
.)
For a nonnegative integer
k
, let

(1.0.6)

\displaystyle\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k)}:=\coprod_{\{\{\lambda_{0},\ldots,\lambda_{k}\}~|\lambda_{i}\in\Lambda,\lambda_{i}\not=\lambda_{j}(i\not=j)\}}\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda_{0}}\cap\cdots\cap\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda_{k}}

be a scheme over
\overset{\circ}{S}_{0}
well-defined in
[
Nak4
]
.
Let
a^{(k)}\colon\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k)}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{X}
be the natural morphism.
Let
F_{\overset{\circ}{S}_{0}}\colon\overset{\circ}{S}_{0}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{S}_{0}
be the absolute Frobenius
endomorphism of
\overset{\circ}{S}_{0}
and
set
S_{0}^{[p]}:=S_{0}\times_{\overset{\circ}{S}_{0},F_{\overset{\circ}{S}_{0}}}\overset{\circ}{S}_{0}
.
Let

F_{S_{0}/\overset{\circ}{S}_{0}}\colon S_{0}\longrightarrow S_{0}^{[p]}

be the relative Frobenius morphism of
S_{0}
over
\overset{\circ}{S}_{0}
.
Let
S_{0}^{[p]}(S)
be a log formal scheme whose underlying formal scheme
is
\overset{\circ}{S}
and whose log structure
M_{S_{0}^{[p]}(S)}

is a unique sub-log structure of
S

such that the isomorphism

M_{S}/{\cal O}_{S}^{*}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}M_{S_{0}}/{\cal O}_{S_{0}}^{*}
induces
the following isomorphism

(1.0.7)

\displaystyle M_{S_{0}^{[p]}(S)}/{\cal O}_{S}^{*}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}{\rm Im}(F_{S_{0}/\overset{\circ}{S}_{0}}^{*}\colon F_{S_{0}/\overset{\circ}{S}_{0}}^{*}(M_{S_{0}\times_{\overset{\circ}{S}_{0},F_{\overset{\circ}{S}_{0}}}\overset{\circ}{S}_{0}})\longrightarrow M_{S_{0}})/{\cal O}_{S_{0}}^{*}.

(The structural morphism of
M_{S_{0}^{[p]}(S)}
is the composite morphism

M_{S_{0}^{[p]}(S)}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}M_{S}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{S}
.)
We have an obvious morphism
(S,{\cal I},\gamma)\longrightarrow(S_{0}^{[p]}(S),{\cal I},\gamma)
of log PD-formal schemes.
Let
(X/S)_{\rm crys}
be the log crystalline topos of
X/(S,{\cal I},\gamma)

defined in
[
K1
]
and let
{\cal O}_{X/S}
be the structure sheaf of

(X/S)_{\rm crys}
.
Let
X_{\rm zar}
be the zariski topos of
\overset{\circ}{X}
.
Let
u_{X/S}\colon(X/S)_{\rm crys}\longrightarrow X_{\rm zar}
be the canonical projection.
Set
f_{X/S}:=f\circ u_{X/S}
.
Let
{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(f^{-1}({\cal O}_{S}))
be the derived category of
bounded below filtered complexes of
f^{-1}({\cal O}_{S})
-modules
and let
D^{+}(f^{-1}({\cal O}_{S}))
be the derived category of
bounded below complexes of
f^{-1}({\cal O}_{S})
-modules.
In
[
Nak4
]
we have proved the following:

Theorem 1.1

(
[
Nak4
, Existence of the zarisikian
p
-adic Steenbrink complex]
).

Let
\varpi_{\rm crys}^{(m)}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})

(m\in{\mathbb{N}})

be the crystalline orientation sheaf associated to
the set
\{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
. That is,

\varpi_{\rm crys}^{(m)}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})
is
the extension to
(\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(m)}/\overset{\circ}{S})_{\rm crys}

of the direct sum of

\overset{m}{\bigwedge}{\mathbb{Z}}^{E}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda_{0}}\cap\cdots\cap\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda_{m}}}

in the Zariski topos
\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(m)}_{\rm zar}
of
\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(m)}

for the subsets
E=\{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda_{0}},\ldots,\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda_{m}}\}
’s
of
\{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}

with
\#E=m+1
.
Then there exists a filtered complex

(1.1.1)

\displaystyle(A_{\rm zar}(X/S),P)\in{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(f^{-1}({\cal O}_{S}))

with a canonical isomorphism

(1.1.2)

\displaystyle\theta\wedge\colon Ru_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}A_{\rm zar}(X/S)

in
D^{+}(f^{-1}({\cal O}_{S}))

such that

(1.1.3)

\displaystyle{\rm gr}^{P}_{k}A_{\rm zar}(X/S)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\bigoplus_{j\geq\max\{-k,0\}}

\displaystyle a^{(2j+k)}_{*}(Ru^{\rm crys}_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k)}/\overset{\circ}{S}*}({\cal O}_{{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k)}/\overset{\circ}{S}}})

\displaystyle\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\rm crys}^{(2j+k)}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}))(-j-k)[-2j-k]

in
D^{+}(f^{-1}({\cal O}_{S}))
.
Here the Tate twist
(-j-k)
means the Tate twist
with respect to
the morphism
X\longrightarrow X\times_{\overset{\circ}{S}_{0},F_{\overset{\circ}{S}_{0}}}\overset{\circ}{S}_{0}

over
(S,{\cal I},\gamma)\longrightarrow(S_{0}^{[p]}(S),{\cal I},\gamma)

induced by the absolute Frobenius endomorphism
F_{X}\colon X\longrightarrow X
of
X
.

As a corollary of this theorem, we obtain
the weight filtration
P
on
R^{q}f_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S})

(q\in{\mathbb{N}})
:

(1.1.4)

\displaystyle P_{k+q}R^{q}f_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S})

\displaystyle:={\rm Im}(R^{q}f_{X/S*}(P_{k}A_{\rm zar}(X/S))\longrightarrow R^{q}f_{X/S*}(A_{\rm zar}(X/S)))

\displaystyle\simeq{\rm Im}(R^{q}f_{X/S*}(P_{k}A_{\rm zar}(X/S))\longrightarrow R^{q}f_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S})).

See
[
M
]
and
[
Nak1
]
for another approach for the construction of

P
on
R^{q}f_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S})

by the use of log de Rham-Witt complexes in the case where
S
is the canonical lift of
the log point of a perfect field
\kappa
of characteristic
p>0
over
the Witt ring
{\cal W}
of
\kappa
. In this case, in
[
Nak4
]

we have proved that
(A_{\rm zar}(X/S),P)
is
canonically isomorphic to the weight-filtered complex

({\cal W}A^{\bullet}_{X},P)
constructed in

[
M
]
and
[
Nak1
]
.

It is natural to raise the following fundamental problem
for the filtration
P
on
R^{q}f_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S})
: does the cup product

(1.1.5)

\displaystyle\cup\colon R^{q}f_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S})\otimes_{{\cal O}_{S}}R^{q^{\prime}}f_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S})\longrightarrow R^{q+q^{\prime}}f_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S})

induce the following morphism

(1.1.6)

\displaystyle\cup\colon P_{k}R^{q}f_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S})\otimes_{{\cal O}_{S}}P_{k^{\prime}}R^{q^{\prime}}f_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S})\longrightarrow P_{k+k^{\prime}}R^{q+q^{\prime}}f_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S})~?

Let
(T,{\cal J},\delta)
be a
p
-adic formal PD-scheme and let

T_{0}
be a closed subscheme of
T
defined by
{\cal J}
.
Let
(Y,D)
be a proper smooth scheme with a relative SNCD(=simple normal crossing divisor)
over
T_{0}
. By using the theory in
[
NS
]
,
we can give the analogous problem for
(Y,D)/(T,{\cal J},\delta)
.
In this case, it is very easy to solve the problem affirmatively.
Contrary to this case, the problem for
X/(S,{\cal I},\gamma)
is a serious one.
A filtered complex
(A^{\bullet},P)
representing
the filtered complex
(A_{\rm zar}(X/S),P)

is constructed by a certain log de Rham complex.
Because the wedge product of this de Rham complex
induces a morphism
A^{i}\times A^{j}\longrightarrow A^{i+j+1}
,
this morphism does not even induce the morphism (
1.1.5
)
and hence
we do not know whether the problem above is affirmatively solved.
If
\overset{\circ}{S}
is a
p
-adic formal
{\cal V}
-scheme in the sense of
[
O1
]

(
\overset{\circ}{S}
is a noetherian formal scheme over
{\rm Spf}({\cal V})

with the
p
-adic topology which is topologically of finite type
over
{\rm Spf}({\cal V})
),
where
{\cal V}
is a complete discrete valuation ring of mixed characteristics

(0,p)
with perfect residue field, and if one ignores the torsion in (
1.1.6
),
the problem can be solved affirmatively by
using theory of convergent isocrystals
in
[
O1
]
, using the well-known specialization argument explained
in
[
Nak1
]
,
[
NS
]
and
[
Nak4
]
and using the
purity of the weight
for the crystalline cohomology of a proper smooth scheme
(this classical fact has been proved completely in

[
KM
]
,
[
CL
]
and
[
Nak1
]
with an indispensable work in
[
NY
]
) and
the weight spectral sequence of
R^{q}f_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S})

arising from
(A_{\rm zar}(X/S),P)
.
However we are not satisfied with this (ad hoc) method.
We would like to construct the filtered cup product of a weight-filtered complex
such that the cup product of the underlying complex of this filtered complex
is compatible with the cup product of
R^{q}f_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S})
.

For simplicity, assume that there exists an immersion

X\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal P}
into a log smooth log
p
-adic formal scheme
over
S
in this introduction.
(In the text we do not assume the existence of this immersion.)
Let
{\cal P}^{\rm ex}
be the exactification of this immersion (
[
Sh3
]
).
Let
{\mathfrak{D}}
be the log PD-envelope of the exact closed immersion

X\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal P}^{\rm ex}
over
(S,{\cal I},\gamma)
.
Set

\widetilde{R}u_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}({\cal O}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}):={\cal O}_{\mathfrak{D}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}}\in D^{+}(f^{-1}({\cal O}_{S})).

One can show that

\widetilde{R}u_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}({\cal O}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}})
is independent of
the choice of the immersion
X\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal P}
.
The complex
\widetilde{R}u_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}({\cal O}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}})

is not equal to
Ru_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}({\cal O}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}})

since
{\cal P}^{\rm ex}
is not log smooth over
\overset{\circ}{S}
in general.
In
[
Nak4
]
we have called
\widetilde{R}u_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}({\cal O}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}})
the
modified log crystalline complex of
X/(\overset{\circ}{S},{\cal I},\gamma)
.
Though the notation
\widetilde{R}u_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}({\cal O}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}})
is misleading (because
this complex may depend on the morphism
X\longrightarrow S
),
we use this notation by mimicking the notation
“
W\widetilde{\Omega}_{X}^{\bullet}
” of the log de Rham-Witt complex
in the case where
\overset{\circ}{S}_{0}
is the spectrum of a perfect field of characteristic
p>0
.

Let
\widetilde{t}
be a local section of
M_{S}

whose image
t
in
\overline{M}_{S}:=M_{S}/{\cal O}_{S}^{*}
is the local generator.
Let
U_{S}
be a free
{\cal O}_{S}
-module of rank 1 with a basis
u
:

U_{S}={\cal O}_{S}u
.
Though we do not ask what
u
is, we consider
u
as
“
\log t
” (not “
\log\widetilde{t}
”) in our mind.
For a local section
\widetilde{t}{}^{\prime}=a\widetilde{t}

(a\in{\cal O}_{S}^{*})
of
M_{S}
,
we do not change
U_{S}
, though it may be very strange at first glance.
Let
\Gamma_{{\cal O}_{S}}(U_{S})

be the PD-polynomial algebra generated by
U_{S}
over
{\cal O}_{S}
:

\Gamma_{{\cal O}_{S}}(U_{S}):={\cal O}_{S}\langle u\rangle=\bigoplus_{n=0}^{\infty}{\cal O}_{S}u^{[n]}
.
Because

{\cal O}_{\mathfrak{D}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}}

is a dga over
S
,
we obtain the PD-Hirsch extension

{\cal O}_{\mathfrak{D}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}}\langle u\rangle:=\Gamma_{{\cal O}_{S}}(U_{S})\otimes_{{\cal O}_{S}}{\cal O}_{\mathfrak{D}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}}

of
{\cal O}_{\mathfrak{D}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}}

by using a
f^{-1}({\cal O}_{S})
-linear map

\displaystyle\varphi\colon\Gamma_{{\cal O}_{S}}(U_{S})\owns au^{[n]}\longmapsto au^{[n-1]}d\log\widetilde{t}\in

\displaystyle{\rm Ker}({\cal O}_{\mathfrak{D}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{1}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{\mathfrak{D}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{2}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}})

\displaystyle(a\in f^{-1}({\cal O}_{S})),

which is independent of the choice of
\widetilde{t}
.
(Strictly speaking, we should denote two
\Gamma_{{\cal O}_{S}}(U_{S})
’s above
by
f^{-1}(\Gamma_{{\cal O}_{S}}(U_{S}))
.)
The first result needed for the construction of our desired filtered complex
is the following:

Theorem 1.2
.

Set

\widetilde{R}u_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}({\cal O}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}\langle u\rangle):={\cal O}_{\mathfrak{D}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}}\langle u\rangle\in D^{+}(f^{-1}({\cal O}_{S})).

Then the natural morphism defined by

u^{[n]}\otimes\omega\longmapsto 0

(n\geq 1,\omega\in{\cal O}_{\mathfrak{D}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}})
and
the projection
\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}}\longrightarrow\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/S}

induce the following canonical isomorphism
:

(1.2.1)

\displaystyle\widetilde{R}u_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}({\cal O}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}\langle u\rangle)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}Ru_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S}).

The complex
\widetilde{R}u_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}({\cal O}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}\langle u\rangle)

in the case where
\overset{\circ}{S}_{0}
is a point of characteristic
p

is a log crystalline analogue of the complex

\Omega^{\bullet}_{X/{\mathbb{C}}}[\log\tau]
in (
1.0.3
).
Moreover we can define a filtered complex

(\widetilde{R}u_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}({\cal O}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}\langle u\rangle),P)\in{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(f^{-1}({\cal O}_{S}))

by counting the number of log poles of local sections of

\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{S}}

and by giving the weight
2n
for
u^{[n]}

(n\in{\mathbb{N}})
.
However we see that the graded complex

{\rm gr}^{P}_{0}\widetilde{R}u_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}({\cal O}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}\langle u\rangle)

is “mixed”.
Consequently we need another filtered complex.
Because we can prove that

{\cal P}^{\rm ex}
is a formal SNCL scheme, we
can define
\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(m)}

(m\in{\mathbb{N}})
as in (
1.0.6
).
Let
{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(m)}
be the log formal scheme whose underlying formal scheme is

\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(m)}
and whose log structure
is the inverse image of
{\cal P}^{\rm ex}

by the natural morphisms

\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{(m)}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{\cal P}
.
Because
\{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(m)}\}_{m\in{\mathbb{N}}}
defines
a semi-simplicial log formal scheme,
we can consider the double complex

{\cal O}_{\mathfrak{D}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex}}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}/\overset{\circ}{S}}\langle u\rangle

with appropriate signs and the associated
single complex

s({\cal O}_{\mathfrak{D}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex}}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}/\overset{\circ}{S}}\langle u\rangle)
.
Let
P
be the diagonal filtration (
[
D1
]
) on

s({\cal O}_{\mathfrak{D}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex}}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}/\overset{\circ}{S}}\langle u\rangle)

by counting the number of log poles

\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(m)}/\overset{\circ}{S}}

of local sections of

{\cal O}_{\mathfrak{D}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex}}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(m)}/\overset{\circ}{S}}
for each
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
by giving the weight
2n
for
u^{[n]}
and by raising the
weight according to the semi-simplicial degree of

{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}
.
Then we prove the following:

Theorem 1.3

(
Existence of the PD-Hirsch weight-filtered complex).

Set

(H_{\rm zar}(X/S),P):=(s({\cal O}_{\mathfrak{D}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex}}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}/\overset{\circ}{S}}\langle u\rangle),P)\in{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(f^{-1}({\cal O}_{S})).

The natural morphism
{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(0)}\longrightarrow{\cal P}^{\rm ex}

induces the following isomorphism

(1.3.1)

\displaystyle\widetilde{R}u_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}*}({\cal O}_{X/\overset{\circ}{S}}\langle u\rangle)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}H_{\rm zar}(X/S)

in
D^{+}(f^{-1}({\cal O}_{S}))
.
The graded complex
{\rm gr}_{k}^{P}(H_{\rm zar}(X/S))
is canonically isomorphic to

(1.3.2)

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{m\geq 0}\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\lambda}=m+1}\bigoplus_{j\geq 0}\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\mu}=k+m-2j}

\displaystyle a_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}*}Ru_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}}/\overset{\circ}{S}*}({\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}}/\overset{\circ}{S}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{{\rm crys},\underline{\mu}}(\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}}/\overset{\circ}{S}))(-k-m+j)[-k-2m+2j],

where

\underline{\lambda}:=\{\lambda_{0},\ldots,\lambda_{m}\}
,
\underline{\mu}:=\{\mu_{0},\ldots,\mu_{k+m-2j-1}\}
,

\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}}:=\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda_{0}}\cap\cdots\cap\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda_{m}}\cap\overset{\circ}{X}_{\mu_{0}}\cap\cdots\cap\overset{\circ}{X}_{\mu_{k+m-2j-1}}
,

a_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}}\colon\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overset{\circ}{X}
is the natural closed immersion
and
\varpi_{{\rm crys},\underline{\mu}}(\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}}/\overset{\circ}{S})

is the crystalline orientation sheaf.
The filtered complex
(H_{\rm zar}(X/S),P)

and the isomorphism
(
1.3.1
)

are independent of the choice of the immersion

X\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal P}
over
S
.
If
\overset{\circ}{X}
is quasi-compact, then there exists the following morphism

(1.3.3)

\cup\colon(H_{\rm zar}(X/S),P)\otimes^{L}_{f^{-1}({\cal O}_{S})}(H_{\rm zar}(X/S),P)\longrightarrow(H_{\rm zar}(X/S),P)

in
{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(f^{-1}({\cal O}_{S}))

fitting into the following commutative diagram

(1.3.4)

\begin{CD}H_{\rm zar}(X/S)\otimes^{L}_{f^{-1}({\cal O}_{S})}H_{\rm zar}(X/S)@>{\cup}>{}>H_{\rm zar}(X/S)\\
@A{\simeq}A{}A@A{\simeq}A{}A\\
\widetilde{R}u_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S}\langle u\rangle)\otimes^{L}_{f^{-1}({\cal O}_{S})}\widetilde{R}u_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S}\langle u\rangle)\widetilde{R}u_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S}\langle u\rangle)\\
@V{\simeq}V{}V@V{\simeq}V{}V\\
Ru_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S})\otimes^{L}_{f^{-1}({\cal O}_{S})}Ru_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S})@>{\cup}>{}>Ru_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S}).\end{CD}

Here the lower
\cup
in
(
1.3.4
)
is the usual cup product
of
Ru_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S})
.

We call
(H_{\rm zar}(X/S),P)

the
PD-Hirsch weight-filtered log crystalline complex
of
X/(S,{\cal I},\gamma)
.
By using (
1.2
) and (
1.3
),
we obtain the following:

Corollary 1.4
.

There exists a canonical isomorphism

(1.4.1)

\displaystyle Ru_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}H_{\rm zar}(X/S)

in
D^{+}(f^{-1}({\cal O}_{S}))
.
If
\overset{\circ}{X}
is quasi-compact, then there exists the following convergent
spectral sequence

(1.4.2)

\displaystyle E_{1}^{-k,q+k}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{m\geq 0}\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\lambda}=m+1}\bigoplus_{j\geq 0}\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\mu}=k+m-2j}

\displaystyle R^{q+2j-k-2m}f_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}}/\overset{\circ}{S}*}({\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}}/\overset{\circ}{S}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{{\rm crys},\underline{\mu}}(\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}}/\overset{\circ}{S}))(-k-m+j)\Longrightarrow R^{q}f_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S}).

We should remark that
“the filtration
P
on
H_{\rm zar}(X/S)
”
is not “finite” in general.
However, because the filtration
P
on
H_{\rm zar}(X/S)

is exhaustive and bounded below if
\overset{\circ}{X}
is quasi-compact,
we obtain the convergent weight spectral sequence (
1.4.2
).
If
\overset{\circ}{S}
is a
p
-adic formal
{\cal V}
-scheme, then
we can prove that the spectral sequence (
1.4.2
)
modulo torsion degenerates at
E_{2}
.

Let
P^{\prime}
be the induced filtration on
R^{q}f_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S})
by

(H_{\rm zar}(X/S),P)
:

(1.4.3)

\displaystyle P^{\prime}_{k+q}R^{q}f_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S})

\displaystyle:={\rm Im}(R^{q}f_{X/S*}(P_{k}H_{\rm zar}(X/S))\longrightarrow R^{q}f_{X/S*}(H_{\rm zar}(X/S)))

\displaystyle\simeq{\rm Im}(R^{q}f_{X/S*}(P_{k}H_{\rm zar}(X/S))\longrightarrow R^{q}f_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S})).

As an immediate corollary of (
1.3
) and (
1.4
),
if
\overset{\circ}{X}
is quasi-compact, then
we see that the cup product (
1.1.5
)
induces the following morphism

(1.4.4)

\displaystyle\cup\colon P^{\prime}_{k}R^{q}f_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S})\otimes_{{\cal O}_{S}}P^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}R^{q^{\prime}}f_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S})\longrightarrow P^{\prime}_{k+k^{\prime}}R^{q+q^{\prime}}f_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S}),

which answers the problem (
1.1.6
) affirmatively if one replaces

P
with
P^{\prime}
.

Though we think that there is no direct simple relation
between
(H_{\rm zar}(X/S),P)
and

(A_{\rm zar}(X/S),P)
, we can prove the following:

Theorem 1.5

(
cf.
[
F2
]
).

There exists a filtered morphism

(1.5.1)

\displaystyle\psi\colon(A_{\rm zar}(X/S),P)\longrightarrow(H_{\rm zar}(X/S),P)

such that the underlying morphism

A_{\rm zar}(X/S)\longrightarrow H_{\rm zar}(X/S)

is an isomorphism in
D^{+}(f^{-1}({\cal O}_{S}))

fitting into the following commutative diagram

(1.5.2)

\begin{CD}A_{\rm zar}(X/S)@>{\psi,~\sim}>{}>H_{\rm zar}(X/S)\\
@A{\theta\wedge}A{\simeq}A@A{(\ref{ali:ixuoa})}A{\simeq}A\\
Ru_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S})=Ru_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S}).\end{CD}

The morphism
\psi
is the log crystalline analogue of the morphism constructed by
Fujisawa in
[
F2
]
. (
1.5
) tells us that

P_{k}R^{q}f_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S})\subset P^{\prime}_{k}R^{q}f_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S})
.
Though we do not know whether
P_{k}=P^{\prime}_{k}
on
R^{q}f_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S})
,
we obtain the following:

Theorem 1.6

(
Comparison theorem of weight filtrations).

Let
{\cal V}
be a complete discrete valuation ring of mixed characteristics
(0,p)

with perfect residue field.
Assume that
\overset{\circ}{S}
is a
p
-adic formal
{\cal V}
-scheme.
Let
P
and
P^{\prime}
be the filtrations
(
1.1.4
)
and
(
1.4.3
)

on
R^{q}f_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S})

(q\in{\mathbb{N}})
, respectively.
Then
P\otimes_{\cal V}K=P^{\prime}\otimes_{\cal V}K
.

Next we would like to give relations
between (
1.3
) and preceding results.

Let
s=({\rm Spec}(\kappa),{\mathbb{N}}\oplus\kappa^{*}\longrightarrow\kappa)
be the log point of
\kappa
.
Let
{\cal W}
be the Witt ring of
\kappa
.
Let
{\cal W}(s)
be the canonical lift of
s
over
{\cal W}
of
\kappa
.
Let
u
be a variable over
{\cal W}
.
Let
Y
be a proper SNCL scheme over
s
.
Let
g\colon Y\longrightarrow s\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal W}(s)
be the structural morphism.
Let
{\cal W}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{Y}
and

{\cal W}\widetilde{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{Y}
be the log de Rham-Witt complexes of
Y/\kappa

constructed in
[
M
]
(cf.
[
Hy
]
).
Let
{\cal W}\widetilde{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{Y^{(\bullet)}}
be the semi-simplicial version of

{\cal W}\widetilde{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{Y}
, where
Y^{(\bullet)}
is the semi-simplicial log scheme
whose underlying semi-simplicial scheme is

\{\overset{\circ}{Y}{}^{(k)}\}_{k\in{\mathbb{N}}}
and whose log structure
is the inverse image of
Y
given by the natural morphisms

\overset{\circ}{Y}{}^{(k)}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{Y}
’s.
Let
b^{(\bullet)}\colon Y^{(\bullet)}\longrightarrow Y
be the augmentation morphism.
In
[
KH
]
Kim and Hain have constructed a complex

{\cal W}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\bullet}_{Y}\langle u\rangle
and
a filtered complex
(s(b^{(\bullet)}_{*}({\cal W}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\bullet}_{Y^{(\bullet)}}\langle u\rangle)),P)

(in their article
(s(b^{(\bullet)}_{*}({\cal W}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\bullet}_{Y^{(\bullet)}}\langle u\rangle)),P)

is denoted by
(s(C(W\widetilde{\omega}[u])),P)
).
For simplicity of notation, let us denote

(s(b^{(\bullet)}_{*}({\cal W}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\bullet}_{Y^{(\bullet)}}\langle u\rangle)),P)
by

({\cal W}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\bullet}_{Y^{(*)}}\langle u\rangle,P)
.
We can prove that there exist the following canonical isomorphisms

(1.6.1)

\displaystyle{\cal W}\Omega^{\bullet}_{Y}\overset{\sim}{\longleftarrow}{\cal W}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\bullet}_{Y}\langle u\rangle\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}{\cal W}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\bullet}_{Y^{(*)}}\langle u\rangle

in
D^{+}(g^{-1}({\cal W}))
.
(See (
21.14
) and
(
4.10
) (3) below for the problem for the construction of

({\cal W}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\bullet}_{Y^{(*)}}\langle u\rangle,P)

and the problem for the proof of the existence of the isomorphisms (
1.6.1
).)
In the text we give a simpler construction of

({\cal W}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\bullet}_{X^{(*)}}\langle u\rangle,P)

than Kim and Hain’s construction by replacing the local admissible lift defined in
[
M
]

by the local immersion into a log smooth scheme over
{\rm Spf}({\cal W}\{t\})

with the log structure defined by the ideal
(t)\subset{\cal W}\{t\}
.
We show that the complex
{\cal W}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\bullet}_{Y^{(*)}}\langle u\rangle

has a natural product
\wedge

and the following diagram is commutative:

(1.6.2)

\begin{CD}{\cal W}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\bullet}_{Y^{(*)}}\langle u\rangle\otimes_{g^{-1}({\cal W})}{\cal W}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\bullet}_{Y^{(*)}}\langle u\rangle @>{\wedge}>{}>{\cal W}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\bullet}_{Y^{(*)}}\langle u\rangle\\
@A{\simeq}A{}A@A{\simeq}A{}A\\
{\cal W}\Omega^{\bullet}_{Y}\otimes_{g^{-1}({\cal W})}{\cal W}\Omega^{\bullet}_{Y}@>{\wedge}>{}>{\cal W}\Omega^{\bullet}_{Y}.\end{CD}

In this book we prove the following comparison theorem:

Theorem 1.7

(
Comparison theorem of the filtered complexes).

Let the notations be as in
(
1.3
)
.
Assume that
\overset{\circ}{S}={\rm Spf}({\cal W})
.
Then there exists a canonical isomorphism

(1.7.1)

\displaystyle(H_{\rm zar}(X/S),P)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}({\cal W}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\bullet}_{X^{(*)}}\langle u\rangle,P)

in
{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(f^{-1}({\cal W}))
.
If
\overset{\circ}{X}
is quasi-compact, then the following diagram

(1.7.2)

\begin{CD}(H_{\rm zar}(X/S),P)\otimes^{L}_{f^{-1}({\cal O}_{S})}(H_{\rm zar}(X/S),P)@>{\cup}>{}>H_{\rm zar}(X/S)\\
@V{\simeq}V{}V@V{}V{\simeq}V\\
{\cal W}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\bullet}_{X^{(*)}}\langle u\rangle\otimes_{f^{-1}({\cal W})}{\cal W}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\bullet}_{X^{(*)}}\langle u\rangle @>{\wedge}>{}>{\cal W}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\bullet}_{X^{(*)}}\langle u\rangle\end{CD}

is commutative.
(
Note that

{\cal W}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\bullet}_{X^{(*)}}\langle u\rangle
is
a complex of flat
f^{-1}({\cal W})
-modules.
)

The underlying isomorphism
H_{\rm zar}(X/S)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}{\cal W}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\bullet}_{X^{(*)}}\langle u\rangle

and the isomorphism

Ru_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}{\cal W}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X}

in
[
HK
]
and
[
Nak1
]

give the compatibility of
(
1.3.4
)

with
(
1.6.2
)
in the obvious sense.

Consequently we see that
our filtered complex
(H_{\rm zar}(X/S),P)
is
a generalization of
({\cal W}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\bullet}_{Y^{(*)}}\langle u\rangle,P)

to the case where the base log scheme is more general.

On the other hand, in
[
F2
]

Fujisawa has defined a filtered complex
(K_{\mathbb{C}},W)
for
a proper SNCL scheme
X
over the log point
O
.
In fact, he has defined a mixed Hodge complex for
X/O

under the assumption that the analytifications
of the irreducible components of
\overset{\circ}{X}
are Kähler.
Our filtered complex
(H_{\rm zar}(X/S),P)

is the log crystalline analogue of a generalization of

(K_{\mathbb{C}},W)
.

Next we would like to raise another problem.
Assume that the relative dimension of

\overset{\circ}{X}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{S}_{0}
is of pure dimension
d
.
Let
L
be a relatively ample line bundle on

\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}_{0}
.
As in
[
BO2
, §3]
,
we obtain the Chern class

\eta:=c_{1,{\rm crys}}(L)
of
L

in
R^{2}f_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S})
.
(In the text we recall the construction of
\eta
.)
Stimulated by a letter of K. Kato to me (
[
K3
]
),
I have conjectured the following in
[
Nak4
]
:

Conjecture 1.8

(
p
-adic variational filtered log hard Lefschetz conjecture).

(1)
The following cup product

(1.8.1)

\eta^{i}\colon R^{d-i}f_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}\longrightarrow R^{d+i}f_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}

is an isomorphism.

(2)

In fact,
\eta^{i}
is the following isomorphism of filtered sheaves:

(1.8.2)

\eta^{i}\colon(R^{d-i}f_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}},P)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}((R^{d+i}f_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}})(i),P).

Here
(i)
means the Tate twist:

P_{k}(R^{d+i}f_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}})(i))=P_{k+2i}R^{d+i}f_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}.

In
[
Nak2
]
and
[
Nak4
]
we have proved that (
1.8
) (1) is true
if there exists an exact closed point
s
of each connected component of
S

such that
X_{s}
is the log special fiber of
a projective strict semistable family over a complete discrete valuation ring
of any characteristic.
However we have proved nothing about (
1.8
) (2) in [loc. cit.].

In this book we prove the following:

Theorem 1.9
.

If
\overset{\circ}{S}
is a
p
-adic formal
{\cal V}
-scheme,
then
(
1.8
) (1)
implies
(
1.8
) (2)
.

Though the
p
-adic monodromy-weight conjecture
in mixed characteristics in
[
M
]

has not been solved yet in general
(the conjecture is solved in various special cases),
we can prove (
1.8
) (2) for the special fiber of a projective semistable family
in mixed characteristics by combining (
1.9
) and the result for (
1.8
) (1):

Theorem 1.10
.

If there exists a point
s
of each connected component of
S

such that
X_{s}
is the log special fiber of
a projective strict semistable family over a complete discrete valuation ring,
then the conjecture
(
1.8
) (2)
is true.

We obtain the following as a corollary of (
1.10
).

Corollary 1.11

(
Lefschetz decomposition with weight filtrations).

Let the assumptions be as in
(
1.10
)
.
Let
k
and
i
be a nonnegative integers.
Set

(1.11.1)

\displaystyle(P_{k}R^{d-i}f_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}})_{0}:={\rm Ker}(\eta^{i+1}\colon

\displaystyle P_{k}R^{d-i}f_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow P_{k}\{R^{d+i+2}f_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}})(i+1)).

Moreover, assume that
\overset{\circ}{S}
is connected.
Let
b_{k}^{q}
be the rank of locally free sheaf

P_{k}R^{q}f_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}
on
\overset{\circ}{S}
.
Then

\displaystyle P_{k}R^{q}f_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}=

\displaystyle(P_{k}R^{q}f_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}})_{0}\oplus\eta(P_{k-2}R^{q-2}f_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}})_{0}

\displaystyle\oplus\eta(P_{k-4}R^{q-2}f_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}})_{0}\oplus\cdots.

Consequently

b_{k}^{0}\leq b_{k+2}^{2}\leq b_{k+4}^{4}\leq\cdots

up to
b_{*}^{d}
and

b_{k}^{1}\leq b_{k+2}^{3}\leq b_{k+4}^{5}\leq\cdots

up to
b_{*}^{d}
.
In particular,

b_{\infty}^{0}\leq b_{\infty}^{2}\leq b_{\infty}^{4}\leq\cdots

up to
b_{\infty}^{d}
and

b_{\infty}^{1}\leq b_{\infty}^{3}\leq b_{\infty}^{5}\leq\cdots

up to
b_{\infty}^{d}
.

Let the notations be as in (
1.6
).
Set
{\cal K}_{S}:={\cal O}_{S}\otimes_{\cal V}K
.
For an
{\cal O}_{S}
-module
{\cal M}
, denote

{\cal M}\otimes_{\cal V}K
by
{\cal M}_{K}
.
Assume that any smooth component
\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda}

is projective over
\overset{\circ}{S}
.
Assume also that
\overset{\circ}{X}
is of relative pure dimension
d
over
\overset{\circ}{S}
.
Then, by using the trace morphism constructed in
[
O1
]
,
we define the following trace morphism

(1.11.2)

\displaystyle{\rm Tr}_{X/S}\colon R^{2d}f_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S})_{K}\longrightarrow{\cal K}_{S}(-d).

Our construction of the trace morphism (
1.11.2
) is different from
the log crystalline analogue of Fujisawa’s trace morphism and
it is simpler than Fujisawa’s trace morphism in the case where the base field
is the complex number field (
[
F2
]
).

Theorem 1.12
.

Let the notations be as in
(
1.6
)
.
Assume that
\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda}
for any
\lambda
is projective over
\overset{\circ}{S}

and that
\overset{\circ}{X}
is of pure dimension
d
over
\overset{\circ}{S}
.
Then the following composite morphism

(1.12.1)

\displaystyle\langle~.~\rangle:={\rm Tr}_{X/S}\circ(~\cup~)\colon R^{q}f_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S})_{K}\otimes_{{\cal K}_{S}}R^{2d-q}f_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S})_{K}\longrightarrow{\cal K}_{S}(-d)

is a perfect pairing and it is
strictly compatible with the weight filtration, that is,
the following isomorphism induced by the pairing
\langle~,~\rangle

(1.12.2)

\displaystyle R^{q}f_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S})_{K}\longrightarrow{\cal H}{\it om}_{{\cal K}_{S}}(R^{2d-q}f_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S})_{K},{\cal K}_{S}(-d))

is an isomorphism of filtered
{\cal K}_{S}
-modules with respect to
P
.

As is well-known, in the complex analytic case, the (log) polarization on
the (mixed) Hodge structure is obviously an indispensable notion for the moduli
problem of polarized (mixed) Hodge structures
(
[
Gr
]
,
[
U
]
,
[
KU
]
).
To define the (log) polarization, we obviously need the (positive definite)
bilinear forms for cohomologies.
Especially we need the product structure for cohomologies
which behaves well with respect to the weight filtration,
the monodromy operator and the cohomology of an ample line bundle.
Using (
1.12
) and (
1.10
),
we obtain the following bilinear form on the primitive part of
the log crystalline cohomology sheaf of
X/(S,{\cal I},\gamma)

for
q\leq d
if
\overset{\circ}{X}
is projective over
\overset{\circ}{S}

as in the classical case:

\displaystyle(P_{k}R^{d-q}f_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S})_{K})_{0}\otimes_{{\cal K}_{S}}(P_{k}R^{d-q}f_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S})_{K})_{0}

\displaystyle\overset{{\rm id}\otimes\eta^{q}}{\longrightarrow}(P_{k}R^{d-q}f_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S})_{K})_{0}\otimes_{{\cal K}_{S}}R^{d+q}f_{X/S*}({\cal O}_{X/S})_{K}(q)\overset{\cup}{\longrightarrow}{\cal K}_{S}(-(d-q)).

Lastly in this introduction, we give the dga version of
(H_{\rm zar}(X/S),P)
.

Let the notations be as in (
1.3
).
Set
{\cal K}_{S}:={\cal O}_{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}
.
Let
{\rm A}^{\geq 0}{\rm F}(f^{-1}({\cal K}_{S}))

be the category of
filtered positively-graded graded commutative dga’s over
f^{-1}({\cal K}_{S})

and let
{\rm Ho}({\rm A}^{\geq 0}{\rm F}(f^{-1}({\cal K}_{S})))

be the localized category of

{\rm A}^{\geq 0}{\rm F}(f^{-1}({\cal K}_{S}))

inverting the weakly equivalent filtered isomorphisms
in
{\rm A}^{\geq 0}{\rm F}(f^{-1}({\cal K}_{S}))

(cf.
[
Nav
]
,
[
GeM
]
).
Let

\displaystyle\Phi\colon{\rm Ho}({\rm A}^{\geq 0}{\rm F}(f^{-1}({\cal K}_{S})))\longrightarrow{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(f^{-1}({\cal K}_{S}))

be an obvious forgetful functor.
In the text we prove the following:

Theorem 1.13
.

There exists an object

(H_{{\rm zar},{\rm TW}}(X/S),P)\in{\rm Ho}({\rm A}^{\geq 0}{\rm F}(f^{-1}({\cal K}_{S})))

such that

\Phi((H_{{\rm zar},{\rm TW}}(X/S),P))=(H_{\rm zar}(X/S),P)\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}.

We call
(H_{{\rm zar},{\rm TW}}(X/S),P)

the
Hirsch weight-filtered log crystalline dga
of
X/(S,{\cal I},\gamma)
.
Because this book is long enough as a book,
we do not give an application of

(H_{{\rm zar},{\rm TW}}(X/S),P)
for
the rational homotopy theory of a proper SNCL scheme
in characteristic
p>0
in this book unlike
[
KH
]
.

Notations.

(1) For a log (formal) scheme
X
in the sense of
Fontaine-Illusie-Kato (
[
K1
]
,
[
K2
]
),
we denote by
\overset{\circ}{X}
(resp.
M_{X}=(M_{X},\alpha_{X})
)
the underlying (formal) scheme (resp. the log structure) of
X
.
In this book
we consider the log structure on the Zariski site
on
\overset{\circ}{X}
.
We denote
M_{X}/{\cal O}_{X}^{*}
by
\overline{M}_{X}
.
For a closed point
\overset{\circ}{x}
of
\overset{\circ}{X}
,
we endow
\overset{\circ}{x}
with the inverse image of
the log structure of
M_{X}
and we denote it by

x
and call it an exact closed point of
X
.
More generally, for a quasi-coherent ideal

{\cal J}
of
{\cal O}_{X}
, we denote
by
X~{\rm mod}~{\cal J}
the exact closed log subscheme of
X

defined by
{\cal J}
.
For a morphism
f\colon X\longrightarrow Y
of log schemes,

\overset{\circ}{f}
denotes the underlying morphism

\overset{\circ}{X}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{Y}
of
f
.

(2) For a (formal) scheme
T

and a commutative monoid
P
with
unit element
e
, we denote by

(T,P\oplus{\cal O}_{T}^{*})
a log (formal) scheme
whose underlying (formal) scheme is
T
and whose log structure is the
association of a morphism

P\owns x\longmapsto 0\in{\cal O}_{T}

(x\not=e)

with natural morphism
P\oplus{\cal O}_{T}^{*}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{T}

of sheaves of monoids in
X_{\rm zar}
.

(3) For a commutative monoid
P
with unit element and
for a commutative ring
A
with unit element,

{\rm Spec}^{\log}(A[P])
is, by definition, the log scheme
whose underlying scheme is
{\rm Spec}(A[P])

and whose log structure is the association of the natural
inclusion
P\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}A[P]
. If
A
has
an
I
-adic topology (
I
is an ideal of
A
), then

A\{P\}
denotes
\varprojlim_{n}(A/I^{n}[P])
and

{\rm Spf}^{\log}(A\{P\})
denotes the log scheme
whose underlying scheme is
{\rm Spf}(\varprojlim_{n}(A/I^{n}[P]))

and whose log structure is the association of the natural
inclusion
P\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}A\{P\}
.

(4)
For a morphism
Y\longrightarrow T
of log
schemes, we denote by

{\Omega}^{i}_{Y/T}
(
=\omega^{i}_{Y/T}
in

[
K1
]
)
(i\in{\mathbb{N}})

the sheaf of relative logarithmic differential
forms on
Y/T

of degree
i
.
For a log point
s
whose underlying scheme is
the spectrum of a perfect field of characteristic
p>0
and
for a morphism
Y\longrightarrow s
of fine log schemes,

{\cal W}_{n}{\Omega}^{i}_{Y}(=W_{n}\omega^{i}_{Y}
in

[

]
and
[
M
]
)
(n\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1},i\in{\mathbb{N}})

denotes the log de Rham-Witt sheaf of logarithmic differential
forms on
Y/s
of degree
i
.

(5) (S)NCL=(simple) normal crossing log,
(S)NCD=(simple) normal crossing divisor.

(6) For a module
M
over a commutative ring
A

with unit element and for a commutative

-algebra

.

(7) For a log scheme
T
and

{\cal J}
of

(resp.

\underline{\rm Spec}_{\overset{\circ}{T}}({\cal O}_{T}/{\cal J})

(resp.

\underline{\rm Spec}_{\overset{\circ}{T}}({\cal O}_{T}/{\cal J})_{\rm red})

and whose log structure is the inverse image of

As already stated in (1), we denote

\underline{\rm Spec}^{\log}_{T}({\cal O}_{T}/{\cal J})

by

(8) By following
[

,
for a fine log formal scheme
S
with

S_{1}
and
S_{0}

and
\underline{\rm Spec}^{\log}_{S}({\cal O}_{S}/p{\cal O}_{S})_{\rm red}
,
respectively, and whose log structures are the inverse image of the log structure of

.

(9) Let
(T,{\cal J},\delta)
be a fine log PD-scheme with quasi-coherent PD-ideal sheaf

{\cal J}
of

and divided power structure

g\colon Y\longrightarrow(T~{\rm mod}~{\cal J})

In the previous articles

(resp.

For a scheme
Z

by
{\rm Zar}(Z)
and the associated topos to

{\rm Zar}(Z)
by
Z_{\rm zar}
.

(T,{\cal J},\delta)

(11) For a ringed topos
({\cal T},{\cal A})

(resp.

the category of bounded below

(resp. the derived category of bounded below

Let

{\rm C}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal A})

(resp.
{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal A})
)
be the category of

{\cal A}
-modules
(resp. the derived category of

{\cal A}
-modules).

(12) For a short exact sequence

0\longrightarrow E^{\bullet}\overset{f}{\longrightarrow}F^{\bullet}\overset{g}{\longrightarrow}G^{\bullet}\longrightarrow 0

of bounded below complexes of objects of
{\rm C}^{+}({\cal A})
,
let
{\rm MC}(f):=E^{\bullet}[1]\oplus F^{\bullet}
be the mapping cone of
f
.
We fix an isomorphism
“
E^{\bullet}[1]\oplus F^{\bullet}\owns(x,y)\longmapsto g(y)\in G^{\bullet}
”
in the derived category
D^{+}({\cal A})
.

Let
{\rm MF}(g):=F^{\bullet}\oplus G^{\bullet}[-1]
be
the mapping fiber of
g
.
We fix an isomorphism
“
E^{\bullet}\owns x\longmapsto(f(x),0)\in F^{\bullet}\oplus G^{\bullet}[-1]
”
in the derived category
D^{+}({\cal A})
.

(13) For a morphism
f\colon E^{\bullet}\longrightarrow F^{\bullet}

of complexes, we identify

{\rm MF}(f)=E^{\bullet}\oplus F^{\bullet}[-1]
and

{\rm MC}(f)[-1]=E^{\bullet}\oplus F^{\bullet}[-1]

with the following isomorphism
“
{\rm MF}(f)\owns(x,y)\longmapsto(-x,y)\in{\rm MC}(f)[-1]
”.

(14) For a semi-cosimplicial complex
A^{\bullet\bullet}
in an abelian category
(the first
\bullet
is the semi-cosimplicial degree and the second

\bullet
is the complex degree),
the boundary morphisms of
A^{\bullet\bullet}
are the following:

\begin{CD}\cdots @>{}>{}>\cdots @>{}>{}>\cdots @>{}>{}>\cdots\\
@A{d}A{}A@A{-d}A{}A@A{d}A{}A\\
A^{0,m+1}@>{}>{}>A^{1,m+1}@>{}>{}>A^{1,m+2}@>{}>{}>\cdots\\
@A{d}A{}A@A{-d}A{}A@A{d}A{}A\\
A^{0m}@>{}>{}>A^{1m}@>{}>{}>A^{1,m+2}@>{}>{}>\cdots\\
@A{d}A{}A@A{-d}A{}A@A{d}A{}A\\
\cdots @>{}>{}>\cdots @>{}>{}>\cdots @>{}>{}>\cdots,\\
\end{CD}

where the horizontal morphisms
are standard Čech morphisms.

(15) For a ringed topos
({\cal T},{\cal A})
and
for two complexes
(A^{\bullet},d^{\bullet})
and
(B^{\bullet},d^{\bullet})

of
{\cal A}
-modules, the boundary morphism
d=\{d^{n}\}_{n\in{\mathbb{Z}}}
of

A^{\bullet}\otimes_{\cal A}B^{\bullet}
is defined as usual:

d^{n}=\sum_{p+q=n}(d^{p}\otimes{\rm id}+(-1)^{p}{\rm id}\otimes d^{q}).

Conventions.

We omit the second “log” in the word
“log smooth log scheme”.
Following
[
O2
]
,
we say that a morphism
X\longrightarrow Y
of log schemes is solid if the log
structure of
X
is the inverse image of that of
Y
.

2
SNCL schemes

In this section we recall the definition of
a
(formal) SNCL(=simple normal crossing log) scheme
in
[
Nak4
]
.

Let
S
be a log (formal) scheme whose log structure
is
{\mathbb{N}}\oplus{\cal O}_{S}^{*}
with an augmentation

{\mathbb{N}}\owns x\longmapsto 0\in{\cal O}_{S}

(x\not=0)
.
We have called this log structure
the
free hollow log structure of rank
1
on
\overset{\circ}{S}
. This is a special case of
the hollow constant log structure defined in

[
O2
, Definition 4]
.

More generally, we consider a fine log (formal) scheme
S

such that there exists an open covering

S=\bigcup_{i\in I}S_{i}
such that
M_{S_{i}}

is the free hollow log structure of rank 1.
In
[
Nak4
]
we have called
S

a
(
formal
)
family of log points
.
When
(\overset{\circ}{S},{\cal I},\gamma)
is
a (formal) PD-scheme with quasi-coherent PD-ideal sheaf
and PD-structure,
we call
(S,{\cal I},\gamma)
a
(
formal
)
PD-family of log points
.

Let
S=\bigcup_{i\in I}S_{i}
be an open covering
of
S
such that

M_{S_{i}}/{\cal O}^{*}_{S_{i}}\simeq{\mathbb{N}}
.
Take a local section

t_{i}\in\Gamma(S_{i},M_{S})

(i\in I)
such that
the image of
t_{i}
in
\Gamma(S_{i},M_{S}/{\cal O}^{*}_{S})
is a generator.
Set
S_{ij}:=S_{i}\cap S_{j}
.
Then there exists a unique section

u_{ji}\in\Gamma(S_{ij},{\cal O}^{*}_{S})
such that

t_{j}|_{S_{ij}}=u_{ji}(t_{i}|_{S_{ij}})

in
\Gamma(S_{ij},M_{S})
.
First consider the case
where
\overset{\circ}{S}
is a scheme (not a formal scheme).
Consider the scheme

{\mathbb{A}}^{1}_{\overset{\circ}{S}_{i}}=\underline{\rm Spec}_{\overset{\circ}{S}_{i}}({\cal O}_{S_{i}}[\tau_{i}])

and the log scheme

({\mathbb{A}}^{1}_{\overset{\circ}{S}_{i}},({\mathbb{N}}\owns 1\longmapsto\tau_{i}\in{\cal O}_{S_{i}}[\tau_{i}])^{a})
.
Denote this log scheme by
\overline{S}_{i}
.
Then, by patching
\overline{S}_{i}
and
\overline{S}_{j}
along

\overline{S}_{ij}:=\overline{S}_{i}\cap\overline{S}_{j}
by the equation

\tau_{j}|_{\overline{S}_{ij}}=u_{ji}\tau_{i}|_{\overline{S}_{ij}}
,
we have a log scheme
\overline{S}=\bigcup_{i\in I}\overline{S}_{i}
.
The ideal sheaves
\tau_{i}{\cal O}_{\overline{S}_{i}}
’s (
i\in I
)
patch together and we denote by

{\cal I}_{\overline{S}}

the resulting ideal sheaf of

{\cal O}_{\overline{S}}
.
The isomorphism class of the log scheme
\overline{S}

is independent of the choice of the system of
generators
\tau_{i}
’s.
We see that the isomorphism class
of the log scheme
\overline{S}
and the ideal sheaf

{\cal I}_{\overline{S}}

are also independent of
the choice of the open covering
S=\bigcup_{i\in I}S_{i}
.
The natural morphism
\overline{S}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{S}
is log smooth
by the criterion of the log smoothness (
[
K1
, (3.5)]
).
For a log scheme
Y
over
\overline{S}
,
we denote

{\cal I}_{\overline{S}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\overline{S}}}{\cal O}_{Y}

by
{\cal I}_{Y}
by abuse of notation.
If
\overset{\circ}{Y}
is flat over
\overset{\circ}{\overline{S}}
,
then
{\cal I}_{Y}
can be considered as
an ideal sheaf of
{\cal O}_{Y}
.
When
\overset{\circ}{S}
is a formal scheme with ideal sheaf of definition

{\cal J}
, we obtain the analogue of
the object
\overline{S}
above, which we denote by
\overline{S}
again.

By killing
{\cal I}_{\overline{S}}
,
we have a natural exact closed immersion

S\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{S}
over
\overset{\circ}{S}
.

In
[
Nak4
, (1.1.5), (1.1.6), (1.1.7)]
we have proved
the following two easy propositions:

Proposition 2.1

(
A special case of
[
Nak4
, (1.1.5)]
).

Let
u\colon S\longrightarrow S^{\prime}
be a morphism of families of log points.
Then
u
induces a morphism

\overline{u}\colon\overline{S}\longrightarrow\overline{S}{}^{\prime}

fitting into the following commutative diagram

\begin{CD}S@>{u}>{}>S^{\prime}\\
@V{\bigcap}V{}V@V{}V{\bigcap}V\\
\overline{S}@>{\overline{u}}>{}>\overline{S}{}^{\prime}.\end{CD}

Let
B
be a scheme.
For two nonnegative integers
a
and
d
such that

a\leq d
,
consider the following scheme

\overset{\circ}{\mathbb{A}}_{B}(a,d):=\underline{{\rm Spec}}_{B}({\cal O}_{B}[x_{0},\ldots,x_{d}]/(\prod_{i=0}^{a}x_{i})).

Definition 2.2

(
[
Nak4
, (1.1.9)]
).

Let
Z
be a scheme over
B

with structural morphism
g\colon Z\longrightarrow B
.
We call
Z
an

SNC
(
=simple normal crossing
)
scheme
over

B
if
Z
is a union of smooth schemes

\{Z_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
over
B
(
\Lambda
is a set)
and if, for any point of
z\in Z
,
there exist an open neighborhood
V
of

z
and an open neighborhood
W
of

g(z)
such that
there exists an étale morphism

V\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{\mathbb{A}}_{W}(a,d)

such that

(2.2.1)

\{Z_{\lambda}|_{V}\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}=\{\{x_{i}=0\}\}_{i=0}^{a},

where
a
and
d
are nonnegative integers
such that
a\leq d
,
which depend on
zariskian local neighborhoods in
Z
.
Here
\{Z_{\lambda}|_{V}\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
means the set of

Z_{\lambda}|_{V}
’s such that
Z_{\lambda}|_{V}\not=\emptyset
by abuse of notation.
We call the set
\{Z_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}

a
decomposition of
Z
by smooth components of

Z
over
B
.
We call
Z_{\lambda}
a

smooth component
of
Z
over
B
.

Set
\Delta:=\{Z_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
.
For an open subscheme
V
of
Z
,
set
\Delta_{V}:=\{Z_{\lambda}|_{V}\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
.

In
[
Nak4
]

we have proved the following proposition:

Proposition 2.3

(
[
Nak4
, (1.1.11)]
).

Let
\Delta
and
\Delta^{\prime}
be decompositions of
Z

by smooth components of
Z
.
Then, for any point
z\in Z
,
there exists an open neighborhood
V
of

z
in
Z
such that
\Delta_{V}=\Delta^{\prime}_{V}
.

Let
P(\Lambda)
be a category whose objects are
nonempty subsets of
\Lambda
and whose morphisms
are inclusions of subsets of
\Lambda
.
In
[
F2
, (2.4)]

this category has been denoted by
{\cal S}^{+}(\Lambda)
.
For a nonnegative integer
m
and an object

\underline{\lambda}=\{\lambda_{0},\cdots,\lambda_{m}\}\in P(\Lambda)

(\lambda_{i}\not=\lambda_{j}~{\rm if}~i\not=j,\lambda_{i}\in\Lambda)
,
set

(2.3.1)

Z_{\underline{\lambda}}:=Z_{\lambda_{0}}\cap\cdots\cap Z_{\lambda_{m}}.

Set

(2.3.2)

Z^{(m)}:=\underset{\#\underline{\lambda}=m+1}{\coprod}Z_{\underline{\lambda}}

for
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
and

Z_{\phi}=Z
(
\phi
is the empty set) and
Z^{(-1)}=Z
.
We also set
Z^{(m)}=\emptyset
for
m\leq-2
.

In
[
Nak4
]
we have proved the following
by using (
2.3
):

Proposition 2.4

(
[
Nak4
, (1.1.12)]
).

The scheme
Z^{(m)}

is independent of the choice of
\Delta
.

We have the natural morphism

b^{(m)}\colon Z^{(m)}\longrightarrow Z
.

As in
[
D1
, (3.1.4)]
and
[
NS
, (2.2.18)]
,
we have an orientation sheaf
\varpi^{(m)}_{\rm zar}(Z/B)

(m\in{\mathbb{N}})
in
Z^{(m)}_{\rm zar}

associated to the set
\Delta
.
If
B
is a closed subscheme of
B^{\prime}
defined by
a quasi-coherent nil-ideal sheaf
{\cal J}

which has a PD-structure
\delta
,
then
\varpi^{(m)}_{\rm zar}(Z/B)

extends to an abelian sheaf

\varpi^{(m)}_{\rm crys}(Z/B^{\prime})
in

(Z^{(m)}/(B^{\prime},{\cal J},\delta))_{\rm crys}
.

Assume that
S
is a scheme until (
2.15
)
and that
M_{S}
is the free hollow log structure of rank 1
for the time being.
We fix an isomorphism

\displaystyle(M_{S},\alpha_{S})\simeq({\mathbb{N}}\oplus{\cal O}_{S}^{*}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{S})

globally on
S
.
Let
M_{S}(a,d)
be the log structure on

{\mathbb{A}}_{\overset{\circ}{S}}(a,d)
associated to the following morphism

(2.4.1)

{\mathbb{N}}^{{\oplus}(a+1)}\owns(0,\ldots,0,\overset{i}{1},0,\ldots,0)\longmapsto x_{i-1}\in{\cal O}_{S}[x_{0},\ldots,x_{d}]/(\prod_{i=0}^{a}x_{i}).

Let
{\mathbb{A}}_{S}(a,d)
be the resulting log scheme over
S
.
The diagonal morphism
{\mathbb{N}}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{N}}^{{\oplus}(a+1)}

induces a morphism
{\mathbb{A}}_{S}(a,d)\longrightarrow S
of log schemes.

Definition 2.5
.

Let
S
be a family of log points
(we do not assume that
M_{S}
is free).
Let
f\colon X(=(\overset{\circ}{X},M_{X}))\longrightarrow S

be a morphism of log schemes
such that
\overset{\circ}{X}
is
an SNC scheme over
\overset{\circ}{S}

with a decomposition

\Delta:=\{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}

of
\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}
by its smooth components.
We call
f
(or
X/S
) an

SNCL
(
=simple normal crossing log
)
scheme
if,
for any point of
x\in\overset{\circ}{X}
,
there exist an open neighborhood
\overset{\circ}{V}
of

x
and an open neighborhood
\overset{\circ}{W}
of

\overset{\circ}{f}(x)
such that
M_{W}
is
the free hollow log structure of rank 1
and such that
f|_{V}
factors through
a solid and étale morphism

V{\longrightarrow}{\mathbb{A}}_{W}(a,d)

such that
\Delta_{\overset{\circ}{V}}=\{x_{i}=0\}_{i=0}^{a}
in
\overset{\circ}{V}
.
(Similarly we can give the definition of
a formal SNCL scheme over
S

in the case where
\overset{\circ}{S}
is a formal scheme.)

Let
X
be a (formal) SNCL scheme over
S

with a decomposition

\Delta:=\{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}

of
\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}

by its smooth components.
For an element
\lambda
of
\Lambda
,
let
a_{\lambda}\colon\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overset{\circ}{X}

be the natural closed immersion.
For an element

\underline{\lambda}=\{\lambda_{0},\cdots,\lambda_{m}\}

(\lambda_{i}\not=\lambda_{j}~{\rm if}~i\not=j,\lambda_{i}\in\Lambda)
of
P(\Lambda)
,
endow
\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}}
with the inverse image of
the log structure of
X
by
the natural morphism
\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{X}

and let
X_{\underline{\lambda}}
be the resulting log scheme.
We set
X_{\emptyset}:=X
for convenience of notation.
For an integer
m\geq-1
,
let
X^{(m)}
be the log scheme
whose underlying scheme
is
\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(m)}
and
whose log structure is the inverse image of the log structure of
X

by the natural morphism
\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(m)}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{X}
.
For a negative integer
m\leq-2
,
set
X^{(m)}=\emptyset
.
Let
a_{\underline{\lambda}}\colon X_{\underline{\lambda}}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}X

and
a^{(m)}\colon X^{(m)}\longrightarrow X

be the natural exact closed immersion and the natural morphism.

Because the structural morphism
X\longrightarrow S
is integral,

X_{T}:=X\times_{S}T
is also integral for a morphism

T\longrightarrow S
of fine log schemes. Note that
(X_{T})^{\circ}=\overset{\circ}{X}\times_{\overset{\circ}{S}}\overset{\circ}{T}
.
We denote
(X_{T})^{\circ}
by
\overset{\circ}{X}_{T}
.

Assume that
M_{S}
is the free hollow log structure of rank 1.
Then
\overline{S}=({\mathbb{A}}^{1}_{\overset{\circ}{S}},({\mathbb{N}}\owns 1\longmapsto t\in{\cal O}_{S}[t])^{a})
.
Set

\overset{\circ}{\mathbb{A}}_{\overline{S}}(a,d):=\underline{\rm Spec}_{\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal O}_{S}[x_{0},\ldots,x_{d},t]/(x_{0}\cdots x_{a}-t)).

Then we have a natural structural morphism

\overset{\circ}{{\mathbb{A}}}_{\overline{S}}(a,d)\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{\overline{S}}
.
Let
\overline{M}_{\overline{S}}(a,d)

be the log structure associated to
a morphism

{\mathbb{N}}^{a+1}\owns e_{i}=(0,\ldots,0,\overset{i}{1},0,\ldots,0)\longmapsto x_{i-1}\in{\cal O}_{S}[x_{0},\ldots,x_{d},t]/(x_{0}\cdots x_{a}-t)
.
Set

{\mathbb{A}}_{\overline{S}}(a,d):=(\underline{\rm Spec}_{\overset{\circ}{S}}({\cal O}_{S}[x_{0},\ldots,x_{d},t]/(x_{0}\cdots x_{a}-t)),\overline{M}_{\overline{S}}(a,d)).

Then we have the following natural morphism

(2.5.1)

{\mathbb{A}}_{\overline{S}}(a,d)\longrightarrow\overline{S}.

By killing “
t
”, we have the following natural exact closed immersion

(2.5.2)

{\mathbb{A}}_{S}(a,d)\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\mathbb{A}}_{\overline{S}}(a,d)

of fs(=fine and saturated) log schemes
over
S\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{S}

if the log structure of
S
is
the free hollow log structure of rank 1.

In
[
Nak4
]
we have proved the following (1)
as a special case of [loc. cit., (1.1.6)]
(the following (2) is a special case of (1)):

Lemma 2.6

(
A special case of
[
Nak4
, (1.1.6)]
).

Let
S
be a family of log points. Then the following hold
:

(1)
Let
Y\longrightarrow S
be a log smooth scheme
which has a global chart
{\mathbb{N}}\longrightarrow P
.
Then, Zariski locally on
Y
,
there exists a log smooth scheme

\overline{Y}
over
\overline{S}
fitting into
the following cartesian diagram

(2.6.1)

\begin{CD}Y@>{\subset}>{}>\overline{Y}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
S\times_{{\rm Spec}^{\log}({\mathbb{Z}}[{\mathbb{N}}])}{\rm Spec}^{\log}({\mathbb{Z}}[P])@>{\subset}>{}>\overline{S}\times_{{\rm Spec}^{\log}({\mathbb{Z}}[{\mathbb{N}}])}{\rm Spec}^{\log}({\mathbb{Z}}[P])\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
S@>{\subset}>{}>\overline{S},\end{CD}

where the vertical morphism

\overline{Y}\longrightarrow\overline{S}\times_{{\rm Spec}^{\log}({\mathbb{Z}}[{\mathbb{N}}])}{\rm Spec}^{\log}({\mathbb{Z}}[P])

is solid and étale.

(2)
Let
S
be a family of log points
and let
X
be an SNCL scheme over
S
.
Zariski locally on
X
, there exists a log scheme

\overline{X}
over
\overline{S}
fitting into
the following cartesian diagram

(2.6.2)

\begin{CD}X@>{\subset}>{}>\overline{X}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
{\mathbb{A}}_{S}(a,d)@>{\subset}>{}>{\mathbb{A}}_{\overline{S}}(a,d)\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
S@>{\subset}>{}>\overline{S},\end{CD}

where the vertical morphism

\overline{X}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{A}}_{\overline{S}}(a,d)
is solid and étale.

Definition 2.7

(
A special case of
[
Nak4
, (1.1.16)]
).

Let
\overline{f}\colon\overline{X}\longrightarrow\overline{S}

be a morphism of log schemes (on the Zariski sites).
Set
X:=\overline{X}\times_{\overline{S}}S
.
We call
\overline{f}
(or
\overline{X}/\overline{S}
)
a
strict semistable log scheme
over
\overline{S}

if
\overset{\circ}{\overline{X}}
is a smooth scheme over
\overset{\circ}{S}
,
if
\overset{\circ}{X}
is a relative SNCD on

\overset{\circ}{\overline{X}}/\overset{\circ}{\overline{S}}

(with some decomposition

\Delta:=\{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
of

\overset{\circ}{X}
by smooth components of

\overset{\circ}{X}
over
\overset{\circ}{S}
)
and if, for any point of
x\in\overset{\circ}{\overline{X}}
,
there exist an open neighborhood
\overset{\circ}{\overline{V}}
of

x
and an open neighborhood

\overset{\circ}{\overline{W}}\simeq\underline{\rm Spec}_{W}({\cal O}_{W}[t])

(where
W:=\overline{W}\times_{\overline{S}}S
) of

\overset{\circ}{f}(x)
such that

M_{\overline{W}}\simeq({\mathbb{N}}\owns 1\longmapsto t\in{\cal O}_{W}[t])^{a}
and such that

\overline{f}|_{\overline{V}}
factors through
a solid and étale morphism

\overline{V}{\longrightarrow}{\mathbb{A}}_{\overline{W}}(a,d)

such that

\Delta_{\overset{\circ}{\overline{V}}}=\{x_{i}=0\}_{i=0}^{a}
in
\overset{\circ}{\overline{V}}
.
(Similarly we can give the definition of
a strict semistable log formal scheme over
\overline{S}
.)

Next we recall the following(=the exactification)
which has been proved in
[
Sh3
]
.

Proposition 2.8
.

(
[
Sh3
, Proposition 2.10]
)

Let
T
be a fine log formal
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-scheme.
Let
{\cal C}^{\rm ex}_{\rm hom}

(
resp.
{\cal C})

be the category of homeomorphic exact immersions

(
resp. the category of immersions
)
of
fine log formal
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-schemes over
T
.
Let
\iota
be the natural functor

{\cal C}^{\rm ex}_{\rm hom}\longrightarrow{\cal C}
.
Then
\iota
has a right adjoint functor

(~)^{\rm ex}\colon{\cal C}\longrightarrow{\cal C}^{\rm ex}_{\rm hom}
.

We recall the construction of
(~)^{\rm ex}
quickly.

By the universality, the problem is local.
We may assume that immersions are closed immersions.
We may assume that
an object

Y\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal Q}
in
{\cal C}
has
a global chart
(P\longrightarrow Q)
.
Let
P^{\rm ex}
be the inverse image of

Q
by the morphism
P^{\rm gp}\longrightarrow Q^{\rm gp}
.
Then we have a fine log formal
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-scheme

{\cal Q}^{{\rm prex}}:={\cal Q}\widehat{\times}_{{\rm Spf}^{\log}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\{P\})}{\rm Spf}^{\log}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\{P^{\rm ex}\})

over
{\cal Q}
with an exact immersion

Y\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal Q}^{{\rm prex}}
.
(“prex” is the abbreviation of
“pre-exactification”.)
Let
{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}
be the formal completion of

{\cal Q}^{{\rm prex}}
along
Y
.
Since
\overset{\circ}{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}=\overset{\circ}{Y}

and

M_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex},x}/{\cal O}^{*}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex},x}=M_{Y,x}/{\cal O}^{*}_{Y,x}
,
we see that the morphism
Y\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}

is the desired homeomorphic exact immersion over
T
.

Proposition 2.9

(
Base change of exactifications (
[
Nak4
, (1.1.27)]
)).

Let
T^{\prime}\longrightarrow T
be a morphism of
fine log formal schemes.
Let
{\cal Q}
be a fine log formal scheme over
T
.
Let
Y\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal Q}
be an immersion
of fine log formal schemes over
T
.
Let
Y\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}
be
the exactification of
Y\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal Q}
.
Then the immersion
Y\times_{T}T^{\prime}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}\times_{T}T^{\prime}

is the exactification of the immersion

Y\times_{T}T^{\prime}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal Q}\times_{T}T^{\prime}
over
T^{\prime}
.

We recall the following (
[

, (2.1.5)]
) describing
the local structure of an exact closed immersion,
which will be used in this section and later sections:

Proposition 2.10

(
[
NS
, (2.1.5)]
).

(

(

{\small{\begin{CD}Y@>{}>{}>{\cal Q}^{\prime}@>{}>{}>{\cal Q}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\

where the vertical morphisms are solid and étale and
the lower second horizontal morphism is the base change of
the zero section
T\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\mathbb{A}}^{c}_{T}

and
{\cal Q}^{\prime}:={\cal Q}\times_{{\mathbb{A}}^{c}_{T}}T
.

Let
S_{0}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}S

be a nil-immersion of families of log points.

X/S_{0}
be an SNCL scheme.
Let

Proposition 2.11

Proposition 2.12

(
[
Nak4
, (1.1.41)]
).

(1)

be the exactification of

.

(2)

Then
\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}

is a strict semistable family over

\overset{\circ}{\overline{\cal P}}{}^{\rm ex}

such that

\overset{\circ}{\cal D}:=\overset{\circ}{\overline{\cal P}}{}^{\rm ex}\times_{\overset{\circ}{\overline{S}}}\overset{\circ}{S}

For a smooth component

\overset{\circ}{X}
,

\overset{\circ}{\overline{\cal P}}{}^{\rm ex}

\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}

\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overset{\circ}{\overline{\cal P}}{}^{\rm ex}_{\lambda}

which is an isomorphism as topological spaces.
In the case where we consider the immersion

, we set

\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{\lambda}:=\overset{\circ}{\overline{\cal P}}{}^{\rm ex}_{\lambda}\times_{\overset{\circ}{\overline{S}}}\overset{\circ}{S}
.
For an object
\underline{\lambda}=\{\lambda_{0},\cdots,\lambda_{m}\}\in P(\Lambda)

(m\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0},\lambda_{i}\in\Lambda,\lambda_{i}\not=\lambda_{j}~{\rm if}~i\not=j)

we can define a closed formal subscheme

\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{\underline{\lambda}}
of

\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}

\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}}
.
Because

is a formal SNCL scheme over

,

\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(m)}

for a nonnegative integer

.
Indeed,

(2.12.1)

\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(m)}=\underset{\#\underline{\lambda}=m+1}{\coprod}\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{\underline{\lambda}}.

Endow
\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{\underline{\lambda}}

with the pull-back of the log structure of
{\cal P}^{\rm ex}

and let
{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\underline{\lambda}}
be the resulting log formal scheme.
