\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(m)}

with the pull-back of the log structure of
{\cal P}^{\rm ex}

and let
{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(m)}
be the resulting log formal scheme.
Then

(2.12.2)

{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(m)}=\underset{\#\underline{\lambda}=m+1}{\coprod}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{\underline{\lambda}}.

Let
S
be a family of log points. Let
M_{S}=(M_{S},\alpha_{S})
be the log structure of
S
.
In
[
Nak4
]
we have defined a log PD-enlargement

((T,{\cal J},\delta),z)
of
S
as follows (cf.
[
O2
]
):

(T,{\cal J},\delta)
is a fine log PD-scheme such that
{\cal J}
is quasi-coherent and

z\colon T_{0}\longrightarrow S
is a morphism of fine log schemes,
where
T_{0}:=T\mod{\cal J}
.
When we are given a morphism
S\longrightarrow S^{\prime}
of families of log points,
we can define a morphism of log PD-enlargements over
the morphism
S\longrightarrow S^{\prime}
in an obvious way.
Endow
\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}
with the inverse image of the log structure of
S
.
We denote the resulting log scheme by
S_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}
.
It is easy to see that the natural morphism
z^{*}(M_{S})\longrightarrow M_{T_{0}}
is injective
(
[
Nak4
, (1.1.4)]
);
we consider
z^{*}(M_{S})
is the sub log structure of
M_{T_{0}}
.
Let
M
be the sub log structure of the log structure
(M_{T},\alpha_{T})
of
T
such that
the natural morphism
M_{T}\longrightarrow M_{T_{0}}
induces an isomorphism

M/{\cal O}_{T}^{*}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}z^{*}(M_{S})/{\cal O}_{T_{0}}^{*}
.
Let
S(T)
be the log scheme
(\overset{\circ}{T},(M,\alpha_{T}|_{M}))
.
Since
M/{\cal O}_{T}^{*}
is constant, we can consider the hollowing out

S(T)^{\natural}
of
S(T)
(
[
O2
, Remark 7]
);
the log scheme
S(T)^{\natural}
is a family of log points.
Set
X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}:=X\times_{S}S_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}=X\times_{\overset{\circ}{S}}\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}

(we can consider
X
as a fine log scheme over
\overset{\circ}{S}
).
By abuse of notation, we denote by the same symbol
f

the structural morphism

X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}\longrightarrow S_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}
.

Let
X^{\prime}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}=\coprod_{i\in I}X_{i}
be the disjoint union
of an affine open covering of
X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}
over
S_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}

(
X_{i}
is a log open subscheme of
X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}
).
Assume that
f(\overset{\circ}{X}_{i})
is contained in
an affine open subscheme of

\overset{\circ}{T}=(S_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}})^{\circ}

such that the restriction of
M_{S_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}}

to this open subscheme is free of rank 1.
Assume also that there exists a solid and étale morphism

X_{i}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{A}}_{S_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}}(a,d)
.
Then, replacing
X_{i}
by a small log open subscheme of

X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}
,
we can assume that there exists a log smooth scheme

\overline{\cal P}{}^{\prime}_{i}/\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}
fitting into the following commutative diagram

(2.12.3)

\begin{CD}X_{i}@>{\subset}>{}>\overline{\cal P}{}^{\prime}_{i}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
{\mathbb{A}}_{S_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}}(a,d)@>{\subset}>{}>{\mathbb{A}}_{\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}}(a,d)\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
S_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}@>{\subset}>{}>\overline{S(T)^{\natural}},\end{CD}

where the morphism

\overline{\cal P}{}^{\prime}_{i}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{A}}_{\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}}(a,d)

is solid and étale ((
2.6
)).
Set
\overline{\cal P}{}^{\prime}:=\coprod_{i\in I}\overline{\cal P}{}^{\prime}_{i}
.
Set also

(2.12.4)

X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0},n}:={\rm cosk}_{0}^{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}}(X^{\prime}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}})_{n}\quad(0\leq m\leq N,n\in{\mathbb{N}})

and

(2.12.5)

\overline{\cal P}_{n}:={\rm cosk}_{0}^{\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}}(\overline{\cal P}{}^{\prime})_{n}\quad(n\in{\mathbb{N}}).

Then we have a simplicial SNCL scheme
X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}

and an immersion

(2.12.6)

X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal P}_{\bullet}

into a log smooth simplicial log scheme over
\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}
.
Thus we have obtained the following:

Proposition 2.13
.

The following hold
:

(1)
There exist a Čech diagram
X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}

of
X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}
and an immersion

(2.13.1)

\begin{CD}X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}@>{\subset}>{}>\overline{\cal P}_{\bullet}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
S_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}@>{\subset}>{}>\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}\end{CD}

into a log smooth simplicial log scheme over
\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}
.

(2)
There exists a Čech diagram
X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}
of
X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}

and an immersion

(2.13.2)

\begin{CD}X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}@>{\subset}>{}>{\cal P}_{\bullet}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
S_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}@>{\subset}>{}>S(T)^{\natural}\end{CD}

into a log smooth simplicial log scheme over
S(T)^{\natural}
.

Corollary 2.14
.

Let the notations be as above.
Then there exist an
immersion

(2.14.1)

\begin{CD}X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}@>{\subset}>{}>\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
S_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}@>{\subset}>{}>\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}\end{CD}

into a simplicial strict semistable log formal scheme over

\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}
and an immersion

(2.14.2)

\begin{CD}X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}@>{\subset}>{}>{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
S_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}@>{\subset}>{}>S(T)^{\natural}\end{CD}

into a simplicial formal SNCL scheme over
S(T)^{\natural}
.

Set
X_{\underline{\lambda},\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}:=X_{\underline{\lambda}}\times_{\overset{\circ}{S}}\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}
and

X_{\underline{\lambda},\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}:=X_{\underline{\lambda},\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}\times_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}}X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}
.
Then we have simplicial log schemes

\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}}
and
{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}}

with the following exact immersions:

\displaystyle X_{\underline{\lambda},\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}}.

Here note that the turns of
\bullet
and
\underline{\lambda}
in

\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}}
; there does not necessarily exist an immersion
“
X_{\underline{\lambda},\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{\underline{\lambda}}
” into an
ideally log smooth immersion over
\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}
: if it exists, then we can use the notation

\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{\underline{\lambda}\bullet}
.

We conclude this section by recalling
the “mapping degree function” defined in
[
Nak4
]
.

Let
v\colon S\longrightarrow S^{\prime}
be a morphism of families of log points.
Let
y
be a point of
\overset{\circ}{S}
.
Let
h\colon{\mathbb{N}}=M_{S^{\prime},v(x)}/{\cal O}^{*}_{S^{\prime},v(x)}\longrightarrow M_{S,y}/{\cal O}^{*}_{S,y}={\mathbb{N}}

be the induced morphism.
Let
d\in{\mathbb{N}}
be the image of
1\in{\mathbb{N}}
by
h
.

Definition 2.15

(
A special case of
[
Nak4
, (1.1.42)]
).

We call
\deg(v)_{x}:=d

the
(
mapping
)
degree
of
v
at
x
.
We call
\deg(v)\colon\overset{\circ}{S}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}

the
(
mapping
)
degree function
of
v
.

In the case where
\overset{\circ}{S}
is a formal scheme,
we can obtain the analogous results to the results in this section.

3
PD-Hirsch extensions of dga’s

In this section we give the definition of the PD-Hirsch extension of a dga
and we give fundamental properties of it.
Any dga in the case of geometric applications in this book
has an extra structure, which we call
a differential graded PD-algebra structure.
We also define the PD-Hirsch extension of it.

Definition 3.1
.

Let
A
be a commutative ring with unit element.

(1) Let
(B,J,\delta)
be a PD-algebra such that
B
is an
A
-algebra.
Denote
\delta_{i}(b)
by
b^{[i]}

(i\in{\mathbb{N}},b\in J)
for simplicity of notation.
Let
\Omega^{\bullet}
be a dga over
A
such that each
\Omega^{q}

(q\in{\mathbb{Z}})
is
a
B
-module. Assume that we are given a
B
-linear morphism

\psi\colon\Omega^{\bullet}_{B/A}\longrightarrow\Omega^{\bullet}
of dga’s.
Then we say that
\Omega^{\bullet}
is
a
B
-
pd-dga
(=
PD-differential graded algebra
)
over
A
(precisely speaking,
(B,J,\delta)
-
pd-dga
with respect to
\psi
)
if
d(b\omega)=d\psi(b)\wedge\omega+bd\omega
for
b\in B
and
\omega\in\Omega^{\bullet}

and if

d(\psi(b^{[i]}))=b^{[i-1]}d\psi(b)
for
b\in J

(i\geq 1)
.

(2) Let
M
be a projective
A
-module.
Let
\Gamma_{A}(M)
be the PD-algebra over
A
generated by
M

(see
[
BO1
, Appendix A]
).
Let
\Omega^{\bullet}=(\Omega^{\bullet},d)
be a dga over
A
.
Let
\varphi\colon M\longrightarrow{\rm Ker}(d\colon\Omega^{1}\longrightarrow\Omega^{2})
be
an
A
-linear morphism.
The
PD-Hirsch extension
of
\Omega^{\bullet}
by
M

is, by definition, an injective morphism

(3.1.1)

\displaystyle\Omega^{\bullet}=A\otimes_{A}\Omega^{\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\Gamma_{A}(M)\otimes_{A}\Omega^{\bullet}.

We denote
\Gamma_{A}(M)\otimes_{A}\Omega^{\bullet}
by

\Omega^{\bullet}\langle M\rangle
.
We denote by
P_{k}\Gamma_{A}(M)

a submodule of
\Gamma_{A}(M)

generated by

m_{i_{1}}^{[i_{1}]}\cdots m_{i_{r}}^{[i_{r}]}\quad(i_{1}+\cdots+i_{r}\leq\lfloor\frac{k}{2}\rfloor)\quad(m_{i_{1}},\ldots,m_{i_{r}}\in M),

where
\lfloor?\rfloor
is the floor function:

\lfloor x\rfloor:=\max\{n\in{\mathbb{Z}}~|~n\leq x\}
for
x\in{\mathbb{R}}
.
(The filtration
P
on
\Gamma_{A}(M)
will be necessary for geometric applications.)

Remark 3.2
.

(1) If
J=0
, then the notion of the
B
-pd-dga is equal to that of a
B
-dga over
A
.

(2) When
A
is a
{\mathbb{Q}}
-algebra, the notion of the pd-dga
(resp. the PD-Hirsch extension) is equivalent to
that of the dga (resp. the Hirsch extension).

Proposition 3.3
.

Let
\Omega^{\bullet}
be a dga over
A
.
The
A
-module
\Omega^{\bullet}\langle M\rangle
becomes a dga over
A
naturally.
If
\Omega^{\bullet}
is a
B
-pd-dga, then

\Omega^{\bullet}\langle M\rangle
becomes a
B
-pd-dga over
A
naturally.

Proof.

We define the degree of

\Gamma_{A}(M)\otimes_{A}\Omega^{q}

(q\in{\mathbb{Z}})
as
q
.
We define the algebra structure of
\Gamma_{A}(M)\otimes_{A}\Omega^{\bullet}

as the induced algebra structure by those of
\Gamma_{A}(M)
and
\Omega^{\bullet}
.
We define the following two
A
-linear morphisms as follows:

(3.3.1)

d_{M}(m^{[i]}):=d_{M,\varphi}(m^{[i]}):=\begin{cases}0&(i=0),\\
m^{[i-1]}\otimes\varphi(m)&(i\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1})\end{cases}

and

(3.3.2)

\displaystyle d_{\Omega^{\bullet}\langle M\rangle}(\sum m^{[i_{1}]}_{1}\cdots m^{[i_{r}]}_{r}\otimes\omega_{i_{1}\cdots i_{r}})

\displaystyle:=\sum(\sum_{j=1}^{r}m^{[i_{1}]}_{1}\cdots m^{[i_{j-1}]}_{j-1}m^{[i_{j+1}]}_{j+1}\cdots m^{[i_{r}]}_{r}d_{M}(m^{[i_{j}]}_{j})\wedge\omega_{i_{1}\cdots i_{r}}

\displaystyle+m^{[i_{1}]}_{1}\cdots m^{[i_{r}]}_{r}\otimes d\omega_{i_{1}\cdots i_{r}})\quad(m_{1},\ldots,m_{r}\in M,\omega_{i_{1}\cdots i_{r}}\in\Omega^{q}).

We have to check that the following five relations hold:

(i)
d_{\Omega^{\bullet}\langle M\rangle}^{2}=0
,

(ii)

d_{M}((am)^{[i]})=a^{i}d_{M}(m^{[i]})

(a\in A)
,

(iii)
d_{M}(m^{[i]}m^{[j]})=\{(i+j)!/i!j!\}d_{M}(m^{[i+j]})
,

(iv)
d_{M}((m+n)^{[i]})=\sum_{j_{1}+j_{2}=i}d_{M}(m^{[j_{1}]}n^{[j_{2}]})
,

(v)
d_{M}((m^{[i]})^{[j]})=\dfrac{(ij)!}{(i!)^{j}j!}d_{M}(m^{[ij]})
.

By noting that
\varphi(m)\in{\rm Ker}(d\colon\Omega^{1}\longrightarrow\Omega^{2})
,
the first relation can be checked.
The second relation is obvious since
\varphi
is
A
-linear.
The third relation is equivalent to the following equality

\displaystyle m^{[i-1]}m^{[j]}\otimes\varphi(m)+m^{[i]}m^{[j-1]}\otimes\varphi(m)=\{(i+j)!/i!j!\}m^{[i+j-1]}\otimes\varphi(m),

which is easily checked.
The fourth relation is equivalent to
the following equality

\displaystyle(m+n)^{[i-1]}\otimes\varphi(m+n)=\sum_{j_{1}+j_{2}=i}\{m^{[j_{1}-1]}n^{[j_{2}]}\otimes\varphi(m)+m^{[j_{1}]}n^{[j_{2}-1]}\otimes\varphi(n)\},

which is also easily checked.
The fifth relation is equivalent to the following equality

\displaystyle(m^{[i]})^{[j-1]}m^{[i-1]}=\dfrac{(ij)!}{(i!)^{j}j!}m^{[ij-1]},

which is also easily checked.
∎

Proposition 3.4

(
Functoriality of PD-Hirsch extensions).

Let the notations be as above.
Let
A\longrightarrow A^{\prime}
and
B\longrightarrow B^{\prime}
be morphisms of commutative rings with unit elements.
Assume that
B^{\prime}
is an
A^{\prime}
-algebra and the following diagram

(3.4.1)

\begin{CD}A@>{}>{}>A^{\prime}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
B@>{}>{}>B^{\prime}\end{CD}

is commutative. Let
\Omega^{\prime}{}^{\bullet}
be a dga over
B^{\prime}
and
let
\varphi^{\prime}\colon M\longrightarrow{\rm Ker}(\Omega^{\prime}{}^{1}\longrightarrow\Omega^{\prime}{}^{2})
be
an
A
-linear morphism.

(
Here we consider
\Omega^{\prime}{}^{\bullet}
as an
A
-module.
)

Let
(B^{\prime},J^{\prime},\delta^{\prime})
be an analogous PD-algebra to
(B,J,\delta)
.
Let
h\colon\Omega^{\bullet}\longrightarrow\Omega^{\prime}{}^{\bullet}
be a morphism of complexes
fitting into the following commutative diagram

(3.4.2)

\begin{CD}M=M\\
@V{\varphi}V{}V@V{}V{\varphi^{\prime}}V\\
{\rm Ker}(\Omega^{1}\longrightarrow\Omega^{2})@>{h}>{}>{\rm Ker}(\Omega^{\prime}{}^{1}\longrightarrow\Omega^{\prime}{}^{2}).\end{CD}

Then
h
,
\varphi
and
\varphi^{\prime}
induce the following natural morphism

(3.4.3)

\Omega^{\bullet}\langle M\rangle\longrightarrow\Omega^{\prime}{}^{\bullet}\langle M\rangle.

This morphism is compatible with the compositions of
two
(
3.4.1
)
’s and two
(
3.4.2
)
’s in the obvious sense.

Proof.

We leave the proof to the reader because the proof is straightforward.
∎

Example 3.5
.

Let
Y\longrightarrow S
be a morphism of log schemes.
Let
Y\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\mathfrak{D}}
be a closed immersion into a log scheme over
S

defined by a PD-ideal sheaf of
{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}}
with PD-structure
[~]
.
Let
g\colon{\mathfrak{D}}\longrightarrow S
be the structural morphism.
Let
\Omega^{\bullet}
be a sheaf of dga’s on
{\mathfrak{D}}_{\rm zar}

over
g^{-1}({\cal O}_{S})

such that each
\Omega^{q}

(q\in{\mathbb{Z}})
is
an
{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}}
-module.
Let
\Omega^{\bullet}_{[~]}
be a quotient sheaf of
\Omega^{\bullet}
divided by
the ideal sheaf generated by
a^{[i]}-a^{[i-1]}da
’s for a local section
a

of
{\rm Ker}({\cal O}_{\mathfrak{D}}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{Y})
.
Let
{\cal M}
be a locally free
g^{-1}({\cal O}_{S})
-module and
let
\varphi\colon{\cal M}\longrightarrow{\rm Ker}(\Omega^{1}_{[~]}\longrightarrow\Omega^{2}_{[~]})
be a
morphism of
g^{-1}({\cal O}_{S})
-modules.
Then, as in (
3.1
), we have the notion of
an
{\cal O}_{\mathfrak{D}}
-pd-dga and
the PD-Hirsch extension of
\Omega^{\bullet}_{[~]}
by
{\cal M}
.

Proposition 3.6
.

The following hold
:

(1)
Let
s
be a fine log scheme whose underlying scheme is
the spectrum of a perfect field
\kappa
.
Let
\star
be a positive integer
n
or nothing.
Let
{\cal W}_{\star}(s)
be the
(
formal
)
canonical lift of
s
over

{\rm Spf}({\cal W}_{\star})
.
Let
Y/s
be a log smooth scheme of Cartier type
and let
g\colon Y\longrightarrow s\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal W}_{\star}(s)
be
the structural morphism.
Let
{\cal W}_{\star}\Omega^{\bullet}_{Y}
be the log de Rham-Witt dga
of
Y/{\cal W}_{\star}(s)
.

(
Here note that each
{\cal W}_{\star}\Omega^{q}_{Y}

(q\in{\mathbb{N}})

is a sheaf of
{\cal W}_{\star}({\cal O}_{Y})^{\prime}
-modules by
the isomorphism
{\cal W}_{\star}({\cal O}_{Y})^{\prime}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}{\cal W}_{\star}\Omega^{0}_{Y}
,
where
{\cal W}_{\star}({\cal O}_{Y})^{\prime}
is the obverse Witt sheaf of
Y

in the sense of
[
Nak1
, (7.8)]
and
{\cal W}_{\star}({\cal O}_{Y})^{\prime}
has the
PD-ideal sheaf
V{\cal W}_{\star}({\cal O}_{Y})^{\prime}.)

Then
{\cal W}_{\star}\Omega^{\bullet}_{Y}
is a sheaf of

{\cal W}_{\star}({\cal O}_{Y})^{\prime}
-pd-dga’s over
g^{-1}({\cal W}_{\star})
.

(2)
Let the notations be as in
(1)
.
Assume that
s
is the log point of
\kappa
.
Let
{\cal W}_{\star}\widetilde{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{Y}
be
the log de Rham-Witt complex defined in

[
M
]
and
[
Nak1
]

(
cf.
[
Hy
]
)
.
Then
{\cal W}_{\star}\widetilde{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{Y}
is a sheaf of

{\cal W}_{\star}({\cal O}_{Y})^{\prime}
-pd-dga’s over

g^{-1}({\cal W}_{\star})
.

(3)
Let
(T,{\cal J},\delta)
be a fine log PD-scheme.
Let
Y\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal Y}
be an immersion of fine log schemes
over
T
and let
g\colon{\cal Y}\longrightarrow T
be the structural morphism.
Let
{\mathfrak{E}}
be the log PD-envelope over
(T,{\cal J},\delta)
. Then

{\cal O}_{\mathfrak{E}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\cal Y}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Y}/U}

for
U=T
or
\overset{\circ}{T}
is
a sheaf of
{\cal O}_{\mathfrak{E}}
-pd-dga’s over
g^{-1}({\cal O}_{T})
.

Proof.

(1): It is well-known that

{\cal W}_{\star}\Omega^{\bullet}_{Y}
is a sheaf of dga’s over

g^{-1}({\cal W}_{\star})
.
As in the proof of
[
HK
, (4.19)]
, we can easily check
that
{\cal W}_{\star}\Omega^{\bullet}_{Y}
is a

{\cal W}_{\star}({\cal O}_{Y})^{\prime}
-pd-dga over

g^{-1}({\cal W}_{\star})
as follows.

Let
a
be a section of
V{\cal W}_{\star}({\cal O}_{Y})^{\prime}
.
Then

\displaystyle i!d(a^{[i]})=da^{i}=ia^{i-1}da=i!a^{[i-1]}da.

Because
{\cal W}\Omega^{q}_{Y}
is a torsion-free sheaf
(
[
Nak1
, (6.8)]
),

d(a^{[i]})=a^{[i-1]}da
in
{\cal W}\Omega^{1}_{Y}
.
Because the natural projection
{\cal W}\Omega^{q}_{Y}\longrightarrow{\cal W}_{\star}\Omega^{q}_{Y}
is surjective
(
[
Nak1
, (6.4.5)]
),

d(a^{[i]})=a^{[i-1]}da
in
{\cal W}_{\star}\Omega^{1}_{Y}
.

(2): The proof of (2) is the same as that of (1).

(3): By
[
Nak4
, (1.3.28)]
(=the log version of
[
Il
, 0 (3.1.6)]
)

(3.6.1)

\displaystyle\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\mathfrak{E}}/U,[~]}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}{\cal O}_{\mathfrak{E}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Y}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Y}/U}.

(3) follows from this equality.
∎

Example 3.7
.

(1) (
[
KH
]
) Let the notations be as in (
3.6
) (2).
Let
u
be a variable over
{\cal W}_{\star}

and consider the free
{\cal W}_{\star}
-module

{\cal W}_{\star}u
over
{\cal W}_{\star}
of rank 1.
Here we define the degree of
u
as 0.
Consider the following morphism

(3.7.1)

\displaystyle\varphi\colon{\cal W_{\star}}u\owns u\longmapsto\theta\in{\rm Ker}(d\colon{\cal W}_{\star}\widetilde{\Omega}^{1}_{Y}\longrightarrow{\cal W}_{\star}\widetilde{\Omega}^{2}_{Y}),

where
\theta=
“
d\log t
” is a 1-form defined in
[
Hy
]
,
[
M
]
and
[
Nak1
]
.
Set

\displaystyle{\cal W}_{\star}\widetilde{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{Y}\langle u\rangle:={\cal W}_{\star}\widetilde{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{Y}\langle{\cal W}_{\star}u\rangle.

(Strictly speaking, we should denote

{\cal W}_{\star}\widetilde{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{Y}\langle{\cal W}_{\star}u\rangle

by

{\cal W}_{\star}\widetilde{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{Y}\langle g^{-1}({\cal W}_{\star}u)\rangle
.)
Then we obtain the following PD-Hirsch extension

\displaystyle{\cal W}_{\star}\widetilde{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{Y}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal W}_{\star}\widetilde{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{Y}\langle u\rangle.

(2) Let the notations be as in (
3.6
) (3).
Let
r
be a positive integer.
Assume that
\overline{M}_{T}:=M_{T}/{\cal O}_{T}^{*}

is locally isomorphic to
{\mathbb{N}}^{r}

(r\in{\mathbb{N}})
,
where
r
does
not
depend on local open log subschemes of
T
.
(If
T
is a family of log points of virtual dimension
r
defined in
[
Nak4
, (1.1)]
,
then
\overline{M}_{T}
is locally isomorphic to
{\mathbb{N}}^{r}
.)
Let us consider the following morphism

\displaystyle d\log\colon g^{-1}(M_{T})\owns m\longmapsto d\log m\in\Omega^{1}_{{\cal Y}/\overset{\circ}{T}}.

Since
d\log a=0

(a\in{\cal O}_{T}^{*})
in
\Omega^{1}_{{\cal Y}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
,
the morphism
d\log
induces the following morphism

\displaystyle d\log\colon g^{-1}(\overline{M}_{T})\owns m\longmapsto d\log m\in\Omega^{1}_{{\cal Y}/\overset{\circ}{T}}.

Let
t_{1},\ldots,t_{r}
be the local generators of
\overline{M}_{T}
.
Set

(3.7.2)

\displaystyle U_{T}:=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{r}{\cal O}_{T}u_{i},

where
u_{i}
’s are independent formal variables over
{\cal O}_{T}
.
Here the variable
u_{i}
corresponds to
t_{i}
.
(We consider
u_{i}
as
\log t_{i}
in our mind.)
Note that the multiplication in
M_{T}
by a local section of
{\cal O}_{T}^{*}

and the multiplication in
U_{T}
by a local section of

{\cal O}_{T}
is essentially different.
The local section

g^{*}(d\log t_{i})\in\Omega^{1}_{{\cal Y}/\overset{\circ}{T}}

is a well-defined 1-form on
{\cal Y}
.
By abuse of notation, denote
g^{*}(d\log t_{i})
by
d\log t_{i}
.
Since
{\rm Aut}({\mathbb{N}}^{r})={\mathfrak{S}}_{r}
(
[
Nak3
, p. 47]
), the sheaf

U_{T}
is a well-defined
{\cal O}_{T}
-module.
Here we define the degree of
U_{T}
as 0.
Consider the following morphism

\displaystyle\varphi\colon U_{T}\owns\sum_{i=1}^{r}a_{i}u_{i}\longmapsto\sum_{i=1}^{r}a_{i}d\log t_{i}\in{\cal O}_{\mathfrak{E}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Y}}}\Omega^{1}_{{\cal Y}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\quad(a_{i}\in{\cal O}_{T}).

Then we have the following PD-Hirsch extension

\displaystyle{\cal O}_{\mathfrak{E}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Y}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Y}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal O}_{\mathfrak{E}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Y}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Y}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle U_{T}\rangle.

(Strictly speaking, we should denote
U_{T}
by
g^{-1}(U_{T})
.)
The sheaf
\Gamma_{{\cal O}_{T}}(U_{T})
is non-canonically locally isomorphic to

{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}(\overline{T})}
,
where
\overline{T}
is a log scheme constructed in
[
Nak4
, §2]
and

{\mathfrak{D}}(\overline{T})
is the log PD-envelope of the exact closed immersion

T\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{T}
over the PD scheme
(\overset{\circ}{T},(0),[~])
.

In the case of characteristic 0, this (PD-)Hirsch extension has appeared in

[
F1
]
and
[
F2
]
.
In this book this PD-Hirsch extension for the case
r=1
plays an important role.
In
[
Nak5
]
we have considered the case where
r
is general and we give
an important complementary result to
[
F1
]
.

(3) The following observation will be important in this book.

Let
S
and
S^{\prime}
be (formal) families
of log points of virtual dimensions
r
and
r^{\prime}
, respectively (
[
Nak4
, §2]
).
Let
v\colon S\longrightarrow S^{\prime}
be a morphism of (formal) families of log points.
This morphism induces a morphism
v^{*}\colon\overline{M}_{S^{\prime}}\longrightarrow v_{*}(\overline{M}_{S})
.
Locally on
S
, this morphism is equal to a morphism

v^{*}\colon{\mathbb{N}}^{r^{\prime}}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{N}}^{r}
.
Set

e_{i}=(0,\ldots,0,\overset{i}{1},0,\ldots,0)\in{\mathbb{N}}^{s}
for
s=r
or
r^{\prime}

(1\leq i\leq r)
.
Let
A=(a_{ij})_{1\leq i\leq r,1\leq j\leq r^{\prime}}\in M_{rr^{\prime}}({\mathbb{N}})

be the representing matrix of
v^{*}
:

v^{*}(e_{1},\ldots,e_{r^{\prime}})=(e_{1},\ldots,e_{r})A.

Let
\widetilde{t}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{t}_{r}
and
\widetilde{t}{}^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{t}{}^{\prime}_{r^{\prime}}

be local sections of
M_{S}
and
M_{S^{\prime}}

whose images in
\overline{M}_{S}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}{\mathbb{N}}^{r}
and

\overline{M}_{S^{\prime}}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}{\mathbb{N}}^{r^{\prime}}
are

e_{1},\ldots,e_{r}
and
e_{1},\ldots,e_{r^{\prime}}
, respectively.
Then there exists a local section
b_{j}\in{\cal O}_{S}^{*}

(1\leq j\leq r^{\prime})
such that

(3.7.3)

\displaystyle v^{*}(\widetilde{t}{}^{\prime}_{j})=b_{j}\widetilde{t}{}^{a_{1j}}_{1}\cdots\widetilde{t}{}^{a_{rj}}_{r}.

Let
t_{i}
and
t_{i}^{\prime}
be the images of
\widetilde{t}_{i}
and
\widetilde{t}{}^{\prime}_{i}
in

\overline{M}_{S}
and
\overline{M}_{S^{\prime}}
, respectively.
Let
u_{1},\ldots,u_{r}
and
u^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,u^{\prime}_{r^{\prime}}
be
the corresponding local sections
to
t_{1},\ldots,t_{r}
and
t^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,t^{\prime}_{r^{\prime}}
of

U_{S}
and
U_{S^{\prime}}
, respectively. Then we define
an
{\cal O}_{S^{\prime}}
-linear morphism

v^{*}\colon\Gamma_{{\cal O}_{S^{\prime}}}(U_{S^{\prime}})\longrightarrow v_{*}(\Gamma_{{\cal O}_{S}}(U_{S}))

by the following formula:

(3.7.4)

\displaystyle v^{*}(u^{\prime}_{j})=a_{1j}u_{1}+\cdots+a_{rj}u_{r}.

and by
\Gamma(v^{*})
for
v^{*}
in (
3.7.4
).
Since
{\rm Aut}({\mathbb{N}}^{s})={\mathfrak{S}}_{s}
,
the morphism
v^{*}\colon\Gamma_{{\cal O}_{S^{\prime}}}(U_{S^{\prime}})\longrightarrow v_{*}(\Gamma_{{\cal O}_{S}}(U_{S}))

is independent of the choice of the local isomorphisms

\overline{M}_{S^{\prime}}\simeq{\mathbb{N}}^{r^{\prime}}
and
\overline{M}_{S}\simeq{\mathbb{N}}^{r}
.
In conclusion,
we obtain the following well-defined morphism

(3.7.5)

\displaystyle v^{*}\colon\Gamma_{{\cal O}_{S^{\prime}}}(U_{S^{\prime}})\longrightarrow v_{*}(\Gamma_{{\cal O}_{S}}(U_{S}))

of sheaves of commutative rings of unit elements on
S^{\prime}
.
This morphism satisfies the usual transitive relation

(3.7.6)

\displaystyle(v\circ v^{\prime})^{*}=v^{\prime}{}^{*}v^{*}.

(In the case
r=r^{\prime}=1
,
a_{11}={\rm deg}(v)
in (
2.15
).)
It is easy to check that the following diagram is commutative:

(3.7.7)

\begin{CD}\Gamma_{{\cal O}_{S^{\prime}}}(U_{S^{\prime}})@>{v^{*}}>{}>v_{*}(\Gamma_{{\cal O}_{S}}(U_{S}))\\
@V{d}V{}V@V{}V{d}V\\
\Omega^{1}_{S^{\prime}/\overset{\circ}{S}{}^{\prime}}\langle U_{S^{\prime}}\rangle @>{v^{*}}>{}>v_{*}(\Omega^{1}_{S/\overset{\circ}{S}{}}\langle U_{S}\rangle).\end{CD}

Indeed,

\displaystyle v^{*}d(u^{\prime}{}^{[l]}_{j})

\displaystyle=v^{*}u^{\prime}{}^{[l-1]}_{j}d\log t^{\prime}_{j}=v^{*}u^{\prime}{}^{[l-1]}_{j}d\log(b_{j}t^{a_{1j}}_{1}\cdots t^{a_{rj}}_{r})

\displaystyle=v^{*}(u^{\prime}{}^{[l-1]}_{j})(a_{1j}d\log t_{1}+\cdots+a_{rj}d\log t_{r})

\displaystyle=(a_{1j}u_{1}+\cdots+a_{rj}u_{r})^{[l-1]}(a_{1j}d\log t_{1}+\cdots+a_{rj}d\log t_{r})

and

\displaystyle dv^{*}(u^{\prime}{}^{[l]}_{j})

\displaystyle=d(a_{1j}u_{1}+\cdots+a_{rj}u_{r})^{[l]}

\displaystyle=(a_{1j}u_{1}+\cdots+a_{rj}u_{r})^{[l-1]}(a_{1j}d\log t_{1}+\cdots+a_{rj}d\log t_{r}).

The remarks (2) and (3) suggest to us that, when
we consider the (PD-)Hirsch extensions in geometric cases,
we have only to consider local sections of
\overline{M}_{S}
;
it is not necessary to consider local sections of
M_{S}
.

Inspired by
[
EY
]
, we give the following definition:

Definition 3.8
.

Let the notations be as in (
3.1
) (2).
Set

\displaystyle\Gamma_{A}(M)^{\wedge}:=\varprojlim_{n}\Gamma_{A}(M)/\Gamma_{A,n}(M)=\varprojlim_{n}\Gamma_{A}(M)/(x^{[n]}~|~x\in M)

and

\displaystyle\Omega^{\bullet}\langle\langle M\rangle\rangle:=\Gamma_{A}(M)^{\wedge}\widehat{\otimes}_{A}\Omega^{\bullet}:=\varprojlim_{n}(\Gamma_{A}(M)\otimes_{A}\Omega^{\bullet}/x^{[n]}\Omega^{\bullet}).

We call the following injective morphism

\displaystyle\Omega^{\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\Omega^{\bullet}\langle\langle M\rangle\rangle

the
completed PD-Hirsch extension
of
\Omega^{\bullet}
by
(M,\varphi)

(or simply by
M
).

The
A
-algebra
\Gamma_{A}(M)^{\wedge}

has a natural decreasing filtration
F
:

\displaystyle F^{i}(\Gamma_{A}(M)^{\wedge}):=\{\sum a_{e_{1}\cdots e_{k},i_{1}\cdots i_{k}}m^{[e_{1}]}_{i_{1}}\cdots m^{[e_{k}]}_{i_{k}}

\displaystyle~|~a_{e_{1}\cdots e_{k},i_{1}\cdots i_{k}}\in A,

\displaystyle m_{i_{1}},\ldots m_{i_{k}}\in M,e_{1}+\cdots+e_{k}\geq i\}\quad(i\in{\mathbb{Z}}).

This filtration induces an increasing filtration

\Gamma_{A}(M)^{\wedge}\widehat{\otimes}_{A}\Omega^{\bullet}
which we denote by
F
by abuse of notation:

(3.8.1)

\displaystyle F_{i}(\Omega^{\bullet}\langle\langle M\rangle\rangle):=F^{-i}(\Gamma_{A}(M)^{\wedge})\widehat{\otimes}_{A}\Omega^{\bullet}\quad(i\in{\mathbb{Z}}).

Note that
F
is indeed a filtration on the complex

\Omega^{\bullet}\langle\langle M\rangle\rangle
.
This filtration also induces an increasing filtration
F

on
\Omega^{\bullet}\langle M\rangle
.

Proposition 3.9
.

Let
\Omega^{\bullet}
be a dga over
A
.
The
A
-module
\Omega^{\bullet}\langle\langle M\rangle\rangle
becomes a dga over
A
naturally.
If
\Omega^{\bullet}
is a
B
-pd-dga, then

\Omega^{\bullet}\langle\langle M\rangle\rangle
becomes a pd-dga over
A
naturally.

Proof.

Set

(3.9.1)

\displaystyle d_{M}(\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}m^{[i]}):=d_{M,\varphi}(\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}m^{[i]}):=\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}m^{[i-1]}\otimes\varphi(m)

and

(3.9.2)

\displaystyle d_{\Omega^{\bullet}\langle\langle M\rangle\rangle}(\sum_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{r}=0}^{\infty}m^{[i_{1}]}_{1}\cdots m^{[i_{r}]}_{r}\otimes\omega_{i_{1}\cdots i_{r}})

\displaystyle:=\sum_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{r}=0}^{\infty}(\sum_{j=1}^{r}m^{[i_{1}]}_{1}\cdots m^{[i_{j-1}]}_{j-1}m^{[i_{j+1}]}_{j+1}\cdots m^{[i_{r}]}_{r}d_{M}(m^{[i_{j}]}_{j})\wedge\omega_{i_{1}\cdots i_{r}}

\displaystyle+m^{[i_{1}]}_{1}\cdots m^{[i_{r}]}_{r}\otimes d\omega_{i_{1}\cdots i_{r}})\quad(m_{1},\ldots,m_{r}\in M,\omega_{i_{1}\cdots i_{r}}\in\Omega^{q}).

The rest of the proof of this proposition is similar to that of (
3.3
).
∎

More generally, we consider the following PD-Hirsch extension:

Proposition-Definition 3.10
.

Let
n
be a positive integer.
Let
A
and
M
be as in (
3.1
).
Let
B_{i}

(1\leq i\leq n)
be an
A
-algebra.
Let
\Omega^{\bullet}_{i}
be a dga over
A

such that each
\Omega^{q}_{i}

(q\in{\mathbb{Z}})
is a
B_{i}
-module.
Let
E_{i}
be a
B_{i}
-module.
Let
\varphi_{i}\colon M\longrightarrow{\rm Ker}(d\colon\Omega^{1}_{i}\longrightarrow\Omega^{2}_{i})

be a morphism of
A
-modules.
Let
(\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}(E_{i}\otimes_{B_{i}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{i})[-n_{i}],d)

(n_{i}\in{\mathbb{Z}})
be a complex of
A
-modules.

(
Here
d
is not necessarily the direct sum of certain differentials of

E_{i}\otimes_{B_{i}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{i}[-n_{i}].)

Let
\square
be nothing or
\wedge
.
Then the
A
-linear morphism

d_{H}:=d_{H,(\varphi_{1},\ldots,\varphi_{r})}\colon\Gamma_{A}(M)^{\square}\overset{\square}{\otimes}_{A}(\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}(E_{i}\otimes_{B_{i}}\Omega^{q-n_{i}}_{i}))\longrightarrow\Gamma_{A}(M)^{\square}\overset{\square}{\otimes}_{A}(\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}(E_{i}\otimes_{B_{i}}\Omega^{{q+1}-n_{i}}_{i}))

defined by the following formula

\displaystyle d_{H}(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum^{\prime}_{j(i)_{1},\ldots,j(i)_{r}\in{\mathbb{N}}}m^{[j(i)_{1}]}_{1}\cdots m^{[j(i)_{r}]}_{r}\otimes e_{i}\otimes\omega_{i})

\displaystyle:=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum^{\prime}_{j(i)_{1},\ldots,j(i)_{r}\in{\mathbb{N}}}\sum_{k=1}^{r}m^{[j(i)_{1}]}_{1}\cdots m^{[j(i)_{k}-1]}_{k}\cdots m^{[j(i)_{r}]}_{r}\otimes e_{i}\otimes\varphi_{i}(m_{k})\wedge\omega_{i}

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum^{\prime}_{j(i)_{1},\ldots,j(i)_{r}\in{\mathbb{N}}}m^{[j(i)_{1}]}_{1}\cdots m^{[j(i)_{r}]}_{r}\otimes d(e_{i}\otimes\omega_{i})

\displaystyle\quad(m_{1},\ldots m_{r}\in M,e_{i}\in E_{i},\omega\in\Omega^{q-n_{i}}_{i})

makes
\Gamma_{A}(M)^{\square}\overset{\square}{\otimes}_{A}(\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}(E_{i}\otimes_{B_{i}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{i})[-n_{i}])

a complex of
A
-modules.
Here
\overset{{}^{\prime}}{\sum}
means that we allow the infinite sum when
\square=\wedge{}
.
We call the following natural injective morphism

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}(E_{i}\otimes_{B_{i}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{i})[-n_{i}]\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\Gamma_{A}(M)^{\square}\overset{\square}{\otimes}_{A}(\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}(E_{i}\otimes_{B_{i}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{i})[-n_{i}])

of
A
-modules the (
completed
)
PD-Hirsch extension
of

(\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}(E_{i}\otimes_{B_{i}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{i})[-n_{i}],d)

by
(M,(\varphi_{1},\ldots,\varphi_{r}))
.
We denote it by
(\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}(E_{i}\otimes_{B_{i}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{i})[-n_{i}])\{\langle M\rangle\}=(\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}(E_{i}\otimes_{B_{i}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{i})[-n_{i}])\{\langle M\rangle\}_{(\varphi_{1},\ldots,\varphi_{r})}
, where
\{\langle M\rangle\}
is
\langle M\rangle
or

\langle\langle M\rangle\rangle

according to the case where
\square
is nothing or
\wedge
.

Proof.

Because the proof of this proposition is similar to
that of (
3.3
),
we leave the detailed proof of it to the reader.
∎

Let
A\longrightarrow A^{\prime}
and
B\longrightarrow B^{\prime}
be morphisms of commutative rings.
Assume that
B^{\prime}
is an
A^{\prime}
-algebra and the following diagram

\begin{CD}A@>{}>{}>A^{\prime}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
B@>{}>{}>B^{\prime}\end{CD}

is commutative.
Let
\varphi^{\prime}\colon M\longrightarrow{\rm Ker}(\Omega^{\prime}{}^{1}\longrightarrow\Omega^{\prime}{}^{2})
be
an analogous morphism to
\varphi
.
Let
(B^{\prime},J^{\prime},\delta^{\prime})
be an analogous PD-algebra to
(B,J,\delta)
.
Let
h\colon\Omega^{\bullet}\longrightarrow\Omega^{\prime}{}^{\bullet}
be a morphism of complexes
fitting into the following commutative diagram

\begin{CD}M=M\\
@V{\varphi}V{}V@V{}V{\varphi^{\prime}}V\\
{\rm Ker}(\Omega^{1}\longrightarrow\Omega^{2})@>{h}>{}>{\rm Ker}(\Omega^{\prime}{}^{1}\longrightarrow\Omega^{\prime}{}^{2}).\end{CD}

Let
E
(resp.
E^{\prime}
) be a
B
-module (resp.
B^{\prime}
-module).
Let
g\colon E\longrightarrow E^{\prime}
be a morphism of
B
-modules.
(We can consider
E^{\prime}
as a
B
-module by using the morphism
B\longrightarrow B^{\prime}
.)
Let
(E\otimes_{B}\Omega^{\bullet},d)
(resp.
(E^{\prime}\otimes_{B^{\prime}}\Omega^{\prime}{}^{\bullet},d^{\prime})
)
be a complex of
A
-modules (resp. a complex of
A^{\prime}
-modules).
Assume that
f:=g\otimes h\colon E\otimes_{B}\Omega^{\bullet}\longrightarrow E^{\prime}\otimes_{B^{\prime}}\Omega^{\prime}{}^{\bullet}

is a morphism of complexes of
A
-modules.
Let
f_{\{\langle M\rangle\}}\colon E\otimes_{B}\Omega^{\bullet}\{\langle M\rangle\}\longrightarrow E^{\prime}\otimes_{B^{\prime}}\Omega^{\prime\bullet}\{\langle M\rangle\}

be
f_{\langle M\rangle}\colon E\otimes_{B}\Omega^{\bullet}\langle M\rangle\longrightarrow E^{\prime}\otimes_{B^{\prime}}\Omega^{\prime\bullet}\langle M\rangle

or
f_{\langle\langle M\rangle\rangle}\colon E\otimes_{B}\Omega^{\bullet}\langle\langle M\rangle\rangle\longrightarrow E^{\prime}\otimes_{B^{\prime}}\Omega^{\prime\bullet}\langle\langle M\rangle\rangle

according to the case where
\square
is nothing or
\wedge
.
Consider the mapping cone
{\rm MC}(f_{\{\langle M\rangle\}})
of
f_{\{\langle M\rangle\}}
.
The following holds:

\displaystyle{\rm MC}(f_{\{\langle M\rangle\}})^{q}

\displaystyle=\{\Gamma_{A}(M)^{\square}\overset{\square}{\otimes}_{A}(E\otimes_{B}\Omega^{q+1})\}\oplus\{\Gamma_{A}(M)^{\square}\overset{\square}{\otimes}_{A}(E^{\prime}\otimes_{B^{\prime}}\Omega^{\prime q})\}

\displaystyle=\Gamma_{A}(M)^{\square}\overset{\square}{\otimes}_{A}((E\otimes_{B}\Omega^{q+1})\oplus(E^{\prime}\otimes_{B^{\prime}}\Omega^{\prime q}))

\displaystyle=\Gamma_{A}(M)^{\square}\overset{\square}{\otimes}_{A}{\rm MC}(f)^{q}.

The following is the commutativity of the operation of
the mapping cone and that of the PD-Hirsch extension modulo
the signs of
\varphi
.

Lemma 3.11
.

The following diagram is commutative
:

(3.11.1)

\begin{CD}{\rm MC}(f_{\{\langle M\rangle\}})^{q}=\Gamma_{A}(M)^{\square}\overset{\square}{\otimes}_{A}{\rm MC}(f)^{q}\\
@V{d_{H,(\varphi,\varphi^{\prime})}}V{}V@V{}V{d_{H,(-\varphi,\varphi^{\prime})}}V\\
{\rm MC}(f_{\{\langle M\rangle\}})^{q+1}=\Gamma_{A}(M)^{\square}\overset{\square}{\otimes}_{A}{\rm MC}(f)^{q+1}.\end{CD}

Proof.

For simplicity of notation, consider the following simple element

(m^{[j]}\otimes e\otimes\omega,m^{\prime[j^{\prime}]}\otimes e^{\prime}\otimes\omega^{\prime})\in{\rm MC}(f_{\{\langle M\rangle\}})^{q}\quad(m,m^{\prime}\in M,e\in E,\omega\in\Omega^{q+1},e^{\prime}\in E^{\prime},\omega^{\prime}\in\Omega^{\prime}{}^{q}).

By the boundary morphism

\displaystyle{\rm MC}(f_{\{\langle M\rangle\}})^{q}\longrightarrow{\rm MC}(f_{\{\langle M\rangle\}})^{q+1},

this simple element is mapped to

\displaystyle(-\{m^{[j-1]}\otimes e\otimes\varphi(m)\wedge\omega+m^{[j]}\otimes d(e\otimes\omega)\},

\displaystyle\{m^{\prime}{}^{[j^{\prime}-1]}\otimes e^{\prime}\otimes\varphi^{\prime}(m^{\prime})\wedge\omega^{\prime}+m^{\prime}{}^{[j^{\prime}]}\otimes d^{\prime}(e^{\prime}\otimes\omega^{\prime})\}+m^{[j]}\otimes f(e\otimes\omega))

\displaystyle=\{(m^{[j-1]}\otimes e\otimes(-\varphi(m))\wedge\omega,0)+m^{[j]}\otimes(-d(e\otimes\omega),f(e\otimes\omega))\}

\displaystyle+\{m^{\prime}{}^{[j^{\prime}-1]}\otimes(0,e^{\prime}\otimes\varphi^{\prime}(m^{\prime})\wedge\omega^{\prime})+m^{\prime}{}^{[j^{\prime}]}\otimes(0,d^{\prime}(e^{\prime}\otimes\omega^{\prime}))\}

\displaystyle=d_{H,(-\varphi,\varphi^{\prime})}((m^{[j]}\otimes e\otimes\omega,0))+d_{H,(-\varphi,\varphi^{\prime})}((0,m^{\prime}{}^{[j^{\prime}]}\otimes e^{\prime}\otimes\omega^{\prime}))

\displaystyle=d_{H,(-\varphi,\varphi^{\prime})}((m^{[j]}\otimes e\otimes\omega,m^{\prime}{}^{[j^{\prime}]}\otimes e^{\prime}\otimes\omega^{\prime})).

We leave the rigorous proof for a general element of

{\rm MC}(f_{\{\langle M\rangle\}})^{q}
to the serious reader.
∎

Proposition 3.12
.

Let the notations be as in
(
3.10
)
.
Assume that
M
is a direct summand of a free
A
-module of
countable rank.
If
(\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}(E_{i}\otimes_{B_{i}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{i})[-n_{i}],d)
is acyclic,
then
(\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}(E_{i}\otimes_{B_{i}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{i})[-n_{i}])\langle M\rangle
is acyclic.

Proof.

Because the inductive limit with respect to a directed set
preserves the exactness, we may assume that

M
is a direct summand of a free
A
-module of finite rank.
Because the problem is local on
{\rm Spec}(A)
,
we may assume that
M
is a free
A
-module of finite rank.
Decompose
M=L\oplus N
into a direct sum of free
A
-modules,
where
L

(1\leq i\leq r)
is free of rank 1.
Then
(\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}(E_{i}\otimes_{B_{i}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{i})[-n_{i}])\langle M\rangle=(\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}(E_{i}\otimes_{B_{i}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{i})[-n_{i}])\langle L\rangle\langle N\rangle
.
(Here we have identified

\Gamma_{A}(L)\otimes_{A}(\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}E_{i}\otimes_{B_{i}}\Omega^{q-n_{i}}_{i})

with
\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}(\Gamma_{A}(L)\otimes_{A}E_{i})\otimes_{B_{i}}\Omega^{q-n_{i}}_{i}

for each
q\in{\mathbb{N}}
.)
Hence, by using induction on the rank of
M
,
it suffices to prove that
(\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}(E_{i}\otimes_{B_{i}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{i})[-n_{i}]\langle L\rangle
is acyclic.
Let
l
be a basis of
L
.
Consider the following double complex

(3.12.1)

\footnotesize{\begin{CD}\cdots @>{}>{}>\cdots @>{}>{}>\cdots\\
@A{{\rm id}\otimes d}A{}A@A{{\rm id}\otimes d}A{}A@A{d}A{}A\\
Al^{[2]}\otimes_{A}\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}(E_{i}\otimes_{B_{i}}\Omega^{q-n_{i}}_{i})@>{}>{}>Al\otimes_{A}\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}(E_{i}\otimes_{B_{i}}\Omega^{q+1-n_{i}}_{i})@>{}>{}>\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}(E_{i}\otimes_{B_{i}}\Omega^{q+2-n_{i}}_{i})\\
@A{{\rm id}\otimes d}A{}A@A{{\rm id}\otimes d}A{}A@A{d}A{}A\\
Al^{[2]}\otimes_{A}(\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}(E_{i}\otimes_{B_{i}}\Omega^{q-1-n_{i}}_{i}))@>{}>{}>Al\otimes_{A}(\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}(E_{i}\otimes_{B_{i}}\Omega^{q-n_{i}}_{i}))@>{}>{}>\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}(E_{i}\otimes_{B_{i}}\Omega^{q+1-n_{i}}_{i})\\
@A{{\rm id}\otimes d}A{}A@A{{\rm id}\otimes d}A{}A@A{d}A{}A\\
\cdots\cdots\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}(E_{i}\otimes_{B_{i}}\Omega^{q-n_{i}}_{i})\\
@A{d}A{}A\\
\cdots\cdots\cdots,\end{CD}}

where the horizontal arrow is defined by

l^{[j]}\otimes e_{i}\otimes\omega_{i}\longmapsto l^{[j-1]}\otimes e_{i}\otimes\varphi_{i}(l)\wedge\omega_{i}

(e_{i}\in E_{i},\omega_{i}\in\Omega^{*}_{i})
.
Since
Al^{[j]}

(j\in{\mathbb{N}})
is a free
A
-module,
the columns of (
3.12.1
) is exact.
(Here note that
E\otimes_{B}\Omega^{\bullet}\langle L\rangle
is
not equal to the single complex of the double complex (
3.12.1
) in general:

E\otimes_{B}\Omega^{\bullet}\langle L\rangle
is only contained in the single complex.)
Hence, by an elementary argument,

E\otimes_{B}\Omega^{\bullet}\langle L\rangle
is acyclic (cf.
[
W
, (2.7.3)]
).
∎

Corollary 3.13
.

Let
f\colon E\otimes_{B}\Omega^{\bullet}\longrightarrow E^{\prime}\otimes_{B^{\prime}}\Omega^{\prime\bullet}
be
as after
(
3.10
)
.
Assume
that
f
is a quasi-isomorphism of
A
-modules
and that
M
is a direct summand of a free
A
-module of countable rank.
Then the morphism

f_{\langle M\rangle}\colon E\otimes_{B}\Omega^{\bullet}\langle M\rangle\longrightarrow E^{\prime}\otimes_{B^{\prime}}\Omega^{\prime\bullet}\langle M\rangle

is a quasi-isomorphism of
A
-modules.

Proof.

By the assumption
{\rm MC}(f)
is acyclic.
By (
3.12
),
{\rm MC}(f)\langle M\rangle
is acyclic.
By (
3.11
) we see that this means that
f_{\langle M\rangle}\colon E\otimes_{B}\Omega^{\bullet}\langle M\rangle\longrightarrow E^{\prime}\otimes_{B^{\prime}}\Omega^{\prime\bullet}\langle M\rangle

is a quasi-isomorphism.
∎

Remark 3.14
.

One does not know that
{\rm MC}(f)\langle M\rangle
is isomorphic to

{\rm MC}(f_{\langle M\rangle})
as a complex a priori and
it seems non-clear to me that one can obtain (
3.13
) easily
without using (
3.11
) nor (
3.12
)
because I do not know whether
the associated spectral sequence by the filtration of the columns
of (
3.12.1
) is regular in the sense of
[
W
, (5.2.10)]
.

Proposition-Definition 3.15
.

Let
M
be a projective
A
-module.
Let
{\cal C}
be a category whose objects are

(B,E,\Omega^{\bullet},d,\varphi)
’s
such that
(E\otimes_{B}\Omega^{\bullet},d)
’s are bounded below
and whose morphisms
are defined as after (
3.10
).
Let
{\rm Ho}({\cal C})
be the localized category of
{\cal C}

inverting quasi-isomorphisms
in
{\cal C}
(
[
GeM
, III §2]
, cf.
[
Ha
, p. 29 Remark]
).
Then the functor

\langle M\rangle\colon{\cal C}\longrightarrow\{{\rm complexes}~{\rm of}~A{\textrm{-}}{\rm modules}\}

induces the following derived functor

\langle M\rangle{}^{L}\colon{\rm Ho}({\cal C})\longrightarrow D^{+}(\{{\rm complexes}~{\rm of}~A{\textrm{-}}{\rm modules}\}).

We call the functor
\langle M\rangle{}^{L}

the
derived PD-Hirsch extension by

M
.

Proof.

This follows from (
3.13
).
∎

Proposition-Definition 3.16
.

Let
M
be a projective
A
-module of countable rank.
Let
{\cal C}
be a category whose objects are

(B,\Omega^{\bullet},d,\varphi)
’s
such that
(\Omega^{\bullet},d)
’s are bounded below
and whose morphisms
are defined as after (
3.10
).
Let
{\rm Ho}({\cal C})
be the localized category of
{\cal C}

inverting weakly equivalent isomorphisms
in
{\cal C}
. Let
{\rm DGA}(A)
be the category of dga’s over
A
.
Let
{\rm Ho}({\rm DGA}(A))
be the localized category of
{\rm DGA}(A)

inverting weakly equivalent isomorphisms in
{\rm DGA}(A)
.
Then the functor

\langle M\rangle\colon{\cal C}\longrightarrow{\rm DGA}(A)

induces the following derived functor

\langle M\rangle{}^{L}\colon{\rm Ho}({\cal C})\longrightarrow{\rm Ho}({\rm DGA}(A)).

We call the functor
\langle M\rangle{}^{L}

the
derived PD-Hirsch extension by

M
.

Proof.

This follows from (
3.13
).
∎

Now we apply the theory above for the derived direct images of
morphisms of ringed topoi and the Thom-Whitney derived direct images of
morphisms of dga’s over
{\mathbb{Q}}
in ringed topoi which have enough points.
In the following dga’s are always assumed to be positively graded.

Let
f\colon({\cal T}^{\prime},{\cal A}^{\prime})\longrightarrow({\cal T},{\cal A})
be a morphism of ringed topoi.
Assume that
{\cal A}^{\prime}=f^{*}({\cal A})
.
First let us recall the single complex functor
s
which has been denoted by
{\bf s}

in
[
Nak3
, §2]
(cf.
[
D1
, (5.1.9)]
).

For a positive integer
r
,
let
({\cal T}_{t_{1}\cdots t_{r}},{\cal A}^{t_{1}\cdots t_{r}})_{t_{1},\ldots,t_{r}\in{\mathbb{N}}}

be the constant
r
-semi-simplicial ringed topos
defined by
({\cal T},{\cal A})
:

{\cal T}_{t_{1}\cdots t_{r}}={\cal T}
,

{\cal A}^{t_{1}\cdots t_{r}}={\cal A}
.
Let
{\cal L}
be an object of the category

{\rm C}({\cal A}^{\bullet\cdots\bullet})
of complexes of

{\cal A}^{\bullet\cdots\bullet}
-modules (
r
-points)
(
{\cal L}
is an
r
-semi-simplicial complexes of
{\cal A}^{\bullet\cdots\bullet}
-modules).
For simplicity of notation, set
\underline{t}:=(t_{1},\ldots,t_{r})

and
\underline{t}_{j}:=t_{1}+\cdots+t_{j}
and
\underline{t}_{0}:=0
.
We also set
\underline{\bullet}:=\bullet\cdots\bullet

(
r
-points). The object
{\cal L}
defines an

(r+1)
-uple complex

{\cal L}^{\underline{\bullet}\bullet}=({\cal L}^{t_{1}\cdots t_{r}\bullet})_{t_{1},\ldots,t_{r}\in{\mathbb{N}}}

of
{\cal A}
-modules whose boundary
morphisms will be fixed
in (
3.16.1
) below.
Let
d_{\cal L}\colon{\cal L}^{\underline{t}s}\longrightarrow{\cal L}^{\underline{t},s+1}

be the boundary morphism arising from the boundary
morphism of the complex
{\cal L}
and let

\delta^{i}_{j}\colon{\cal L}^{t_{1}\cdots t_{j}\cdots t_{r}s}\longrightarrow{\cal L}^{t_{1}\cdots t_{j-1},t_{j}+1,t_{j+1}\cdots t_{r}s}

(1\leq j\leq r,0\leq i\leq t_{j}+1)
be
a standard coface morphism.
Consider the single complex
s({\cal L})

with the following boundary morphism
(cf.
[
D2
, (5.1.9.1), (5.1.9.2)]
):

(3.16.1)

s({\cal L})^{n}=\bigoplus_{t_{1}+\cdots+t_{r}+s=n}{\cal L}^{t_{1}\cdots t_{r}s};

\displaystyle d(x^{\underline{t}s})=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{t_{1}+1}(-1)^{i}\delta^{i}_{1}(x^{\underline{t}s})+(-1)^{\underline{t}_{1}}\sum_{i=0}^{t_{2}+1}(-1)^{i}\delta^{i}_{2}(x^{\underline{t}s})+\cdots+

\displaystyle(-1)^{\underline{t}_{r-1}}\sum_{i=0}^{t_{r}+1}(-1)^{i}\delta^{i}_{r}(x^{\underline{t}s})+(-1)^{\underline{t}_{r}}d_{\cal L}(x^{\underline{t}s})\quad(x^{\underline{t}s}\in{\cal L}^{\underline{t}s}).

For a morphism
g\colon{\cal L}^{\underline{\bullet}\bullet}\longrightarrow{\cal N}^{\underline{\bullet}\bullet}
in

{\rm C}({\cal A}^{\underline{\bullet}})={\rm C}({\cal A}^{\bullet\cdots\bullet})
, we define

s(g)\colon s({\cal L}^{\underline{\bullet}\bullet})\longrightarrow s({\cal N}^{\underline{\bullet}\bullet})
as the naturally
induced morphism by
g
(without change of signs);

s
gives a functor

(3.16.2)

s\colon{\rm C}({\cal A}^{\underline{\bullet}})\longrightarrow{\rm C}({\cal A}).

The functor (
3.16.2
)
induces the following functors
:

(3.16.3)

s\colon D^{\star}({\cal A}^{\underline{\bullet}})\longrightarrow D^{\star}({\cal A})\quad(\star=\text{$+$ {\rm or} {\rm nothing}}).

We have the morphism
f_{\underline{\bullet}}\colon({\cal T}^{\prime}_{\underline{\bullet}},{\cal A}^{\prime}{}^{\underline{\bullet}})\longrightarrow({\cal T}_{\underline{\bullet}},{\cal A}^{\underline{\bullet}})

of ringed topoi induced by
f
and the following diagram is commutative:

\begin{CD}D^{\star}({\cal A}^{\prime}{}^{\underline{\bullet}})@>{s}>{}>D^{\star}({\cal A}^{\prime})\\
@V{f_{\underline{\bullet}*}}V{}V@V{}V{f_{*}}V\\
D^{\star}({\cal A}{}^{\underline{\bullet}})@>{s}>{}>D^{\star}({\cal A}).\end{CD}

Now let
{\cal M}
be a locally free
{\cal A}
-module of countable rank.
Assume that the topos
{\cal T}^{\prime}
has enough points.
Let
{\cal B}
,
{\cal E}
,
\Omega^{\bullet}
,
({\cal E}\otimes_{\cal B}\Omega^{\bullet},d)

and
\varphi\colon f^{*}({\cal M})\longrightarrow{\rm Ker}(\Omega^{1}\longrightarrow\Omega^{2})

be analogous objects in

({\cal T}^{\prime},{\cal A}^{\prime})
to
B
,
E
,
\Omega^{\bullet}
,
(E\otimes_{B}\Omega^{\bullet},d)
and

\varphi\colon{\cal M}\longrightarrow{\rm Ker}(\Omega^{1}\longrightarrow\Omega^{2})
, respectively.
For example,
{\cal B}
is a sheaf of
{\cal A}^{\prime}
-algebras.
More generally, we need the
r
-semi-cosimplicial version
of
{\cal B}
,
{\cal E}
,
\Omega^{\bullet}
,
({\cal E}\otimes_{\cal B}\Omega^{\bullet},d)

and
\varphi\colon f^{*}({\cal M})\longrightarrow{\rm Ker}(\Omega^{1}\longrightarrow\Omega^{2})
.
That is, we need
(not necessarily constant)

r
-semi-cosimplicial objects

{\cal B}^{\underline{\bullet}}
,
{\cal E}^{\underline{\bullet}}
,
\Omega^{\underline{\bullet}\bullet}
,

({\cal E}^{\underline{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal B}^{\underline{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\underline{\bullet}\bullet},d)

and
\varphi^{\underline{\bullet}}\colon f^{*}_{\underline{\bullet}}({\cal M}^{\underline{\bullet}})\longrightarrow{\rm Ker}(\Omega^{\underline{\bullet}1}\longrightarrow\Omega^{\underline{\bullet}2})
.
Here
{\cal M}^{\underline{\bullet}}
is
the constant
r
-semi-cosimplicial
{\cal A}^{\underline{\bullet}}
-modules defined by
{\cal M}
.
Let
{\cal C}_{({\cal T}^{\prime}_{\underline{\bullet}},{\cal A}^{\prime}{}^{\underline{\bullet}})}

be the analogous category to
{\cal C}
for

{\cal B}^{\underline{\bullet}}
’s,
{\cal E}^{\underline{\bullet}}
’s,

\Omega^{\underline{\bullet}\bullet}
’s,
({\cal E}^{\underline{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal B}^{\underline{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\underline{\bullet}\bullet},d)
’s
and
\varphi^{\underline{\bullet}}\colon f^{*}_{\underline{\bullet}}({\cal M}^{\underline{\bullet}})\longrightarrow{\rm Ker}(\Omega^{\underline{\bullet}1}\longrightarrow\Omega^{\underline{\bullet}2})
’s above.
By considering the constant case for

{\cal C}_{({\cal T}^{\prime}_{\underline{\bullet}},{\cal A}^{\prime}{}^{\underline{\bullet}})}
,
we obtain the category
{\cal C}_{({\cal T}^{\prime},{\cal A}^{\prime})}
.
We also obtain the analogous category
{\cal C}_{({\cal T},{\cal A})}
to

{\cal C}_{({\cal T}^{\prime},{\cal A}^{\prime})}
.
By using
s
in (
3.16.1
),
we obtain the following functors

(3.16.4)

s\colon{\cal C}_{({\cal T}_{\underline{\bullet}},{\cal A}^{\underline{\bullet}})}\longrightarrow{\cal C}_{({\cal T},{\cal A})}

and

(3.16.5)

s\colon{\cal C}_{({\cal T}^{\prime}_{\underline{\bullet}},{\cal A}^{\prime}{}^{\underline{\bullet}})}\longrightarrow{\cal C}_{({\cal T}^{\prime},{\cal A}^{\prime})}.

We also obtain the following functors

(3.16.6)

f\colon{\cal C}_{({\cal T}^{\prime},{\cal A}^{\prime})}\longrightarrow{\cal C}_{({\cal T},{\cal A})}

and

(3.16.7)

f_{\underline{\bullet}*}\colon{\cal C}_{({\cal T}^{\prime}_{\underline{\bullet}},{\cal A}^{\prime}{}^{\underline{\bullet}})}\longrightarrow{\cal C}_{({\cal T}_{\underline{\bullet}},{\cal A}^{\underline{\bullet}})}.

Obviously the following diagram is commutative:

(3.16.8)

\begin{CD}{\cal C}_{({\cal T}^{\prime}_{\underline{\bullet}},{\cal A}^{\prime}{}^{\underline{\bullet}})}@>{s}>{}>{\cal C}_{({\cal T}^{\prime},{\cal A}^{\prime})}\\
@V{f_{\underline{\bullet}*}}V{}V@V{}V{f_{*}}V\\
{\cal C}_{({\cal T}_{\underline{\bullet}},{\cal A}^{\underline{\bullet}})}@>{s}>{}>{\cal C}_{({\cal T},{\cal A})}.\end{CD}

For each
i
, let
I^{\underline{\bullet}\bullet i}
be
the Godement resolution of

{\cal E}^{\underline{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal B}^{\underline{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\underline{\bullet}i}

of
{\cal A}^{\prime}
-modules.
Then we obtain a morphism

\varphi^{0}\colon f^{*}_{\underline{\bullet}}({\cal M}^{\underline{\bullet}})\longrightarrow{\rm Ker}(I^{\underline{\bullet}01}\longrightarrow I^{\underline{\bullet}02})

fitting into the following commutative diagram:

\begin{CD}{\rm Ker}(\Omega^{\underline{\bullet}1}\longrightarrow\Omega^{\underline{\bullet}2})@>{}>{}>{\rm Ker}(I^{\underline{\bullet}01}\longrightarrow I^{\underline{\bullet}02})\\
@A{\varphi}A{}A@A{}A{\varphi^{0}}A\\
f^{*}_{\underline{\bullet}}({\cal M}^{\underline{\bullet}})=f^{*}_{\underline{\bullet}}({\cal M}^{\underline{\bullet}}).\end{CD}

By using this diagram and the analogous diagrams repeatedly,
we have a natural morphism

\varphi^{\underline{\bullet}\bullet}\colon f^{*}_{\underline{\bullet}}({\cal M}^{\underline{\bullet}})\longrightarrow{\rm Ker}(I^{\underline{\bullet}\bullet 1}\longrightarrow I^{\underline{\bullet}\bullet 2})
.
This morphism induces the morphism

\varphi^{\underline{\bullet}\bullet}\colon{\cal M}^{\underline{\bullet}}\longrightarrow{\rm Ker}(f_{\underline{\bullet}*}(I^{\underline{\bullet}\bullet 1})\longrightarrow f_{\underline{\bullet}*}(I^{\underline{\bullet}\bullet 2}))
.
We have the following equality:

(3.16.8)

\displaystyle s(f_{\underline{\bullet}*}(I^{\underline{\bullet}\bullet\bullet}\langle f^{*}_{\underline{\bullet}}({\cal M})\rangle))=s(f_{\underline{\bullet}*}(I^{\underline{\bullet}\bullet\bullet}))\langle{\cal M}\rangle

in the category
{\cal C}_{({\cal T},{\cal A})}
.
Indeed,

(3.16.9)

\displaystyle s(f_{\underline{\bullet}*}(I^{\underline{\bullet}\bullet\bullet}\langle f^{*}_{\underline{\bullet}}({\cal M})\rangle))^{q}

\displaystyle=s(f_{\underline{\bullet}*}(\Gamma_{{\cal A}^{\prime}}(f^{*}({\cal M}))\otimes_{{\cal A}^{\prime}}I^{\underline{\bullet}\bullet\bullet}))^{q}

\displaystyle=\Gamma_{\cal A}({\cal M})\otimes_{\cal A}s(f_{\underline{\bullet}*}(I^{\underline{\bullet}\bullet\bullet}))^{q}=s(f_{\underline{\bullet}*}(I^{\underline{\bullet}\bullet\bullet}))\langle{\cal M}\rangle^{q}

and the equality above gives the equality

s(f_{\underline{\bullet}*}(I^{\underline{\bullet}\bullet\bullet}\langle f^{*}_{\underline{\bullet}}({\cal M})\rangle))=s(f_{\underline{\bullet}*}(I^{\underline{\bullet}\bullet\bullet}))\langle{\cal M}\rangle

of complexes.
Set

(3.16.10)

\displaystyle Rf_{*}({\cal E}^{\underline{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal B}^{\underline{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\underline{\bullet}\bullet})\langle{\cal M}\rangle^{L}

\displaystyle:=f_{*}(s(I^{\underline{\bullet}\bullet}\langle f^{*}_{\underline{\bullet}}({\cal M})\rangle))=s(f_{\underline{\bullet}*}(I^{\underline{\bullet}\bullet\bullet}\langle f^{*}_{\underline{\bullet}}({\cal M})\rangle))

\displaystyle=s(f_{\underline{\bullet}*}(I^{\underline{\bullet}\bullet\bullet}))\langle{\cal M}\rangle

in
{\rm Ho}({\cal C}_{({\cal T},{\cal A})})
.

Definition 3.17
.

We call
Rf_{*}({\cal E}^{\underline{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal B}^{\underline{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\underline{\bullet}\bullet})\langle{\cal M}\rangle^{L}

the
derived PD-Hirsch extension
of

Rf_{*}({\cal E}^{\underline{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal B}^{\underline{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\underline{\bullet}\bullet})
by
{\cal M}=({\cal M},\varphi^{\underline{\bullet}})
.
By (
3.13
),
Rf_{*}(?)\langle{\cal M}\rangle^{L}
is the following functor

(3.17.1)

\displaystyle Rf_{*}(?)\langle{\cal M}\rangle^{L}\colon{\rm Ho}({\cal C}_{({\cal T}^{\prime}_{\underline{\bullet}},{\cal A}^{\prime}{}^{\underline{\bullet}})})\longrightarrow{\rm Ho}({\cal C}_{({\cal T},{\cal A})}).

Remark 3.18
.

(1) Consider the case
r=0
. Let
J^{\bullet}
be an
f_{*}
-acyclic resolution of

{\cal E}\otimes_{\cal B}\Omega^{\bullet}
.
We should note that we
cannot define the PD-Hirsch extensions of
J^{\bullet}
nor
f_{*}(J^{\bullet})
in general.

(2) Note that there exists a natural functor
from the category
{\rm Ho}({\cal C}_{({\cal T},{\cal A})})
to
the derived category
D^{+}({\cal A})
of bounded below complexes of
{\cal A}
-modules.
By abuse of notation, we also denote by

Rf_{*}(?)\langle{\cal M}\rangle^{L}
the composite functor of the functor (
3.17.1
)
and this natural functor.
If
J^{\underline{\bullet}\bullet}
is an
f_{\underline{\bullet}*}
-acyclic resolution of

{\cal E}^{\underline{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal B}^{\underline{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\underline{\bullet}\bullet}\langle f^{*}_{\underline{\bullet}}({\cal M})\rangle
,
then

Rf_{*}(?)\langle{\cal M}\rangle^{L}=f_{*}(s(J^{\underline{\bullet}\bullet}))
in
D^{+}({\cal A})

since
I^{\underline{\bullet}\bullet}\langle f^{*}_{\underline{\bullet}}({\cal M})\rangle

is
f_{\underline{\bullet}*}
-acyclic by
(
3.16.9
) and (
3.12
).

(3) We also have the category

{\rm Ho}{\rm F}({\cal C}_{({\cal T}^{\prime}_{\underline{\bullet}},{\cal A}^{\prime}{}^{\underline{\bullet}})})
of
“objects of

{\rm Ho}{\rm F}({\cal C}_{({\cal T}^{\prime}_{\underline{\bullet}},{\cal A}^{\prime}{}^{\underline{\bullet}})})
with increasing filtrations” and
we have the following functor

(3.18.1)

\displaystyle Rf_{*}(?)\langle{\cal M}\rangle^{L}\colon{\rm Ho}{\rm F}({\cal C}_{({\cal T}^{\prime}_{\underline{\bullet}},{\cal A}^{\prime}{}^{\underline{\bullet}})})\longrightarrow{\rm Ho}{\rm F}({\cal C}_{({\cal T},{\cal A})}).

by using the Godement resolution and giving a filtration
on
{\cal M}
which is compatible with
the multiplicative structure of
\Gamma_{\cal A}({\cal M})
.

Let
\Omega^{\underline{\bullet}\bullet}
be a dga over
{\cal A}^{\prime}

(
\Omega^{\underline{\bullet}\bullet}
is assumed to be positively graded.).
Assume that
{\cal A}
is a sheaf of
{\mathbb{Q}}
-algebras.
Let
I^{\underline{\bullet}\bullet\bullet}
be the Godement resolution of
\Omega^{\underline{\bullet}\bullet}
.
Assume that we are given a morphism

\varphi^{\underline{\bullet}}\colon f^{*}_{\underline{\bullet}}({\cal M})\longrightarrow{\rm Ker}(\Omega^{\underline{\bullet}1}\longrightarrow\Omega^{\underline{\bullet}2})
.
Then we have a natural morphism

\varphi^{\underline{\bullet}}\colon f^{*}_{\underline{\bullet}}({\cal M})\longrightarrow{\rm Ker}(I^{\underline{\bullet}\bullet 1}\longrightarrow I^{\underline{\bullet}\bullet 2})
.
Let
s_{\rm TW}
be the single complex functor defined in
[
Nav
, p. 24]
.
Here we set
k:={\cal A}
in [loc. cit.].
Then

({\underset{r~{\rm times}}{\underbrace{s_{\rm TW}\circ\cdots\circ s_{\rm TW}}}}\circ f_{\underline{\bullet}*}(I^{\underline{\bullet}\bullet\bullet}\langle f^{*}_{\underline{\bullet}}({\cal M})\rangle))

is a dga over
{\cal A}
(
[
Nav
, p. 27]
).
For simplicity, denote this dga by

s_{\rm TW}\circ f_{\underline{\bullet}*}(I^{\underline{\bullet}\bullet\bullet}\langle f^{*}_{\underline{\bullet}}({\cal M})\rangle).

Let
{\mathfrak{A}}
be
{\cal A}^{\prime}
or
{\cal A}
.
Let
A^{\geq 0}({\mathfrak{A}})
be the category of positively graded dga’s over
{\mathfrak{A}}
.
Let
{\rm Ho}(A^{\geq 0}({\mathfrak{A}}))

be the localized category of

A^{\geq 0}({\mathfrak{A}})

inverting the weakly equivalent filtered isomorphisms
in
A^{\geq 0}({\mathfrak{A}})
(cf.
[
Nav
]
,
[
GeM
]
).
Let
{\rm A}^{\underline{\bullet},\geq 0}({\mathfrak{A}})

be the category of positively-graded
r
-semi-cosimplicial dga’s
over
{\mathfrak{A}}
.
The dga
s_{\rm TW}\circ f_{\underline{\bullet}*}(I^{\underline{\bullet}\bullet\bullet}\langle f^{*}_{\underline{\bullet}}({\cal M})\rangle)
defines an object
of
{\rm Ho}(A^{\geq 0}({\cal A}))
.
We denote this object by
R_{\rm TW}f_{*}(\Omega^{\underline{\bullet}\bullet})\langle{\cal M}\rangle^{L}
.

Definition 3.19
.

Let
{\rm Ho}({\rm A}^{\underline{\bullet},\geq 0}({\mathfrak{A}}))

be the localized category of
{\rm A}^{\underline{\bullet},\geq 0}({\mathfrak{A}})

inverting the weakly equivalent filtered isomorphisms.
Set

\displaystyle R_{\rm TW}f_{*}(\Omega^{\underline{\bullet}\bullet}):=s_{\rm TW}\circ f_{\underline{\bullet}*}(I^{\underline{\bullet}\bullet\bullet})\in{\rm Ho}(A^{\geq 0}({\cal A})).

We call the object

the

of

.

We also have the category

\displaystyle Rf_{*}(?)\langle{\cal M}\rangle^{L}\colon{\rm Ho}({\rm A}^{\underline{\bullet},\geq 0}{\rm F}({\cal A}^{\prime}))\longrightarrow{\rm Ho}({\rm A}^{\geq 0}{\rm F}({{\cal A}})).

by using the Godement resolution and giving weights
of local sections of
{\cal M}
which is compatible with

.

4
PD-Hirsch extensions of log crystalline complexes

Let
S
be a family of log points on which a prime number
p
is locally nilpotent.
Let
M_{S}
be the log structure of
S
.
Let
(T,{\cal J},\delta)
be a fine log PD-scheme of
S

such that
{\cal J}
is quasi-coherent.
Set
T_{0}:=T~{\rm mod}~{\cal J}
.
Let
((T,{\cal J},\delta),z)
be a log PD-enlargement of
S
, where

z\colon T_{0}\longrightarrow S
is the structural morphism.
Let
S_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}
,
S(T)
and
S(T)^{\natural}
be as in §
2
.
Let
\widetilde{t}
be a local section of
M_{S(T)^{\natural}}
which gives a local generator
t

of
\overline{M}_{S(T)^{\natural}}:=M_{S(T)^{\natural}}/{\cal O}_{T}^{*}
.
Then

\displaystyle d\log t:=d\log\widetilde{t}\in\Omega^{1}_{S(T)^{\natural}/\overset{\circ}{T}}

is independent of the choice of
\widetilde{t}
;
d\log t
depends only on
t
.
Let
X/S
be an SNCL scheme (
[
Nak4
, (1.1)]
).
Set
X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}:=X\times_{S}S_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}
.
In this section we assume that there exists an immersion

X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal P}
into a log smooth scheme
over
\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}
and
we prove fundamental properties of
the PD-Hirsch extensions of pre-weight filtered log crystalline complexes obtained by
this immersion for a flat quasi-coherent crystal on
\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}
.

Let
{\mathfrak{D}}(\overline{S(T)^{\natural}})
be the log PD-envelope of
the immersion
S_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}
over

(\overset{\circ}{T},{\cal J},\delta)
;

{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}(\overline{S(T)^{\natural}})}
is locally isomorphic
to the PD-polynomial algebra
{\cal O}_{T}\langle\tau\rangle=\bigoplus_{n=0}^{\infty}{\cal O}_{T}\tau^{[n]}
in one variable.

For the time being, we consider a more general case than the SNCL case.

Let
Y
be a log smooth scheme over
S
.
Set
Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}:=Y\times_{S}S_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}=Y\times_{\overset{\circ}{S}}\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}
.
Assume that there exists an immersion

Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal Q}
into
a log smooth scheme over
\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}
.
Set
{\cal Q}:=\overline{\cal Q}\times_{\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}}S(T)^{\natural}
.
Let
F
be a flat quasi-coherent crystal of

{\cal O}_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
-modules.
(In the case where
F={\cal O}_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
,
it is enough to assume that there exists an immersion

Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal Q}

into a log smooth scheme over
S(T)^{\natural}

(we do not need
\overline{\cal Q}
in this case).)
Let
\overline{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}
and
{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}

be the exactifications of the immersions

Y\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal Q}
and

Y\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal Q}
, respectively.
Let
\overline{\mathfrak{E}}
be
the log PD-envelope of
the immersion
Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal Q}
over

(\overset{\circ}{T},{\cal J},\delta)
.

Let
(\overline{\cal F},\overline{\nabla})

be the quasi-coherent
{\cal O}_{\overline{\mathfrak{E}}}
-module
with integrable connection corresponding to the log crystal
F
:

(4.0.1)

\overline{\nabla}\colon\overline{\cal F}\longrightarrow\overline{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\overline{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{1}_{\overline{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}}.

In fact, we have the complex

\overline{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\overline{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
.
Set
{\cal F}:=\overline{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}(\overline{S(T)^{\natural}})}}{\cal O}_{S(T)^{\natural}}
.
Then we see that
\overline{\nabla}
induces the following connection:

(4.0.2)

\nabla\colon{\cal F}\longrightarrow{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{1}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}}.

Indeed, we obtain the connection
\nabla
because

(4.0.3)

\displaystyle df^{[i]}=f^{[i-1]}df\quad(i\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1})

for a local section

f
of the PD-ideal sheaf of
{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}(\overline{S(T)^{\natural}})}
.
Here we have used the formula (
3.6.1
).
When
i\geq 2
,
f^{[i-1]}df=0
in
{\Omega}^{1}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
;
when
i=1
,
d\tau=\tau d\log\tau=0
in

{\Omega}^{1}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}}

for a local section
\tau

of
{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}(\overline{S(T)^{\natural}})}
such that

{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}(\overline{S(T)^{\natural}})}\simeq{\cal O}_{T}\langle\tau\rangle
.
In fact, we have the complex

{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
.

By using natural surjective morphisms

\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\longrightarrow\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}/S(T)^{\natural}}
,

\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\longrightarrow\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/S(T)^{\natural}}
,
we also have the following log de Rham complexes

\overline{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\overline{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}/S(T)^{\natural}}
and

{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/S(T)^{\natural}}
.

Remark 4.1
.

In
[
Nak4
, (1.3.4)]
we have proved that the natural morphism

(4.1.1)

{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\cal Q}}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal Q}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\longrightarrow\Omega^{i}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\quad(i\in{\mathbb{N}})

is an isomorphism.
Consequently the natural morphism

(4.1.2)

{\cal O}_{\mathfrak{E}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\cal Q}}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal Q}/T}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{\mathfrak{E}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/T}

is an isomorphism.
Hence we can replace
{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}
by
{\cal Q}
in the argument above.
However
{\cal Q}
will be not an appropriate object in the SNCL case in general;
in this SNCL case we consider the pre-weight filtered crystalline complex
in which case
\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
is not a good complex in general.
For this reason, we consider only
{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}
henceforth.

Set

\displaystyle U_{S(T)^{\natural}}:={\cal O}_{T}u,

where
u
is a formal variable over
{\cal O}_{T}

and
consider the following sheaf

\displaystyle\Gamma_{{\cal O}_{T}}(U_{S(T)^{\natural}}):=\bigoplus_{n=0}^{\infty}{\cal O}_{T}u^{[n]}

of PD-polynomial algebras over
{\cal O}_{T}
.
We consider the following PD-Hirsch extension

{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{{\rm ex}}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}{}^{{\rm ex}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle:=\Gamma_{{\cal O}_{T}}(U_{S(T)^{\natural}})\otimes_{{\cal O}_{T}}{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{{\rm ex}}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}{}^{{\rm ex}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}

of
{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}{}^{{\rm ex}}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}{}^{{\rm ex}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}

by the PD-algebra

\Gamma_{{\cal O}_{T}}(U_{S(T)^{\natural}})

over
{\cal O}_{T}
with respect to the morphism

U_{S(T)^{\natural}}\owns u\longmapsto d\log t\in{\rm Ker}(\Omega^{1}_{{\cal Q}{}^{{\rm ex}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\longrightarrow\Omega^{2}_{{\cal Q}{}^{{\rm ex}}/\overset{\circ}{T}})

(cf.
[
KH
, p. 1260]
for the case of log de Rham-Witt complexes).
The connection

\nabla\colon{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{{\rm ex}}}}\Omega^{j}_{{\cal Q}^{{\rm ex}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle\longrightarrow{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}{}^{{\rm ex}}}}\Omega^{j+1}_{{\cal Q}{}^{{\rm ex}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle\quad(j\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0})

is, by definition, an
{\cal O}_{T}
-linear morphism defined by the following

(4.1.3)

\nabla(u^{[i]}\otimes\omega)=u^{[i-1]}d\log t\wedge\omega+u^{[i]}\otimes\nabla(\omega)\quad(~\omega\in{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{j}_{{\cal Q}{}^{{\rm ex}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}~).

By (
3.10
) or an easy direct calculation,
\nabla^{2}=0
.

The completion
\Gamma_{{\cal O}_{T}}(U_{S(T)^{\natural}})^{\wedge}=\prod_{m\in{\mathbb{N}}}{\cal O}_{T}u^{[m]}
of

\Gamma_{{\cal O}_{T}}(U_{S(T)^{\natural}})
(cf.
[
EY
]
)
is isomorphic to the completed PD-polynomial algebra of one variable
over
{\cal O}_{T}
.
Then we have the following natural inclusion morphism

(4.1.4)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{{\cal O}_{T}}(U_{S(T)^{\natural}})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\Gamma_{{\cal O}_{T}}(U_{S(T)^{\natural}})^{\wedge}.

We also consider the following PD-Hirsch extension by the
completed PD-polynomial algebra of one variable over
{\cal O}_{T}

with respect to the morphism
{\cal O}_{T}u\owns u\longmapsto d\log t\in{\rm Ker}(\Omega^{1}_{{\cal Q}{}^{{\rm ex}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\longrightarrow\Omega^{2}_{{\cal Q}{}^{{\rm ex}}/\overset{\circ}{T}})
:

(4.1.5)

{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{{\rm ex}}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}{}^{{\rm ex}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle\langle u\rangle\rangle:=\Gamma_{{\cal O}_{T}}\langle U_{S(T)^{\natural}}\rangle^{\wedge}\overset{\wedge}{\otimes}_{{\cal O}_{T}}({\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{{\rm ex}}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}{}^{{\rm ex}}/\overset{\circ}{T}})

of
{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}{}^{{\rm ex}}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}{}^{{\rm ex}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
;
the boundary morphism

(4.1.6)

\nabla\colon{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{{\rm ex}}}}\Omega^{j}_{{\cal Q}^{{\rm ex}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle\langle u\rangle\rangle\longrightarrow{\cal F}{\otimes}_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}{}^{{\rm ex}}}}\Omega^{j+1}_{{\cal Q}{}^{{\rm ex}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle\langle u\rangle\rangle\quad(j\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0})

is, by definition, an
{\cal O}_{T}
-linear morphism defined by the following

(4.1.7)

\nabla(\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}a_{i}u^{[i]}\otimes\omega)=\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}a_{i}u^{[i-1]}d\log t\wedge\omega+\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}a_{i}u^{[i]}\otimes\nabla(\omega)\quad(~a_{i}\in{\cal O}_{T},\omega\in{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{j}_{{\cal Q}{}^{{\rm ex}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}~).

We can easily check an equality
\nabla^{2}=0
for
\nabla
in (
4.1.6
).
The complex

\Gamma_{{\cal O}_{T}}\langle\langle U_{S(T)^{\natural}}\rangle\rangle\widehat{\otimes}_{{\cal O}_{T}}{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{{\rm ex}}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{{\rm ex}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}

is the single complex of the following double complex:

(4.1.8)

\begin{CD}\cdots @>{}>{}>\cdots @>{}>{}>\cdots\\
@A{{\rm id}\otimes\nabla}A{}A@A{{\rm id}\otimes\nabla}A{}A@A{{\rm id}\otimes\nabla}A{}A\\
\Gamma_{{\cal O}_{T},2}(U_{S(T)^{\natural}})\otimes_{{\cal O}_{T}}{\cal F}@>{(?)^{\prime}d\log t\wedge}>{}>\Gamma_{{\cal O}_{T},1}(U_{S(T)^{\natural}})\otimes_{{\cal O}_{T}}{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{1}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}}@>{(?)^{\prime}d\log t\wedge}>{}>{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{2}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\\
@A{{\rm id}\otimes\nabla}A{}A@A{{\rm id}\otimes\nabla}A{}A\\
\Gamma_{{\cal O}_{T},1}(U_{S(T)^{\natural}})\otimes_{{\cal O}_{T}}{\cal F}@>{(?)^{\prime}d\log t\wedge}>{}>{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{1}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\\
@A{\nabla}A{}A\\
{\cal F},\end{CD}

where the horizontal arrow
(?)^{\prime}d\log t\wedge
is defined by

u^{[i]}\otimes f\otimes\omega\longmapsto u^{[i-1]}\otimes f\otimes(d\log t\wedge\omega)
.
Note that the columns and the rows of (
4.1.8
) correspond to
the rows and the columns of the double complexes in
[
KH
, p. 1260]
and

[
EY
, (3.19)]
, respectively; we use the general principle about rows and columns
of double complexes stated in
[
Nak3
, §2]
.

The double complex (
4.1.8
) is
naturally augmented to

{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/S(T)^{\natural}}
since

d\log t=0
in
\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/S(T)^{\natural}}
.
Hence we have the following morphism of complexes:

(4.1.9)

{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle\langle u\rangle\rangle\longrightarrow{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/S(T)^{\natural}}.

The morphism (
4.1.4
) induces the following inclusion morphism

(4.1.10)

\displaystyle{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle\langle u\rangle\rangle.

By composing (
4.1.10
) with (
4.1.9
), we have the following morphism

(4.1.11)

{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle\longrightarrow{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/S(T)^{\natural}}.

We would like to prove that the morphism (
4.1.9
) is a quasi-isomorphism.
In order to prove this, we need the following key lemma, which is a quite nontrivial work to
formulate it.

Lemma 4.2
.

Let
C
be a site. Let
{\cal B}
be a sheaf of commutative rings with unit elments on
C
.
Let
{\cal F}^{\bullet\bullet}=(\prod_{i\in{\mathbb{N}},j\in{\mathbb{Z}}}{\cal F}^{-i,j},\prod_{i\in{\mathbb{N}},j\in{\mathbb{Z}}}d^{-i,j},\prod_{i\in{\mathbb{N}},j\in{\mathbb{Z}}}d^{\prime-i,j})

be a double complex of
{\cal B}
-modules on
C
,
where
d^{-i,j}\colon{\cal F}^{-i,j}\longrightarrow{\cal F}^{-i+1,j}
and

d^{\prime}{}^{-i,j}\colon{\cal F}^{-i,j}\longrightarrow{\cal F}^{-i,j+1}
are morphism of
{\cal B}
-modules on
C

such that
d^{-i+1j}\circ d^{-i,j}=0
,
d^{\prime-i,j+1}\circ d^{\prime}{}^{-i,j}=0
and

d^{\prime}{}^{-i+1,j}\circ d^{-i,j}+d^{-i,j+1}\circ d^{\prime}{}^{-i,j}=0
.
Let
{\cal G}^{\bullet}
be a complex of
{\cal B}
-modules on
C
.
Let
\epsilon^{j}\colon{\cal F}^{0j}\longrightarrow{\cal G}^{j}

(j\in{\mathbb{Z}})
be a morphism of
{\cal B}
-modules on
C

such that
\epsilon^{\bullet}:=\{\epsilon^{j}\}_{j\in{\mathbb{Z}}}
induces morphisms

{\cal F}^{0\bullet}\longrightarrow{\cal G}^{\bullet}
and
{\cal F}^{-1,j}\overset{d^{-1,j}}{\longrightarrow}{\cal F}^{0j}\overset{\epsilon^{j}}{\longrightarrow}{\cal G}^{j}

(j\in{\mathbb{Z}})
of complexes of
{\cal B}
-modules.
Let
\{V_{\mu}\}_{\mu\in M}
be a basis of
C
. That is, for any covering family

\{U_{\lambda}\}
of any object
U
of
C
and for any
\lambda
, there exists a covering family

\{V_{\mu}\}_{\mu\in M^{\prime}}

(M^{\prime}\subset M)
of
U_{\lambda}
.
Assume that the following sequence

(4.2.1)

\displaystyle\cdots\longrightarrow\Gamma(V_{\mu},{\cal F}^{-i,j})\longrightarrow\Gamma(V_{\mu},{\cal F}^{-i+1,j})\longrightarrow\cdots\longrightarrow\Gamma(V_{\mu},{\cal F}^{0j})\longrightarrow\Gamma(V_{\mu},{\cal G}^{j})\longrightarrow 0

is exact for any
\mu\in M
and any
j\in{\mathbb{Z}}
.
Then the natural morphism
s({\cal F}^{\bullet\bullet})\longrightarrow{\cal G}^{\bullet}

is a quasi-isomorphism.

Proof.

(The proof of (
4.2
) is not obvious
because we consider the product of infinitely many sheaves;
taking the product of infinitely many sheaves and taking stalks of sheaves
does not necessarily commute as pointed out by our referee.)
We prove that the induced morphism

{\cal H}^{q}(s({\cal F}^{\bullet\bullet}))\longrightarrow{\cal H}^{q}({\cal G}^{\bullet})

(q\in{\mathbb{N}})

is an isomorphism.
This is a local problem.
The degree
q
-part of the complex
s({\cal F}^{\bullet\bullet})
is

\prod_{i+j=q}{\cal F}^{ij}
.
For any object
U
of
C
,

(4.2.2)

\displaystyle\Gamma(U,\prod_{i+j=q}{\cal F}^{ij})=\prod_{i+j=q}\Gamma(U,{\cal F}^{ij}).

By using the exact sequence (
4.2.1
), we see that
the following morphism

\displaystyle{\rm Ker}(\Gamma(V_{\mu},\prod_{i+j=q}{\cal F}^{ij})\longrightarrow\Gamma(V_{\mu},\prod_{i+j=q+1}{\cal F}^{ij}))\longrightarrow{\rm Ker}(\Gamma(V_{\mu},{\cal G}^{q})\longrightarrow\Gamma(V_{\mu},{\cal G}^{q+1}))

induced by
\epsilon^{\bullet}
is surjective.
Since
\{V_{\mu}\}_{\mu\in M}
is a basis of
C
, this means that
the morphism

\displaystyle{\rm Ker}(\prod_{i+j=q}{\cal F}^{ij}\longrightarrow\prod_{i+j=q+1}{\cal F}^{ij})\longrightarrow{\rm Ker}({\cal G}^{q}\longrightarrow{\cal G}^{q+1})

is surjective. Hence
the morphism
{\cal H}^{q}(s({\cal F}^{\bullet\bullet}))\longrightarrow{\cal H}^{q}({\cal G}^{\bullet})

is surjective.

The rest we have to prove is the injective of the morphism

{\cal H}^{q}(s({\cal F}^{\bullet\bullet}))\longrightarrow{\cal H}^{q}({\cal G}^{\bullet})
.
Let
\omega^{\prime}
be a local section of
{\cal H}^{q}(s({\cal F}^{\bullet\bullet}))
.
We may assume that there exist
V_{\mu}
and
a section
\omega=(\omega_{ij})_{i+j=q}

(\omega_{ij}\in\Gamma(V_{\mu},{\cal F}^{ij}))

of

{\rm Ker}(\Gamma(V_{\mu},\prod_{i+j=q}{\cal F}^{ij})\longrightarrow\Gamma(V_{\mu},\prod_{i+j=q+1}{\cal F}^{ij}))

whose image in
{\cal H}^{q}(s({\cal F}^{\bullet\bullet}))

is equal to
\omega^{\prime}
.
Assume that the image of
\omega^{\prime}
in
{\cal H}^{q}({\cal G}^{\bullet})
is zero.
Then we may assume that
the image of
\omega

in

\Gamma(V_{\mu},{\cal H}^{q}({\cal F}^{\bullet}))
is zero.
Then there exists a covering
\{V_{\mu^{\prime}}\}_{\mu^{\prime}}
of
V_{\mu}
such that
the image of
\omega_{0q}|_{V_{\mu^{\prime}}}
in

\Gamma(V_{\mu^{\prime}},{\cal G}^{q})
is the image of an element of
\Gamma(V_{\mu^{\prime}},{\cal G}^{q-1})
.
Because the morphism

\Gamma(V_{\mu^{\prime}},{\cal F}^{0,q-1})\longrightarrow\Gamma(V_{\mu^{\prime}},{\cal G}^{q-1})

is surjective by the exactness of (
4.2.1
),
we can take an element
\eta_{0,q-1}
whose image in

\Gamma(V_{\mu^{\prime}},{\cal G}^{q-1})
is the element above.
By the exactness of (
4.2.1
), we see that
there exists an element
\eta_{-1,q}
of
\Gamma(V_{\mu^{\prime}},{\cal F}^{-1,q})

such that

\omega_{0q}|_{V_{\mu^{\prime}}}=d^{\prime}{}^{0,q-1}(\eta_{0,q-1})+d^{-1,q}(\eta_{-1,q})
.
(It is not necessary to take an object of
C
which is smaller
than
V_{\mu^{\prime}}
to find
\eta_{-1,q}
. This is the point of this proof.)
Because
d^{-1,q+1}(\omega_{-1,q+1})+d^{\prime}{}^{0q}(\omega_{0q})=0
,

\displaystyle d^{-1,q+1}(\omega_{-1,q+1}|_{V_{\mu^{\prime}}})

\displaystyle=-d^{\prime}{}^{0q}(\omega_{0q}|_{V_{\mu^{\prime}}})=-d^{\prime}{}^{0q}(d^{\prime}{}^{0,q-1}(\eta_{0,q-1})+d^{-1,q}(\eta_{-1,q}))

\displaystyle=d^{-1,q+1}(d^{\prime-1,q}(\eta_{-1,q})),

there exists an element

\eta_{-2.q+1}
of

\Gamma(V_{\mu^{\prime}},{\cal F}^{-2,q+1})
such that

\omega_{-1,q+1}|_{V_{\mu^{\prime}}}=d^{-1,q}(\eta_{-1,q})+d^{-2,q+1}(\eta_{-2,q+1})

by the exactness of (
4.2.1
) again.
Continuing this argument repeatedly,
we obtain an element

(\eta_{ij})_{i+j=q-1}
of

\Gamma(V_{\mu^{\prime}},\{{\cal F}^{ij}\}_{i+j=q-1})

whose coboundary is equal to
\omega|_{V_{\mu^{\prime}}}
.
Hence the class
[\omega|_{V_{\mu^{\prime}}}]
in

H^{q}(\Gamma(V_{\mu^{\prime}},s({\cal F}^{\bullet\bullet})))
is zero.
Because we have a natural composite morphism

\displaystyle{\rm Ker}(\Gamma(V_{\mu},\prod_{i+j=q}{\cal F}^{ij})\longrightarrow\Gamma(V_{\mu},\prod_{i+j=q+1}{\cal F}^{ij}))\longrightarrow H^{q}(\Gamma(V_{\mu^{\prime}},s({\cal F}^{\bullet\bullet})))\longrightarrow\Gamma(V_{\mu^{\prime}},{\cal H}^{q}(s({\cal F}^{\bullet\bullet})))

and because the image of
\omega|_{V_{\mu^{\prime}}}
is equal to
\omega^{\prime}|_{V_{\mu^{\prime}}}
,

\omega^{\prime}|_{V_{\mu^{\prime}}}=0
. Hence
\omega^{\prime}=0
.
∎

Now we can prove the following:

Theorem 4.3
.

The morphism
(
4.1.9
)
is a quasi-isomorphism.

Proof.

By (
4.2
) we have only to prove that

(4.3.1)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\Gamma(W,{\cal F})\overset{d\log t\wedge}{\longrightarrow}\Gamma(W,{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{1}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}})\overset{d\log t\wedge}{\longrightarrow}\cdots\overset{d\log t\wedge}{\longrightarrow}\Gamma(W,{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{j-2}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}})

\displaystyle\overset{d\log t\wedge}{\longrightarrow}\Gamma(W,{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{j-1}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}})\overset{d\log t\wedge}{\longrightarrow}\Gamma(W,{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{j}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/S(T)^{\natural}})\longrightarrow 0.

is exact for a log affine open subscheme
W
of
{\mathfrak{E}}
.
In
[
Nak4
, (1.7.26)]
we have proved that
the following complex is exact for an integer
j
:

(4.3.2)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\longrightarrow{\cal F}\overset{d\log t\wedge}{\longrightarrow}{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{1}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\overset{d\log t\wedge}{\longrightarrow}\cdots\overset{d\log t\wedge}{\longrightarrow}{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{j-2}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}}

\displaystyle\overset{d\log t\wedge}{\longrightarrow}{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{j-1}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\overset{d\log t\wedge}{\longrightarrow}{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{j}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/S(T)^{\natural}}\longrightarrow 0.

Because
H^{q}(Z,{\cal G})=0

(q\geq 1)
for an affine scheme
Z

and a quasi-coherent
{\cal O}_{Z}
-module
{\cal G}
(
[
EGA III-1
, (1.3.1)]
), because

{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}}

(i\in{\mathbb{N}})
is a quasi-coherent

{\cal O}_{\mathfrak{E}}
-module and because the morphism
d\log t\wedge
is
an
{\cal O}_{\mathfrak{E}}
-linear morphism,
the complex (
4.3.1
) is exact.
∎

Remark 4.4
.

In
[
KH
, Lemma 6]
Kim and Hain
have claimed that the natural morphism

(4.4.1)

{\cal W}\widetilde{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{Y}\langle u\rangle\longrightarrow{\cal W}\Omega^{\bullet}_{Y}

is a quasi-isomorphism for a log smooth scheme
Y
of Cartier type
