over the log point of a perfect field of characteristic
p>0
.
However, in
[
EY
, (3.36) (2)]
Ertl and Yamada have pointed out that
there is a non-small gap of the proof of Kim and Hain’s claim above.
In
[
KH
, Lemma 6]
Kim and Hain do not either prove that
the natural morphism

(4.4.2)

{\cal W}\widetilde{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{Y}\langle\langle u\rangle\rangle\longrightarrow{\cal W}\Omega^{\bullet}_{Y}

is a quasi-isomorphism because
the operations taking the direct product of sheaves and taking stalks
do not commute in general as pointed out by our referee;
we do not know whether we can use
[
W
, (2.7.3) 2]
immediately.
In §
21
we shall prove that the morphisms (
4.4.1
) and
(
4.4.2
) are isomorphisms.
Note also that the difference between the claim that (
4.4.1
)
is a quasi-isomorphism
and the claim that (
4.4.2
) is a quasi-isomorphism is big.

Definition 4.5
.

We say that the crystal
F
has
no poles

if the morphism

\overline{\nabla}\colon\overline{\cal F}\longrightarrow\overline{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\overline{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{1}_{\overline{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}}

factors through the natural morphism

\overline{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\overline{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{1}_{\overset{\circ}{\overline{\cal Q}}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\longrightarrow\overline{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\overline{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{1}_{\overline{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
.

Example 4.6
.

Let
\overset{\circ}{Y}_{T_{0}}
be the underlying scheme of
Y_{T_{0}}
.
Let
F^{\prime}
be a flat crystal of
{\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{Y}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
-modules.
Let

\epsilon\colon((Y_{\overset{\circ}{T_{0}}}/\overset{\circ}{T})_{\rm crys},{\cal O}_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T_{0}}}/\overset{\circ}{T}})\longrightarrow((\overset{\circ}{Y}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}_{0})_{\rm crys},{\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{Y}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}})

be the induced morphism of ringed topoi by the morphism

Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{Y}_{T_{0}}
forgetting the log structures of

Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}
over
\overset{\circ}{T}
.
If
F=\epsilon^{*}(F^{\prime})
, then
F
has no poles
if
the underlying (formal) scheme
(\overline{\cal Q})^{\circ}
is (formally) smooth over
\overset{\circ}{T}
.
Indeed,
F^{\prime}
corresponds to
a quasi-coherent
{\cal O}_{\overline{\mathfrak{E}}}
-module

\overline{\cal F}
with integrable connection

\overline{\nabla}\colon\overline{\cal F}\longrightarrow\overline{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\overline{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{1}_{(\overline{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex})^{\circ}/\overset{\circ}{T}}.

In particular,
{\cal O}_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}

has no poles.

Definition 4.7

(
cf.
[
KtN
, (0.4), (0.5)]
).

If, locally on
Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}
, there exists a finite increasing filtration

\{F_{i}\}_{i\in{\mathbb{Z}}}
of
flat quasi-coherent
{\cal O}_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
-modules on
F

such that
{\rm gr}_{i}F:=F_{i}/F_{i-1}

(\forall i\in{\mathbb{Z}})

is a flat quasi-coherent
{\cal O}_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
-module
and such that
{\rm gr}_{i}F
has no poles,
then we say that
F
is

a flat locally nilpotent quasi-coherent

{\cal O}_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
-modules
.

In the following we always assume that
F
is
a flat locally nilpotent quasi-coherent

{\cal O}_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
-modules.
The following theorem is a first key theorem in this book.

Theorem 4.8
.

The morphism
(
4.1.11
)

is a quasi-isomorphism.

Proof.

(The following proof has come to me by
recalling theory of log de Rham-Witt complexes
(see
[
Nak4
, (2.2.3) (1)]
): (
4.8.5
) below is a key point for the proof.)
This is a local problem.
In
[
Nak4
, (1.7.22.1)]
we have proved that
the following sequence

(4.8.1)

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{\mathfrak{E}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}}/S(T)^{\natural}}[-1]\overset{d\log t\wedge}{\longrightarrow}{\cal O}_{\mathfrak{E}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{\mathfrak{E}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}/S(T)^{\natural}}\longrightarrow 0

is exact.
In fact, the following sequence is exact:

(4.8.2)

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/S(T)^{\natural}}[-1]\overset{d\log t\wedge}{\longrightarrow}{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\longrightarrow{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/S(T)^{\natural}}\longrightarrow 0.

Here note that the morphism

\displaystyle d\log t\wedge\colon{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/S(T)^{\natural}}[-1]\longrightarrow{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}}

is indeed a morphism of complexes.
(The exact sequence in
[
HK
, (3.6)]
is mistaken because

\wedge d\log t
in [loc. cit.] is not a morphism of complexes.)
Assume that there exists a flat quasi-coherent

{\cal O}_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
-submodules
F^{\prime}
of
F

such that
{\rm gr}F:=F/F^{\prime}
has no poles
and such that
{\rm gr}F
is a flat quasi-coherent

{\cal O}_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
-module.
Let
({\cal F}^{\prime},\nabla^{\prime})
be the integrable connection corresponding to
F^{\prime}
.
The crystals
F^{\prime}
and
{\rm gr}F:=F/F^{\prime}
give us the log de Rham complexes

{\cal F}^{\prime}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
and

{\cal F}/{\cal F}^{\prime}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
, respectively.
Set
{\rm gr}{\cal F}:={\cal F}/{\cal F}^{\prime}
.
Let
\iota\colon{\cal F}^{\prime}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
be the natural inclusion
and let
{\rm MC}(\iota)
be the mapping cone of
\iota
.
Then we have the natural quasi-isomorphism

{\rm MC}(\iota)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}{\rm gr}{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
.
By (
3.13
) we have a quasi-isomorphism

\displaystyle{\rm MC}(\iota)\langle u\rangle\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}{\rm gr}{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle.

Hence, by (
3.11
), we obtain the following isomorphism

\displaystyle{\rm MC}(\iota_{\langle U_{S(T)^{\natural}}\rangle})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}{\rm gr}{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle.

This means that the upper sequence of the following commutative diagram
is exact:

(4.8.3)

\begin{CD}0@>{}>{}>{\cal F}_{0}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle @>{}>{}>{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
0@>{}>{}>{\cal F}_{0}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/S(T)^{\natural}}@>{}>{}>{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/S(T)^{\natural}}\end{CD}

\begin{CD}@>{}>{}>{\rm gr}{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle\longrightarrow 0\\
@V{}V{}V\\
@>{}>{}>{\rm gr}{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/S(T)^{\natural}}\longrightarrow 0.\end{CD}

Assume that the morphism (
4.1.11
) is a quasi-isomorphism for

{\rm gr}F
. Then, by (
4.8.3
),
the morphism (
4.1.11
) for
F
is a quasi-isomorphism if and only if
the morphism (
4.1.11
) for
F^{\prime}
is a quasi-isomorphism.
By making the same argument for
F^{\prime}
as above repeatedly,
we may assume that
F
has no poles
since
F
is flat and locally nilpotent.

For simplicity of notation, set
\Omega^{\bullet}:={\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
and

\Omega^{\bullet}_{/S(T)^{\natural}}:={\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/S(T)^{\natural}}
.
We claim that the following sequence

(4.8.4)

0\longrightarrow{\cal H}^{q-1}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{/S(T)^{\natural}})\overset{d\log t\wedge}{\longrightarrow}{\cal H}^{q}(\Omega^{\bullet})\longrightarrow{\cal H}^{q}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{/S(T)^{\natural}})\longrightarrow 0

for
q\in{\mathbb{N}}
obtained by (
4.8.1
) is exact.
We have only to prove
that the following inclusion holds:

(4.8.5)

\displaystyle\nabla({\Omega}^{q-1})\cap\{d\log t\wedge({\Omega}^{q-1})\}\subset d\log t\wedge(\nabla({\Omega}^{q-2})).

Set
\omega_{1}:=d\log t
.
Let us take a local basis
\{\omega_{1},\ldots,\omega_{r}\}
of

\Omega^{1}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
.
Take a local section

f:=\sum_{1\leq i_{1}<\cdots<i_{q-1}\leq r}f_{i_{1}\cdots i_{q-1}}\omega_{i_{1}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\omega_{i_{q-1}}\quad(f_{i_{1}\cdots i_{q-1}}\in{\cal F})

of
{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}{\Omega}^{q-1}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
.
Decompose
f
as follows:

f=\sum_{2\leq i_{2}<\cdots<i_{q-1}\leq r}f_{1i_{2}\cdots i_{q-1}}\omega_{1}\wedge\omega_{i_{2}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\omega_{i_{q-1}}+\sum_{2\leq i_{1}<\cdots<i_{q-1}\leq r}f_{i_{1}\cdots i_{q-1}}\omega_{i_{1}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\omega_{i_{q-1}}.

Then

(4.8.6)

\displaystyle\nabla(f)

\displaystyle=\sum_{2\leq i_{2}<\cdots<i_{q-1}\leq r}\nabla(f_{1i_{2}\cdots i_{q-1}})\wedge\omega_{1}\wedge\omega_{i_{2}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\omega_{i_{q-1}}

\displaystyle-\sum_{2\leq i_{2}<\cdots<i_{q-1}\leq r}f_{1i_{2}\cdots i_{q-1}}\omega_{1}\wedge d(\omega_{i_{2}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\omega_{i_{q-1}})

\displaystyle+\sum_{2\leq i_{1}<\cdots<i_{q-1}\leq r}\nabla(f_{i_{1}\cdots i_{q-1}}\omega_{i_{1}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\omega_{i_{q-1}}).

Because
F
has no poles,
the third term on the right hand side of (
4.8.6
) can be expressed as follows:

(4.8.7)

\displaystyle\sum_{2\leq i_{1}<\cdots<i_{q-1}\leq r}g_{i_{1}\cdots i_{q-1}}\tau\omega_{1}\wedge\omega_{i_{1}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\omega_{i_{q-1}}+\sum_{2\leq i_{1}<\cdots<i_{q}\leq r}h_{i_{1}\cdots i_{q}}\omega_{i_{1}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\omega_{i_{q}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{2\leq i_{1}<\cdots<i_{q}\leq r}h_{i_{1}\cdots i_{q}}\omega_{i_{1}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\omega_{i_{q}},

for some
g_{i_{1}\cdots i_{q}}\in{\cal F}
and
h_{i_{1}\cdots i_{q}}\in{\cal F}
.
Assume that
\nabla(f)\in\omega_{1}\wedge({\Omega}^{q-1})
.
Then the last term in (
4.8.7
) vanishes.
Hence

\displaystyle\nabla(f)

\displaystyle=-\omega_{1}\wedge(\sum_{2\leq i_{2}<\cdots<i_{q-1}\leq r}\nabla(f_{1i_{2}\cdots i_{q-1}})\wedge\omega_{i_{2}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\omega_{i_{q-1}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{2\leq i_{2}<\cdots<i_{q-1}\leq r}f_{1i_{2}\cdots i_{q-1}}\wedge d(\omega_{i_{2}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\omega_{i_{q-1}}))

\displaystyle=-\omega_{1}\wedge\nabla(\sum_{2\leq i_{2}<\cdots<i_{q-1}\leq r}f_{1i_{2}\cdots i_{q-1}}\omega_{i_{2}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\omega_{i_{q-1}}).

Thus we have proved that the inclusion (
4.8.5
) holds and consequently
we see that the sequence (
4.8.4
) is exact.

By the exactness of (
4.8.4
), the following sequence is exact:

(4.8.8)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\longrightarrow{\cal H}^{0}({\Omega}^{\bullet})\overset{d\log t\wedge}{\longrightarrow}{\cal H}^{1}({\Omega}^{\bullet})\overset{d\log t\wedge}{\longrightarrow}\cdots\cdots\overset{d\log t\wedge}{\longrightarrow}{\cal H}^{q-1}({\Omega}^{\bullet})\overset{d\log t\wedge}{\longrightarrow}{\cal H}^{q}({\Omega}^{\bullet})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow{\cal H}^{q}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{/S(T)^{\natural}})\longrightarrow 0.

By the exactness of (
4.8.8
) and the lemma (
4.9
) below,
this implies that the following natural morphism

(4.8.9)

\displaystyle{\cal H}^{q}(\Omega^{\bullet}\langle u\rangle)\longrightarrow{\cal H}^{q}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{/S(T)^{\natural}})\quad(q\in{\mathbb{N}})

is an isomorphism.

We complete the proof of this theorem.
∎

Lemma 4.9
.

Let
A
be a commutative ring with unit element.
Let

M^{\bullet\bullet}=(\prod_{i\in{\mathbb{N}},j\in{\mathbb{Z}}}M^{-i,j},\prod_{i\in{\mathbb{N}},j\in{\mathbb{Z}}}d^{-i,j},\prod_{i\in{\mathbb{N}},j\in{\mathbb{Z}}}d^{\prime-i,j})

be a double complex of
A
-modules,
where
d^{-i,j}\colon M^{-i,j}\longrightarrow M^{-i+1,j}
and

d^{\prime}{}^{-i,j}\colon M^{-i,j}\longrightarrow M^{-i,j+1}
are morphism of
A
-modules
such that
d^{-i+1j}\circ d^{-i,j}=0
,
d^{\prime-i,j+1}\circ d^{\prime}{}^{-i,j}=0
and

d^{\prime}{}^{-i+1,j}\circ d^{-i,j}+d^{-i,j+1}\circ d^{\prime}{}^{-i,j}=0
.
Let
N^{\bullet}
be a complex of
A
-modules.
Let
\epsilon^{j}\colon M^{0j}\longrightarrow N^{j}

(j\in{\mathbb{Z}})
be a morphism of
A
-modules
such that
\epsilon^{\bullet}:=\{\epsilon^{j}\}_{j\in{\mathbb{Z}}}
induces morphisms

M^{0\bullet}\longrightarrow N^{\bullet}
and
M^{-1,j}\overset{d^{-1,j}}{\longrightarrow}M^{0j}\overset{\epsilon^{j}}{\longrightarrow}N^{j}

(j\in{\mathbb{Z}})
of complexes of
A
-modules.
Assume that the following sequence

(4.9.1)

\displaystyle\cdots\overset{d^{-i-1,j}}{\longrightarrow}M^{-i,j}\overset{d^{-i,j}}{\longrightarrow}M^{-i+1,j}\overset{d^{-i+1,j}}{\longrightarrow}\cdots M^{0j}\overset{\epsilon^{j}}{\longrightarrow}N^{j}\longrightarrow 0

is exact and that
the following natural sequence

(4.9.2)

\displaystyle H^{j}(M^{-i,\bullet},d^{\prime}{}^{-i,\bullet})\overset{H^{j}(d^{-i,j})}{\longrightarrow}H^{j}(M^{-i+1,\bullet},d^{\prime}{}^{-i+1,\bullet})\overset{H^{j}(d^{-i+1,j})}{\longrightarrow}H^{j}(M^{-i+2,\bullet},d^{\prime}{}^{-i+2,\bullet})

is exact.
Denote the sub-double complex

(\bigoplus_{i,j\in{\mathbb{N}}}M^{-i,j},\bigoplus_{i,j\in{\mathbb{N}}}d^{-i,j},\bigoplus_{i,j\in{\mathbb{N}}}d^{\prime-i,j})_{i\in{\mathbb{N}},j\in{\mathbb{Z}}}

of
M^{\bullet\bullet}

simply by
\bigoplus_{i,j\in{\mathbb{N}}}M^{-i,j}
.
Then
the natural morphism
s(\bigoplus_{i,j\in{\mathbb{N}}}M^{-i,j})\longrightarrow N^{\bullet}
of complexes of
A
-modules
induces the following injective morphism

(4.9.3)

\displaystyle H^{q}(s(\bigoplus_{i,j\in{\mathbb{N}}}M^{-i,j}))\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}H^{q}(N^{\bullet})\quad(q\in{\mathbb{Z}}).

Furthermore, assume that the following natural morphism

{\rm Ker}(M^{0j}\longrightarrow M^{0,j+1})\longrightarrow{\rm Ker}(N^{0j}\longrightarrow N^{0,j+1})

is surjective.
Then the natural morphism
s(\bigoplus_{i,j\in{\mathbb{N}}}M^{-i,j})\longrightarrow N^{\bullet}
of complexes
induces the following isomorphism

(4.9.4)

\displaystyle H^{q}(s(\bigoplus_{i,j\in{\mathbb{N}}}M^{-i,j}))\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}H^{q}(N^{\bullet})\quad(q\in{\mathbb{Z}}).

Proof.

If we omit the proof of this lemma whose proof is elementary
(I do not say that this lemma is elementary),
then the reader has to have some time to give the proof.
Hence we give the proof for the reader’s convenience.

Let
q
be an integer and let
k
be a non-negative integer.
Let
\sum_{i=0}^{k}\omega^{-i,q+i}

(\omega^{-i,q+i}\in M^{-i,q+i})
be an element of

{\rm Ker}(\sum_{-i+j=q}(d^{-i,j}+d^{\prime}{}^{-i,j})\colon s(\bigoplus_{-i+j=q}M^{-i,j})\longrightarrow s(\bigoplus_{-i+j=q+1}M^{-i,j})).

Then

(4.9.5)

\displaystyle d^{\prime}{}^{-k,q+k}(\omega^{-k,q+k})=0.

Assume that
\epsilon^{q}(\omega^{0q})\in{\rm Im}(\partial^{q-1})
.
By the exactness of (
4.9.1
), there exists an element

\eta^{0,q-1}\in M^{0,q-1}
such that

\epsilon^{q}(\omega^{0q}-d^{\prime 0,q-1}(\eta^{0,q-1}))=0
.
By the exactness of (
4.9.1
) again, there exists an element

\eta^{-1,q}\in M^{-1,q}
such that

\omega^{0q}=d^{-1,q}(\eta^{-1,q})+d^{\prime 0,q-1}(\eta^{0,q-1}).

By the exactness of (
4.9.1
) again, there exists an element

\eta^{-2,q+1}\in M^{-2,q+1}
such that

\omega^{-1,q+1}=d^{-2,q+1}(\eta^{-2,q+1})+d^{\prime-1,q}(\eta^{-1,q})

as in the proof of (
4.2
).
Continuing this process repeatedly,
we obtain
\eta^{0,q-1},\ldots,\eta^{-k,q+k-1}
.
Because
\omega^{-k,q+k}-d^{\prime}{}^{-k,q+k-1}(\eta^{-k,q+k-1})\in{\rm Ker}(d^{-k,q+k})

and because

d^{\prime}{}^{-k,q+k}(\omega^{-k,q+k}-d^{\prime}{}^{-k,q+k-1}(\eta^{-k,q+k-1}))=0

by (
4.9.5
),
there exists an element
\eta^{-k-1,q+k}\in{\rm Ker}(d^{\prime}{}^{-k-1,q+k})
such that

\omega^{-k,q}=d^{-k-1,q}(\eta^{-k-1,q})+d^{\prime}{}^{-k,q+k-1}(\eta^{-k,q+k-1})

by the exactness of (
4.9.2
).
Hence

\sum_{i=0}^{k}\omega^{-i,q+i}
is the coboundary of

\sum_{i=0}^{k+1}\eta^{-i,q+i-1}
.
This means that the morphism (
4.9.3
) is injective.
The surjectivity of the morphism (
4.9.4
) is obvious.
∎

Remark 4.10
.

(1) As pointed out in
[
Nak4
, (2.2.3) (1)]
, the formula

(4.10.1)

\displaystyle(d\widetilde{\omega}^{i-1}_{{\cal Y}_{n}}\cap(\widetilde{\omega}^{i-1}_{{\cal Y}_{n}}\wedge(dT/T)))\subset d\widetilde{\omega}^{i-2}_{{\cal Y}_{n}}\wedge dT/T

in
[
Hy
, p. 246]

is an obvious formula about which
the editorial comment
[
Hy
, (6), (11A)]
is unnecessary.
Because the boundary morphism

{\cal H}^{i}(\Omega_{{\cal Y}_{n}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})})\longrightarrow{\cal H}^{i+1}(\Omega_{{\cal Y}_{n}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})})

is “
p^{-n}d
”,
the above formula is not a precisely necessary ingredient
for the injectivity needed in [loc. cit.].
The boundary morphism of
{\cal H}^{i}(\Omega_{{\cal Y}_{n}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})})\longrightarrow{\cal H}^{i+1}(\Omega_{{\cal Y}_{n}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})})
has been misunderstood in
[loc. cit.] and the editorial comment for (
4.10.1
).

(2) As pointed out in
[
Nak4
, (2.2.3) (2)]
,
the proof of the injectivity of the morphism

\theta_{n}\wedge\colon W_{n}\omega^{q-1}_{Y}\longrightarrow W_{n}\widetilde{\omega}^{q}_{Y}

has not been given in
[
M
]
, though it has been claimed
in [loc. cit., p. 311].

(3)
I think that no general theorem, e. g., the classical convergent theorem
[
W
, (5.5.1)]

nor the complete convergence theorem
[
W
, (5.5.10)]
implies
(
4.8
) because the filtration by columns
of
\Omega^{\bullet}\langle u\rangle
is
not
complete.
Let us check that this claim is true by giving the following argument more precisely.

Let
F
be an increasing filtration
on
\Omega^{\bullet}\langle U_{S(T)^{\natural}}\rangle

defined analogously as (
3.8.1
).
That is,

\displaystyle F_{i}(\Omega^{\bullet}\langle u\rangle):=\bigoplus_{j\geq-i}\Gamma_{{\cal O}_{T},j}(U_{S(T)^{\natural}})\otimes_{{\cal O}_{T}}\Omega^{\bullet}\quad(i\in{\mathbb{Z}}).

For
i,j\in{\mathbb{N}}
, set

d^{\prime\prime}:={\rm id}\otimes\nabla\colon{\cal O}_{T}u^{[i]}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{T}}\Omega^{j-i}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{T}u^{[i]}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{T}}\Omega^{j+1-i}.

Let

{}^{\prime\prime}{\cal H}^{q}

be the cohomology with respect to
d^{\prime\prime}
.
Consider the following spectral sequence with respect to
F

(4.10.2)

\displaystyle E_{1}^{-p,p+q}={\cal H}^{q}({\rm gr}^{-p}_{F}(\Omega^{\bullet}\langle u\rangle))\Longrightarrow{\cal H}^{q}(\Omega^{\bullet}\langle u\rangle).

This spectral sequence degenerates at
E_{2}
.
Indeed, this is a local problem.
We have the following equality:

\displaystyle E_{1}^{-p,p+q}

\displaystyle=^{\prime\prime}\!{\cal H}^{q+p}({\cal O}_{T}u^{[p]}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{T}}{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}})={\cal H}^{q+p}({\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}}).

The exact sequence (
4.8.8
) tells us that

E_{2}^{0q}={\cal H}^{q}({\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/S(T)^{\natural}})

and
E_{2}^{pq}=0
for
p\not=0
.
In particular,
E_{\infty}^{pq}=E_{2}^{pq}=0
.
By (
4.8
)

\displaystyle{\cal H}^{q}({\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle)=E_{2}^{0q}.

This means that
F_{0}{\cal H}^{q}({\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle)={\cal H}^{q}({\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle)
and

F_{-1}{\cal H}^{q}({\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle)=0
.
Because the filtration
F
on

{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle

is
not
complete, the problem whether
the spectral sequence (
4.10.2
) is convergent is a non-trivial problem;
(
4.8
) tells us that (
4.10.2
) is indeed convergent.

The following is a generalization of the log crystalline sheafied version including torsions
(not only the log crystalline cohomological version) of
[
EY
, (3.35) (4)]
:

Corollary 4.11
.

The morphism
(
4.1.10
)
is a quasi-isomorphism.

Proof.

This follows from (
4.3
) and (
4.8
).
∎

Remark 4.12
.

(1) In
[
EY
, (3.25) (4)]
Yamada and Ertl have proved
that the induced morphism of the morphism (
4.1.10
)
in the context of log rigid cohomologies
is a quasi-isomorphism
when
Y/S
is the log special fiber of
a strictly semistable scheme over a complete discrete valuation ring of mixed characteristics
with perfect residue field by a different method from our method:
the proof of the corresponding statement to (
4.8
)
in the context of log rigid cohomologies is different from ours.

(2) In
[
EY
]
the completed PD-Hirsch extension (
4.1.5
)
of
\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}}

in the case of the trivial coefficient for the strict semistable family
has a very important role. In this book we suppress to use
(
4.1.5
) as an important ingredient.

Remark 4.13
.

(1)
Define decreasing filtrations
F
’s on

{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{{\cal Q}}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{{\cal Q}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle

and
{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{{\cal Q}}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{{\cal Q}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}

by the following formulas, respectively:

(4.13.1)

\displaystyle F^{i}({\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{{\cal Q}}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{{\cal Q}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle):=\sum_{j\in{\mathbb{N}}}{\cal O}_{T}u^{[j]}\otimes{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{{\cal Q}}}}\Omega^{\bullet\geq i-j}_{{{\cal Q}}/\overset{\circ}{T}},

(4.13.2)

\displaystyle F^{i}({\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{{\cal Q}}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{{\cal Q}}/S(T)^{\natural}}):={\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{{\cal Q}}}}\Omega^{\bullet\geq i}_{{{\cal Q}}/S(T)^{\natural}}.

(
Note that the former filtration
F
is not finite.
)

Then we can prove that the following morphism induced by (
4.1.11
)

(4.13.3)

\displaystyle{\rm gr}_{F}^{i}({\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{{\cal Q}}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{{\cal Q}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle)\longrightarrow{\rm gr}_{F}^{i}({\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{{\cal Q}}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{{\cal Q}}/S(T)^{\natural}})

is a quasi-isomorphism.
This is a
p
-adic generalized analogue of
[
F1
, (5.3)]
.
Furthermore assume that
\overset{\circ}{\cal Q}
is quasi-compact.
As a corollary of this result, we can prove that
there exists a positive integer
i_{0}
such that,
for all
i\geq i_{0}
,
the following morphism

(4.13.4)

\displaystyle{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{{\cal Q}}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{{\cal Q}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle/F^{i}\longrightarrow{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{{\cal Q}}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{{\cal Q}}/S(T)^{\natural}}/F^{i}

induces the following quasi-isomorphism:

(4.13.5)

\displaystyle{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{{\cal Q}}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{{\cal Q}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle/F^{i}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{{\cal Q}}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{{\cal Q}}/S(T)^{\natural}}.

However we do not
use these results in this book because
the filtrations
F
’s (
4.13.1
) and (
4.13.2
)
are non-canonical in our case.

(2)
Let the notations be as in
[
F1
, (5.9)]
.
By using
[
F1
, (5.3)]
(cf. (
4.13.5
)),
Fujisawa has proved that
there exists a morphism

(4.13.6)

\displaystyle K_{\mathbb{C}}\longrightarrow\omega^{\bullet}_{X/*^{k}}\quad(k\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1})

in a derived category of bounded above
{\mathbb{C}}_{X}
-modules
which induces an isomorphism

(4.13.7)

\displaystyle H^{q}(X,K_{\mathbb{C}})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}H^{q}(X,\omega^{\bullet}_{X/*^{k}})

by using the general theory of Hodge-Deligne
(
[
F1
, (5.9)]
). However he has not proved that the morphism
(
4.13.6
) itself is an isomorphism.
The obvious analogues of (
4.8
)
and (
4.18
) below in the complex analytic case
are new results. They tell us that the morphism
(
4.13.6
) is indeed an isomorphism for the case
k=1
.
Consequently we do not need any result of
Hodge-Deligne theory for the proof of (
4.13.7
).
Hence the obvious analogue of (
4.8
) can play
an important role in [loc. cit., (5.9)] at least in the case
k=1
.
We can also prove that the morphism (
4.13.6
) is an isomorphism
for a general
k
.
However we do not discuss this more in this book.
See
[
Nak5
]
for details.

Now let
X
be an SNCL scheme over
S_{0}
.
Let
(T,{\cal J},\delta)
,
T_{0}
and
z\colon T_{0}\longrightarrow S
be
as in the beginning of this section.
For simplicity of notation, we denote
\overset{\circ}{X}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}:=\overset{\circ}{X}\times_{\overset{\circ}{S}}\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}
by

\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}(:=(X_{T_{0}})^{\circ})
.
Indeed,
\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}
is the underlying scheme of

X_{T_{0}}
since the structural morphism
X\longrightarrow S
is integral.
Let
\Delta:=\{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda,T_{0}}\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
be a decomposition
of
\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}
by its smooth components.
In this section we assume that there exists
an immersion
X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal P}

into a log smooth scheme over
\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}
.
Let
\overline{\mathfrak{D}}
be the log PD-envelope of

X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal P}
over

(\overset{\circ}{T},{\cal J},\delta)
.
Set
{\mathfrak{D}}:=\overline{\mathfrak{D}}\times_{{\mathfrak{D}}(\overline{S(T)^{\natural}})}S(T)^{\natural}

and

{\cal P}^{\rm ex}:=\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}\times_{\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}}S(T)^{\natural}
.
Then
{\cal P}^{\rm ex}
is the exactification of
the immersion
X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal P}
((
2.9
)).

Because
\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}

and
\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}

are topologically isomorphic,
we can define a closed subscheme

\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{\underline{\lambda}}
of

\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}

corresponding to
\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda},T_{0}}

for an element

\underline{\lambda}=\{\lambda_{0},\cdots,\lambda_{m}\}\in P(\Lambda)

(m\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0},\lambda_{i}\in\Lambda,\lambda_{i}\not=\lambda_{j}~{\rm if}~i\not=j)

as explained in §
2
, where
\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda_{i},T_{0}}
denotes
a smooth component of
\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}
in this section.
Endow
\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{\underline{\lambda}}

with the pull-back of the log structure of
{\cal P}^{\rm ex}

and let
{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\underline{\lambda}}
be the resulting log scheme.
Let
m
be a nonnegative integer.
Endow
\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(m)}

with the pull-back of the log structure of

{\cal P}^{\rm ex}

and let
{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(m)}
be the resulting log scheme.
Then, by (
2.12.1
),

(4.13.8)

{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(m)}=\underset{\#\underline{\lambda}=m+1}{\coprod}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{\underline{\lambda}}.

For the convenience of notation, we
set
{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(-1)}:={\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}
.
As usual,
\{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(m)}\}_{m\in{\mathbb{N}}}
defines
a semi-simplicial log scheme in a standard way.

Remark 4.14
.

It is easy to show that the log scheme

{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\underline{\lambda}}
is not log smooth over
S(T)^{\natural}

for
\underline{\lambda}\not=\emptyset

(cf.
[
GK
, p. 2947]
).
Let
L
be the subsheaf defined by the preimage of

{\rm Ker}({\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{{\cal P}_{\underline{\lambda}}})

by the structural morphism

M_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}
.
Let
L_{\underline{\lambda}}
be the pull-back of
L

by the closed immersion
{\cal P}_{\underline{\lambda}}\longrightarrow{\cal P}^{\rm ex}
.
Then
{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\underline{\lambda}}
with the ideal sheaf

L_{\underline{\lambda}}
of
M_{{\cal P}_{\underline{\lambda}}}

is ideally log smooth over
S(T)^{\natural}

(cf.
[
GK
, Remarks 1.10]
) since
{\cal P}^{\rm ex}
is log smooth over
S(T)^{\natural}
.

Proposition 4.15
.

Set
U=S(T)^{\natural}
or
U=\overset{\circ}{T}
.
Let
i
be a nonnegative integer.
Then the following hold
:

(1)

The canonical morphism

(4.15.1)

\Omega^{i}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/U}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\underline{\lambda}}}\longrightarrow\Omega^{i}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\underline{\lambda}/U}}

is an isomorphism.

(2)
Let
m
be a nonnegative integer.
The canonical morphism

(4.15.2)

\Omega^{i}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/U}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(m)}}\longrightarrow\Omega^{i}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(m)}/U}

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

We have only to prove (1).
Let
{\cal I}_{\underline{\lambda}}
be the defining ideal sheaf of
the immersion
{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\underline{\lambda}}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal P}^{\rm ex}
.
Then, by
[
NS
, Lemma 2.1.3]
(cf.
[
KtN
, (3.6)(2)]
),
we have the following second fundamental exact sequence

(4.15.3)

{\cal I}_{\underline{\lambda}}/{\cal I}_{\underline{\lambda}}^{2}\overset{d}{\longrightarrow}\Omega^{1}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/U}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\underline{\lambda}}}\longrightarrow\Omega^{1}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\underline{\lambda}/U}}\longrightarrow 0.

Let
m
be a local section of
M_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}
such that

\alpha(m)\in{\cal I}_{\underline{\lambda}}
, where
\alpha\colon M_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}

is the structural morphism.
Then
d\alpha(m)=\alpha(m)d\log m=0
in

\Omega^{1}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/U}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\underline{\lambda}}}
. Because

{\cal I}_{\underline{\lambda}}
has a system of generators coming from local
sections of
M_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}
, the morphism
d
in
(
4.15.3
) is zero. Hence we obtain (1).
∎

Let
E
be a flat quasi-coherent crystal of

{\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
-modules.

Let

\epsilon_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\colon((X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T})_{\rm crys},{\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}})\longrightarrow((\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T})_{\rm crys},{\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}})

be the morphism of ringed topoi induced by the morphism

\epsilon_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}\colon X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}

over
\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}
forgetting the log structure of
X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}
.

Let
(\overline{\cal E},\overline{\nabla})

be the quasi-coherent
{\cal O}_{\overline{\mathfrak{D}}}
-module
with integrable connection corresponding to
the log crystal
\epsilon_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(E)
:

(4.15.4)

\overline{\nabla}\colon\overline{\cal E}\longrightarrow\overline{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{1}_{\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}}.

Set
({\cal E},{\nabla}):=(\overline{\cal E},\overline{\nabla})\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}(\overline{S(T)^{\natural}})}}{\cal O}_{S(T)^{\natural}}
:

(4.15.5)

\nabla\colon{\cal E}\longrightarrow{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{1}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}}.

In fact, we have the log de Rham complexes

\overline{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
and

{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
.

Lemma 4.16
.

The family

\{{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\underline{\lambda}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\}_{i\geq 0}

forms a complex.
Consequently the family

\{{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(m)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\}_{i\geq 0}

for
m\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}
forms a complex.

Proof.

The problem is local.
We may assume that there exists a solid and étale morphism

{\cal P}^{\rm prex}{}^{\prime}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{A}}_{S(T)^{\natural}}(a,d^{\prime})
in (
2.11.1
).
Let
\lambda
be an element of
\underline{\lambda}
.
Let
x_{\lambda}
be a coordinate of the affine ring of

{\mathbb{A}}_{S(T)^{\natural}}(a,d^{\prime})
such that
{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\lambda}
is
defined by
x_{\lambda}=0
.
Then, for
e\in{\cal E}
and
\omega\in\Omega^{i}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
,

\displaystyle\nabla(x_{\lambda}e\otimes\omega)=e\otimes dx_{\lambda}\wedge\omega+x_{\lambda}\nabla(e\otimes\omega)=x_{\lambda}(e\otimes d\log x_{\lambda}\wedge\omega+\nabla(e\otimes\omega)).

Hence we obtain the complex

{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\underline{\lambda}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
by
(
4.15.1
).
∎

By using the natural surjective morphisms

\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\longrightarrow\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}/S(T)^{\natural}}
,

\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\longrightarrow\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/S(T)^{\natural}}
,

\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\underline{\lambda}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\longrightarrow\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\underline{\lambda}}/S(T)^{\natural}}

and

\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(m)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\longrightarrow\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(m)}/S(T)^{\natural}}
,
we have the log de Rham complexes

\overline{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}/S(T)^{\natural}}
,

{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/S(T)^{\natural}}
,

{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\underline{\lambda}}/S(T)^{\natural}}

and

{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(m)}/S(T)^{\natural}}

for
m\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}
as in (
4.16
).

In a standard way,
the following functor

\displaystyle P(\Lambda)\owns\underline{\lambda}\longmapsto{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\underline{\lambda}}\in{\rm Log}(/S(T)^{\natural})

defines a cubical log scheme over
S(T)^{\natural}
indexed by
P(\Lambda)
,
where
{\rm Log}(/S(T)^{\natural})
is the category of fine log schemes
over
S(T)^{\natural}
.
For convenience of notation, we set

{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\emptyset}:={\cal P}^{\rm ex}
.
Let
b_{\underline{\lambda}}\colon{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\underline{\lambda}}\longrightarrow{\cal P}^{\rm ex}

and
b^{(m)}\colon{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(m)}\longrightarrow{\cal P}^{\rm ex}

be the natural morphisms.
Set
U=S(T)^{\natural}
or
U=\overset{\circ}{T}
.
Fix a total order on
\Lambda
once and for all.
Then we have the Čech complex

{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}b^{(0)}_{*}(\Omega^{i}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(0)}/U})\longrightarrow{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}b^{(1)}_{*}(\Omega^{i}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(1)}/U})\longrightarrow\cdots,

defined by the following boundary morphism.
For an element
\underline{\lambda}=\{\lambda_{0},\ldots,\lambda_{m}\}

(m\in{\mathbb{N}},\lambda_{i}<\lambda_{j}~{\rm if}~i<j,\lambda_{i}\in\Lambda)
,
set
\underline{\lambda}_{j}:=\underline{\lambda}\setminus\{\lambda_{j}\}

and let
\iota_{\underline{\lambda}_{j},\underline{\lambda}}\colon{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\underline{\lambda}}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\underline{\lambda}_{j}}

be the natural inclusion. Then the boundary morphism

\displaystyle{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}b^{(m-1)}_{*}(\Omega^{i}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(m-1)}/U})\longrightarrow{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}b^{(m)}_{*}(\Omega^{i}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(m)}/U})

is, by definition,

(4.16.1)

\displaystyle\underset{\{\underline{\lambda}|\#\underline{\lambda}=m+1\}}{\sum}\sum_{j=0}^{m}(-1)^{j}\iota^{*}_{\underline{\lambda}_{j},\underline{\lambda}}.

We denote this complex by

{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}}}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}/U}
.
This complex is not an example of the complex defined in
[
F2
, (2.5)]

because we fix a total order on
\Lambda
.
In a standard (non-standard?) way (
[
Nak3
, p. 24]
),
we have the double complex

{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}/U}
as follows:

(4.16.2)

\begin{CD}\cdots @>{}>{}>\cdots @>{}>{}>\cdots\\
@A{\nabla}A{}A@A{-\nabla}A{}A\\
{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(0)}}}\Omega^{1}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(0)}/U}@>{}>{}>{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(1)}}}\Omega^{1}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(1)}/U}@>{}>{}>\cdots\\
@A{\nabla}A{}A@A{-\nabla}A{}A\\
{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(0)}}}{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(0)}}@>{}>{}>{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(1)}}}{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(1)}}@>{}>{}>\cdots\\
\end{CD}

and the associated single complex

s({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}/U})
.

Then the following holds:

Proposition 4.17
.

For a nonnegative integer
i
,
the following natural morphism

(4.17.1)

0\longrightarrow{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}/U}\longrightarrow{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(0)}}}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(0)}/U}\longrightarrow{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(1)}}}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(1)}/U}\longrightarrow\cdots

is exact.

Proof.

Since
{\cal E}
is a flat
{\cal O}_{\mathfrak{D}}
-module,
we may assume that
{\cal E}={\cal O}_{\mathfrak{D}}
.
The exactness is a local question.
We may assume that
{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}\simeq{\cal O}_{S}[x_{0},\ldots,x_{d}][[x_{d+1},\ldots,x_{d^{\prime}}]]/(x_{0}\cdots x_{a})

for some
0\leq a\leq d\leq d^{\prime}
((
2.10
)).
Set
{\cal P}^{\rm ex}{}^{\prime}:=\underline{\rm Spec}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}^{\log}({\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}/(x_{d+1},\ldots,x_{d^{\prime}}))
.
Then we have a natural retraction

{\cal P}^{\rm ex}\longrightarrow{\cal P}^{\rm ex}{}^{\prime}
of the immersion

{\cal P}^{\rm ex}{}^{\prime}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal P}^{\rm ex}
.

(4.17.2)

{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(m)}={\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}{}^{\prime},(m)}\times_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}{}^{\prime}}{\cal P}^{\rm ex}.

The exactness of the following sequence

(4.17.3)

0\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}{}^{\prime}}\longrightarrow{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}{}^{\prime},(0)}}}\longrightarrow{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}{}^{\prime},(1)}}}\longrightarrow\cdots

is well-known (we have only to replace
W
in
[
KH
, Lemma 11]

by
{\cal O}_{S}
or we have only to replace
{\mathbb{C}}
in

[
F1
, (4.15)]
by
{\cal O}_{S}
).
Consider the tensorization

\otimes_{{\cal O}_{S}}{\cal O}_{S}\langle x_{d+1},\ldots,x_{d^{\prime}}\rangle
of (
4.17.3
).
Then this sequence is exact since

{\cal O}_{S}\langle x_{d+1},\ldots,x_{d^{\prime}}\rangle

is a free
{\cal O}_{S}
-module.
Using (
4.17.2
) and noting

{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}{}^{\prime}}\langle x_{d+1},\ldots,x_{d^{\prime}}\rangle\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}}}{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(m)}}}={\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}{}^{\prime}}\langle x_{d+1},\ldots,x_{d^{\prime}}\rangle\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}}}{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}{}^{\prime},(m)}}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}{}^{\prime}}}{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}}}={\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}{}^{\prime}}\langle x_{d+1},\ldots,x_{d^{\prime}}\rangle\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}{}^{\prime}}}{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}{}^{\prime},(m)}}}
,
we see that the resulting sequence is equal to the following exact sequence

0\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{\mathfrak{D}}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{\mathfrak{D}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}}}{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(0)}}}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{\mathfrak{D}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}}}{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(1)}}}\longrightarrow\cdots.

Now we have only to take the tensorization

\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}/U}

and to use (
4.15
) (2).
∎

We consider the following PD-Hirsch extension

{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex}}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle

of

{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
as before
and we consider the following PD-Hirsch extension

{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle

of

{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
.
Consequently we can consider the following PD-Hirsch extension

{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(m)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle\quad(m\geq-1)

of

{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(m)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
.

Proposition 4.18
.

Let

s({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle)

be the single complex of the double complex
(
4.16.2
)
.
Then we have the following commutative diagram
:

(4.18.1)

\begin{CD}s({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle)@>{}>{}>s({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}/S(T)^{\natural}})\\
@A{\simeq}A{}A@A{}A{\simeq}A\\
{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle @>{\sim}>{}>{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}/S(T)^{\natural}}.\end{CD}

Proof.

This immediately follows from (
4.17
), (
4.8
)
and (
3.13
).
∎

We also obtain the following, which will play an important role for the construction
of the PD-Hirsch weight-filtered complex:

Theorem 4.19
.

For an element
\underline{\lambda}
of
P(\Lambda)\cup\{\emptyset\}
,
the following natural morphism

(4.19.1)

{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle\longrightarrow{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}}/S(T)^{\natural}}

is a quasi-isomorphism.

Proof.

In (
4.8
) we have already proved (
4.19
) for the case
\underline{\lambda}=\emptyset
.

By (
4.8.1
) the following sequence is exact:

(4.19.2)

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}/S(T)^{\natural}}[-1]\overset{d\log t\wedge}{\longrightarrow}{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\longrightarrow{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}/S(T)^{\natural}}\longrightarrow 0.

By (
4.15
), (
4.16
) and (
4.19.2
),
the following sequence is exact:

(4.19.3)

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\underline{\lambda}}}/S(T)^{\natural}}[-1]\overset{d\log t\wedge}{\longrightarrow}{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\underline{\lambda}}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\longrightarrow{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\underline{\lambda}}}/S(T)^{\natural}}\longrightarrow 0.

Furthermore, by the same proof as that of (
4.8
),
the following sequence obtained by (
4.19.3
)
is exact:

(4.19.4)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\longrightarrow{\cal H}^{q-1}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\underline{\lambda}}}/S(T)^{\natural}})\overset{d\log t\wedge}{\longrightarrow}{\cal H}^{q}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\underline{\lambda}}}/\overset{\circ}{T}})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow{\cal H}^{q}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\underline{\lambda}}}/S(T)^{\natural}})\longrightarrow 0.

The rest of the proof is the same as that of (
4.8
).
∎

5
Preweight filtrations on PD-Hirsch extensions of log crystalline complexes

Let the notations be as in §
4
.
In this section we recall a filtration on

{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}}

defined in
[
Nak4
]
and
we define filtrations on

{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle
,

{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
,

{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle
,

{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(m)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}

and

{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(m)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle
.
We calculate the graded complexes of them.

Let
Y
be a fine log
(formal) scheme over a fine log (formal) scheme
T
.
As in
[
Nak3
, (4.0.2)]
, we define the
pre-weight filtration

P
on the sheaf
{\Omega}^{i}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{T}}

(i\in{\mathbb{N}})

of log differential forms on
Y_{\rm zar}
as follows:

(5.0.1)

P_{k}{\Omega}^{i}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{T}}=\begin{cases}0&(k<0),\\
{\rm Im}({\Omega}^{k}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{T}}{\otimes}_{{\cal O}_{Y}}\Omega^{i-k}_{\overset{\circ}{Y}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\longrightarrow{\Omega}^{i}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{T}})&(0\leq k\leq i),\\
{\Omega}^{i}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{T}}&(k>i).\end{cases}

A morphism
g\colon Y\longrightarrow Z
of fine log (formal) schemes over

\overset{\circ}{T}

induces the following morphism of filtered complexes:

(5.0.2)

g^{*}\colon(\Omega^{\bullet}_{Z/\overset{\circ}{T}},P)\longrightarrow g_{*}((\Omega^{\bullet}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{T}},P)).

More generally, for a flat
{\cal O}_{Y}
-module
{\cal E}

and a flat
{\cal O}_{Z}
-module
{\cal F}
with a morphism

h\colon{\cal F}\longrightarrow g_{*}({\cal E})
of
{\cal O}_{Z}
-modules,
we have the following morphism of filtered complexes:

(5.0.3)

h\colon({\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{Z}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{Z/\overset{\circ}{T}},P)\longrightarrow g_{*}(({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{Y}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{T}},P)).

Especially we obtain the filtrations
P
’s on

\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{{\rm ex}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
,

\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}

and
\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(m)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}

(m\in{\mathbb{N}})
.

In
[
Nak4
]
we have proved the following:

Proposition 5.1

(
[
Nak4
, (1.3.4)]
).

The natural morphism

(5.1.1)

{\cal O}_{\mathfrak{E}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}P_{k}{\Omega}^{i}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{\mathfrak{E}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}}}{\Omega}^{i}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\quad(i,k\in{\mathbb{Z}})

is injective.

Proposition 5.2
.

The natural morphism

(5.2.1)

{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}P_{k}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\underline{\lambda}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\longrightarrow{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\quad(i,k\in{\mathbb{Z}})

is injective.
Consequently the natural morphism

(5.2.2)

{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}P_{k}{\Omega}^{i}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(m)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\longrightarrow{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}{\Omega}^{i}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(m)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\quad(i,k\in{\mathbb{Z}})

is injective.

Proof.

The same proof as that of
[
Nak4
, (1.3.4)]
works for this proposition.
∎

Remark 5.3
.

The proof of the convergent version of
the injectivity of (
5.2.2
) is necessary in
[
GK
]
,
which has not been proved in [loc. cit.].

Because
E
in the previous section is a flat quasi-coherent crystal of

{\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{T}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
-modules
and because the image of the connection

\nabla\colon{\cal E}\longrightarrow{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}\Omega^{1}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
has no log poles,
we can prove the following as in
[
Nak4
, (1.3.17)]
:

Proposition 5.4
.

The complex

{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\underline{\lambda}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}

gives the subcomplex

{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}P_{k}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\underline{\lambda}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}

(k\in{\mathbb{Z}})
.

We denote by
P
the filtrations on

{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}

and

{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(m)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}

induced by the injective morphisms
(
5.2.1
) and (
5.2.2
), respectively.

In
[
Nak4
]
we have proved the following:

Proposition 5.5

(
[
Nak4
, (1.3.14), (1.3.21)]
).

Identify the points of
\overset{\circ}{\mathfrak{D}}

with those of
\overset{\circ}{X}
.
Identify also the images of the points of
\overset{\circ}{X}

in
\overset{\circ}{\cal P}
with the points of
\overset{\circ}{X}
.
Let
y
be a point of
\overset{\circ}{\mathfrak{D}}
.

(
Let
r
be a nonnegative integer such that

M_{X,y}/{\cal O}_{X,y}^{*}\simeq{\mathbb{N}}^{r}
.
)

Then, for a positive integer
k
,
the following morphism

(5.5.1)

{\rm Res}\colon P_{k}({\cal O}_{\mathfrak{D}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}})\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{\mathfrak{D}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}b^{(k-1)}_{*}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(k-1)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k-1)}_{\rm zar}(\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}))[-k]

\sigma\otimes d\log x_{\lambda_{0},y}\cdots d\log x_{\lambda_{k-1},y}\omega\longmapsto\sigma\otimes b^{*}_{\lambda_{0}\cdots\lambda_{k-1}}(\omega)\otimes({\rm orientation}~(\lambda_{0}\cdots\lambda_{k-1}))

(\sigma\in{\cal O}_{\mathfrak{D}},\omega\in P_{0}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}})

(cf.
[
D1
, (3.1.5)]
)

induces the following “Poincaré residue isomorphism”

(5.5.2)

\displaystyle{\rm gr}^{P}_{k}({\cal O}_{\mathfrak{D}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}})

\displaystyle\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}{\cal O}_{\mathfrak{D}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}b^{(k-1)}_{*}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(k-1)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k-1)}_{\rm zar}(\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}))[-k].

Here
x_{\lambda_{l}}

(l\in{\mathbb{N}})
is
the corresponding local coordinate to
{\cal P}_{\lambda_{l}}

((
2.11.1
), (
2.11.2
))
.

(2)
The following sequence

(5.5.3)

0\longrightarrow P_{0}({\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}})\longrightarrow c^{(0)}_{*}({\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}^{(0)}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(0)}}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(0)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(0)}_{\rm zar}(\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}))

\overset{\iota^{(0)*}}{\longrightarrow}{c}{}^{(1)}_{T*}({\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}^{(1)}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(1)}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(1)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(1)}_{\rm zar}(\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}))\overset{\iota^{(1)*}}{\longrightarrow}\cdots

is exact, where
{\mathfrak{D}}^{(k)}:={\mathfrak{D}}\times_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(k)}

(k\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0})
and

c^{(k)}\colon{\mathfrak{D}}^{(k)}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{D}}
is the natural morphism and

\iota^{(k)*}

(k\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0})

is the standard Čech morphism
(
4.16.1
)
.

Lemma 5.6
.

The following single complex

(5.6.1)

\displaystyle s[c^{(0)}_{*}({\cal E}{\otimes}_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(0)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(0)}_{\rm zar}(\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T})))\overset{\iota^{(0)*}}{\longrightarrow}(c^{(1)}_{*}({\cal E}{\otimes}_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(1)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(1)}_{\rm zar}(\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T})),-d)

\displaystyle\overset{\iota^{(1)*}}{\longrightarrow}c^{(2)}_{*}({\cal E}{\otimes}_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(2)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(2)}_{\rm zar}(\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T})))\overset{\iota^{(2)*}}{\longrightarrow}\cdots]

is independent of the choice of the immersion

X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal P}
over
\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}
.

Proof.

To give the proof is a routine work.
Indeed, let

X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal P}{}^{\prime}
be another immersion
over
\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}
. Then, by considering the fiber product

\overline{\cal P}\times_{\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}}\overline{\cal P}{}^{\prime}
, we may assume that
there exists a morphism
\overline{\cal P}\longrightarrow\overline{\cal P}{}^{\prime}
over

\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}
such that the composite morphism

X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal P}\longrightarrow\overline{\cal P}{}^{\prime}

is the given immersion
X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal P}{}^{\prime}
.
Set
{\cal P}^{\prime}:=\overline{\cal P}{}^{\prime}\times_{\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}}S(T)^{\natural}
and
let
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\prime}
be the log PD-envelope of the immersion

X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal P}{}^{\prime}

over
S(T)^{\natural}
and
set
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\prime}{}^{(k)}:={\mathfrak{D}}^{\prime}\times_{{\cal P}^{\prime}{}^{\rm ex}}{\cal P}^{\prime}{}^{{\rm ex},(k)}

(k\in{\mathbb{N}})
and
let
c^{\prime}{}^{(k)}\colon{\mathfrak{D}}^{\prime}{}^{(k)}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{D}}^{\prime}
be the natural morphism.
Denote the complex (
5.6.1
) by
C^{\bullet}
and let

C^{\prime}{}^{\bullet}
be the analogous complex to (
5.6.1
)
obtained by the immersion
X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal P}{}^{\prime}
.
Let
{\cal E}^{\prime}
be an analogous sheaf to
{\cal E}
for the immersion

X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal P}{}^{\prime}
.
Then we have two morphisms

{\cal E}^{\prime}{\otimes}_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\prime}{}^{{\rm ex}}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\prime{\rm ex},(i)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(i)}_{\rm zar}(\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\prime\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T})\longrightarrow{\cal E}{\otimes}_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(i)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(i)}_{\rm zar}(\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T})

and

C^{\prime}{}^{\bullet}\longrightarrow C^{\bullet}
of complexes.
By the following two spectral sequences

E_{1}^{ij}={\cal H}^{j}(c^{(i)}_{*}({\cal E}{\otimes}_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(i)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(i)}_{\rm zar}(\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T})))\Longrightarrow{\cal H}^{i+j}(C^{\bullet})

and

E_{1}^{ij}={\cal H}^{j}(c^{\prime}{}^{(i)}_{*}({\cal E}^{\prime}{\otimes}_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{\prime{\rm ex}}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\prime{\rm ex},(i)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(i)}_{\rm zar}(\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\prime\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T})))\Longrightarrow{\cal H}^{i+j}(C{}^{\prime\bullet}),

it suffices to prove that
the morphism

c^{\prime}{}^{(i)}_{*}({\cal E}^{\prime}{\otimes}_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{\prime{\rm ex}}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\prime{\rm ex},(i)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(i)}_{\rm zar}(\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\prime\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}))\longrightarrow c^{(i)}_{*}({\cal E}{\otimes}_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex}}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(i)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(i)}_{\rm zar}(\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}))

is a quasi-isomorphism.
This is clear because

\displaystyle c^{\prime}{}^{(i)}_{*}({\cal E}^{\prime}{\otimes}_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{\prime{\rm ex}}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\prime{\rm ex},(i)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(i)}_{\rm zar}(\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\prime\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}))

\displaystyle=a^{(i)}_{T_{0}*}Ru_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(i)}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(E_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(i)}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(i)}_{\rm crys}(\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}))

\displaystyle=c^{(i)}_{*}({\cal E}^{\prime}{\otimes}_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{\prime{\rm ex}}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\prime{\rm ex},(i)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(i)}_{\rm zar}(\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\prime\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}))

in
D^{+}(f^{-1}({\cal O}_{T}))

by the crystalline Poincaré lemma.

∎

Definition 5.7
.

We denote the complex (
5.6.1
) by

Ru_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(\star)}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(E_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(\star)}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(\star)}_{\rm crys}(\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T})).

As a corollary of (
5.5
) (1) and (2),
we have essentially proved the following in
[
Nak4
]

by using (
5.6
):

Proposition 5.8

(
[
Nak4
, (1.3.19), (1.3.22)]
).

(1)

Let
k
be a positive integer.
Then there exists the following isomorphism
:

(5.8.1)

{\rm gr}^{P}_{k}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}a^{(k-1)}_{T_{0}*}Ru_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k-1)}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(E_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k-1)}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k-1)}_{\rm crys}(\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}))[-k]

in
D^{+}(f^{-1}_{T}({\cal O}_{T}))
.

(2)
There exists the following isomorphism in
D^{+}(f^{-1}({\cal O}_{T})):

(5.8.2)

\displaystyle P_{0}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}Ru_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(\star)}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(E_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(\star)}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(\star)}_{\rm crys}(\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T})).

The isomorphism
(
5.8.2
)

is independent of the choice of the immersion

X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal P}
over
\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}
.
In particular,

P_{0}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}})

is independent of the choice of the immersion

X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal P}
.

In the case
\underline{\lambda}\not=\emptyset
,
the description of
P_{0}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}}/\overset{\circ}{T}})
is
totally different from that of

P_{0}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex}}/\overset{\circ}{T}})
:

Proposition 5.9
.

(1)
Assume that
\underline{\lambda}\not=\emptyset
.
For a nonnegative integer
k
,
the Poincaré residue morphism

(5.9.1)

{\rm Res}\colon P_{k}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}}/\overset{\circ}{T}})\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\mu}=k}{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}b_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}*}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{{\rm zar},\underline{\mu}}(\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T})))[-k]

\sigma\otimes d\log x_{\lambda_{0},x}\cdots d\log m_{\lambda_{k-1},x}\omega\longmapsto\sigma\otimes b^{*}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}}(\omega)\otimes({\rm orientation}~(\underline{\mu}))

(\sigma\in{\cal E},\omega\in P_{0}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\underline{\mu}}/\overset{\circ}{T}})

(cf.
[
D1
, (3.1.5)]
)
induces
the following “Poincaré residue isomorphism”

(5.9.2)

\displaystyle{\rm gr}_{k}^{P}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}}/\overset{\circ}{T}})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\mu}=k}{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}b_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}*}(\Omega^{\bullet-k}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{{\rm zar},\underline{\mu}}(\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}))[-k]

in
D^{+}(f^{-1}({\cal O}_{T}))
.

Proof.

For the case where
k
is a positive integer,
we can prove (
5.9
) in a standard way
as in e.g., in
[
Nak4
, (1.3.14)]
.
(However see the last remark in (
5.11
) below.)

When
k=0
, then the isomorphism (
5.9.2
)
is nothing but an isomorphism

(5.9.3)

\displaystyle P_{0}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}}/\overset{\circ}{T}})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}

\displaystyle{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}b_{\underline{\lambda}*}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}),

which is equivalent to
the injectivity of the following natural morphism

(5.9.4)

\displaystyle{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\longrightarrow

\displaystyle{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}b_{\underline{\lambda}*}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}).

This is a local question.
Hence we may assume
that
\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm prex}=\overline{\cal P}
and that
there exists
the commutative diagram (
2.11.2
).
In fact, we can assume that
\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm prex}{}^{\prime}=\overline{\cal P}
.
Set
{\cal P}:=\overline{\cal P}\times_{\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}}S(T)^{\natural}
.
We have only to prove that the natural morphism

(5.9.5)

\Omega^{i}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal P}_{\underline{\lambda}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\longrightarrow\Omega^{i}_{{\cal P}_{\underline{\lambda}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\quad(i\in{\mathbb{Z}})

is injective.

First consider the essential case
i=1
.
Let
x_{0},\ldots x_{d^{\prime}}
be the coordinates of

{\mathbb{A}}_{S(T)^{\natural}}(a,d^{\prime})
.
Let
x_{0}=x_{1}=\cdots=x_{m}=0

(0\leq m\leq a)

be the defining equations of
{\cal P}_{\underline{\lambda}}
in

{\cal P}
.
Then

(5.9.6)

\Omega^{1}_{{\cal P}_{\underline{\lambda}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}=\bigoplus_{j=1}^{a}{\cal O}_{{\cal P}}/(x_{0},\ldots,x_{m})d\log x_{j}\oplus\bigoplus_{j=a+1}^{d^{\prime}}{\cal O}_{{\cal P}}/(x_{0},\ldots,x_{m})dx_{j}

and

(5.9.7)

\Omega^{1}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal P}_{\underline{\lambda}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}=\bigoplus_{j=m+1}^{a}{\cal O}_{{\cal P}}/(x_{0},\ldots,x_{m})dx_{j}\oplus\bigoplus_{j=a+1}^{d^{\prime}}{\cal O}_{{\cal P}}/(x_{0},\ldots,x_{m})dx_{j}.

The natural morphism

{\cal O}_{T}[x_{m+1},\ldots,x_{d}]dx_{j}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{T}[x_{m+1},\ldots,x_{d}]d\log x_{j}

(m+1\leq j\leq a)
is injective since

x_{j}
is a non-zero divisor in
{\cal O}_{T}[x_{m+1},\ldots,x_{d}]
.
Because

{\cal O}_{{\cal P}}/(x_{0},\ldots,x_{m})
is étale
over
{\cal O}_{T}[x_{0},\ldots,x_{d}]/(x_{0},\ldots,x_{m})={\cal O}_{T}[x_{m+1},\ldots,x_{d}]
,
the desired injectivity of the morphism
(
5.9.5
) for the case
i=1
is clear.

Now the desired injectivity for the general case
follows.
∎

Remark 5.10
.

Note that morphism (
5.9.4
) for the case
\underline{\lambda}=\emptyset

(5.10.1)

\displaystyle{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\longrightarrow

\displaystyle{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}.

is not injective because the image of
1\otimes(x_{0}\cdots x_{a-1}dx_{a})\in{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{1}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
in

{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}

by the morphism (
5.10.1
) is zero.

Example 5.11
.

In this example we consider (
5.9
) in the nontrivial simplest case
to see what happens in (
5.9
).

Consider the case
S_{0}
=
S
and
X={\cal P}={\mathbb{A}}_{S}(1,1)

with standard coordinates
x
and
y
.
Let
X_{0}
and
X_{1}
be exact closed log subschemes of
X
defined by

x=0
and
y=0
, respectively. Consider the case
\underline{\lambda}=\{0\}
.
Then
X_{\underline{\lambda}}=X_{0}

and

\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{0}/\overset{\circ}{S}}=({\cal O}_{S}[y]\overset{d}{\longrightarrow}{\cal O}_{S}[y]d\log x\oplus{\cal O}_{S}[y]d\log y\overset{d}{\longrightarrow}{\cal O}_{S}[y]d\log x\wedge d\log y).

The complexes
P_{k}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{0}/\overset{\circ}{S}}
for
k=0,1,2
are as follows:

P_{0}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{0}/\overset{\circ}{S}}=({\cal O}_{S}[y]\overset{d}{\longrightarrow}{\cal O}_{S}[y]dy),

P_{1}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{0}/\overset{\circ}{S}}=({\cal O}_{S}[y]\overset{d}{\longrightarrow}{\cal O}_{S}[y]d\log x\oplus{\cal O}_{S}[y]d\log y\overset{d}{\longrightarrow}{\cal O}_{S}[y]d\log x\wedge dy),

P_{2}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{0}/\overset{\circ}{S}}=({\cal O}_{S}[y]\overset{d}{\longrightarrow}{\cal O}_{S}[y]d\log x\oplus{\cal O}_{S}[y]d\log y\overset{d}{\longrightarrow}{\cal O}_{S}[y]d\log x\wedge d\log y).

The complexes
{\rm gr}^{P}_{k}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{0}/\overset{\circ}{S}}
for
k=0,1,2
are as follows:

{\rm gr}^{P}_{0}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{0}/\overset{\circ}{S}}=a_{0*}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{0}/\overset{\circ}{S}}),

(5.11.1)

\displaystyle{\rm gr}^{P}_{1}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{0}/\overset{\circ}{S}}

\displaystyle=a_{01*}({\cal O}_{S})[-1]\oplus(0\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{S}[y]d\log x\overset{d}{\longrightarrow}{\cal O}_{S}[y]d\log x\wedge dy)

\displaystyle\simeq a_{01*}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{01}/\overset{\circ}{S}})[-1]\oplus(0\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{S}[y]\overset{d}{\longrightarrow}{\cal O}_{S}[y]dy)

\displaystyle=a_{01*}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{01}/\overset{\circ}{S}})[-1]\oplus a_{0*}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{0}/\overset{\circ}{S}})[-1],

{\rm gr}^{P}_{2}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{0}/\overset{\circ}{S}}=a_{01*}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{01}/\overset{\circ}{S}})[-2].

Here note that the isomorphism in (
5.11.1
)
is independent of the choice of the local coordinate

x
since
d\log w\in P_{0}\Omega^{1}_{X_{0}/\overset{\circ}{S}}

for a local section
w
of
({\cal O}_{S}[y])^{*}
.

Proposition 5.12
.

The sheaves

{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}{\Omega}^{i}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\underline{\lambda}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
,

{\rm gr}^{P}_{k}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}{\Omega}^{i}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\underline{\lambda}}/\overset{\circ}{T}})

(i,k\in{\mathbb{N}})

and

P_{k}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}{\Omega}^{i}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\underline{\lambda}}/\overset{\circ}{T}})

are locally free
{\cal O}_{T}
-modules.

Proof.

We may assume that
{\cal E}
is trivial.
When
\underline{\lambda}=\emptyset
, we have already proved
(
5.12
) in
[
Nak4
, (1.3.15)]
.
Assume that
\underline{\lambda}\not=\emptyset
.
Since the question is local on
X
,
we may assume
that
X={\mathbb{A}}_{S}(a,d)
and

{\cal P}^{\rm prex}={\cal P}={\mathbb{A}}_{S(T)^{\natural}}(a,d^{\prime})

(a\in{\mathbb{N}},d\leq d^{\prime})
by (
2.10
)
(cf. the proof of
[
Nak4
, (1.3.5)]
).
Then it is clear that

{\cal O}_{\mathfrak{E}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\underline{\lambda}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}

is a locally free
{\cal O}_{T}
-module.
The target of the isomorphism (
5.9.2
)
consists of locally free
{\cal O}_{T}
-modules.
Hence the descending induction on
k
shows
the locally freeness of

P_{k}({\cal O}_{\mathfrak{E}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}{\Omega}^{i}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\underline{\lambda}}/\overset{\circ}{T}})
.
∎

For an element
\underline{\lambda}
of
P(\Lambda)\cup\{\emptyset\}
,
we define a filtration
P
on

{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex}}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle

for
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
as follows:

(5.12.1)

P_{k}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle):=\bigoplus_{j\geq 0}\Gamma_{{\cal O}_{T},j}(U_{S(T)^{\natural}})\otimes_{{\cal O}_{T}}P_{k-2j}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}).

Note that
P_{-1}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle)=0
.

For an integer
m\geq 0
,
we define a pre-weight filtration
P
on

{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(m)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle

for
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
as follows:

(5.12.2)

P_{k}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(m)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle):=\bigoplus_{j\geq 0}\Gamma_{{\cal O}_{T},j}(U_{S(T)^{\natural}})\otimes_{{\cal O}_{T}}P_{k-2j}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(m)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}).

Indeed we easily see that the differential

\nabla
preserves the filtrations
P
’s on

{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(m)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle

and

{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(m)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle
.
Obviously,

(5.12.3)

\displaystyle P_{-1}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(m)}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(m)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle)=0.

Remark 5.13
.

In the case where
{\cal E}
is trivial,
the filtration
P
on

{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle

is useless for the calculation of the pre-weight filtration on
the log crystalline cohomology

R^{q}f_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}/S(T)^{\natural}}}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}/S(T)^{\natural}}})

because
the
E_{1}
-term of the associated spectral sequence
of
R^{q}f_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}/S(T)^{\natural}}}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}/S(T)^{\natural}}})

to the filtration
P
is “mixed” ((
5.5.3
)).
(Note that I do not say that the filtration
P
on
{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle

itself is useless. Indeed,
we use this
P
in (
19.6
) below.)

In the case
\underline{\lambda}\not=\emptyset
,
we have the following isomorphism for any integer
k
by (
5.9
)
since the differential of
u^{[i]}
induces the zero morphism on

{\rm gr}_{k}^{P}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle)
by (
5.12.2
):

(5.13.1)

\displaystyle{\rm gr}_{k}^{P}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{j\geq 0}\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\mu}=k-2j}(\Gamma_{{\cal O}_{T},j}(U_{S(T)^{\natural}})\otimes_{{\cal O}_{T}}{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}b_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}*}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{{\rm zar},\underline{\mu}}(\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T})))[-(k-2j)].

Hence

(5.13.2)

\displaystyle{\rm gr}_{k}^{P}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(m)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\lambda}=m+1}{\rm gr}_{k}^{P}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle)

\displaystyle\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\lambda}=m+1}\bigoplus_{j\geq 0}\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\mu}=k-2j}(\Gamma_{{\cal O}_{T},j}(U_{S(T)^{\natural}})\otimes_{{\cal O}_{T}}{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}b_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}*}(\Omega^{\bullet-k}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{{\rm zar},\underline{\mu}}(\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T})))[-(k-2j)].

Consider the following double complex

{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle

(see the Notation (15)) and the single complex

s({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle)
.
Let
\delta(L,P)
be the diagonal filtration
on

s({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle)

(
[
D1
]
)
obtained by
P
shifted by a semi-cosimplicial degree:

\delta(L,P)_{k}s({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle):=(\cdots\longrightarrow P_{k+m}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(m)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle)\longrightarrow\cdots).

For simplicity of notation, we denote
\delta(L,P)
simply by
P
.
If
\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}
is quasi-compact, then

\displaystyle P_{k}s({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle)=\delta(L,P)_{k}s({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle)=0

for
k<<0
.
Since the horizontal boundary morphism

\displaystyle d\log t\wedge\colon

\displaystyle{\rm gr}_{k+m}^{P}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(m)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle)\longrightarrow

\displaystyle{\rm gr}_{k+m+1}^{P}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(m)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle)\quad(m\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0})

of the double complex
{\rm gr}_{k}^{P}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle)

is zero,
we obtain the following isomorphism

(5.13.3)

\displaystyle{\rm gr}_{k}^{P}(s({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle))\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{m\geq 0}\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\lambda}=m+1}\bigoplus_{j\geq 0}\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\mu}=k+m-2j}(\Gamma_{{\cal O}_{T},j}(U_{S(T)^{\natural}})\otimes_{{\cal O}_{T}}{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}b_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}*}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}

\displaystyle\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{{\rm zar},\underline{\mu}}({\cal P}^{\rm ex}/T)))[-k-2m+2j].

Note that, locally on
X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}
,
the right hand side of (
5.13.3
) is a finite direct sum.
If
(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}})^{\circ}
is quasi-compact,
then the right hand side is a finite direct sum.
Note also that

v^{*}(u^{[j]})={\rm deg}(v)^{j}u^{[j]}
for a morphism
v\colon S\longrightarrow S^{\prime}
of family of log points
((
3.7.4
)). The last note will become important later in this book in the consideration of
the “
D
-twist”, especially, the twist of the abrelative Frobenius morphism.

Proposition 5.14
.

Locally on
X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}
,

P_{k}(s({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle))=0

for
k<<0
.
If
\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}
is quasi-compact, then

P_{k}(s({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle))=0

for
k<<0
. That is, the filtration
P
on

s({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle)

is bounded below.

Proof.

This follows from (
5.12.3
).
∎

Remark 5.15
.

It is clear that the filtration
P
on

s({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle)

is exhaustive because we allow only finite sum with respect to
u^{[j]}

(j\in{\mathbb{N}})
locally.
This observation is very important for the existence of the convergent spectral sequence
(
6.18.2
) below.
This is why we cannot use

s({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle\langle u\rangle\rangle)
.

6
PD-Hirsch pre-weight-filtered log crystalline complexes

Let
(T,{\cal J},\delta)
,
T_{0}\longrightarrow S
,
Y/S
and
Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}

be as in §
4
.
In this section we do not assume that there exists an immersion
from
Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}
