into a log smooth scheme over
\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}
.
Let
g\colon Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}\longrightarrow S_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}\longrightarrow S(T)^{\natural}
be the structural morphism.
Let
\overline{F}
be a flat quasi-coherent crystal of

{\cal O}_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
-modules.
The first aim in this section is
to recall a complex

\widetilde{R}u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\overline{F})\in D^{+}(g^{-1}({\cal O}_{T}))

and
to construct a new complex

\widetilde{R}u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\overline{F}\langle u\rangle)\in D^{+}(g^{-1}({\cal O}_{T})),

which we call
\widetilde{R}u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\overline{F}\langle u\rangle)
the
derived PD-Hirsch extension
of

\widetilde{R}u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\overline{F})
.
Let
X/S
be an SNCL scheme
and let
f\colon X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}\longrightarrow S(T)^{\natural}
be the
structural morphism.
Let
E
be a flat quasi-coherent crystal of

{\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
-modules.
The second aim in this section is to construct a filtered complex

(H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E),P):=(\widetilde{R}u_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(E^{(\star)})\langle u\rangle),P)\in{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(f^{-1}_{T}({\cal O}_{T})),

which we call the
PD-Hirsch filtered crystalline complex
of
E
.

Let
Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}
be the Čech diagram of
an affine open covering of
Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}
.
By abuse of notation, denote the structural morphism

Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}\longrightarrow S(T)^{\natural}
by
g_{\bullet}
.
Then, by a standard method using (
2.6
),
we have a simplicial immersion

Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal Q}_{\bullet}
into
a simplicial log smooth scheme over the immersion

S_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}
if
each member of the affine open covering is small enough.
By taking the exactification of the immersion

Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal Q}_{\bullet}
,
we have the simplicial immersion

Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}
.
Let
\overline{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}
be the log PD-envelope of
this immersion over
(\overset{\circ}{T},{\cal J},\delta)
.
Set
{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}:=\overline{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}\times_{\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}}S(T)^{\natural}

and

{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}:=\overline{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}\times_{\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}}S(T)^{\natural}
.
Let
\overline{F}{}^{\bullet}
be the quasi-coherent

{\cal O}_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
-module obtained by
\overline{F}
.
Let
(\overline{\cal F}{}^{\bullet},\overline{\nabla})

be the crystal of
{\cal O}_{\overline{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}
-module
with integrable connection corresponding to
the log crystal
\overline{F}
:

(6.0.1)

\overline{\nabla}\colon\overline{\cal F}{}^{\bullet}\longrightarrow\overline{\cal F}{}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\overline{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{1}_{\overline{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}.

Set
({\cal F}^{\bullet},{\nabla}):=(\overline{\cal F}{}^{\bullet},\overline{\nabla})\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}(\overline{S(T)^{\natural}})}}{\cal O}_{S(T)^{\natural}}
:

(6.0.2)

\nabla\colon{\cal F}^{\bullet}\longrightarrow{\cal F}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{1}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}.

As in (
4.0.2
) we indeed have the connection
\nabla
.

For
U=S(T)^{\natural}
or
U=\overset{\circ}{T}
,
we have the cosimplicial log de Rham complexes

\overline{\cal F}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\overline{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/U}
and

{\cal F}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/U}
as in §
4
.

Let

(6.0.3)

\epsilon_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\colon((Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural})_{\rm crys},{\cal O}_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}})\longrightarrow((Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T})_{\rm crys},{\cal O}_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}})

be the morphism of ringed topoi forgetting the log structure of
S(T)^{\natural}
.
Set

F:=\epsilon_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}/\overset{\circ}{T}}^{*}(\overline{F})
.
Let

(6.0.4)

\displaystyle\pi_{\rm zar}\colon((\overset{\circ}{Y}_{T_{0}\bullet})_{\rm zar},g^{-1}_{\bullet}({\cal O}_{T}))\longrightarrow((\overset{\circ}{Y}_{T_{0}})_{\rm zar},g^{-1}({\cal O}_{T}))

be the natural morphism of ringed topoi.
In
[
Nak4
, (1.7.27.4)]
we have proved that
there exists the following triangle

(6.0.5)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow R\pi_{{\rm zar}*}({\cal F}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}})\longrightarrow Ru_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(F)\overset{N_{\rm zar}}{\longrightarrow}Ru_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(F),

where
N_{\rm zar}\colon Ru_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(F)\longrightarrow Ru_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(F)

is the monodromy operator defined in [loc. cit.], which will be recalled in §
10
.
In
[
Nak4
, (1.7.27)]
we have proved the following:

Proposition-Definition 6.1

(
[
Nak4
, (1.7.27)]
).

The complex
R\pi_{{\rm zar}*}({\cal F}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}})

is independent of the choice of
an open covering of
Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}
and a simplicial immersion

Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal Q}_{\bullet}

over
\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}
.
Set

\displaystyle\widetilde{R}u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\overline{F}):=R\pi_{{\rm zar}*}({\cal F}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}})\in D^{+}(g^{-1}({\cal O}_{T})).

In
[
Nak4
]
we have called

\widetilde{R}u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\overline{F})

the
modified crystalline complex
of
\overline{F}

on
Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}
.
When
\overline{F}={\cal O}_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
,
we have called

\widetilde{R}u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}({\cal O}_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}})

the
modified crystalline complex
of

Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}/\overset{\circ}{T}
.

Definition 6.2
.

We denote
Rg_{*}\widetilde{R}u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\overline{F})

by
\widetilde{R}g_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\overline{F})
.
We denote
{\cal H}^{q}(\widetilde{R}g_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\overline{F}))

by

\widetilde{R}{}^{q}g_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\overline{F})
.

Definition 6.3
.

(1) Set

\displaystyle\widetilde{R}u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\overline{F}\langle u\rangle):=R\pi_{{\rm zar}*}({\cal F}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle)\in D^{+}(g^{-1}({\cal O}_{T})).

We call
\widetilde{R}u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\overline{F}\langle u\rangle)

the
derived PD-Hirsch extension

of
\widetilde{R}u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\overline{F})
.

The following is a starting result for investigating log crystalline cohomological sheaves;
we see that the complex

\widetilde{R}u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\overline{F}\langle u\rangle)

is indeed well-defined.

Proposition 6.4
.

(1)
There exists a canonical isomorphism

(6.4.1)

\widetilde{R}u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\overline{F}\langle u\rangle)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}Ru_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(F).

In particular,
\widetilde{R}u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\overline{F}\langle u\rangle)

is independent of the choice of an open covering of
Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}
and
a simplicial immersion

Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal Q}_{\bullet}

over
\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}
.
The isomorphism
(
6.4.1
)
is contravariantly functorial.

Proof.

By the cohomological descent and the log Poincaré lemma,
we have the following formula

(6.4.2)

Ru_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(F)=R\pi_{{\rm zar}*}({\cal F}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural}}).

By (
4.8
) we see that the natural morphism

(6.4.3)

{\cal F}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle\longrightarrow{\cal F}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural}}

is a quasi-isomorphism.
By taking the derived direct image
R\pi_{{\rm zar}*}
of (
6.4.3
),
we have the isomorphism (
6.4.1
).

Let
Y^{\prime}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}
be the Čech diagram of
another affine open covering of
Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}

and let
Y^{\prime}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal Q}{}^{\prime}_{\bullet}

be another simplicial immersion into
a log smooth scheme over
\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}
.
Set
\overline{\cal Q}{}^{\prime\prime}_{\bullet}:=\overline{\cal Q}{}_{\bullet}\times_{\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}}\overline{\cal Q}{}^{\prime}_{\bullet}
.
Then, by considering the refinement of these affine open coverings,
we see that there exists the Čech diagram
Y^{\prime\prime}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}

of an affine open covering of
Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}
fitting into
the following commutative diagram

\begin{CD}Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}@>{\subset}>{}>\overline{\cal Q}_{\bullet}\\
@A{}A{}A@A{}A{}A\\
Y^{\prime\prime}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}@>{\subset}>{}>\overline{\cal Q}{}^{\prime\prime}_{\bullet}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
Y^{\prime}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}@>{\subset}>{}>\overline{\cal Q}{}^{\prime}_{\bullet}.\end{CD}

Hence we may assume that there exists the following commutative diagram:

(6.4.4)

\begin{CD}Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}@>{\subset}>{}>\overline{\cal Q}_{\bullet}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
Y^{\prime}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}@>{\subset}>{}>\overline{\cal Q}{}^{\prime}_{\bullet}.\end{CD}

Let
({\cal F}^{\prime}{}^{\bullet},\nabla^{\prime})
be an analogous connection to

({\cal F}^{\bullet},\nabla)
for the immersion

Y^{\prime}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal Q}{}^{\prime}_{\bullet}
.
The diagram (
6.4.4
) gives us the following commutative diagram

(6.4.5)

\begin{CD}{\cal F}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle @>{\sim}>{}>{\cal F}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural}}\\
@A{}A{}A@A{}A{}A\\
{\cal F}^{\prime}{}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}^{\prime}{}^{{\rm ex}}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}^{\prime}{}^{{\rm ex}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle @>{\sim}>{}>{\cal F}^{\prime}{}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}^{\prime}{}^{{\rm ex}}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}^{\prime}{}^{{\rm ex}}/S(T)^{\natural}}.\end{CD}

The commutative diagram
R\pi_{{\rm zar}*}((\ref{cd:sotcq}))

tells us the independence of

\widetilde{R}u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\overline{F}\langle u\rangle)
.

We leave the proof of the contravariant functoriality of the isomorphism
(
6.4.1
) to the reader.
∎

Let us consider the complex

(6.4.6)

\displaystyle R\pi_{{\rm zar}*}({\cal F}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}})\langle u\rangle^{L}:=R\pi_{{\rm zar}*}({\cal F}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}})\langle U_{S(T)^{\natural}}\rangle^{L}\in{\rm Ho}({\cal C}_{(Y_{\rm zar},g^{-1}({\cal O}_{T}))}),

which is a special case of the complex in (
3.17.1
).

Proposition 6.5
.

There exists a canonical isomorphism

(6.5.1)

\widetilde{R}u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\overline{F}\langle u\rangle)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}R\pi_{{\rm zar}*}({\cal F}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}})\langle u\rangle^{L}

in
D^{+}(g^{-1}({\cal O}_{T}))
. Consequently the complex
(
6.4.6
)

is well-defined.

Proof.

Let
{\cal F}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\longrightarrow I^{\bullet\bullet\bullet}

be the Godement resolution of
{\cal F}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
.
Then

\displaystyle R\pi_{{\rm zar}*}({\cal F}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}})\langle U_{S(T)^{\natural}}\rangle^{L}=U_{S(T)^{\natural}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{T}}\pi_{{\rm zar}*}(s(I^{\bullet\bullet\bullet}))=\pi_{{\rm zar}*}(U_{S(T)^{\natural}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{T}}s(I^{\bullet\bullet\bullet})).

Here we have used (
3.16.8
).
The complex
U_{S(T)^{\natural}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{T}}I^{\bullet\bullet\bullet}

is a
\pi_{{\rm zar}*}
-acyclic resolution of

{\cal F}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
because

R^{q}\pi_{{\rm zar}*}(U_{S(T)^{\natural}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{T}}I^{\bullet\bullet\bullet})=U_{S(T)^{\natural}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{T}}R^{q}\pi_{{\rm zar}*}(I^{\bullet\bullet\bullet})=0

(q>0)
.
By the definition of
\widetilde{R}u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\overline{F}\langle u\rangle)
and
this acyclicity,

\displaystyle\widetilde{R}u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\overline{F}\langle u\rangle)=R\pi_{{\rm zar}*}({\cal F}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle)=\pi_{{\rm zar}*}(U_{S(T)^{\natural}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{T}}s(I^{\bullet\bullet\bullet})).

∎

Theorem 6.6

(
Künneth formula).

(1)
Let
Z/S
be an analogous object to
Y/S
.
Assume that
Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}
and
Z_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}
are quasi-compact.
Let
\overline{G}
be an analogous
{\cal O}_{Z_{{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}}
-module
to
\overline{F}
and set
G:=\epsilon_{Z_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}/\overset{\circ}{T}}^{*}(\overline{G})
.
Set
YZ_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}:=Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}\times_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}Z_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}
and
let
p\colon YZ_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}\longrightarrow Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}
and

q\colon YZ_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}\longrightarrow Z_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}
be the projections
and let
h\colon YZ_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{T}
be the structural morphism.
Then there exists a canonical isomorphism

(6.6.1)

\displaystyle Lp^{*}(\widetilde{R}u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\overline{F}\langle u\rangle))\otimes^{L}_{h^{-1}({\cal O}_{T})}Lq^{*}(\widetilde{R}u_{Z_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\overline{G}\langle u\rangle))\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}

\displaystyle\widetilde{R}u_{YZ_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}((p^{*}_{\rm crys}(\overline{F})\otimes_{{\cal O}_{YZ_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}}q^{*}_{\rm crys}(\overline{G}))\langle u\rangle)

fitting into the following commutative diagram
:

(6.6.2)

\begin{CD}Lp^{*}(\widetilde{R}u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\overline{F}\langle u\rangle))\otimes^{L}_{h^{-1}({\cal O}_{T})}Lq^{*}(\widetilde{R}u_{Z_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\overline{G}\langle u\rangle))@>{\sim}>{}>\\
@V{Lp^{*}(\ref{eqn:exfte})\otimes^{L}Lq^{*}(\ref{eqn:exfte})}V{\simeq}V\\
Lp^{*}(Ru_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(F))\otimes^{L}_{h^{-1}({\cal O}_{T})}Lq^{*}(Ru_{Z_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(G))@>{\sim}>{}>\end{CD}

\begin{CD}\widetilde{R}u_{YZ_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}((p^{*}_{\rm crys}(\overline{F})\otimes_{{\cal O}_{YZ_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}}q^{*}_{\rm crys}(\overline{G}))\langle u\rangle)\\
@V{\simeq}V{(\ref{eqn:exfte})}V\\
Ru_{YZ_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(p^{*}_{\rm crys}(F)\otimes_{{\cal O}_{YZ_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}}q^{*}_{\rm crys}(G)).\end{CD}

(2)
Let the notations be as in
(1)
.
Assume that
Z_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}=Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}
.
There exists the following morphism

(6.6.3)

\displaystyle\cup\colon\widetilde{R}u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\overline{F}\langle u\rangle)\otimes^{L}_{g^{-1}({\cal O}_{T})}\widetilde{R}u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\overline{G}\langle u\rangle)\longrightarrow\widetilde{R}u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}((\overline{F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}}\overline{G})\langle u\rangle)

fitting into the following commutative diagram
:

(6.6.4)

\begin{CD}\widetilde{R}u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\overline{F}\langle u\rangle)\otimes^{L}_{g^{-1}({\cal O}_{T})}\widetilde{R}u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\overline{G}\langle u\rangle)@>{\cup}>{}>\widetilde{R}u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}((\overline{F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}}}\overline{G})\langle u\rangle)\\
@V{(\ref{eqn:exfte})\otimes^{L}(\ref{eqn:exfte})}V{\simeq}V@V{\simeq}V{(\ref{eqn:exfte})}V\\
Ru_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(F)\otimes^{L}_{g^{-1}({\cal O}_{T})}Ru_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(G)@>{\cup}>{}>Ru_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(F\otimes_{{\cal O}_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}}}G).\end{CD}

Here the lower
\cup
is the cup product of log crystalline complexes.

Proof.

(1): Let
W
be
Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}
,
Z_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}
or

YZ_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}
. Let
g_{W\bullet}\colon W_{\bullet}\longrightarrow T

and
g_{W}\colon W\longrightarrow T
be the structural morphism.
Let
\pi_{W,{\rm zar}}\colon(W_{\bullet})_{\rm zar}\longrightarrow W_{\rm zar}
be the
morphism (
6.0.4
).
Consider the following commutative diagram:

(6.6.5)

\begin{CD}((Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet})_{\rm zar},g^{-1}_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}}({\cal O}_{T}))@<{p_{\bullet}}<{}<(((YZ_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet})_{\rm zar},g^{-1}_{YZ_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}}({\cal O}_{T}))@>{q_{\bullet}}>{}>((Z_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet})_{\rm zar},g^{-1}_{Z_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}}({\cal O}_{T}))\\
@V{\pi_{Y,{\rm zar}}}V{}V@V{\pi_{YZ,{\rm zar}}}V{}V@V{\pi_{Z,{\rm zar}}}V{}V\\
((Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}})_{\rm zar},g^{-1}_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}}({\cal O}_{T}))@<{p}<{}<((YZ_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}})_{\rm zar},g^{-1}_{YZ_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}}({\cal O}_{T}))@>{q}>{}>((Z_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}})_{\rm zar},g^{-1}_{Z_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}}({\cal O}_{T})).\end{CD}

Let
{\cal R}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}
and
{\cal G}^{\bullet}
be
the corresponding objects to

{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}
and
{\cal F}^{\bullet}
, respectively,
for
Z
and
\overline{G}
.
Set
{\cal QR}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}:={\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}\times_{\overset{\circ}{T}}{\cal R}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}

and
let
h_{\bullet}\colon{\cal QR}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{T}
be the structural morphism.
To prove the existence of the morphism (
6.6.1
), it suffices to prove that
there exists the following morphism by using the relations

p\circ\pi_{YZ,{\rm zar}}=\pi_{Y,{\rm zar}}\circ p_{\bullet}

and
q\circ\pi_{YZ,{\rm zar}}=\pi_{Z,{\rm zar}}\circ q_{\bullet}

in a standard way:

(6.6.6)

\displaystyle p_{\bullet}^{*}({\cal F}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle)\otimes_{h^{-1}_{\bullet}({\cal O}_{T})}q_{\bullet}^{*}({\cal G}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal R}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal R}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow{{\cal O}_{{\cal QR}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}}}{\cal F}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal QR}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}}}{{\cal O}_{{\cal QR}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal R}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}}}{\cal G}^{\bullet}\otimes_{\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal QR}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal QR}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle.

Using the morphism

p_{\bullet}^{*}(\Omega^{i}_{{\cal Q}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}})\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal QR}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}}}q_{\bullet}^{*}(\Omega^{j}_{{\cal R}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}})\longrightarrow\Omega^{i+j}_{{\cal QR}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\quad(i,j\in{\mathbb{N}})

and the multiplicative structure

(6.6.7)

\displaystyle{\cal O}_{T}\langle u\rangle\otimes_{{\cal O}_{T}}{\cal O}_{T}\langle u\rangle\owns u^{[i]}\otimes u^{[i^{\prime}]}\longmapsto\begin{pmatrix}i+i^{\prime}\\
i\end{pmatrix}u^{[i+i^{\prime}]}\in{\cal O}_{T}\langle u\rangle.

of
\Gamma_{{\cal O}_{T}}(U_{S(T)^{\natural}})
,
we have the morphism (
6.6.6
).
It is clear that
the diagram (
6.6.2
) with the replacement of
the upper horizontal
\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}
by
\longrightarrow

is commutative.
Because we see that the lower horizontal morphism
in (
6.6.2
) is an isomorphism by
local calculations as in
[
B2
, p. 379]
,
the morphism
(
6.6.1
) is an isomorphism.
(Note that because (
6.6.7
) is not an isomorphism of
{\cal O}_{T}
-modules at all,
we cannot see that (
6.6.1
) is an isomorphism only by local calculations for
the source and the target of the morphism (
6.6.1
).)

(2):
Applying the derived pull-back
L\Delta^{*}
of the diagonal immersion

\Delta\colon Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}YY_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}:=Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}\times_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}
over
\overset{\circ}{T}

to the morphism (
6.6.1
),
we obtain the following isomorphism

(6.6.8)

\displaystyle\widetilde{R}u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\overline{F}\langle u\rangle))\otimes^{L}_{g^{-1}({\cal O}_{T})}\widetilde{R}u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\overline{G}\langle u\rangle))\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}

\displaystyle L\Delta^{*}\widetilde{R}u_{YY_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}((p^{*}_{\rm crys}(\overline{F})\otimes_{{\cal O}_{YY_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}}q^{*}_{\rm crys}(\overline{G}))\langle u\rangle)

\displaystyle=L\Delta^{*}R\pi_{YY_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}*}({{\cal O}_{{\cal QQ}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}}}{\cal F}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}}}{\cal G}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{{\cal O}_{{\cal QQ}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal QQ}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle).

Consider the following commutative diagram of topoi:

(6.6.9)

\begin{CD}Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0},\bullet,{\rm zar}}@>{\Delta_{\bullet}}>{}>YY_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0},\bullet,\rm zar}\\
@V{\pi_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}}}V{}V@V{}V{\pi_{YY_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}}}V\\
Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0},{\rm zar}}@>{\Delta}>{}>YY_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0},{\rm zar}}.\end{CD}

Using this diagram and the flatness of
{\cal F}^{\bullet}
over

g^{-1}({\cal O}_{T})
, we obtain the following morphism as usual:

(6.6.10)

\displaystyle L\Delta^{*}R\pi_{YY_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}*}({{\cal O}_{{\cal QQ}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}}}{\cal F}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}}}{\cal G}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal QQ}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal QQ}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow

\displaystyle R\pi_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}*}L\Delta^{*}_{\bullet}({{\cal O}_{{\cal QQ}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}}}{\cal F}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}}}{\cal G}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal QQ}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal QQ}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle)

\displaystyle=R\pi_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}*}({\cal F}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}}}{\cal G}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle).

The composite morphism of the morphisms (
6.6.8
) and (
6.6.10
)
gives us the desired morphism (
6.6.3
).
∎

Now let
X/S
be an SNCL scheme
and let
f\colon X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}\longrightarrow S(T)^{\natural}
be the
structural morphism.
Let
X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}
be the simplicial log scheme
obtained by an affine open covering of
X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}
.
Then, by (
2.13
) (1),
we have a simplicial immersion

X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal P}_{\bullet}
into
a simplicial log smooth scheme over
\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}
.
Then, by taking the exactification of this simplicial immersion,
we have the simplicial immersion

X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}
.
Let
\overline{\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet}
be the log PD-envelope of
this immersion over
(\overset{\circ}{T},{\cal J},\delta)
.
Set
{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}:=\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}\times_{\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}}S(T)^{\natural}
,

{\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet}:=\overline{\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet}\times_{\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}}S(T)^{\natural}
.

Let
E
be a flat quasi-coherent crystal of

{\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
-modules.
Let
E^{\bullet}
be the crystal of

{\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
-modules
obtained by
E
.
Let
(\overline{\cal E}{}^{\bullet},\overline{\nabla})

(
\overline{\nabla}\colon\overline{\cal E}{}^{\bullet}\longrightarrow\overline{\cal E}{}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{1}_{\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
)
be the quasi-coherent

{\cal O}_{\overline{\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet}}
-module
with integrable connection corresponding to

\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(E^{\bullet})
.
Set

({\cal E}^{\bullet},\nabla)=(\overline{\cal E}{}^{\bullet},\overline{\nabla})\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\overline{\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet}}}{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet}}
.
For
U=S(T)^{\natural}
or
U=\overset{\circ}{T}
,
we have the log de Rham complexes

\overline{\cal E}{}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/U}
,

{\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/U}
,

{\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}}/U}

and

{\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(m)}_{\bullet}/U}

for
m\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}
as in the previous section.

Let

\displaystyle\pi_{{\rm zar}}\colon((\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}\bullet})_{\rm zar},f^{-1}_{\bullet}({\cal O}_{T}))\longrightarrow((\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}})_{\rm zar},f^{-1}({\cal O}_{T}))

be the natural morphism of ringed topoi.

Let
\epsilon_{X^{(m)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}\colon X^{(m)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(m)}_{T_{0}}

be the morphism forgetting the log structure of

X^{(m)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}
.
For
U=S(T)^{\natural}
or
\overset{\circ}{T}
, let

\displaystyle\epsilon_{X^{(m)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/U}\colon((X^{(m)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/U)_{\rm crys},{\cal O}_{X^{(m)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/U})\longrightarrow((\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(m)}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T})_{\rm crys},{\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(m)}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}})

be the morphism forgetting the log structure.
Let
E^{(m)}
be the restriction of

E
to
\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(m)}_{T_{0}}
.

To define

(\widetilde{R}u_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(E^{(\star)})\langle u\rangle),P)\in{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(f^{-1}({\cal O}_{T})),

we need the following lemma:

Lemma 6.7
.

The family
\{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(k)}_{n}\}_{k,n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
of log formal schemes
forms a semi-simplicial and simplicial family of log formal schemes with respect to
the upper degrees and the lower degrees, respectively.
This semi-simplicial and simplicial log scheme

{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}_{\bullet}

is augmented to the simplicial log scheme

{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}
.

Proof.

First we have to construct a morphism

{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(k)}_{n}\longrightarrow{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(k)}_{n^{\prime}}

for a morphism
[n^{\prime}]\longrightarrow[n]
in the standard simplicial category
\Delta
.
This construction is the same as that of
[
Nak4
, (1.4.2)]
.
Next we have to construct a morphism

{\cal P}_{\underline{\lambda},n}\longrightarrow{\cal P}_{\underline{\lambda}^{\prime},n}

for a morphism
\underline{\lambda}\longrightarrow\underline{\lambda}^{\prime}
in
P(\Lambda)
.
This is obvious. Hence we have a natural semi-simplicial and simplicial log scheme

{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}_{\bullet}
.
Because we have a natural morphism

{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(0)}_{n}\longrightarrow{\cal P}^{{\rm ex}}_{n}
,

{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}_{\bullet}
is augmented to

{\cal P}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}
.
∎

Let

\displaystyle\pi_{{\rm zar}}\colon((\overset{\circ}{X}_{\bullet,T_{0}})_{\rm zar},f^{-1}_{\bullet}({\cal O}_{T}))\longrightarrow((\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}})_{\rm zar},f^{-1}({\cal O}_{T}))

be the natural morphism of ringed topoi.

Definition 6.8
.

(1) Set

\displaystyle\widetilde{R}u_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(E)):=R\pi_{{\rm zar}*}({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}_{\bullet}^{{\rm ex}}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}_{\bullet}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}})\in D^{+}(f^{-1}({\cal O}_{T})).

(2) Set

\displaystyle\widetilde{R}u_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(E^{(\star)})):=R\pi_{{\rm zar}*}(s({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}_{\bullet}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}))\in D^{+}(f^{-1}({\cal O}_{T})).

(3)
Set

Ru_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}(E^{(\star)})):=R\pi_{{\rm zar}*}(s({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}_{\bullet}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural}}))\in D^{+}(f^{-1}({\cal O}_{T})).

(4)
Set

\displaystyle(\widetilde{R}u_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(E)\langle u\rangle),P):=R\pi_{{\rm zar}*}(({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle,P))

\displaystyle\in D^{+}(f^{-1}({\cal O}_{T})).

We call
\widetilde{R}u_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(E)\langle u\rangle)

the
derived PD-Hirsch extension
of

\widetilde{R}u_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(E))
.

(5) Set

\displaystyle(H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E),P):=

\displaystyle(\widetilde{R}u_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(E^{(\star)})\langle u\rangle),P)

\displaystyle:=R\pi_{{\rm zar}*}(s({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}_{\bullet}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle),P))

\displaystyle\in{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(f^{-1}({\cal O}_{T})).

We call

(H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E),P)

the
PD-Hirsch filtered crystalline complex
of
E
.
(Recall that we denote the diagonal filtration
\delta(L,P)
simply by
P

in the last equality of the definition of
(H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E),P)
.)

Remark 6.9
.

(1) By (
6.1
) the complex
\widetilde{R}u_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(E))
is well-defined.
The complexes

\widetilde{R}u_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(E^{(\star)}))
,

Ru_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}(E^{(\star)}))

and

\widetilde{R}u_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(E)\langle u\rangle)

will be checked to be well-defined
((
6.11
), (
6.12
), (
6.13
)).
The filtered complex

(H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E),P)

will also be checked to be well-defined ((
6.16
)).

(2) Strictly speaking, in the definitions of (
6.8
) (2), (3) and (5),
we have to take the direct image

{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}_{\bullet}\longrightarrow{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}

for

\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}

and

\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural}}
.

Consider the case where
E
is trivial, which will be important in this book:

Definition 6.10
.

(1) Set

\displaystyle\widetilde{R}u_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}):=\widetilde{R}u_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}({\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}})).

(2) Set

\displaystyle\widetilde{R}u_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}({\cal O}_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}):=\widetilde{R}u_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}({\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(\star)}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}})).

(3)
Set

Ru_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}):=Ru_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}({\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(\star)}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}})).

(4)
Set

\displaystyle\widetilde{R}u_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle):=\widetilde{R}u_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}({\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}})\langle u\rangle).

We call
\widetilde{R}u_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle)

the
modified Hirsch crystalline complex
of
X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}
.

(5) Set

\displaystyle(H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}),P):=

\displaystyle(\widetilde{R}u_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}({\cal O}_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle),P).

We call

(H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}),P)

the
Hirsch pre-weight-filtered log crystalline complex

of
X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}/\overset{\circ}{T}
.

Proposition 6.11
.

(1)
There exists a canonical isomorphism

(6.11.1)

Ru_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}(E))\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}Ru_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}(E^{(\star)})).

In particular,

Ru_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}(E^{(\star)}))
is independent of
the choice of an affine open covering of
X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}

and a simplicial immersion
X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal P}_{\bullet}
over
\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}
.

(2)
There exists a canonical isomorphism

(6.11.2)

\widetilde{R}u_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(E))\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\widetilde{R}u_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(E^{(\star)})).

In particular,

\widetilde{R}u_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(E^{(\star)}))
is independent of the choice of an affine open covering of
X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}

and a simplicial immersion

X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal P}_{\bullet}
over
\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}
.

Proof.

(1), (2):
Let
X^{\prime}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}
be the Čech diagram of
another affine open covering of
X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}

and let
X^{\prime}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal P}{}^{\prime}_{\bullet}

be another simplicial immersion into
a log smooth scheme over
\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}
.
As in (
6.4.4
),
by considering the refinement of two affine open coverings,
we may assume that there exists the following commutative diagram:

(6.11.3)

\begin{CD}X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}@>{\subset}>{}>\overline{\cal P}_{\bullet}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
X^{\prime}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}@>{\subset}>{}>\overline{\cal P}{}^{\prime}_{\bullet}\end{CD}

and we may assume that
the morphism
X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}\longrightarrow X^{\prime}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}

induces a morphism

X_{\underline{\lambda},\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}\longrightarrow X^{\prime}_{\underline{\lambda},\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}
.
Hence the morphism

\overline{\cal P}_{\bullet}\longrightarrow\overline{\cal P}{}^{\prime}_{\bullet}
induces a morphism

{\cal P}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}}\longrightarrow{\cal P}{}^{\prime}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}}
.
Set
U:=S(T)^{\natural}
or
\overset{\circ}{T}
.
Then we obtain the following commutative diagram

(6.11.4)

\begin{CD}{\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/U}@>{}>{}>s({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}_{\bullet}/U})\\
@A{}A{}A@A{}A{}A\\
{\cal E}^{\prime}{}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}_{\bullet}^{\prime}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}_{\bullet}^{\prime}{}^{\rm ex}/U}@>{}>{}>s({\cal E}{}^{\prime\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}_{\bullet}^{\prime}{}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}_{\bullet}^{\prime}{}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}/U}).\end{CD}

By (
4.17
) we see that the horizontal morphisms
in (
6.11.4
) are quasi-isomorphisms.
Now we have only to apply
R\pi_{{\rm zar}*}
to (
6.11.4
).
∎

The following proposition tells us that
the complex

\widetilde{R}u_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(E)\langle u\rangle)

is also independent of
the choice of an open covering of
X

and a simplicial immersion

X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal P}_{\bullet}
over
\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}
.

Proposition 6.12
.

There exists a canonical isomorphism

(6.12.1)

\widetilde{R}u_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(E)\langle u\rangle)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}Ru_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}(E)).

Proof.

This is a special case of (
6.4
).
∎

Proposition 6.13
.

There exists a canonical isomorphism

(6.13.1)

H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E)=\widetilde{R}u_{X^{(\star)}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(E^{(\star)})\langle u\rangle)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}Ru_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}(E^{(\star)})).

In particular,

H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E)

is independent of the choice of an affine open covering of
X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}

and a simplicial immersion

X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal P}_{\bullet}
over
\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}
.

Proof.

By the definition (
6.8
) (3),
we have the following formula

(6.13.2)

Ru_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}(E^{(\star)}))=R\pi_{{\rm zar}*}(s({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},{(\bullet)}}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural}})).

Consider the following natural morphism:

(6.13.3)

s({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle)\longrightarrow s({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural}})

By (
4.19
) we see that this morphism is a quasi-isomorphism.
Hence we obtain the isomorphism (
6.13.1
).
We can prove the independence of

H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E)

as in the proof of (
6.11
).
∎

Proposition 6.14
.

The following diagram

(6.14.1)

\begin{CD}\widetilde{R}u_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(E^{(\star)})\langle u\rangle)@>{(\ref{eqn:eaxte}),\sim}>{}>Ru_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}(E^{(\star)}))\\
@A{}A{}A@A{(\ref{eqn:eetxte})}A{\simeq}A\\
\widetilde{R}u_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(E)\langle u\rangle)@>{(\ref{eqn:exte}),\sim}>{}>Ru_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}(E)).\end{CD}

is commutative.

Proof.

The commutativity follows from the local descriptions of
the four morphisms in (
6.14.1
).
∎

Corollary 6.15
.

The following hold
:

(1)
There exists the following isomorphism

(6.15.1)

\displaystyle Ru_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}(E))\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\widetilde{R}u_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(E^{(\star)})\langle u\rangle)=H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E).

(2)
The canonical morphism

(6.15.2)

\displaystyle\widetilde{R}u_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(E)\langle u\rangle)\longrightarrow\widetilde{R}u_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(E^{(\star)})\langle u\rangle)

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

This corollary follows from (
6.14
).
∎

Theorem 6.16
.

The filtered complex

(H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E),P)

is independent of the choice of an affine open covering of
X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}

and a simplicial immersion

X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal P}_{\bullet}
over
\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}
.

Proof.

Let
X^{\prime}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}
be the Čech diagram of
another affine open covering of
X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}

and let
X^{\prime}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal P}{}^{\prime}_{\bullet}

be another simplicial immersion into
a log smooth scheme over
\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}
.
We may assume that there exists the commutative diagram (
6.11.3
)
and that the morphism
X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}\longrightarrow X^{\prime}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}

induces a morphism

X_{\underline{\lambda},\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}\longrightarrow X^{\prime}_{\underline{\lambda},\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}
.
By (
6.13
),
H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E)

is independent of the choice of an affine open covering of
X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}

and a simplicial immersion

X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal P}_{\bullet}
over
\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}
.
Hence, by using descending induction, we have only to prove that

{\rm gr}^{P}_{k}(H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E))

is independent of the choice of an affine open covering of
X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}

and a simplicial immersion

X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal P}_{\bullet}
over
\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}
.
Let

\displaystyle\pi_{\underline{\lambda},{\rm crys}}\colon((\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0},\underline{\lambda},\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T})_{\rm crys},{\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0},\underline{\lambda},\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}})\longrightarrow(\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda},T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T})_{\rm crys},{\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda},T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}})

be the natural morphism of ringed topoi.
Let
E_{\underline{\lambda}}
and
E^{\bullet}_{\underline{\lambda}}
be the inverse images of
E
and
E^{\bullet}
to

(\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda},T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T})_{\rm crys}
and

(\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda},T_{0},\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T})_{\rm crys}
, respectively.
By using (
5.13.3
),
[
NS
, (1.3.4)]
and the cohomological descent,
the graded complex

{\rm gr}^{P}_{k}(H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E))

can be calculated as follows:

(6.16.1)

\displaystyle{\rm gr}^{P}_{k}(H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E))={\rm gr}^{P}_{k}(\widetilde{R}u_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(E^{(\star)})\langle u\rangle))

\displaystyle={\rm gr}^{P}_{k}R\pi_{{\rm zar}*}(s({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle))

\displaystyle=R\pi_{{\rm zar}*}{\rm gr}_{k}^{P}(s({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle))

\displaystyle\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\bigoplus_{m\geq 0}\bigoplus_{\sharp\underline{\lambda}=m+1}\bigoplus_{j\geq 0}\bigoplus_{\sharp\underline{\mu}=k+m-2j}

\displaystyle R\pi_{{\rm zar}*}({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}b_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}*}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{{\rm zar},\underline{\mu}}(\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T})))[-k-2m+2j]

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{m\geq 0}\bigoplus_{\sharp\underline{\lambda}=m+1}\bigoplus_{j\geq 0}\bigoplus_{\sharp\underline{\mu}=k+m-2j}

\displaystyle R\pi_{{\rm zar}*}a_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu},{T}_{0}*}Ru_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu},{T}_{0},\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}*}(E^{\bullet}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{{\rm crys},\underline{\mu}}(\overset{\circ}{X}{}_{\underline{\mu},{T}_{0},\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}))[-k-2m+2j]

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{m\geq 0}\bigoplus_{\sharp\underline{\lambda}=m+1}\bigoplus_{j\geq 0}\bigoplus_{\sharp\underline{\mu}=k+m-2j}

\displaystyle a_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu},{T}_{0}*}Ru_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu},{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}*}R\pi_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu},{\rm crys}*}(E^{\bullet}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{{\rm crys},\underline{\mu}}(\overset{\circ}{X}{}_{\underline{\mu},{T}_{0},\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}))[-k-2m+2j]

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{m\geq 0}\bigoplus_{\sharp\underline{\lambda}=m+1}\bigoplus_{j\geq 0}\bigoplus_{\sharp\underline{\mu}=k+m-2j}

\displaystyle a_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu},{T}_{0}*}Ru_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu},{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}*}(E_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{{\rm crys},\underline{\mu}}(\overset{\circ}{X}{}_{\underline{\mu},{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}))[-k-2m+2j].

Moreover we obtain the following commutative diagram

(6.16.2)

\begin{CD}R\pi_{{\rm zar}*}{\rm gr}_{k}^{P}(s({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle))=\\
@A{}A{}A\\
R\pi_{{\rm zar}*}{\rm gr}_{k}^{P}(s({\cal E}^{\prime}{}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\prime}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}_{\bullet}^{\prime}{}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle))=\\
\displaystyle{\bigoplus_{m\geq 0}\bigoplus_{\sharp\underline{\lambda}=m+1}\bigoplus_{j\geq 0}\bigoplus_{\sharp\underline{\mu}=k+m-2j}}a_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu},{T}_{0}*}Ru_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu},{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}*}(E_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{{\rm crys},\underline{\mu}}(\overset{\circ}{X}{}_{\underline{\mu},{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}))[-k-2m+2j]\\
\Big\|\\
\displaystyle{\bigoplus_{m\geq 0}\bigoplus_{\sharp\underline{\lambda}=m+1}\bigoplus_{j\geq 0}\bigoplus_{\sharp\underline{\mu}=k+m-2j}}a_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu},{T}_{0}*}Ru_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu},{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}*}(E_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{{\rm crys},\underline{\mu}}(\overset{\circ}{X}{}_{\underline{\mu},{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}))[-k-2m+2j].\end{CD}

Hence we obtain the desired independence of

{\rm gr}^{P}_{k}(H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E))
.
∎

Remark 6.17
.

Because
X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0},n}

(n\in{\mathbb{N}})
is an open subscheme of an affine scheme,

P_{l}s({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}_{n}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle)=0

for
l<<0
.

Corollary 6.18
.

There exists a spectral sequence whose
E_{1}
-terms are as follows
:

(6.18.1)

\displaystyle E_{1}^{-k,q+k}=\bigoplus_{m\geq 0}\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\lambda}=m+1}\bigoplus_{j\geq 0}\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\mu}=k+m-2j}R^{q+2j-k-2m}f_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu},T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(E|_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu},T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}

\displaystyle\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{{\rm crys},\underline{\mu}}(\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu},T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T})).

If
\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}
is quasi-compact, then
there exists the following convergent spectral sequence
:

(6.18.2)

\displaystyle E_{1}^{-k,q+k}=\bigoplus_{m\geq 0}\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\lambda}=m+1}\bigoplus_{j\geq 0}\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\mu}=k+m-2j}R^{q+2j-k-2m}f_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu},T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(E|_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu},T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}

\displaystyle\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{{\rm crys},\underline{\mu}}(\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu},T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}))\Longrightarrow R^{q}f_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}(E))\quad(q\in{\mathbb{N}}).

Proof.

The existence of the spectral sequence follows from (
6.16.1
).
The existence of the convergent spectral sequence follows from (
5.14
)
(this implies that (
6.18.2
) is regular),
(
5.15
) and the cohomological version of
the
[
W
, Classical Convergence Theorem 5.5.1]
and (
6.15.1
).
∎

Definition 6.19
.

We denote by
P_{H}
the induced filtration on

R^{h}f_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}(E))
