).

We use the following in §
11
below.

Proposition 6.20
.

If
\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}
is quasi-compact, then

(H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E),P)
is bounded above.

Proof.

First we note that
H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E)
is bounded above.
Indeed,
H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E)
is isomorphic to

Ru_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}(E))
.
This complex
is bounded above by the log Poincaré lemma
because
\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}
is quasi-compact,

By (
6.17
),

P_{l}H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E)=0

for
l<<0
because
\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}
is quasi-compact.
By (
6.16.1
),

\displaystyle{\rm gr}^{P}_{k}(H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E))

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{m\geq 0}\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\lambda}=m+1}\bigoplus_{j\geq 0}\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\mu}=k+m-2j}Ru_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu},{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}*}(E_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{{\rm crys},\underline{\mu}}(\overset{\circ}{X}{}_{\underline{\mu},{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}))[-k-2m+2j].

Because
X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}
is quasi-compact, there exists a positive integer
q

such that

R^{q}u_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu},{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}*}(E_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{{\rm crys},\underline{\mu}}(\overset{\circ}{X}{}_{\underline{\mu},{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}))=0

for any
\underline{\lambda}
and
\underline{\mu}
by the Poincaré lemma.
We complete the proof of (
6.20
).
∎

The last aim in this section is to prove necessary results for the comparison
of
(H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E),P)

with Kim-Hain’s filtered complex in the case where
S
is the log point
s
of
a perfect field of characteristic
p>0
and
T={\cal W}_{n}(s)
.

Recall that
E_{\underline{\lambda}}
is the inverse image of
E
to

(\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda},T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T})_{\rm crys}
.
Let
f_{\underline{\lambda}}\colon X_{\underline{\lambda},\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}\longrightarrow S(T)^{\natural}
be the structural morphism.
For
U=S(T)^{\natural}
or
\overset{\circ}{T}
,
let

\displaystyle\epsilon_{X_{\underline{\lambda},\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/U}\colon((X_{\underline{\lambda},\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/U)_{\rm crys},{\cal O}_{X_{\underline{\lambda},\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/U})\longrightarrow((\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda},\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T})_{\rm crys},{\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda},T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}),

be the morphism forgetting the log structure.

Proposition 6.21
.

For a nonempty set
\underline{\lambda}
,
set

\displaystyle(\widetilde{R}u_{X_{\underline{\lambda},\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\underline{\lambda},\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(E_{\underline{\lambda}})),P):=R\pi_{\underline{\lambda},{\rm zar}*}((s({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}),P))

in
{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(f_{\underline{\lambda}}^{-1}({\cal O}_{T}))
.
Let
a_{\underline{\lambda},\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu},\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}\colon\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu},T_{0}}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda},T_{0}}
be the natural closed immersion.
For an integer
k
,
there exists a natural isomorphism

(6.21.1)

\displaystyle{\rm gr}_{k}^{P}\widetilde{R}u_{X_{\underline{\lambda},\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\underline{\lambda},\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(E_{\underline{\lambda}}))\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\mu}=k}a_{\underline{\lambda},\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu},T_{0}*}Ru_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu},T}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(E_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{{\rm crys},\underline{\mu}}(\overset{\circ}{X}_{T}/\overset{\circ}{T}))[-k].

Proof.

As in (
6.16.1
), we obtain the isomorphism
(
6.21.1
) by using (
5.9.2
).
∎

Corollary 6.22
.

The filtered complex

(\widetilde{R}u_{X_{\underline{\lambda},\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\underline{\lambda},\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(E_{\underline{\lambda}})),P)

is independent of the choice of an open covering of
X

and an immersion

X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0},\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal P}_{\bullet}
over
\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}
.

Proof.

Note that
P_{-1}\widetilde{R}u_{X_{\underline{\lambda},\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\underline{\lambda},\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(E_{\underline{\lambda}}))=0
.
By using (
6.21.1
), ascending induction on
k

shows (
6.22
).
∎

Definition 6.23
.

Set

(6.23.1)

\displaystyle(\widetilde{R}u_{X^{(m)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X^{(m)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(E)):=\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\lambda}=m+1}(\widetilde{R}u_{X_{\underline{\lambda},\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\underline{\lambda},\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(E_{\underline{\lambda}})),P).

Proposition 6.24
.

Assume that
\underline{\lambda}\not=\emptyset
.
Let

(6.24.1)

\pi_{\underline{\lambda},{\rm zar}}\colon((X_{\underline{\lambda},\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet})_{\rm zar},f^{-1}_{\bullet}({\cal O}_{T}))\longrightarrow((X_{\underline{\lambda},\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}})_{\rm zar},f^{-1}({\cal O}_{T}))

be the natural morphism of ringed topoi.
Set

(6.24.2)

\displaystyle Ru_{X_{\underline{\lambda},\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\underline{\lambda},\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}(E_{\underline{\lambda}}))

\displaystyle=R\pi_{\underline{\lambda},{\rm zar}}(s({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex},\bullet}}{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}}}))

\displaystyle=R\pi_{\underline{\lambda},{\rm zar}}(s({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}}/S(T)^{\natural}}))

in
{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(f_{\underline{\lambda}}^{-1}({\cal O}_{T}))
.
Then

Ru_{X_{\underline{\lambda},\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\underline{\lambda},\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}(E_{\underline{\lambda}}))

is independent of the choice of an affine open covering of
X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}

and an immersion

X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0},\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal P}_{\bullet}
over
\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}
.

Proof.

We have the following exact sequence:

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow s({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural}})[-1]\overset{d\log t\wedge}{\longrightarrow}s({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}})\longrightarrow s({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural}})\longrightarrow 0.

Since this is locally split, the following sequence is also locally split:

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow s({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}}})[-1]\overset{d\log t\wedge}{\longrightarrow}s({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}}})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow s({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}}})\longrightarrow 0.

Hence we have the following triangle:

\displaystyle R\pi_{\underline{\lambda}*}(s({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}}}))[-1]\overset{d\log t\wedge}{\longrightarrow}R\pi_{\underline{\lambda}*}(s({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}}}))

\displaystyle\longrightarrow R\pi_{\underline{\lambda}*}(s({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}}}))\overset{+1}{\longrightarrow}.

By (
4.19.4
), for each
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
the following sequence

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow R^{i-1}\pi_{\underline{\lambda}*}(s({\cal E}^{n}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{n}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{n}/S(T)^{\natural}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{n}}}{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{n\underline{\lambda}}}))\overset{d\log t\wedge}{\longrightarrow}R^{i}\pi_{\underline{\lambda}*}(s({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{n}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{n}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{n}}}{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{n\underline{\lambda}}}))

\displaystyle\longrightarrow R^{i}\pi_{\underline{\lambda}*}(s({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{n}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{n}/S(T)^{\natural}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{n}}}{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{n\underline{\lambda}}}))\longrightarrow 0

is exact.
Hence, by using the standard spectral sequence, we see that
the morphism

\displaystyle R^{i}\pi_{\underline{\lambda}*}(s({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}}}))\longrightarrow R^{i}\pi_{\underline{\lambda}*}(s({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}}}))

is surjective. Consequently we have the following exact sequence:

(6.24.3)

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow R\pi^{i-1}_{\underline{\lambda}*}(s({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}}}))\overset{d\log t\wedge}{\longrightarrow}\widetilde{R}{}^{i}u_{X_{\underline{\lambda},\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\underline{\lambda},\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(E_{\underline{\lambda}}))

\displaystyle\longrightarrow R^{i}\pi_{\underline{\lambda}*}(s({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}}}))\longrightarrow 0.

Induction on
i
tells us that

R^{i}\pi_{\underline{\lambda}*}(s({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}}}))

is independent of the choice of an affine open covering of
X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}

and an immersion

X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0},\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal P}_{\bullet}
over
\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}
.
Because the rest of the proof is a routine work, we leave the rest to the reader.
∎

Set

(6.24.4)

\displaystyle Ru_{X^{(m)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X^{(m)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}(E)):=\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\lambda}=m+1}Ru_{X_{\underline{\lambda},\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\underline{\lambda},\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}(E_{\underline{\lambda}})).

Then

(6.24.5)

\displaystyle Ru_{X^{(*)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X^{(*)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}(E))=s(a^{(\bullet)}_{*}Ru_{X^{(\bullet)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X^{(\bullet)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}(E))).

7
Hirsch weight-filtered log crystalline dga’s

Let the notations be as in the previous section.
In this section we assume that
\overset{\circ}{T}
is a
p
-adic formal scheme
and we ignore
p
-torsions of
{\cal O}_{T}
-modules.
Let
g\colon Y\longrightarrow T
and
f\colon X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}\longrightarrow S(T)^{\natural}
be the structural morphism
as in the previous sections.
Let
A^{\geq 0}(g^{-1}({\cal O}_{T})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}})

be the category of positively graded dga’s
over
g^{-1}({\cal O}_{T})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}
.
Let
{\rm A}^{\geq 0}{\rm F}(f^{-1}({\cal O}_{T})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}})

be the category of positively graded dga’s
over
f^{-1}({\cal O}_{T})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}
with increasing filtrations.
As in the classical case
[
B2
, III Proposition (2.1.10)]
,
the topos
(Y/T)_{\rm crys}
has enough points.
In this section, by using the Thom-Whitney derived functor constructed in
[
Nav
]
,
we construct an object

\widetilde{R}_{\rm TW}u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}({\cal O}_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}})_{\mathbb{Q}}\in{\rm Ho}(A^{\geq 0}(g^{-1}({\cal O}_{T})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}})).

Moreover, by using the derived PD-Hirsch extension

\langle U_{S(T)^{\natural}}\rangle{}^{L}
,
we construct objects

\displaystyle\widetilde{R}_{\rm TW}u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}})_{\mathbb{Q}}\langle u\rangle^{L}:=\widetilde{R}_{\rm TW}u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}})_{\mathbb{Q}}\langle U_{S(T)^{\natural}}\rangle^{L}\in{\rm Ho}(A^{\geq 0}(g^{-1}({\cal O}_{T})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}))

and

\displaystyle(H_{{\rm zar},{\rm TW}}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural})_{\mathbb{Q}},P)\in{\rm Ho}(A^{\geq 0}F(f^{-1}({\cal O}_{T})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}})).

Though the proof of the following proof is easy, the result itself is interesting.

Proposition 7.1
.

Let
(U,{\cal K},\epsilon)
be a fine log
p
-adic formal PD-scheme.
Set
U_{0}:=\underline{\rm Spec}^{\log}_{U}(U/{\cal K})
.
Let
Z
be a fine log scheme over
U_{0}

and let
h\colon Z\longrightarrow U
be the structural morphism.
Assume that there exists an immersion
Z\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal Z}

into a log smooth log scheme over
U
.
Let
A^{\geq 0}({\cal O}_{Z/U})

be the category of positively graded pd-dga’s over
{\cal O}_{Z/U}
.
Then there exists a canonical object

\displaystyle\Omega^{\bullet}_{(Z/U)_{\rm crys}}\in{\rm Ho}(A^{\geq 0}({\cal O}_{Z/U}))

whose image in
D^{+}({\cal O}_{Z/U})

is isomorphic to
{\cal O}_{Z/U}
.

Proof.

Let
{\mathfrak{F}}
be the log PD-envelope of the immersion

Z\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal Z}
over
(U,{\cal K},\epsilon)
.
Let

\displaystyle L_{\rm crys}\colon\{{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{F}}}{\textrm{-}}{\rm modules}\}\longrightarrow\{{\cal O}_{Z/U}{\textrm{-}}{\rm modules}\}

be the log linearization functor for
{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{F}}}
-modules.
Then we obtain a sheaf of cosimplicial dga ’s

\displaystyle(L_{\rm crys}({\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{F}}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Z}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Z}/U}),L(d))=(L_{\rm crys}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\mathfrak{F}}/U,[~]}),L(d))

over
h^{-1}_{Z/U}({\cal O}_{U})
.
Here we have used the equality

{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{F}}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Z}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Z}/U}=\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\mathfrak{F}}/U,[~]}
,
which has been proved in
[
Nak4
, (1.3.28)]
which is the log version of

[
Il
, 0 (3.1.4), (3.1.6)]
.

Set

\displaystyle\Omega^{\bullet}_{(Z/U)_{\rm crys}}:=(L_{\rm crys}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\mathfrak{F}}/U,[~]}),L(d)).

We claim that
\Omega^{\bullet}_{(Z/U)_{\rm crys}}
is independent of the choice of
an immersion
Z\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal Z}
.

Let
Z\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal Z}^{\prime}
be
another immersion into a log smooth scheme over
U
.
By considering the product
{\cal Z}\times_{U}{\cal Z}^{\prime}
,
we may assume that we have a morphism
{\cal Z}\longrightarrow{\cal Z}^{\prime}
such that
the composite morphism
Z\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal Z}\longrightarrow{\cal Z}^{\prime}
is the given immersion.
Let
{\mathfrak{F}}^{\prime}
be the log PD-envelope of this composite immersion over

(U,{\cal K},\epsilon)
. Then we have a natural morphism
{\mathfrak{F}}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{F}}^{\prime}

and hence we have a morphism

L_{\rm crys}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\mathfrak{F}}^{\prime}/U,[~]})\longrightarrow L_{\rm crys}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\mathfrak{F}}/U,[~]})
fitting into
the following commutative diagram

\begin{CD}{\cal O}_{Z/U}@>{\sim}>{}>L_{\rm crys}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\mathfrak{F}}/U,[~]})\\
\Big\|@A{}A{}A\\
{\cal O}_{Z/U}@>{\sim}>{}>L_{\rm crys}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\mathfrak{F}}^{\prime}/U,[~]}).\end{CD}

Here we have used the log Poincaré lemma (
[
NS
, (2.2.7)]
).
Now we can complete the proof of (
7.1
).
∎

Theorem 7.2
.

Let the notations be as in
(
7.1
)
without
assuming the immersion
Z\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal Z}
.
Assume that
(U,{\cal K},\epsilon)
is
p
-adic.
Then there exists a canonical object

\displaystyle R_{\rm TW}u_{Z/U*}({\cal O}_{Z/U})_{\mathbb{Q}}\in{\rm Ho}(A^{\geq 0}(h^{-1}({\cal K}_{U})))

whose image in
D^{+}(h^{-1}({\cal K}_{U}))

is equal to
Ru_{Z/U*}({\cal O}_{Z/U})\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}
.

Proof.

Let
Z_{\bullet}
be a simplicial scheme obtained by an open covering of
Z

such that there exists an immersion

Z_{\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal Z}_{\bullet}

into a simplicial log smooth scheme over
U
as usual.
Let
{\mathfrak{F}}_{\bullet}
be the log PD-envelope of this immersion over

(U,{\cal K},\epsilon)
.
Let
v_{\bullet}\colon(Z_{\bullet}/U)_{\rm crys}\longrightarrow U_{\rm zar}
be the morphism of topoi
obtained by
v
.
Then we obtain a sheaf of dga’s

\displaystyle{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{F}}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Z}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Z}_{\bullet}/U}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}=\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\mathfrak{F}}_{\bullet}/U,[~]}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}

over
v^{-1}_{\bullet}({\cal K}_{U})
. Set

\displaystyle R_{\rm TW}u_{Z/U*}({\cal O}_{Z/U})_{\mathbb{Q}}:=R_{\rm TW}\pi_{{\rm zar}*}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\mathfrak{F}}_{\bullet}/U,[~]}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}).

We claim that
R_{\rm TW}u_{Z/U*}({\cal O}_{Z/U})_{\mathbb{Q}}

is independent of the choices of
an open covering of
Z

and a simplicial immersion
Z_{\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal Z}_{\bullet}

into a log smooth simplicial log formal scheme as usual.
Let
Z^{\prime}_{\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal Z}^{\prime}_{\bullet}
be another simplicial immersion.
Let
{\mathfrak{F}}^{\prime}_{\bullet}
be the log PD-envelope of this immersion over

(U,{\cal K},\epsilon)
.
Then, by taking a refinement of two open coverings of
Z
,
we may assume that there exists the following commutative diagram

\begin{CD}Z_{\bullet}@>{\subset}>{}>{\cal Z}_{\bullet}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
Z^{\prime}_{\bullet}@>{\subset}>{}>{\cal Z}^{\prime}_{\bullet}\end{CD}

over
U
.
By
[
Nav
, (4.4)]
the following diagram is commutative

\begin{CD}R_{\rm TW}\pi_{{\rm zar}*}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\mathfrak{F}}_{\bullet}/U,[~]}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}})@>{\sim}>{}>R\pi_{{\rm zar}*}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\mathfrak{F}}_{\bullet}/U,[~]}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}})\\
@A{}A{}A@A{}A{}A\\
R_{\rm TW}\pi_{{\rm zar}*}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\mathfrak{F}}^{\prime}_{\bullet}/U,[~]}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}})@>{\sim}>{}>R\pi_{{\rm zar}*}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\mathfrak{F}}^{\prime}_{\bullet}/U,[~]}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}})\end{CD}

over
U
. If
I
is a
\pi_{{\rm zar}*}
-acyclic sheaf on
Z_{\bullet}
, then

I\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}
is also a
\pi_{{\rm zar}*}
-acyclic sheaf on
Z_{\bullet}
.
Hence

R\pi_{{\rm zar}*}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\mathfrak{F}}_{\bullet}/U,[~]}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}})=R\pi_{{\rm zar}*}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\mathfrak{F}}_{\bullet}/U,[~]})\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}=R\pi_{{\rm zar}*}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\mathfrak{F}}^{\prime}_{\bullet}/U,[~]})\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}=R\pi_{{\rm zar}*}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\mathfrak{F}}^{\prime}_{\bullet}/U,[~]}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}})
.
Consequently we obtain the following commutative diagram

\begin{CD}R_{\rm TW}\pi_{{\rm zar}*}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\mathfrak{F}}_{\bullet}/U,[~]}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}})@>{\sim}>{}>R\pi_{{\rm zar}*}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\mathfrak{F}}_{\bullet}/U,[~]})\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}\\
@A{}A{}A\Big\|\\
R_{\rm TW}\pi_{{\rm zar}*}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\mathfrak{F}}^{\prime}_{\bullet}/U,[~]}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}})@>{\sim}>{}>R\pi_{{\rm zar}*}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\mathfrak{F}}^{\prime}_{\bullet}/U,[~]})\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}.\end{CD}

This diagram tells us that (
7.2
) holds.
∎

The following complex

\displaystyle{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle=\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T},[~]}\langle u\rangle:=\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T},[~]}\langle u\rangle

is, in fact, a sheaf of dga’s over
g^{-1}_{\bullet}({\cal O}_{T})
in the topos
Y_{\bullet,{\rm zar}}
.
Then we have the following sheaf of dga’s over

g^{-1}({\cal O}_{T})
in the topos
Y_{\rm zar}
by (
3.19.1
):

\displaystyle\widetilde{R}_{\rm TW}u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}({\cal O}_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle)_{\mathbb{Q}}:=R_{\rm TW}\pi_{{\rm zar}*}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T},[~]}\langle u\rangle\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}).

Definition 7.3
.

We call
\widetilde{R}_{\rm TW}u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}({\cal O}_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle)_{\mathbb{Q}}

the
PD-Hirsch extensions
of

\widetilde{R}_{\rm TW}u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}({\cal O}_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}})_{\mathbb{Q}}
.

Proposition 7.4
.

There exists the following canonical isomorphisms

(7.4.1)

\displaystyle\widetilde{R}_{\rm TW}u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}({\cal O}_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle)_{\mathbb{Q}}

\displaystyle\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}R_{\rm TW}u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*})_{\mathbb{Q}}

in
{\rm Ho}(A^{\geq 0}(g^{-1}({\cal O}_{T})_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}})
.
In particular,

\widetilde{R}_{\rm TW}u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}({\cal O}_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle)_{\mathbb{Q}}

is independent of the choice of an affine open covering of
Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}

and a simplicial immersion

Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal Q}_{\bullet}

over
\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}
.

Proof.

By (
6.4.1
) we obtain (
7.4
).
We leave the detail of the proof to the reader.
∎

Now we consider the case where
Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}=X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}
.
For a subset
\underline{\lambda}
of
\Lambda
, set

\displaystyle(\widetilde{R}_{\rm TW}u_{X_{\underline{\lambda},\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}({\cal O}_{X_{\underline{\lambda},\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle),P)

\displaystyle:=s_{\rm TW}\pi_{\underline{\lambda},T{\rm zar}*}((G({\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}})\langle U_{S(T)^{\natural}}\rangle),G(P))

in
{\rm Ho}(A^{\geq 0}(f^{-1}({\cal O}_{T}))\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}})
.
Here
G
means the Godement resolution.
For a nonnegative integer
m
, we obtain
the filtered dga

\displaystyle(\widetilde{R}_{\rm TW}u_{X^{(m)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}({\cal O}_{X^{(m)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle),P)

\displaystyle:=\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\lambda}=m+1}s_{\rm TW}\pi_{\underline{\lambda},T{\rm zar}*}((G({\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}})\langle U_{S(T)^{\natural}}\rangle),G(P))

in
{\rm Ho}(A^{\geq 0}(f^{-1}({\cal O}_{T})_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}})
.
The semi-cosimplicial filtered dga

\displaystyle\{\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\lambda}=m+1}s_{\rm TW}\pi_{\underline{\lambda},T{\rm zar}*}(((G({\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}})\langle U_{S(T)^{\natural}}\rangle),G(P)))\}_{m\in{\mathbb{N}}}

defines the following filtered dga:

\displaystyle(H_{{\rm zar},{\rm TW}}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural})_{\mathbb{Q}},P)

\displaystyle:=s_{\rm TW}\{\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\lambda}=m+1}s_{\rm TW}\pi_{\underline{\lambda},T{\rm zar}*}(((G({\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet}}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}})\langle U_{S(T)^{\natural}}\rangle),G(P)))\}_{m\in{\mathbb{N}}}.

Definition 7.5
.

We call

\displaystyle(H_{{\rm zar},{\rm TW}}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural})_{\mathbb{Q}},P)

the
PD-Hirsch extension of the semi-cosimplicial zariskian
filtered crystalline dga
of
X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}/\overset{\circ}{T}
.

Proposition 7.6
.

Let

\displaystyle\Phi\colon{\rm Ho}(A^{\geq 0}(f^{-1}({\cal O}_{T}))\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}})\longrightarrow{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(f^{-1}({\cal O}_{T})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}})

be the forgetful functor.
Then

(7.6.1)

\displaystyle\Phi((H_{{\rm zar},{\rm TW}}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural})_{\mathbb{Q}},P))=(H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural})_{\mathbb{Q}},P).

Proof.

This follows from the general theory (
[
Nav
, (6.3), (6.14)]
).
∎

Remark 7.7
.

In the following we mainly give the proofs of results only for
(H_{\rm zar},P)
,
not for
(H_{{\rm zar},{\rm TW}},P)
, though we also give the statements for
not only
(H_{\rm zar},P)
but also
(H_{{\rm zar},{\rm TW}},P)
;
we leave the proofs of
the statements for
(H_{{\rm zar},{\rm TW}},P)
to the reader because
we have only to give additional minor arguments to the proofs of
results for
(H_{\rm zar},P)
.

8
Contravariant functorialities of
PD-Hirsch pre-weight-filtered log crystalline complexes
and Hirsch weight-filtered log crystalline dga’s

Let the notations be as in the previous section.
In this section we show the contravariant functoriality of

\widetilde{R}u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(F)\langle u\rangle)
.
We also show the contravariant functoriality of

(H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E),P)

with respect to a certain morphism which includes the
abrelative Frobenius morphism defined in
[
Nak4
]
.
As a consequence, we obtain the contravariant functoriality of
the spectral sequence (
6.18.2
).
In particular, we can define a generalization of the Tate twist
which appears in (
6.18.2
).

In the following we need the (not so standard) argument in
[
Nak4
, (1.5)]

because we consider non-nil immersions.

Let the notations be as in the previous section.
Let
S^{\prime}
and
(T^{\prime},{\cal J}^{\prime},\delta^{\prime})
be the analogues of
S
and
(T,{\cal J},\delta)
, respectively.
Let
S\longrightarrow S^{\prime}
be a morphism of log schemes and
let
(T,{\cal J},\delta)\longrightarrow(T^{\prime},{\cal J}^{\prime},\delta^{\prime})
be
a morphism of log PD-enlargements of
S
and
S^{\prime}
, respectively.
Let
\star
be nothing or

Proposition 8.1
.

(1)
The morphism
(

and

into log smooth schemes over

and

and the morphism

.

(2)
Let
S^{\prime}\longrightarrow S^{\prime\prime}

,

and

{\rm id}^{*}_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}_{0}}}={\rm id}_{\widetilde{R}u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\overline{F}\langle u\rangle)}
.

Proof.

(1), (2): The proof needs only a routine work once
one constructs the morphism (
8.0.6
)
and know the well-definedness of

The following theorem plays an important role in this book.

Theorem 8.2

(
Contravariant functoriality).

Let
f^{\prime}\colon Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}_{0}}\longrightarrow S^{\prime}(T^{\prime})^{\natural}
be the structural morphism.
Then the following hold
:

(1)
The morphism
g\colon X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}\longrightarrow Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}_{0}}

induces the following well-defined morphism

(8.2.1)

\displaystyle g^{*}\colon(H_{\rm zar}(Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}_{0}}/S^{\prime}(T^{\prime})^{\natural},F),P)\longrightarrow Rg_{*}((H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E),P))

in
{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(f^{\prime}{}^{-1}({\cal O}_{T^{\prime}}))
.

(2)
Let
Z/S^{\prime\prime}
,
(T^{\prime\prime},{\cal J}^{\prime},\delta^{\prime\prime})

be similar objects to
Y/S^{\prime}

and
(T^{\prime},{\cal J}^{\prime},\delta^{\prime})
,
respectively.
Let
h\colon Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}_{0}}\longrightarrow Z_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime\prime}_{0}}
be a similar morphism to

g\colon X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}\longrightarrow Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}_{0}}
.
Let
\overset{\circ}{h}{}^{*}_{\rm crys}(G)\longrightarrow F
be a similar morphism to

\overset{\circ}{g}{}^{*}_{\rm crys}(F)\longrightarrow E
.
Let
f^{\prime\prime}\colon Z_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime\prime}_{0}}\longrightarrow S^{\prime\prime}(T^{\prime\prime})^{\natural}

be the structural morphism.
Then

(8.2.2)

\displaystyle(h\circ g)^{*}=Rh_{*}(g^{*})\circ h^{*}\colon

\displaystyle(H_{\rm zar}(Z_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime\prime}_{0}}/S^{\prime\prime}(T^{\prime\prime})^{\natural},G),P)\longrightarrow R(h\circ g)_{*}((H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E),P))

in
{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(f^{\prime\prime}{}^{-1}({\cal O}_{T^{\prime\prime}}))
and

{\rm id}^{*}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}}={\rm id}_{(H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E),P)}
.

(3)

The isomorphisms
(
6.13.1
)
and
(
6.11.1
)

are functorial, that is, there exists a morphism

(8.2.3)

\displaystyle g^{*}\colon Ru_{Y^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}_{0}}/S^{\prime}(T^{\prime})^{\natural}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{Y^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}_{0}}/S^{\prime}(T^{\prime})^{\natural}}(F^{(\star)}))\longrightarrow Rg_{*}Ru_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}(E^{(\star)}))

fitting into the following commutative diagram
:

(8.2.4)

\begin{CD}H_{\rm zar}(Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}_{0}}/S^{\prime}(T^{\prime})^{\natural},F,u^{\prime})@>{g^{*}}>{}>Rg_{*}(H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E))\\
@V{\simeq}V{}V@V{}V{\simeq}V\\
Ru_{Y^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}_{0}}/S^{\prime}(T^{\prime})^{\natural}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{Y^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}_{0}}/S^{\prime}(T^{\prime})^{\natural}}(F^{(\star)}))@>{g^{*}}>{}>Rg_{*}Ru_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}(E^{(\star)}))\\
@A{\simeq}A{}A@A{}A{\simeq}A\\
Ru_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}_{0}}/S^{\prime}(T^{\prime})^{\natural}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S^{\prime}(T^{\prime})^{\natural}}(F))@>{g^{*}}>{}>Rg_{*}Ru_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}(E)).\end{CD}

The morphism
(
8.2.3
)
satisfies
the similar transitive relation to that in
{\rm(2)}
.

(4)
: Assume that
\overset{\circ}{T}
is a
p
-adic formal scheme.
The morphism
g\colon X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}\longrightarrow Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}_{0}}

induces the following morphism

(8.2.5)

\displaystyle g^{*}\colon(H_{{\rm zar},{\rm TW}}(Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}_{0}}/S^{\prime}(T^{\prime})^{\natural}),P)\longrightarrow R_{\rm TW}g_{*}((H_{{\rm zar},{\rm TW}}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}),P))

in
{\rm Ho}({\rm A}^{\geq 0}{\rm F}(f^{\prime}{}^{-1}({\cal O}_{T^{\prime}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}))
.
This satisfies the similar transitive relation to that in
(2)
.

Proof.

(1), (2), (3): The proof is the same as those of
[
Nak4
, (1.5.2)]
and [loc. cit., (1.5.8)],
in which we prove the analogous theorem to (

) for the
p
-adic filtered
Steenbrink complex.

(4): We have only to pay attention to the multiplicative structures of
the crystalline complexes.

In the rest of this section, in addition to (8.1.6),
assume that the following condition holds:

(8.2.6)
:
there exist positive integers
e_{\lambda}
’s

(\lambda\in\Lambda)
such that
there exist local equations
x_{\lambda}=0
and

y_{\lambda}=0
of

\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda}
and
\overset{\circ}{Y}_{\lambda}
,
respectively,
such that
g^{*}(y_{\lambda})=x^{e_{\lambda}}_{\lambda}
.

In
[
Nak4
, (1.7.5)]
we have proved the following:

Proposition 8.3

(
[
Nak4
, (1.7.5)]
).

Let the assumptions and the notations be as above.
Let
\lambda
be an element of
\Lambda
such that

x\in\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda}
.
Then
\deg(v)_{f(x)}=e_{\lambda}
.
In particular,
e_{\lambda}
’s are independent of
\lambda
.

Remark 8.4
.

In
[
Nak4
]
we need additional assumptions
for the morphism
g\colon X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}\longrightarrow Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}_{0}}

satisfying the conditions (8.1.6) and (8.2.6)
(e.g.,
p\nmid\deg(v^{\natural})
) to discuss the
contravariant functoriality of
the filtered complex
(A_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E),P)
,
which will be recalled in §
16
below.
We also need these additional assumptions
for the following spectral sequence obtained by this filtered complex if one considers the compatibility of
the actions
g^{*}
on
R^{q}f_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}(E))
with the action

\overset{\circ}{g}{}^{*}
on the following
E_{1}
-terms into account:

(8.4.1)

\displaystyle E_{1}^{-k,q+k}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{j\geq\max\{-(k+m),0\}}R^{q-2j-k}f_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k)}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(E_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k)}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}

\displaystyle\phantom{R^{q-2j-k-m}f_{(\overset{\circ}{X}^{(k)},Z|_{\overset{\circ}{X}^{(2j+k)}})/S*}({\cal O}}\varpi^{(2j+k)}_{\rm crys}(\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}))(-j-k,v)

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow R^{q}f_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}(E))\quad(q\in{\mathbb{Z}}).

Here
(-j-k,v)
will be explained soon.

In (
8.2.1
) we do not need the additional assumptions.
However the
E_{1}
-terms of the spectral sequence (
8.8.1
) below
have much more direct summands than those of (
8.4.1
).

Definition 8.5
.

Let
w\colon{\cal E}\longrightarrow{\cal F}

be a morphism of

f^{-1}({\cal O}_{T})
-modules (resp.
{\cal O}_{T}
-modules).
The
D
-
twist
(:=degree twist) of
w
by
k
with respect to
v

w(-k)\colon{\cal E}(-k;v)\longrightarrow{\cal F}(-k;v)

is, by definition, the morphism

\deg(v)^{k}w\colon{\cal E}\longrightarrow{\cal F}
.
This definition is well-defined for the derived category

D^{+}(f^{-1}({\cal O}_{T}))

(resp.
D^{+}({\cal O}_{T})
).

Let
v\colon S\longrightarrow S^{\prime}
be a morphism of families of log points
and let
((T,{\cal J},\delta),z)\longrightarrow((T^{\prime},{\cal J}^{\prime},\delta^{\prime}),z^{\prime})
be a morphism of
log PD-enlargements over

.
Let

be the induced morphism
by

and the morphism

.
Let
\overline{t}{}^{\prime}
be a local section of

such that
the image

of
\overline{t}{}^{\prime}
in
\overline{M}_{S^{\prime}(T^{\prime})^{\natural}}=M_{S^{\prime}(T^{\prime})^{\natural}}/{\cal O}_{T^{\prime}}^{*}

is the local generator.
By the definition of
\deg(v^{\natural})_{x}
((
2.15
)),
we have

(8.5.1)

v^{\natural*}(d\log\overline{t}{}^{\prime})=\deg(v^{\natural})d\log\widetilde{t}.

Let us recall the
{\cal O}_{S^{\prime}}
-linear morphism (
3.7.5
)
for the case above:

(8.5.2)

v^{*}\colon\Gamma_{{\cal O}_{S^{\prime}}}(U_{S^{\prime}})\longrightarrow v_{*}(\Gamma_{{\cal O}_{S}}(U_{S})).

Example 8.6
.

(1) In the case where
S
is of characteristic
p>0
,
set
S^{[p]}:=S\times_{\overset{\circ}{S},\overset{\circ}{F}_{S}}\overset{\circ}{S}
,
where
F_{S}\colon S\longrightarrow S
is the absolute Frobenius endomorphism of
S
.
The endomorphism
F_{S}
induces the natural morphism

F_{S/\overset{\circ}{S}}\colon S\longrightarrow S^{[p]}
.
In
[
Nak4
, (1.5.14)]
we have called
F_{S/\overset{\circ}{S}}
the
abrelative Frobenius morphism

of
S
(see also
[
O2
]
).
Let
F_{T,T^{\prime}}\colon((T,{\cal J},\delta),z)\longrightarrow((T^{\prime},{\cal J}^{\prime},\delta^{\prime}),z^{\prime})
be
a morphism of log PD enlargements over

F_{S/\overset{\circ}{S}}\colon S\longrightarrow S^{\prime}:=S^{[p]}
.
We can define the
abrelative Frobenius action

F^{\natural}_{T,T^{\prime}}\colon S(T)^{\natural}\longrightarrow S^{[p]}(T^{\prime})^{\natural}
with respect to
F_{T,T^{\prime}}
.
Here note that
{\rm deg}(F^{\natural}_{T,T^{\prime}})=p
.

Set
X^{[p]}:=X\times_{S}S^{[p]}=X\times_{\overset{\circ}{S},\overset{\circ}{F}_{S}}\overset{\circ}{S}
.
Let
T^{\prime}_{0}
be the exact closed log subscheme of
T^{\prime}
defined by
the PD-ideal sheaf
{\cal J}^{\prime}
.
Then we have the abrelative Frobenius morphism

(8.6.1)

\displaystyle F^{\rm abr}_{X_{S_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}}/S(T)^{\natural},S^{[p]}(T^{\prime})^{\natural}}\colon X_{S_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}}\longrightarrow X^{[p]}_{S^{[p]}_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}_{0}}}

over
S(T)^{\natural}\longrightarrow S^{[p]}(T^{\prime})^{\natural}
.
This abrelative Frobenius morphism satisfies the conditions (8.1.6) and (8.2.6).

When
(T^{\prime},{\cal J}^{\prime},\delta^{\prime})=(T,{\cal J},\delta)
and
z^{\prime}
is the composite morphism

F_{S/\overset{\circ}{S}}\circ z
, then we denote
F^{\natural}_{T,T^{\prime}}
by

F^{\natural}_{T}
. In this case, we also denote the morphism
(
8.6.1
) by

(8.6.2)

\displaystyle F^{\rm abr}_{X_{S_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}}/S(T)^{\natural}}\colon X_{S_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}}\longrightarrow X^{[p]}_{S^{[p]}_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}_{0}}}.

We define the
abrelative Frobenius action

F^{\natural,{\rm abr}*}_{T}
with respect to

F^{\rm abr}_{T}
on
u^{[i]}
by the formula

F^{\natural,{\rm abr}*}_{T}(u^{[i]})=p^{i}u^{[i]}.

Obviously the morphism
F^{\natural,{\rm abr}*}_{T}
is independent of the choice of
u
.

(2) Let the notations be as in (1).
If there exists a PD-endomorphism

F_{T}\colon((T,{\cal J},\delta),z)\longrightarrow((T,{\cal J},\delta),z)

over
F_{S}\colon S\longrightarrow S
,
then we have the endomorphism

S(T)^{\natural}\longrightarrow S(T)^{\natural}
of
S(T)^{\natural}
and
we have the following absolute Frobenius endomorphism

(8.6.3)

\displaystyle F^{\rm abs}_{X_{S_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}}/S(T)^{\natural}}\colon X_{S_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}}\longrightarrow X_{S_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}_{0}}}

This absolute Frobenius endomorphism over
S(T)^{\natural}\longrightarrow S(T)^{\natural}

satisfies the conditions (8.1.6) and (8.2.6).
We can define the
absolute Frobenius action

F^{\natural{\rm abs}*}_{T}
with respect to

F_{T}
on
u^{[i]}
by the formula

F^{\natural,{\rm abs}*}_{T}(u^{[i]})=p^{i}u^{[i]}
.

Let the notations and the assumptions be as in the previous section.
By the same proof as that of
[

, (1.5.9)]
, we see that
the following isomorphism appearing in (

)
is the following isomorphism

(8.6.4)

\displaystyle{\rm gr}_{k}^{P}(s({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle))\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{m\geq 0}\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\lambda}=m+1}\bigoplus_{j\geq 0}\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\mu}=k+m-2j}({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}_{\bullet}^{\rm ex}}}b_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}*}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{\bullet,\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}

\displaystyle\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{{\rm zar},\underline{\mu}}({\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T})))(-(k+m-j);v)[-k-2m+2j].

.

Remark 8.10
.

The
E_{1}
-term in
[
KH
, p. 1271]
is mistaken
because
t
can be greater than
n
in [loc. cit.]; in (

),

in (

) can be greater than
k
.
Furthermore, the complex
G_{i}
in [loc. cit.] has not been made explicit and

depends also on
t
in [loc. cit.];
G_{i}
does not depend only on
i
;

G_{i}
should be denoted by
G_{i,n,t}
; in (
8.9.1
),

G_{i,t}
corresponds to

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\lambda}=m+1}\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\mu}=k+m-2j}R^{q+2j-k-2m}f_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu},T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}

\displaystyle({\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu},T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}

\displaystyle\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{{\rm crys},\underline{\mu}}(\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu},T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}))(-(k+m-j),v),

which depends on
j
,
k
and
m
.

Next we describe the boundary morphism
between the
E_{1}
-terms of (
8.8.1
).
Fix a total order on
\Lambda
once and for all.
Consider
\underline{\mu}\in P(\Lambda)
such that

.
Then

contains the following higher direct image

R^{q+2j-k-2m}f_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu},T_{0}}/T*}(E|_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu},T_{0}}/T}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{{\rm crys},\underline{\mu}}(\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu},T_{0}}/T))(-(k+m-j);v)

with

as a direct factor.
Describe
\underline{\lambda}:=\{\lambda_{0},\ldots,\lambda_{m+1}\}

(\lambda_{0}<\cdots<\lambda_{m+1})

and set
\underline{\lambda}_{l}:=\underline{\lambda}\setminus\{\lambda_{l}\}

(0\leq l\leq m+1)
.
Here
~~\widehat{}~~
means the elimination.
Let
\iota_{\underline{\lambda}_{l},\underline{\lambda},\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}\colon\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu},\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}_{l}\cup\underline{\mu},\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}
be the natural
closed immersion.
If

, then the morphism

is the identity of

\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu},\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}
.
Let

\displaystyle\rho_{\underline{\lambda},\underline{\lambda}_{l}}\colon

\displaystyle R^{q+2j-k-2m}f_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}_{l}\cup\underline{\mu},T_{0}}/T*}(E|_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}_{l}\cup\underline{\mu},T_{0}}/T}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{{\rm crys},\underline{\mu}}(\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}_{l}\cup\underline{\mu},T_{0}}/T))(-(k+m-j))

\displaystyle\longrightarrow R^{q+2j-k-2m}f_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu},T_{0}}/T*}(E|_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu},T_{0}}/T}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{{\rm crys},\underline{\mu}}(\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu},T_{0}}/T))(-(k+m-j))

be the induced morphism from

with

as a direct factor.

:
