9
Filtered base change theorem of PD-Hirsch pre-weight-filtered complexes

In this section we prove the filtered base change theorem
of
(H_{\rm zar},P)
.

Let the notations be as in the previous section.
Assume that
\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}
is quasi-compact.
Let
f\colon X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}\longrightarrow S(T)^{\natural}

be the structural morphism.

Proposition 9.1
.

Assume that
\overset{\circ}{f}\colon\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{T}

is quasi-compact and quasi-separated.
Then
Rf_{*}((H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E),P))

is isomorphic to a bounded filtered complex of

{\cal O}_{T}
-modules.

Proof.

By
[
BO1
, 7.6 Theorem]
,

Rf_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(l)}_{m,T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(E_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(l)}_{m,T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}})

(0\leq m\leq N,l\in{\mathbb{N}})
is bounded.
Hence

Rf_{*}((H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E),P))

is bounded by the spectral sequence (
8.9.1
).
∎

Theorem 9.2

(
Log base change theorem of

(H_{\rm zar},P)
).

Let the assumptions be as in
(
9.1
)
.
Let
(T^{\prime},{\cal J}^{\prime},\delta^{\prime})
be another log PD-enlargement over
S
.
Assume that
{\cal J}^{\prime}
is quasi-coherent.
Set
T^{\prime}_{0}:=\underline{\rm Spec}^{\log}_{T^{\prime}}({\cal O}_{T^{\prime}}/{\cal J}^{\prime})
.
Let
v\colon(S(T^{\prime})^{\natural},{\cal J}^{\prime},\delta^{\prime})\longrightarrow(S(T)^{\natural},{\cal J},\delta)
be
a morphism of fine log PD-schemes.
Let

f^{\prime}\colon X_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}_{0}}=X\times_{S}S_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}_{0}}\longrightarrow S(T^{\prime})^{\natural}

be the base change morphism of
f

by the morphism
S(T^{\prime})^{\natural}\longrightarrow S(T)^{\natural}
.
Let
q\colon X_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}_{0}}\longrightarrow X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}

be the induced morphism by
u
.
Then there exists
the following canonical filtered isomorphism

(9.2.1)

Lu^{*}Rf_{*}((H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E),P))\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}Rf^{\prime}_{*}((H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}_{0}}/S(T^{\prime})^{\natural},\overset{\circ}{q}{}^{*}_{\rm crys}(E)),P))

in
{\rm DF}(f^{\prime}{}^{-1}({\cal O}_{T^{\prime}}))
.

Proof.

Let the notations be as in §
6
.
Set

\overline{\cal P}_{\bullet,\overline{S(T^{\prime})^{\natural}}}:=\overline{\cal P}_{\bullet}\times_{\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}}\overline{S(T^{\prime})^{\natural}}
.
Let

\overline{\mathfrak{D}}{}^{\prime}_{\bullet}

be the log PD-envelope of the immersion

X_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}_{0}}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal P}_{\bullet,\overline{S(T^{\prime})^{\natural}}}

over
(\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime},{\cal J}^{\prime},\delta^{\prime})
.
Then we have the natural morphisms

\overline{\cal P}_{\bullet,\overline{S(T^{\prime})^{\natural}}}\longrightarrow\overline{\cal P}_{\bullet}

and

\overline{\mathfrak{D}}{}^{\prime}_{\bullet}\longrightarrow\overline{\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet}
.
We also have the identity morphism

{\rm id}\colon\overset{\circ}{q}{}^{*}_{\rm crys}(E)\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{q}{}^{*}_{\rm crys}(E)
.
Obviously the morphism

q\colon X_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}_{0}}\longrightarrow X_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}_{0}}
satisfies
the conditions (8.1.6).
Hence we have the following natural morphism

(9.2.2)

(H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E),P)\longrightarrow Rq_{*}((H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}_{0}}/S(T^{\prime})^{\natural},E),P))

by (
8.2
).
By applying
Rf_{*}
to (
9.2.2
) and using
the adjoint property of
L
and
R
(
[
NS
, (1.2.2)]
),
we have the natural morphism (
9.2.1
).
Here we have used the boundedness in
(
9.1
) for the well-definedness of
Lu^{*}
.

The rest of the proof is the same as that of
[
Nak4
, (1.6.2)]
.
∎

Let
\overset{\circ}{Y}
be a smooth scheme over
\overset{\circ}{T}
.
Endow
\overset{\circ}{Y}
with the inverse image of
M_{S_{\overset{\circ}{T}}}

and let
Y
be the resulting log scheme.
Let
{\cal Y}
be a log smooth scheme defined in (
9.3
) below.
Let
D_{{\cal Y}/S(T)^{\natural}}(1)
be the log PD-envelope of the immersion

{\cal Y}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal Y}\times_{S(T)^{\natural}}{\cal Y}
over
(S(T)^{\natural},{\cal J},\delta)
.
As in
[
B2
, V]
and
[
BO1
, §7]
, we have the following two corollaries
(cf.
[
NS
, (2.10.5), (2.10.7)]
) by
using (
6.15.1
), (
9.2
) and
a fact that

p_{i}\colon\overset{\circ}{D}_{{\cal Y}/S(T)}(1)\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{\cal Y}

(i=1,2)

is flat (
[
K1
, (6.5)]
):

Corollary 9.3
.

Let
g\colon X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}\longrightarrow Y
be an SNCL scheme.
Assume that
Y
has a log smooth lift
{\cal Y}
over
S(T)^{\natural}
.
Let
q
be an integer.
Let
g\colon X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}\longrightarrow{\cal Y}

be the structural morphism.
Then there exists a quasi-nilpotent integrable connection

(9.3.1)

\displaystyle P_{k}R^{q}g_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/{\cal Y}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}(E))\overset{\nabla_{k}}{\longrightarrow}

\displaystyle P_{k}R^{q}g_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/{\cal Y}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}(E)){\otimes}_{{\cal O}_{\cal Y}}{\Omega}_{{\cal Y}/S(T)^{\natural}}^{1}

making the following diagram commutative
for any two nonnegative integers
k\leq l:

(9.3.2)

\begin{CD}P_{k}R^{q}g_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/{\cal Y}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}(E))@>{\nabla_{k}}>{}>\\
@V{\bigcap}V{}V\\
P_{l}R^{q}g_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/{\cal Y}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}(E))@>{\nabla_{l}}>{}>\end{CD}

\begin{CD}P_{k}R^{q}g_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/{\cal Y}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}(E^{\bullet\leq N})){\otimes}_{{\cal O}_{\cal Y}}{\Omega}_{{\cal Y}/S(T)^{\natural}}^{1}\\
@V{\bigcap}V{}V\\
P_{l}R^{q}g_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/{\cal Y}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}(E^{\bullet\leq N})){\otimes}_{{\cal O}_{\cal Y}}{\Omega}_{{\cal Y}/S(T)^{\natural}}^{1}.\end{CD}

Proof.

This follows from (
9.2
) as in
[
BO1
, §7]
.
∎

Corollary 9.4
.

Let the notations and the assumptions be as in
(\ref{prop:bdccd})
.
Then

Rf_{*}(P_{k}H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E))\quad(k\in{\mathbb{N}})

has finite tor-dimension.
Moreover, if
\overset{\circ}{T}
is noetherian and
if
\overset{\circ}{f}
is proper,
then
Rf_{*}(P_{k}H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E))

is a perfect complex of
{\cal O}_{T}
-modules.

Using
[
NS
, (2.10.10)]
,
we have the following corollary
(cf.
[
NS
, (2.10.11)]
):

Corollary 9.5
.

Let the notations
and the assumptions be as in
(\ref{coro:fctd})
.
Then the filtered complex

Rf_{*}((H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E),P))

is a filtered perfect
complex of
{\cal O}_{T}
-modules, that is,
locally on
T_{\rm zar}
, filteredly quasi-isomorphic to
a filtered strictly perfect complex
(
[
NS
, (2.10.8)]
)
.

Proof.

(
9.5
)
immediately follows
from (
9.4
) and

[
NS
, (2.10.10)]
.
∎

10
Monodromy operators of PD-Hirsch pre-weight-filtered log crystalline complexes

In this section we first recall the monodromy operators defined
in
[
Nak4
]
, which is an immediate generalization of
those in
[
Hy
]
and
[
HK
]
.

Let
S
,
((T,{\cal J},\delta),z)
and
T_{0}
be as in §
4
.
Let
Y
be a log smooth scheme over
S
.
Let
Y^{\prime}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}
be the disjoint union of the member of
an affine open covering of
Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}
.
By abuse of notation, we also denote by
g

the composite morphism

g\colon Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}\longrightarrow S_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}\longrightarrow S(T)^{\natural}
.
Let
Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}
be the Čech diagram of

Y^{\prime}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}
over
Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}
:

Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0},n}={\rm cosk}_{0}^{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}}(Y^{\prime}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}})_{n}

(n\in{\mathbb{N}})
.
Let
g_{\bullet}\colon Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}\longrightarrow S(T)^{\natural}

be the structural morphism.
For
U=S(T)^{\natural}
or
\overset{\circ}{T}
,
let

\epsilon_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}/U}\colon((Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}/U)_{\rm crys},{\cal O}_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}/U})\longrightarrow((\overset{\circ}{Y}_{T_{0},\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T})_{\rm crys},{\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{Y}_{T_{0},\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}})

be the morphism forgetting the log structures of
Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}
and
U
.
Let

Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal Q}_{\bullet}

be an immersion into a log smooth scheme over
\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}
.
Set
{\cal Q}_{\bullet}:=\overline{\cal Q}_{\bullet}\times_{\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}}S(T)^{\natural}
.
Let
\overline{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}

be the log PD-envelope of
the immersion
Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{{\cal Q}}_{\bullet}
over

(\overset{\circ}{T},{\cal J},\delta)
.
Set
{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}=\overline{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}\times_{{\mathfrak{D}}(\overline{S(T)^{\natural}})}S(T)^{\natural}
.
Let
\theta_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}}\in{\Omega}^{1}_{{{\cal Q}}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}

be the pull-back of
\theta=d\log t\in{\Omega}^{1}_{S(T)^{\natural}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
by the structural morphism

{\cal Q}_{\bullet}\longrightarrow S(T)^{\natural}
.
Let
\overline{F}
be a flat quasi-coherent crystal of

{\cal O}_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
-modules.
Let
\overline{F}{}^{\bullet}
be the crystal of

{\cal O}_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
-modules obtained by
\overline{F}
.
Let
(\overline{\cal F}{}^{\bullet},\overline{\nabla})
be the quasi-coherent

{\cal O}_{\overline{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}
-module
with integrable connection corresponding to

\overline{F}{}^{\bullet}
.
Set

({\cal F}^{\bullet},\nabla)=(\overline{\cal F}{}^{\bullet},\overline{\nabla})\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\overline{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}}{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}
.
Let

(10.0.1)

\nabla\colon{\cal F}^{\bullet}\longrightarrow{\cal F}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{{\cal Q}}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{1}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}

be the induced connection by
\overline{\nabla}
.
Since
{\Omega}^{1}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural}}

is a quotient of

{\Omega}^{1}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
,
we also have the induced connection

(10.0.2)

\nabla_{/S(T)^{\natural}}\colon{\cal F}^{\bullet}\longrightarrow{\cal F}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{{\cal Q}}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{1}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural}}

by
\nabla
.
The object

({\cal F}^{\bullet},\nabla_{/S(T)^{\natural}})

corresponds to the log crystal

F:=\epsilon_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural}}^{*}(\overline{F}{}^{\bullet})
of
{\cal O}_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural}}
-modules.

In
[
Nak4
]
we have proved the following whose proof is not difficult:

Proposition 10.1

(
[
Nak4
, (1.7.22)]
).

The following sequence

(10.1.1)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\longrightarrow{\cal F}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{{\cal Q}}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{{\cal Q}}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural}}[-1]\overset{\theta_{{\cal Q}_{{\bullet}}\wedge}}{\longrightarrow}{\cal F}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{{\cal Q}}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{{\cal Q}}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\longrightarrow{\cal F}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{{\cal Q}}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{{\cal Q}}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural}}\longrightarrow 0

is exact.

Let

(10.1.2)

{\cal F}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{{\cal Q}}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{{\cal Q}}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural}}\longrightarrow{\cal F}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{{\cal Q}}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{{\cal Q}}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural}}

be the boundary morphism of (
10.1.1
)
in the derived category

D^{+}(f^{-1}_{T}({\cal O}_{T}))
.
(We make the convention on the sign of
the boundary morphism as in
[
NS
, p. 12 (4)]
.)
By using the formula

Ru_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(F^{\bullet})={\cal F}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{{\cal Q}}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{{\cal Q}}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural}}

(
[
K1
, (6.4)]
, (cf.
[
NS
, (2.2.7)]
)),
we have the following morphism

(10.1.3)

Ru_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(F^{\bullet})\longrightarrow Ru_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(F^{\bullet}).

Let

(10.1.4)

\pi_{{\rm crys}}\colon((Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural})_{\rm crys},{\cal O}_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural}})\longrightarrow((Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural})_{\rm crys},{\cal O}_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}})

and

(10.1.5)

\pi_{{\rm zar}}\colon((\overset{\circ}{Y}_{T_{0}\bullet})_{\rm zar},g^{-1}_{\bullet}({\cal O}_{T}))\longrightarrow((\overset{\circ}{Y}_{T_{0}})_{\rm zar},g^{-1}({\cal O}_{T}))

be the natural morphisms of ringed topoi.
Applying
R\pi_{{\rm zar}*}
to (
10.1.3
)
and using the formula

\pi_{{\rm zar}*}\circ u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural}}=u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}\circ\pi_{{\rm crys}}

and using the cohomological descent,
we have the following morphism

(10.1.6)

N_{\rm zar}\colon Ru_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(F)\longrightarrow Ru_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(F).

In
[
Nak4
]
we have proved the following whose proof is not difficult:

Proposition 10.2

(
[
Nak4
, (1.7.26), (1.7.30)]
).

The morphism
(
10.1.6
)

is independent of the choice of the disjoint union
of the member of an affine open covering of

Y
and an simplicial immersion

Y_{\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{{\cal Q}}_{\bullet}
over
\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}
.

Let

v\colon(S(T)^{\natural},{\cal J},\delta)\longrightarrow(S(T)^{\natural},{\cal J},\delta)
be
an endomorphism of
(S(T)^{\natural},{\cal J},\delta)
.
Let

(10.2.1)

\begin{CD}Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}@>{g}>{}>Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
S_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}@>{v_{0}}>{}>S_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}\\
@V{\bigcap}V{}V@V{}V{\bigcap}V\\
S(T)^{\natural}@>{v}>{}>S(T)^{\natural}\end{CD}

be a commutative diagram of log schemes.
The morphism (
10.1.6
)
is nothing but a morphism

(10.2.2)

N_{\rm zar}\colon Ru_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(F)\longrightarrow Ru_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(F)(-1;v)

since
v^{*}(\theta_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}})=\deg(v)\theta_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}}
.
In
[
Nak4
]
we have called the morphism (
10.2.2
) the

zariskian monodromy operator

of
Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}
.

By (
6.4.1
) we have the following diagram

(10.2.3)

\begin{CD}Ru_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(F)@>{N_{\rm zar}}>{}>Ru_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(F)\\
@A{\simeq}A{}A@A{}A{\simeq}A\\
\widetilde{R}u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(F\langle u\rangle)\widetilde{R}u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(F\langle u\rangle).\end{CD}

Consider the morphism

(10.2.4)

d/du:{\cal O}_{T}\langle u\rangle\owns u^{[i]}\longmapsto u^{[i-1]}\in{\cal O}_{T}\langle u\rangle.

Because

\displaystyle d/du=d(au)/du\cdot d/d(au)=ad/d(au)

for a local section
a\in{\cal O}_{T}^{*}
,
the morphism (
10.2.4
) is independent of the choice of
u
.
Hence we have the following morphism

(10.2.5)

D:=``d/du{\textrm{'}}{\textrm{'}}\colon\Gamma_{{\cal O}_{T}}(U_{S(T)^{\natural}})\longrightarrow\Gamma_{{\cal O}_{T}}(U_{S(T)^{\natural}}).

This morphism induces the following morphism

(10.2.6)

D:\widetilde{R}u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(F\langle u\rangle)\longrightarrow\widetilde{R}u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(F\langle u\rangle).

We also have the following similar morphism

(10.2.7)

D\colon\Gamma_{{\cal O}_{T}}(U_{S(T)^{\natural}})^{\wedge}\longrightarrow\Gamma_{{\cal O}_{T}}(U_{S(T)^{\natural}})^{\wedge}.

Theorem 10.3
.

Assume that
F
is a flat locally nilpotent quasi-coherent

{\cal O}_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
-modules.
Then the following diagram is commutative:

(10.3.1)

\begin{CD}Ru_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(F)@>{N_{\rm zar}}>{}>Ru_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(F)\\
@A{\simeq}A{}A@A{}A{\simeq}A\\
\widetilde{R}u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(F\langle u\rangle)@>{-D}>{}>\widetilde{R}u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(F\langle u\rangle).\end{CD}

Proof.

We may assume that there exists an immersion

Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal Q}
into a log smooth scheme over
\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}
.
Let the notations be as in the beginning of §
4
.
Set
{\cal Q}:=\overline{\cal Q}\times_{\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}}S(T)^{\natural}
.
Because the following diagram

(10.3.2)

\begin{CD}{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{{\rm ex}}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}{}^{{\rm ex}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle @>{-D}>{}>{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{{\rm ex}}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}{}^{{\rm ex}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle\\
@V{\simeq}V{}V@V{}V{\simeq}V\\
{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{{\rm ex}}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}{}^{{\rm ex}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle\langle u\rangle\rangle @>{-D}>{}>{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{{\rm ex}}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}{}^{{\rm ex}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle\langle u\rangle\rangle.\end{CD}

is commutative,
we have only to prove that the following diagram is commutative:

(10.3.3)

\begin{CD}{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{{\rm ex}}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}{}^{{\rm ex}}/S(T)^{\natural}}@>{N_{\rm zar}}>{}>{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{{\rm ex}}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}{}^{{\rm ex}}/S(T)^{\natural}}\\
@A{\simeq}A{}A@A{}A{\simeq}A\\
{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{{\rm ex}}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}{}^{{\rm ex}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle\langle u\rangle\rangle @>{-D}>{}>{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{{\rm ex}}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}{}^{{\rm ex}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle\langle u\rangle\rangle.\end{CD}

(Here note that to take the completion is important in the following argument.)
The problem is local.
To prove this commutativity, take a local section

\omega_{0}
in

{\rm Ker}(\nabla_{/S(T)^{\natural}}\colon{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{{\rm ex}}}}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal Q}{}^{{\rm ex}}/S(T)^{\natural}}\longrightarrow{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{{\rm ex}}}}\Omega^{i+1}_{{\cal Q}{}^{{\rm ex}}/S(T)^{\natural}})\quad(i\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}).

Let
\widetilde{\omega}_{0}
be a lift of
\omega_{0}
in

{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{{\rm ex}}}}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal Q}{}^{{\rm ex}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
.
Then
N_{\rm zar}(\omega_{0})=\eta
, where

\eta
is a local section of

{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{{\rm ex}}}}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal Q}{}^{{\rm ex}}/S(T)^{\natural}}

such that

(10.3.4)

d\log t\wedge\eta=\nabla(\widetilde{\omega}_{0}).

We would like to construct a local section

\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}u^{[j]}\widetilde{\omega}_{j}
of

{\rm Ker}({\cal O}_{T}\langle\langle u\rangle\rangle\otimes_{{\cal O}_{T}}{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{{\rm ex}}}}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal Q}{}^{{\rm ex}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{T}\langle\langle u\rangle\rangle\otimes_{{\cal O}_{T}}{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{{\rm ex}}}}\Omega^{i+1}_{{\cal Q}{}^{{\rm ex}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}).

Consider the following equation

\displaystyle 0=\nabla(\sum_{j\geq 0}u^{[j]}\widetilde{\omega}_{j})=\nabla(\widetilde{\omega}_{0})+\sum_{j\geq 1}(d\log t\wedge u^{[j-1]}\widetilde{\omega}_{j}+u^{[j]}\nabla(\widetilde{\omega}_{j})).

This is equivalent to the following equation

(10.3.5)

d\log t\wedge\widetilde{\omega}_{j+1}+\nabla(\widetilde{\omega}_{j})=0\quad(j\geq 0).

We would like to find
\widetilde{\omega}_{j}

(j\geq 1)
by using induction on
j
.
Indeed, assume that
\widetilde{\omega}_{i}

(i\leq j)
exists.
Then
\nabla(d\log t\wedge\widetilde{\omega}_{j}+\nabla(\widetilde{\omega}_{j-1}))=0
.
Hence
d\log t\wedge\nabla(\widetilde{\omega}_{j})=0
since
\nabla^{2}=0
.
This means the existence of
\widetilde{\omega}_{j+1}
in (
10.3.5
) by (
4.3.2
).
Let
\omega_{1}
be the image of
\widetilde{\omega}_{1}
in

{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{{\rm ex}}}}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal Q}{}^{{\rm ex}}/S(T)^{\natural}}
.
Then, by the relations (
10.3.4
) and (
10.3.5
),
we have
\omega_{1}=-\eta
because

d\log t\wedge\colon{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{{\rm ex}}}}\Omega^{i-1}_{{\cal Q}{}^{{\rm ex}}/S(T)^{\natural}}\longrightarrow{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{{\rm ex}}}}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}}

is injective.
On the other hand,

(-d/du)(\sum_{j\geq 0}u^{[j]}\widetilde{\omega}_{j})=-\sum_{j\geq 1}u^{[j-1]}\widetilde{\omega}_{j}
whose image in

{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{{\rm ex}}}}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal Q}{}^{{\rm ex}}/S(T)^{\natural}}
is
-\omega_{1}=\eta
.
This tells us that the diagram (
10.3.3
) is commutative.
∎

Remark 10.4
.

In the proof of
[
KH
, Lemma 7]
the existence of the cocycle

a_{0}+a_{1}u^{[1]}+\cdots\in R\Gamma(W\widetilde{\omega})[u]
is unclear.

Let
X/S
be an SNCL scheme.
Let
E
be flat quasi-coherent crystals of

{\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
-modules.
Then
\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(E)
is a flat locally nilpotent quasi-coherent

{\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
-modules.

Corollary 10.5
.

The following diagram is commutative:

(10.5.1)

\begin{CD}Ru_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}(E))@>{N_{\rm zar}}>{}>Ru_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}(E))\\
@A{\simeq}A{}A@A{}A{\simeq}A\\
\widetilde{R}u_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(E)\langle u\rangle)@>{-D}>{}>\widetilde{R}u_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(E)\langle u\rangle)\\
@V{\simeq}V{}V@V{}V{\simeq}V\\
\widetilde{R}u_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(E^{(\star)})\langle u\rangle)@>{-D}>{}>\widetilde{R}u_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(E^{\star})\langle u\rangle).\end{CD}

Proof.

The commutativity of the upper square is a special case of (
10.3.1
).
The commutativity of the lower square is obvious.
∎

Definition 10.6
.

We call the morphisms

\displaystyle-D\colon\widetilde{R}u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(F\langle u\rangle)\longrightarrow\widetilde{R}u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(F\langle u\rangle)

and

\displaystyle-D\colon\widetilde{R}u_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(E^{(\star)})\langle u\rangle)\longrightarrow\widetilde{R}u_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(E^{\star})\langle u\rangle)

the
monodromy operators
of

\widetilde{R}u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(F\langle u\rangle)

and

\widetilde{R}u_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(E^{(\star)})\langle u\rangle)
,
respectively and we denote them by
N_{\rm zar}
.

Proposition 10.7
.

The monodromy operators

N_{\rm zar}\colon\widetilde{R}u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(F\langle u\rangle)\longrightarrow\widetilde{R}u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(F\langle u\rangle)

and

N_{\rm zar}\colon\widetilde{R}u_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(E^{(\star)})\langle u\rangle)\longrightarrow\widetilde{R}u_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(E^{(\star)})\langle u\rangle)

induce the following morphisms, respectively
:

(10.7.1)

\displaystyle N_{\rm zar}\colon P_{k}\widetilde{R}u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(F\langle u\rangle)\longrightarrow P_{k-2}\widetilde{R}u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(F\langle u\rangle),

(10.7.2)

\displaystyle N_{\rm zar}\colon P_{k}\widetilde{R}u_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(E^{(\star)})\langle u\rangle)\longrightarrow P_{k-2}\widetilde{R}u_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(E^{(\star)})\langle u\rangle).

Proof.

These are obvious by the definitions of
N_{\rm zar}
and the local definition of

P
in (
5.12.2
).
∎

11
Cup products of PD-Hirsch pre-weight-filtered
log crystalline complexes

In this section we define the cup product of

(H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E),P)

and give several fundamental properties of it.

Let the notations be as in the previous section.
First let us recall the cup product of log crystalline complexes.

Let
\overline{F}{}^{\prime}
be another flat quasi-coherent crystal of

{\cal O}_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
-modules.
Let

{\cal F}^{\bullet}{}^{\prime}
be the analogous sheaf to

{\cal F}^{\bullet}
for
\overline{F}{}^{\prime}
.
The wedge product

\wedge\colon\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{T}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural}}\longrightarrow\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural}}

induces the following morphism

(11.0.1)

({\cal F}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural}})\otimes_{{\cal O}_{T}}({\cal F}^{\prime}{}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural}})\longrightarrow({\cal F}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}}{\cal F}^{\prime}{}^{\bullet})\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural}}.

Assume that
Ru_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(\overline{F}))
is bounded above.
Then this morphism induces the following cup product

(11.0.2)

\displaystyle\cup\colon

\displaystyle Ru_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(\overline{F}))\otimes^{L}_{{\cal O}_{T}}Ru_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(\overline{F}{}^{\prime}))

\displaystyle\longrightarrow Ru_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}((\epsilon^{*}_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(\overline{F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}}\overline{F}{}^{\prime})).

Similarly the wedge product

\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{T}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\longrightarrow\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}

induces the following morphism

(11.0.3)

({\cal F}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}})\otimes_{{\cal O}_{T}}({\cal F}^{\prime}{}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}})\longrightarrow({\cal F}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}}{\cal F}^{\prime}{}^{\bullet})\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}

Assume that
\widetilde{R}u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\overline{F})
is bounded above.
Then this morphism induces the following cup product

(11.0.4)

\displaystyle\cup\colon

\displaystyle\widetilde{R}u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\overline{F})\otimes^{L}_{{\cal O}_{T}}\widetilde{R}u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\overline{F}{}^{\prime})\longrightarrow\widetilde{R}u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\overline{F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}}\overline{F}{}^{\prime}).

By considering the morphism (
6.6.7
),
we have the following morphism

(11.0.5)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{{\cal O}_{T}}(U_{S(T)^{\natural}})\otimes_{{\cal O}_{T}}\Gamma_{{\cal O}_{T}}(U_{S(T)^{\natural}})\longrightarrow\Gamma_{{\cal O}_{T}}(U_{S(T)^{\natural}}).

The morphisms (
11.0.3
) and (
6.6.7
)
induce the following morphism

(11.0.6)

\displaystyle\cup\colon\widetilde{R}u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\overline{F}\langle u\rangle)\otimes^{L}_{{\cal O}_{T}}\widetilde{R}u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\overline{F}{}^{\prime}\langle u\rangle)\longrightarrow\widetilde{R}u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}((\overline{F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}}\overline{F}{}^{\prime})\langle u\rangle).

Here note that

\widetilde{R}u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\overline{F}\langle u\rangle)

is bounded above by (
4.1.8
) and hence
\otimes^{L}_{{\cal O}_{T}}
in
(
11.0.6
) is well-defined.

Proposition 11.1
.

(11.1.1)

\displaystyle N_{\rm zar}\circ(~\cup~)=N_{\rm zar}\cup{\rm id}+{\rm id}\cup N_{\rm zar}

on

\widetilde{R}u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\overline{F}\langle u\rangle)\otimes^{L}_{g^{-1}({\cal O}_{T})}\widetilde{R}u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\overline{F}{}^{\prime}\langle u\rangle)
.

Proof.

The problem is local.
By (
10.3
) it suffices to prove that

(11.1.2)

-d/du(\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime})=(-d/du(\omega))\wedge\omega^{\prime}+\omega\wedge(-d/du(\omega^{\prime}))

for local sections

\omega\in s({\cal O}_{T}\langle u\rangle\otimes_{{\cal O}_{T}}{\cal F}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}})

and

\omega^{\prime}\in s({\cal O}_{T}\langle u\rangle\otimes_{{\cal O}_{T}}{\cal F}^{\prime}{}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}})
.
This immediately follows from the following elementary formula

\dfrac{(i+i^{\prime}-1)!}{(i-1)!i^{\prime}!}+\dfrac{(i+i^{\prime}-1)!}{i!(i-1)^{\prime}!}=\dfrac{(i+i^{\prime})!}{i!i^{\prime}!}\quad(i,i^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}).

∎

Proposition 11.2
.

Assume that
\widetilde{R}u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\overline{F})
and

\widetilde{R}u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\overline{F}{}^{\prime})
are bounded above.
Let
\overline{F}{}^{\prime\prime}
be an analogous sheaf to
\overline{F}{}^{\prime}
.
Then

(11.2.1)

\displaystyle(?\cup?)\cup?=?\cup(?\cup?)

on

\widetilde{R}u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\overline{F}\langle u\rangle)\otimes^{L}_{g^{-1}({\cal O}_{T})}\widetilde{R}u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\overline{F}{}^{\prime}\langle u\rangle)\otimes^{L}_{g^{-1}({\cal O}_{T})}\widetilde{R}u_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\overline{F}{}^{\prime\prime}\langle u\rangle)
.

Proof.

The problem is local. Let
{\cal F}^{\prime\prime}{}^{\bullet}
be an analogous sheaf to
{\cal F}^{\prime\bullet}
.
It suffices to prove that

(11.2.2)

(\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime})\wedge\omega^{\prime\prime}=\omega\wedge(\omega^{\prime}\wedge\omega^{\prime\prime})

for
\omega\in{\cal O}_{T}\langle u\rangle\otimes_{{\cal O}_{T}}{\cal F}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
,

\omega^{\prime}\in{\cal O}_{T}\langle u\rangle\otimes_{{\cal O}_{T}}{\cal F}^{\prime}{}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}

and

\omega^{\prime\prime}\in{\cal O}_{T}\langle u\rangle\otimes_{{\cal O}_{T}}{\cal F}^{\prime\prime}{}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}{}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
.
This is obvious.
∎

Let us define the cup product of

(H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E),P)
as in
the cup product on the cohomology of singular cochains in topology:
the cup product using the Alexander-Whitney map.
Fujisawa’s explanation
[
F2
, (2.9), (2.10), (2.11)]
is convenient for us.

Let
E^{\prime}
be an analogous crystal of

{\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}}
-modules to
E
.
First note that
(H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E),P)
is bounded above
since
\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}
is quasi-compact ((
6.20
)).
Next take an element
\underline{\lambda}=\{\lambda_{0},\ldots,\lambda_{l+l^{\prime}}\}\in P(\Lambda)

(\lambda_{0}<\cdots<\lambda_{l+l^{\prime}})
.
Then we have exact closed immersions

\iota:{\cal P}_{(\lambda_{0},\ldots,\lambda_{l+l^{\prime}})}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal P}_{(\lambda_{0},\ldots,\lambda_{l})}

and

\iota^{\prime}\colon{\cal P}_{(\lambda_{0},\ldots,\lambda_{l+l^{\prime}})}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal P}_{(\lambda_{l},\ldots,\lambda_{l+l^{\prime}})}
.

The morphism

(11.2.3)

\Omega^{i}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{(\lambda_{0},\ldots,\lambda_{l})\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{T}}\Omega^{i^{\prime}}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{(\lambda_{l},\ldots,\lambda_{l+l^{\prime}})\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\owns\omega\otimes\omega^{\prime}\longmapsto(-1)^{il^{\prime}}\iota^{*}(\omega)\wedge\iota^{\prime}{}^{*}(\omega^{\prime})\in\Omega^{i+i^{\prime}}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{(\lambda_{0},\ldots,\lambda_{l+l^{\prime}})\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}

induces the following natural morphism

(11.2.4)

({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},{(l)}}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural}})\otimes_{{\cal O}_{T}}({\cal E}^{\prime}{}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{i^{\prime}}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},{(l^{\prime})}}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural}})\longrightarrow({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet}}}{\cal E}{}^{\prime\bullet})\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{i+i^{\prime}}_{{\cal P}_{\bullet}^{{\rm ex},{(l+l^{\prime})}}/S(T)^{\natural}}.

Here the sign
(-1)^{il^{\prime}}
arises naturally from our convention on the boundary morphisms
in (
4.16.2
) in order that
the morphism (
11.2.5
) below is a morphism of complexes.
(We leave the reader to check that
the morphism (
11.2.5
) is indeed a morphism of complexes,
which is slightly tedious.)
This induces the following morphism by
[
F2
, (2.10)]

(11.2.5)

s({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}_{\bullet}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}_{\bullet}^{{\rm ex},{(\bullet)}}/S(T)^{\natural}})\otimes_{{\cal O}_{T}}s({\cal E}^{\prime}{}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}_{\bullet}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}_{\bullet}^{{\rm ex},{(\bullet)}}/S(T)^{\natural}})\longrightarrow s({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet}}}{\cal E}{}^{\prime\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}_{\bullet}^{{\rm ex},{(\bullet)}}/S(T)^{\natural}}).

The morphisms (
11.2.5
) and (
11.0.5
) induce the following morphism

(11.2.6)

\displaystyle s({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle)\otimes s({\cal E}{}^{\prime\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow s({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet}}}{\cal E}{}^{\prime\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle).

By the definitions (
5.12.2
) and (
6.8
) (5) (cf.
[
F2
, (2.12)]
),
this morphism induces the following filtered morphism

(11.2.7)

\displaystyle(s({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle),P)\otimes(s({\cal E}{}^{\prime\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle),P)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow(s({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet}}}{\cal E}{}^{\prime\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle),P).

The morphism (
11.2.7
) induces the following morphism

(11.2.8)

\displaystyle(H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E),P)\otimes^{L}_{f^{-1}({\cal O}_{T})}(H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E^{\prime}),P)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow(H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}/T}}E^{\prime}),P).

Definition 11.3
.

We call the morphism (

)
the

of
(H_{\rm zar},P)

and we denote it by

.

Proposition 11.4
.

The following diagram is commutative
:

(11.4.1)

\begin{CD}Ru_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(\epsilon_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}^{*}(E))\otimes^{L}_{f^{-1}({\cal O}_{T})}Ru_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(\epsilon_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}^{*}(E^{\prime}))@>{\cup}>{}>\\
@A{\simeq}A{}A\\
\widetilde{R}u_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\epsilon_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}^{*}(E)\langle u\rangle)\otimes^{L}_{f^{-1}({\cal O}_{T})}\widetilde{R}u_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\epsilon_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}^{*}(E^{\prime})\langle u\rangle)@>{\cup}>{}>\\
@V{\simeq}V{}V\\
H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E)\otimes^{L}_{f^{-1}({\cal O}_{T})}H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E^{\prime})@>{\cup}>{}>\end{CD}

\begin{CD}Ru_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(\epsilon_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}^{*}(E\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}}E^{\prime}))\\
@A{}A{\simeq}A\\
\widetilde{R}u_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\epsilon_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}^{*}(E\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}}E^{\prime})\langle u\rangle)\\
@V{}V{\simeq}V\\
H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}}E^{\prime}).\end{CD}

Proof.

This is obvious by the constructions of the morphisms (
6.6.3
) and
(
11.2.8
).
∎

Proposition 11.5
.

Assume that
\overset{\circ}{X}
is quasi-compact.

\displaystyle N_{\rm zar}\circ(~\cup~)=N_{\rm zar}\cup{\rm id}+{\rm id}\cup N_{\rm zar}

Proposition 11.6
.

\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}

.

\displaystyle(?\cup?)\cup?=?\cup(?\cup?)

.

Let
q
and
q^{\prime}
be integers.
Then the cup product

(11.6.2)

\displaystyle\cup\colon

\displaystyle R^{q}f_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(\epsilon_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}^{*}(E))\otimes_{{\cal O}_{T}}R^{q^{\prime}}f_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(\epsilon_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}^{*}(E^{\prime}))

\displaystyle\longrightarrow R^{q+q^{\prime}}f_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(\epsilon_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}^{*}(E\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}}E^{\prime}))

is compatible with

’s.
That is, the morphism above induces the following morphism

\displaystyle\cup\colon

\displaystyle P_{k}R^{q}f_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(\epsilon_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}^{*}(E))\otimes_{{\cal O}_{T}}P_{k^{\prime}}R^{q^{\prime}}f_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(\epsilon_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}^{*}(E^{\prime}))

\displaystyle\longrightarrow P_{k+k^{\prime}}R^{q+q^{\prime}}f_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(\epsilon_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}^{*}(E\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}}E^{\prime})).

12
Infinitesimal deformation invariance of Hirsch weight-filtered log crystalline complexes
and Hirsch weight-filtered log crystalline dga’s

In this section we prove the infinitesimal deformation invariance
of the pull-back of a morphism of SNCL schemes on filtered Hirsch extensions.
As in
[
BO2
]
, to prove the invariance, we use Dwork’s trick for enlarging
the radius of convergence of log
F
-isocrystals by the use of the relative Frobenius.
Precisely speaking, in our case as in
[
Nak4
]
,
we use the base change by the iteration of the
abrelative

Frobenius morphism (not (usual) relative Frobenius morphism) of the base scheme.

For a filtered complex
(K,P)
in the derived category of filtered complexes
(
[
NS
]
), denote
(K,P)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}^{L}{\mathbb{Q}}
by
(K,P)_{\mathbb{Q}}

for simplicity of notation.

The following is a main result in this section.

Theorem 12.1

(
Infinitesimal deformation invariance of the pull-back of a morphism on
Hirsch weight-filtered log crystalline complexes).

Let
\star
be nothing or
\prime
.
Let
n
be a positive integer.
Let
S^{\star}
be a family of log points.
Assume that
S^{\star}
is of characteristic
p>0
.
Let
F_{S^{\star}}\colon S^{\star}\longrightarrow S^{\star}
be the absolute Frobenius endomorphism.
Set
S^{{\star}[p^{n}]}:=S^{\star}\times_{\overset{\circ}{S}{}^{\star},\overset{\circ}{F}{}^{n}_{S^{\star}}}\overset{\circ}{S}{}^{\star}
.
Let
(T^{\star},{\cal J}^{\star},\delta^{\star})
be
a log
p
-adic formal PD-thickening of
S^{\star}
.
Set
T^{\star}_{0}:=T^{\star}~{\rm mod}~{\cal J}^{\star}
.
Let
f^{\star}\colon X^{\star}\longrightarrow S^{\star}
be an SNCL scheme over
S^{\star}
.
Assume that
\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{\star}_{T_{0}}
is quasi-compact.
Let
\iota^{\star}\colon T^{\star}_{0}(0)\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}T^{\star}_{0}

be an exact closed nilpotent immersion.
Set
S^{\star}_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\star}_{0}}:=S^{\star}\times_{\overset{\circ}{S}{}^{\star}}\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\star}_{0}

and

S^{\star}_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\star}_{0}(0)}:=S^{\star}\times_{\overset{\circ}{S}{}^{\star}}\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\star}_{0}(0)

and
X^{\star}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}:=X^{\star}\times_{S^{\star}}S^{\star}_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\star}_{0}}
,

X^{\star}_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\star}_{0}}(0):=X^{\star}\times_{S^{\star}}S^{\star}_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\star}_{0}(0)}
.
Set
X^{\star}{}^{[p^{n}]}:=X^{\star}\times_{S^{\star}}S^{{\star}[p^{n}]}

and
X^{\star}{}^{[p^{n}]}_{\!\!\!\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}:=X^{\star}{}^{[p^{n}]}\times_{S^{{\star}[p^{n}]}}S^{{\star}[p^{n}]}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}
.

(
Note that the underlying scheme of

X^{\star}{}^{[p^{n}]}_{\!\!\!\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}
is equal to that of

X^{\star}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}
and that we have the log scheme

S^{{\star}[p^{n}]}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}
by using the composite morphism

T_{0}\longrightarrow S^{\star}\longrightarrow S^{\star[p^{n}]}
,
where
the morphism
S^{\star}\longrightarrow S^{\star[p^{n}]}
is the composite of
the abrelative Frobenius morphisms of
S^{[p^{m}]\star}

(0\leq m\leq n-1)

((
8.6
)))
.
Let
n
be a positive integer such that the pull-back morphism

F^{n*}_{T^{\star}_{0}}\colon{\cal O}_{T^{\star}_{0}}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{T^{\star}_{0}}

kills
{\rm Ker}({\cal O}_{T^{\star}_{0}}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{T^{\star}_{0}(0)})
.
Here
S^{\star[p^{n}]}(T^{\star})
is defined by
the composite morphism
T_{0}\longrightarrow S^{\star}\longrightarrow S^{\star[p^{n}]}
.
Let

(12.1.1)

\displaystyle g_{0}\colon X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}(0)\longrightarrow X^{\prime}_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}_{0}}(0)

be a morphism of log schemes over
S(T)^{\natural}\longrightarrow S^{\prime}(T^{\prime})^{\natural}

satisfying the conditions
(8.1.6)
and
(8.2.6)

for

X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}(0)
and
X^{\prime}_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}_{0}}(0)
.
Set
X^{\star}_{T^{\star}_{0}}:=X^{\star}\times_{S^{\star}}T^{\star}_{0}
.
Then the following hold
:

(1)
There exist a canonical filtered morphism

(12.1.2)

\displaystyle g^{*}_{0}

\displaystyle:(H_{\rm zar}(X^{\prime}_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}_{0}}/S^{\prime}(T^{\prime})^{\natural}),P)_{\mathbb{Q}}\longrightarrow Rg_{0*}((H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}),P)_{\mathbb{Q}}).

and a canonical morphism

(12.1.3)

\displaystyle g^{*}_{0}\colon Ru_{X^{\prime}{}^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}_{0}}/S^{\prime}(T^{\prime})^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{X^{\prime}{}^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}_{0}}/S^{\prime}(T^{\prime})^{\natural}})_{\mathbb{Q}}\longrightarrow Rg_{0*}(Ru_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}})_{\mathbb{Q}})

fitting into the following commutative diagram

(12.1.4)

\begin{CD}H_{\rm zar}(X^{\prime}_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}_{0}}/S^{\prime}(T^{\prime})^{\natural})_{\mathbb{Q}}@>{g^{*}_{0}}>{}>Rg_{0*}((H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}))_{\mathbb{Q}})\\
@V{\simeq}V{}V@V{}V{\simeq}V\\
Ru_{X^{\prime}{}^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}_{0}}/S^{\prime}(T^{\prime})^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{X^{\prime(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}_{0}}/S^{\prime}(T^{\prime})^{\natural}})_{\mathbb{Q}}@>{g^{*}_{0}}>{}>Rg_{0*}Ru_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}})_{\mathbb{Q}}\\
@A{\simeq}A{}A@A{}A{\simeq}A\\
Ru_{X^{\prime}_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}_{0}}/S^{\prime}(T^{\prime})^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{X^{\prime}_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}_{0}}/S^{\prime}(T^{\prime})^{\natural}})_{\mathbb{Q}}@>{g^{*}_{0}}>{}>Rg_{0*}Ru_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}})_{\mathbb{Q}}.\end{CD}

Here the last horizontal morphism
g_{0}^{*}
is the morphism constructed in

[
Nak4
, (5.3.1)]
.

(2)
Let
S^{\prime\prime}
,
(T^{\prime\prime},{\cal J}^{\prime\prime},\delta^{\prime\prime})
and
\iota^{\prime\prime}\colon T^{\prime\prime}_{0}(0)\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}T^{\prime\prime}_{0}

be analogous objects to

S^{\prime}
,
(T^{\prime},{\cal J}^{\prime},\delta^{\prime})
and
\iota^{\prime}\colon T^{\prime}_{0}(0)\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}T^{\prime}_{0}
, respectively.
Let
g^{\prime}_{0}\colon X^{\prime}_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}_{0}}(0)\longrightarrow X^{\prime\prime}_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime\prime}_{0}}(0)
be
a similar morphism to
g_{0}
.
Then

(12.1.5)

\displaystyle(g^{\prime}_{0}\circ g_{0})^{*}=Rg^{\prime}_{0*}(g^{*}_{0})\circ g^{\prime}{}^{*}_{\!\!0}\colon Ru_{X^{\prime\prime}_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime\prime}_{0}}/S^{\prime\prime}(T^{\prime\prime})^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{X^{\prime\prime}_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime\prime}_{0}}/S^{\prime\prime}(T^{\prime\prime})^{\natural}})_{\mathbb{Q}}\longrightarrow R(g^{\prime}_{0}\circ g_{0})_{*}Ru_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}})_{\mathbb{Q}}.

(3)

(12.1.6)

\displaystyle{\rm id}^{*}_{X_{T^{\star}_{0}}(0)}={\rm id}_{(H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural})_{\mathbb{Q}},P)}.

(4)
If
g_{0}
has a lift
g\colon X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}\longrightarrow X^{\prime}_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}_{0}}
over

S_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}\longrightarrow S^{\prime}_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}_{0}}
satisfying the conditions
(8.1.6)
and
(8.2.6)
,
then
g^{*}_{0}
in
(\ref{eqn:ldefinvn})
is equal to the induced morphism by
g^{*}

in
(\ref{ali:ccm})

in
E={\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}

and

F={\cal O}_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}_{0}}/S^{\prime}(T^{\prime})^{\natural}}
.

Proof.

(Though the proof of this theorem is the same as that of
[
Nak4
, (5.3.1)]
,
we give it for the completeness of this book.)

(1): Because the exact closed immersion

\overset{\circ}{\iota}\colon\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\star}_{0}(0)\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\star}_{0}
is nilpotent and because

F^{n*}_{T^{\star}_{0}}\colon{\cal O}_{T^{\star}_{0}}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{T^{\star}_{0}}

kills
{\rm Ker}({\cal O}_{T^{\star}_{0}}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{T^{\star}_{0}}(0))
,
we see that there exists a morphism

\overset{\circ}{\rho}{}^{(n)\star}\colon\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\star}_{0}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\star}_{0}(0)

such that
\overset{\circ}{\rho}{}^{(n)\star}\circ\overset{\circ}{\iota}=\overset{\circ}{F}{}^{n}_{T^{\star}_{0}(0)}

and
\overset{\circ}{\iota}\circ\overset{\circ}{\rho}{}^{(n)\star}=\overset{\circ}{F}{}^{n}_{T^{\star}_{0}}
.
Hence

(12.1.7)

\displaystyle X^{\star}{}^{[p^{n}]}_{\!\!\!\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\star}_{0}}

\displaystyle=X^{\star}\times_{\overset{\circ}{S}{}^{\star},\overset{\circ}{F}{}^{n}_{S^{\star}}}\overset{\circ}{S}{}^{\star}\times_{\overset{\circ}{S}{}^{\star}}\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\star}_{0}=X^{\star}_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\star}_{0}}\times_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\star}_{0},\overset{\circ}{F}{}^{n}_{T^{\star}_{0}}}\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\star}_{0}=X^{\star}_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\star}_{0}}\times_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\star}_{0},\overset{\circ}{\iota}\circ\overset{\circ}{\rho}{}^{(n)\star}}\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\star}_{0}

\displaystyle=X^{\star}_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\star}_{0}}(0)\times_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\star}_{0}(0),\overset{\circ}{\rho}{}^{(n)\star}}\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\star}_{0}.

Consider the filtered complex

(H_{\rm zar}(X^{\star[p^{n}]}_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\star}_{0}}/S^{\star[p^{n}]}(T^{\star})^{\natural}),P)_{\mathbb{Q}}
.
Because we are given the morphism

g_{0}\colon X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}(0)\longrightarrow X^{\prime}_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}_{0}}(0)
,
we have the following base change morphism

\displaystyle g^{[p^{n}]}_{0}\colon X^{[p^{n}]}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}=X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}(0)\times_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}(0),\overset{\circ}{\rho}{}^{(n)}}\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\longrightarrow X^{\prime}_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}_{0}}(0)\times_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}_{0}(0),\overset{\circ}{\rho}{}^{(n)^{\prime}}}\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}_{0}=X^{\prime}{}^{[p^{n}]}_{\!\!\!\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}_{0}}.

Because
g_{0}
satisfies the condition (8.1.6),
the morphism
g^{[p^{n}]}_{0}
also satisfies it.
Hence we have the pull-back morphism by the functoriality of

(H_{\rm zar},P)
((
8.2.1
)):

(12.1.8)

\displaystyle(H_{\rm zar}(X^{\prime}{}^{[p^{n}]}_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}_{0}}/S^{\prime}{}^{[p^{n}]}(T^{\prime})^{\natural}),P)_{\mathbb{Q}}\longrightarrow Rg^{[p^{n}]}_{0*}((H_{\rm zar}(X^{[p^{n}]}_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}_{0}}/S^{[p^{n}]}(T)^{\natural}),P)_{\mathbb{Q}}).

The abrelative Frobenius morphism

F^{{\rm ar},n}_{X^{\star}_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\star}_{0}}/S^{\star}(T^{\star})^{\natural}}\colon X^{\star}_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\star}_{0}}\longrightarrow X^{\star[p^{n}]}_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\star}_{0}}

over the morphism
S^{\star}(T^{\star})^{\natural}\longrightarrow S^{\star[p^{n}]}(T^{\star})^{\natural}

induces an isomorphism

\displaystyle F^{{\rm ar},n*}_{X^{\star}_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\star}_{0}}/S^{\star}(T^{\star})^{\natural}}\colon

\displaystyle(H_{\rm zar}(X^{\star[p^{n}]}_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\star}_{0}}/S^{\star[p^{n}]}(T^{\star})^{\natural}),P)_{\mathbb{Q}}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}(H_{\rm zar}(X^{\star}_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\star}_{0}}/S^{\star}(T^{\star})^{\natural}),P)_{\mathbb{Q}}

by (
8.8.1
) because the base change over
\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\star}
of
the abrelative Frobenius morphism induces an isomorphism of
the classical isocrystalline complex of a smooth scheme
(
[
HK
, (2.24)]
, cf.
[
BO2
, (1.3)]
).
Here, as in
[
HK
, (2.24)]
, we identity

(X^{\star}{}^{[p^{n}]}_{\!\!\!\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\star}_{0}})_{\rm zar}

with
(X^{\star}_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\star}_{0}})_{\rm zar}

via the canonical equivalence and we have used the assumption that

g_{0}
satisfies the condition (8.2.6).
Consequently we obtain the following diagram:

(12.1.9)

\begin{CD}(H_{\rm zar}(X^{\prime}{}^{[p^{n}]}_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}_{0}}/S^{{}^{\prime}[p^{n}]}(T^{\prime})^{\natural}),P)_{\mathbb{Q}}@>{g^{[p^{n}]*}_{0}}>{}>Rg^{[p^{n}]}_{0*}((H_{\rm zar}(X^{[p^{n}]}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S^{[p^{n}]}(T)^{\natural}),P)_{\mathbb{Q}}))\\
@V{\simeq}V{F^{{\rm ar},n*}_{X^{\prime}{}_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}_{0}}/S^{\prime}(T^{\prime})^{\natural}}}V@V{\simeq}V{F^{{\rm ar},n*}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}}V\\
(H_{\rm zar}(X^{\prime}_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}_{0}}/S^{\prime}(T^{\prime})^{\natural}),P)_{\mathbb{Q}}(H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}),P)_{\mathbb{Q}}.\end{CD}

This diagram gives us the morphism
g^{*}_{0}
in (
12.1.2
):

g^{*}_{0}:=F^{{\rm ar},n*}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}\circ g^{[p^{n}]*}_{0}\circ(F^{{\rm ar},n*}_{X^{\prime}{}_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}_{0}}/S^{\prime}(T^{\prime})^{\natural}})^{-1}.

This morphism is independent of the choice of
n
by
the transitive relation of the pull-back morphism ((
8.2.1
)).
Similarly we have the middle morphism
g^{*}_{0}

(and even the lower morphism
g^{*}_{0}
) in (
12.1.4
).
By the contravariant functoriality
(
8.2.4
) of the isomorphisms (
6.13.1
) and (
6.11.1
),
we obtain the commutative diagram
(
12.1.4
).

(2): By the transitive relation of the pull-back morphism again,

g^{*}_{0}
and
g^{\prime}{}^{*}_{\!\!0}

are compatible with the composition of

g_{0}
and
g^{\prime}_{0}
.

(3): The formula (
12.1.6
) immediately follows from
the definition of
g^{*}_{0}
.

(4): Assume that
g_{0}
has the lift
g
in the statement of this theorem.
Then we have the relation
g^{*}_{0}=g^{*}
by
the following commutative diagram, the transitivity (
8.2.1
) and
the diagram (
12.1.9
):

\begin{CD}X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}@>{g}>{}>X^{\prime}_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}_{0}}\\
@V{F^{{\rm ar},n}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}}V{}V@V{}V{F^{{\rm ar},n}_{X^{\prime}{}_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}_{0}}/S^{\prime}(T^{\prime})^{\natural}}}V\\
X^{[p^{n}]}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}@>{g_{0}^{[p^{n}]}}>{}>X^{\prime}{}^{[p^{n}]}_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}_{0}}.\end{CD}

∎

Corollary 12.2

(
Infinitesimal deformation invariance of
Hirsch weight-filtered log crystalline complexes).

If
S^{\prime}=S
,
T^{\prime}=T
and
X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}(0)=X^{\prime}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}(0)
, then

(12.2.1)

\displaystyle(H_{\rm zar}(X^{\prime}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}),P)_{\mathbb{Q}}=(H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}),P)_{\mathbb{Q}}.

Corollary 12.3

(
Infinitesimal deformation invariance of log isocrystalline
cohomologies with weight filtrations).

Let the notations be as in
(
12.2
)
.
Let
P
be the induced filtrations on

R^{q}f_{X^{\prime}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{X^{\prime}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}})_{\mathbb{Q}}
and

R^{q}f_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}})_{\mathbb{Q}}
by

(H_{\rm zar}(X^{\prime}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}),P)_{\mathbb{Q}}

and

(H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}),P)_{\mathbb{Q}}
,
respectively.
Then

(12.3.1)

\displaystyle(R^{q}f_{X^{\prime}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{X^{\prime}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}})_{\mathbb{Q}},P)

\displaystyle=(R^{q}f_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}})_{\mathbb{Q}},P).

Moreover, if
T
is restrictively hollow
(
[
Nak4
, (1.1.38)]
)
, that is,
if
S(T)
is hollow,
then

(12.3.2)

\displaystyle(R^{q}f_{X^{\prime}_{T_{0}}/T*}({\cal O}_{X^{\prime}_{T_{0}}/T})_{\mathbb{Q}},P)

\displaystyle=(R^{q}f_{X_{T_{0}}/T*}({\cal O}_{X_{T_{0}}/T})_{\mathbb{Q}},P).

Remark 12.4
.

The analogues of (
12.1
) and (
12.2
) hold
for
(H_{{\rm zar},{\rm TW}},P)
hold.

13
The
E_{2}
-degeneration of the
p
-adic weight spectral sequence

Let
{\cal V}
be a complete discrete valuation ring with perfect residue field
\kappa

of mixed characteristics.
In this section we assume that the underlying scheme
\overset{\circ}{S}
of
the family of log points
S
is a
p
-adic
{\cal V}
-scheme in the sense
of
[
O1
]
. Let
X/S
be a proper SNCL scheme. Let

(T,z)
be a flat log
p
-adic enlargement (see e.g.,
[
O2
]
(or
[
Nak4
]
) for this notion);

z
is a morphism
T_{1}\longrightarrow S
,
where
T_{1}
is an exact log subscheme of
T
defined by
p{\cal O}_{T}
.
Endow
p{\cal O}_{T}
with the canonical PD-structure.
In this section we prove the
E_{2}
-degeneration of
the
p
-adic weight spectral sequence of

X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}
modulo torsion obtained by

(H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}),P)
by using
the infinitesimal deformation invariance of isocrystalline cohomologies
with weight filtrations in the previous section ((
12.3.1
)).

Theorem 13.1

(
E_{2}
-degeneration I).

Let
s
be the log point of a perfect field of characteristic
p>0
.
The spectral sequence
(\ref{ali:sparh})
for the case
S=s

and
E={\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}

degenerates at
E_{2}
.

Proof.

As in the proof of
[
Nak4
, (5.4.1)]
, we may assume that

T={\cal W}(s)
. In this case we have the absolute Frobenius endomorphism

F_{{\cal W}(s)}\colon{\cal W}(s)\longrightarrow{\cal W}(s)
.
If
\overset{\circ}{s}
is the spectrum of a finite field,
then the
E_{1}
-term

E^{-k,q+k}_{1}
of (
8.8.1
) is of pure weight of
q+k

by
[
KM
, Corollary 1. 2)]
,
[
CL
, (1.2)]
and
[
Nak1
, (2.2) (4)]
.
However see
[
NY
, (6.11)]
for the gap of the proof
the weak-Lefschetz conjecture
for a hypersurface of a large degree in
[
B1
]
;
I have filled the gap in
[
NY
, (6.10)]
.

The rest of the proof is the same as that of
[
Nak4
, (5.4.1)]

∎

Theorem 13.2

(
E_{2}
-degeneration II).

Let
T
be a log
p
-adic enlargement of
S/{\cal V}

with structural morphism
T_{1}\longrightarrow S
.
The spectral sequence
(\ref{ali:sparh})
modulo torsion
for the case
E={\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}

degenerates at
E_{2}
.

Proof.

Because the problem is local on
\overset{\circ}{T}
, we may assume that

\overset{\circ}{T}
is quasi-compact.
By virtue of (
13.1
) and (
12.2
),
the proof of this theorem is the same as that of
[
Nak4
, (5.4.3)]
.
∎

14
Convergence of the weight filtration

In this section we prove the log convergence of
the filtration on the log crystalline cohomology modulo torsions
induced by the spectral sequence (
8.8.1
) if
X/S
is proper.

Roughly speaking, we obtain all the results in this section
by using the log base change theorem of

(H_{\rm zar},P)
((
9.2
)) and by
replacing
(H_{{\rm zar},{\mathbb{Q}}},P)
defined in this book
with
(A_{{\rm zar},{\mathbb{Q}}},P)
in
[
Nak4
]
.
Here we have to note that the base change morphism
satisfy the conditions (8.1.6) and (8.2.6) as in the proof of (
9.2
).
For this reason, we omit or sketch the proofs of almost all the results in this section.
We use fundamental notions and results in
[
Nak4
, (5.2)]
.

Let
{\cal V}
be a complete discrete valuation ring of mixed characteristics

(0,p)
with perfect residue field. Let
K
be the fraction field of
{\cal V}
.
Set
B=({\rm Spf}({\cal V}),{\cal V}^{*})
.
Let
S
be a
p
-adic formal family of log points over
B

such that
\overset{\circ}{S}
is a
{\cal V}/p
-scheme.
Let
X/S
be a proper SNCL scheme.

Let
n
be a nonnegative integer.
Let
T
be an object of the category
{\cal E}^{\square}_{p,n}:={\rm Enl}^{\square}_{p}(S^{[p^{n}]}/{\cal V})

of (solid)
p
-adic enlargements of
S^{[p^{n}]}/{\cal V}

(
\square=
sld or nothing) (
[
Nak4
, (5.1.3)]
).
Then the hollowing out
S^{[p^{n}]}(T)^{\natural}
of
S^{[p^{n}]}(T)
is a formal family of log points.
Let
T_{i}\longrightarrow S^{[p^{n}]}

(i=0,1)
be the structural morphism.
Here
T_{1}:=\underline{\rm Spec}^{\log}_{T}({\cal O}_{T}/p)
and
T_{0}:=(T_{1})_{\rm red}
.
Set
X^{[p^{n}]}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{i}}:=X\times_{S}S^{[p^{n}]}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{i}}=X^{[p^{n}]}\times_{\overset{\circ}{S}}\overset{\circ}{T}_{i}
,
where
X^{[p^{n}]}:=X\times_{S}S^{[p^{n}]}
.
Let

f^{[p^{n}]}_{\overset{\circ}{T}}\colon X^{[p^{n}]}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{i}}\longrightarrow S^{[p^{n}]}(T)^{\natural}

be the structural morphism.
(Note that this notation is different from the notation

f
in §
6
since we add the symbol
\overset{\circ}{T}
to
f^{[p^{n}]}
as a subscript.)

Assume that we are given a flat coherent crystal

E_{n}=\{E_{n}(\overset{\circ}{T})\}_{T\in{\cal E}^{\square}_{p,n}}
of

{\cal O}_{\{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{[p^{n}]}_{T_{1}}/\overset{\circ}{T}\}_{T\in{\cal E}^{\square}_{p,n}}}
-modules.
Let
T
be an object of
{\cal E}^{\square}_{p,n}
.
Let
z\colon T_{1}\longrightarrow S^{[p^{n}]}
be the structural morphism.
Because
S^{[p^{n}]}(T)^{\natural}
is a
p
-adic formal family of log points,
we have a base change
X^{[p^{n}]}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S^{[p^{n}]}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}

of a proper SNCL scheme
with an exact PD-closed immersion

S^{[p^{n}]}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}S^{[p^{n}]}(T)^{\natural}
.
Then we obtain the following
p
-adic iso-zariskian filtered complex

(14.0.1)

\displaystyle(H_{\rm zar}(X^{[p^{n}]}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S^{[p^{n}]}(T)^{\natural},E(\overset{\circ}{T})),P)_{\mathbb{Q}}\in{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(f^{-1}_{\overset{\circ}{T}}({\cal K}_{T}))

for each
T\in{\cal E}^{\square}_{p,n}
.

Proposition 14.1

(
cf.
[
Nak4
, (5.2.2)]
).

Let
{\mathfrak{g}}\colon T^{\prime}\longrightarrow T
be a morphism in
{\cal E}^{\square}_{p,n}
.
Then the induced morphism

g\colon X^{[p^{n}]}_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}_{1}}\longrightarrow X^{[p^{n}]}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}

by
{\mathfrak{g}}

gives us the following natural morphism

(14.1.1)

\displaystyle g^{*}\colon

\displaystyle(H_{\rm zar}(X^{[p^{n}]}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S^{[p^{n}]}(T)^{\natural},E(\overset{\circ}{T})),P)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow Rg_{*}((H_{\rm zar}(X^{[p^{n}]}_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}_{1}}/S^{[p^{n}]}(T^{\prime})^{\natural},E(\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime})),P))

of filtered complexes in

{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(f_{\overset{\circ}{T}}^{-1}({\cal K}_{T}))

fitting into the following commutative diagram
:

\begin{CD}H_{\rm zar}(X^{[p^{n}]}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S^{[p^{n}]}(T)^{\natural},E(\overset{\circ}{T}))@>{g^{*}}>{}>\\
@A{\simeq}A{}A\\
Ru_{X^{[p^{n}]}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S^{[p^{n}]}(T)^{\natural}*}(\epsilon_{X^{[p^{n}]}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S^{[p^{n}]}(T)^{\natural}}(E(\overset{\circ}{T})))\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}@>{g^{*}}>{}>\end{CD}

\begin{CD}Rg_{*}((H_{\rm zar}(X^{[p^{n}]}_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}_{1}}/S^{[p^{n}]}(T^{\prime})^{\natural},E(\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime})),P))\\
@A{}A{\simeq}A\\
Rg_{*}(Ru_{X^{[p^{n}]}_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}_{1}}/S^{[p^{n}]}(T^{\prime})^{\natural}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X^{[p^{n}]}_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}_{1}}/S^{[p^{n}]}(T^{\prime})^{\natural}}(E(\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}))\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}).\end{CD}

For a similar morphism
{\mathfrak{h}}\colon T^{\prime\prime}\longrightarrow T^{\prime}
to
{\mathfrak{g}}
and
a similar morphism

h\colon X_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime\prime}_{1}}\longrightarrow X_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}_{1}}
to
g
,
the following relation

\displaystyle(h\circ g)^{*}=Rh_{*}(g^{*})\circ h^{*}

holds.

{\rm id}_{X^{[p^{n}]}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}}^{*}={\rm id}_{(H_{\rm zar}(X^{[p^{n}]}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural},E(\overset{\circ}{T})),P)}.

Proof.

This immediately follows from the contravariant functoriality (
8.2
).
∎

The morphism (
14.1.1
) induces the following morphism

(14.1.2)

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}\colon

\displaystyle Rf^{[p^{n}]}_{\overset{\circ}{T}*}((H_{\rm zar}(X^{[p^{n}]}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S^{[p^{n}]}(T)^{\natural},E(\overset{\circ}{T})),P))

\displaystyle\longrightarrow R{\mathfrak{g}}_{*}Rf^{[p^{n}]}_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}*}((H_{\rm zar}(X^{[p^{n}]}_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}_{1}}/S^{[p^{n}]}(T^{\prime})^{\natural},E(\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime})),P))

of filtered complexes in
{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(f^{-1}({\cal K}_{T}))
.

The following is a key lemma for (
14.4
) below.

Lemma 14.2

(
cf.
[
Nak4
, (5.2.3)]
).

Let
{\rm IsocF}^{\square}_{p}(S/{\cal V})
be the category of filtered log
p
-adically convergent isocrystals on

{\rm Enl}^{\square}_{p}(S/{\cal V})
.
Assume that
M_{S}
is split. Let
k
be a nonnegative integer or
\infty
.
Then there exists an object

(14.2.1)

\displaystyle(R^{q}f^{[p^{n}]}_{*}(P_{k}H_{\rm zar}(X^{[p^{n}]}/K,E)),P)

of
{\rm IsocF}^{\square}_{p}(\overset{\circ}{S}/{\cal V})

such that

(14.2.2)

\displaystyle(R^{q}f^{[p^{n}]}_{*}(P_{k}H_{\rm zar}(X^{[p^{n}]}/K,E)),P)_{\overset{\circ}{T}}=

\displaystyle(R^{q}f^{[p^{n}]}_{\overset{\circ}{T}*}(P_{k}H_{\rm zar}(X^{[p^{n}]}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S^{[p^{n}]}({\overset{\circ}{T})},E(\overset{\circ}{T}))),P)

for any object
\overset{\circ}{T}
in
{\rm Enl}^{\square}_{p}(\overset{\circ}{S}/{\cal V})
.
In particular, there exists an object

(14.2.3)

\displaystyle(R^{q}f^{[p^{n}]}_{*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X^{[p^{n}]}/K}(E_{K})),P)

of
{\rm IsocF}_{p}(\overset{\circ}{S}/{\cal V})

such that

(14.2.4)

\displaystyle(R^{q}f^{[p^{n}]}_{*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X^{[p^{n}]}/K}(E_{K})),P)_{\overset{\circ}{T}}=(R^{q}f^{[p^{n}]}_{X^{[p^{n}]}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S^{[p^{n}]}(\overset{\circ}{T})*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X^{[p^{n}]}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S^{[p^{n}]}(\overset{\circ}{T})}(E(\overset{\circ}{T})))_{\mathbb{Q}},P)

for any object
\overset{\circ}{T}
in
{\rm Enl}_{p}(\overset{\circ}{S}/{\cal V})
.

Proof.

The proof is the same as that of
[
Nak4
, (5.2.3)]
.
∎

Let
A
be a commutative ring with unit element.
Recall that we have said that a filtered
A
-module
(M,P)
is filteredly flat
if
M
and
M/P_{k}M

(\forall k\in{\mathbb{Z}})
are flat
A
-modules
(
[
NS
, (1.1.14)]
). As a corollary of (
14.2
), we obtain the following:

Corollary 14.3

(
cf.
[
Nak4
, (5.2.4)]
).

For a hollow log
p
-adic enlargement
T
of
S^{[p^{n}]}/{\cal V}
,
the filtered sheaf

(14.3.1)

\displaystyle(R^{q}f^{[p^{n}]}_{\overset{\circ}{T}*}(P_{k}H_{\rm zar}(X^{[p^{n}]}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S^{[p^{n}]}(T)^{\natural},E(\overset{\circ}{T}))),P)

is a filteredly flat
{\cal K}_{T}
-modules.
In particular, the filtered sheaf

(14.3.2)

\displaystyle(R^{q}f^{[p^{n}]}_{\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X^{[p^{n}]}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S^{[p^{n}]}(T)^{\natural}}E(\overset{\circ}{T})),P)

is a filteredly flat
{\cal K}_{T}
-module.

Conjecture 14.14

(
Filtered log convergent log hard Lefschetz conjecture).

The following cup product

(14.14.1)

\eta^{i}\colon R^{d-i}f_{*}({\cal O}_{X/K})^{\natural,\square}\longrightarrow R^{d+i}f_{*}({\cal O}_{X/K})^{\natural,\square}(i)\quad(i\in{\mathbb{N}})

Here
P_{k}(R^{d+i}f_{*}({\cal O}_{X/K})^{\natural,\square}(i)):=P_{k+2i}R^{d+i}f_{*}({\cal O}_{X/K})^{\natural,\square}
.

(

15
Strict compatibility

In this section we prove the strict compatibility of the pull-back of a morphism
with respect to the weight filtration.
Because the proofs of the results are the same as those of
[
Nak4
, (5.4.6)]
and

[
Nak4
, (5.4.7)]
, we omit the proofs.

Theorem 15.1

(
Strict compatibility I).

Let the notations be as in
(
13.1
)
and the proof of it.
Let
Y/s^{\prime}
be an analogous object to
X/s
.
Let
f^{\prime}\colon Y\longrightarrow s^{\prime}
be the structural morphism.
Let
h\colon s\longrightarrow s^{\prime}
be a morphism of log schemes.
Let
g\colon X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}\longrightarrow Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}_{0}}

be the morphism in
(
8.1.1
)
for the case
S=s
and
S^{\prime}=s^{\prime}

satisfying the condition
(8.1.6)
.
Let
{\cal W}^{\prime}
be the Witt ring of
\Gamma(s^{\prime},{\cal O}_{s^{\prime}})

and set
K^{\prime}_{0}:={\rm Frac}({\cal W}^{\prime})
.
Assume that
\overset{\circ}{s}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{s}{}^{\prime}
is finite.
Let
q
be a nonnegaitve integer.
Let us endow

H^{q}_{{\rm crys}}(Y/{\cal W}(s^{\prime}))\otimes_{{\cal W}^{\prime}}K_{0}

with the induced filtration by
P
on

H^{q}_{{\rm crys}}(Y/{\cal W}(s^{\prime}))_{K^{\prime}_{0}}
.
Then the induced morphism

(15.1.1)

g^{*}\colon H^{q}_{{\rm crys}}(Y/{\cal W}(s^{\prime}))\otimes_{{\cal W}^{\prime}}K_{0}\longrightarrow H^{q}_{{\rm crys}}(X/{\cal W}(s))\otimes_{\cal W}K_{0}

is strictly compatible with the weight filtration.

Theorem 15.2

(
Strict compatibility II).

Let the notations and the assumption be as in
(
13.2
)

and
(
8.1.1
)
.
Let
Y/S^{\prime}
and
T^{\prime}
be analogous objects to
X/S

and
T
, respectively.
Let
g
be the morphism in
(
8.1.1
)
satisfying the condition
(8.1.6)
.
Let
q
be a nonnegative integer.
Then the induced morphism

(15.2.1)

g^{*}\colon v^{*}(R^{q}f^{\prime}_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}_{1}}/S^{\prime}(T^{\prime})^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}^{\prime}_{1}}/S^{\prime}(T^{\prime})^{\natural}}))_{\mathbb{Q}}\longrightarrow R^{q}f_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}{}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}})_{\mathbb{Q}}

is strictly compatible with the weight filtration.
Consequently the induced morphism

(15.2.2)

g^{*}\colon v^{*}(R^{q}f^{\prime}_{*}({\cal O}_{Y/K^{\prime}})^{\natural,\square})\longrightarrow R^{q}f_{*}({\cal O}_{X/K})^{\natural,\square}

in
F{\textrm{-}}{\rm Isoc}^{\square}(S/{\cal V})

is strictly compatible with the weight filtration.

16
p
-adic filtered Steenbrink complexes and trace morphisms

Let the notations be as in §
14
.
In this section we recall the filtered complex

(A_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E),P)

defined in
[
Nak4
]
and we define the following trace morphism

R^{2d}f_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}})_{\mathbb{Q}}\longrightarrow{\cal K}_{\overset{\circ}{T}}(-d)

when
\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}
is projective over
\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}
and
the relative dimension of
\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}
is of pure dimension
d
,
where
T
is an object of
{\rm Enl}_{p}(S/{\cal V})
.

In
[
F2
]
, to define the trace morphism, Fujisawa has used
the comparison between the Steenbrink (bi)filtered complex and
the (bi)filtered complex obtained by Hirsch extension
(=an analogue of
(H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}),P)
with
Hodge filtration).
We do not use the comparison between

(A_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}),P)
and

(H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}),P)

to define our trace morphism; our proof of the existence of the trace morphism
is much simpler than that in [loc. cit]; our method is also
applicable in the complex analytic case.

Let the notations be as in §
10
.
Set

(16.0.1)

\displaystyle A_{\rm zar}({\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural},{\cal E}^{\bullet})^{ij}

\displaystyle:=({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}{\Omega}^{i+j+1}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}})/P_{j}

\displaystyle:=({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{i+j+1}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}})/P_{j}({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{i+j+1}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}})\quad(i,j\in{\mathbb{N}}).

The sheaf

A_{\rm zar}({\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural},{\cal E})^{ij}

has a quotient filtration
P
obtained by the
filtration
P
on

{\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{i+j+1}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
.
We consider the following boundary morphisms of double complexes:

(16.0.2)

\begin{CD}A_{\rm zar}({\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural},{\cal E}^{\bullet})^{i,j+1}\\
@A{d\log t\wedge}A{}A\\
A_{\rm zar}({\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural},{\cal E}^{\bullet})^{ij}@>{-\nabla}>{}>A_{\rm zar}({\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural},{\cal E}^{\bullet})^{i+1,j}\\
\end{CD}

as in
[
Nak4
, (1.4.0.9)]
.
Then we have the double complex

A_{\rm zar}({\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural},{\cal E}^{\bullet})^{\bullet\bullet}
.
Let

A_{\rm zar}({\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural},{\cal E}^{\bullet})

be the single complex of

A_{\rm zar}({\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural},{\cal E}^{\bullet})^{\bullet\bullet}
:

\displaystyle A_{\rm zar}({\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural},{\cal E}^{\bullet})^{i}=\bigoplus_{j\geq 0}({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{i+1}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}})/P_{j}({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{i+1}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}})

with boundary morphism
-\nabla+d\log t\wedge
.
The double complex

A_{\rm zar}({\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural},{\cal E}^{\bullet})^{\bullet\bullet}

has a filtration
P=\{P_{k}\}_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}

defined by the following formula:

(16.0.3)

P_{k}A_{\rm zar}({\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural},{\cal E}^{\bullet}):=(\cdots P_{2j+k+1}A_{\rm zar}({\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural},{\cal E}^{\bullet})^{i}\cdots)\in{\rm C}^{+}{\rm F}(f^{-1}_{T}({\cal O}_{T})).

Let
(A_{\rm zar}({\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural},{\cal E}^{\bullet}),P)

be the filtered single complex of the filtered double complex

(A_{\rm zar}({\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural},{\cal E}^{\bullet})^{\bullet\bullet},P)
.

In
[
Nak4
, (1.4.4)]
we have proved the following as a special case:

Theorem 16.1

(
[
Nak4
, (1.4.4)]
).

The filtered complex

R\pi_{{\rm zar}*}((A_{\rm zar}({\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural},{\cal E}^{\bullet}),P))

is independent of the choice of the disjoint union
of an affine simplicial open covering of
X
and
a simplicial immersion

X_{\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal P}_{\bullet}
over
\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}
.

Set

(16.1.1)

(A_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E),P):=R\pi_{{\rm zar}*}((A_{\rm zar}({\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural},{\cal E}^{\bullet}),P))

and we have called the filtered complex

\displaystyle d\log t\wedge\colon{\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{i}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural}}\longrightarrow({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{i+1}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}})/P_{j}({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{i+1}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}})\quad(i\in{\mathbb{N}}).

\displaystyle(-j-k,v).

Proposition 16.5

(

\bigoplus_{j\geq\max\{-k,0\}}R^{q-2j-k}f_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k)}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(E_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k)}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(2j+k)}_{\rm crys}(\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}))(-j-k)

Proposition 16.6
.

Assume that
\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}

is pure of relative dimension
d
.
Then the following hold
:

(1)
If
k\not=0
, then
E_{1}^{-k,2d+k}=0
.

(2)
If
k>1
, then
E_{1}^{-k,2d-1+k}=0
.

(3)
If
k\leq 0
, then
E_{1}^{-k,2d+1+k}=0
.

Proof.

(1): The relative dimension of

\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k)}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}
is equal to
d-2j-k
.
Consider the case
q=2d
in (
16.3.3
)
and the inequality
j\geq\max\{-k,0\}
.
We have an easy equivalence:
2d-2j-k\leq 2(d-2j-k)\Leftrightarrow j\leq-2^{-1}k
.
If
k<0
, then
j\geq-k
. There is no
(j,k)
satisfying the three inequalities.
Hence
E_{1}^{-k,2d+k}=0
for
k<0
.
If
k>0
, then there is no
(j,k)
again. Hence
E_{1}^{-k,2d+k}=0
if
k\not=0
.

(2): Consider the case
q=2d-1
in (
16.3.3
) and the inequality
j\geq\max\{-k,0\}
.
We have an easy equivalence:

2d-1-2j-k\leq 2(d-2j-k)\Leftrightarrow 2j+k\leq 1
.
If
k>1
, then there is no
(j,k)
satisfying the inequalities.
Hence
E_{1}^{-k,2d-1+k}=0
.

(3): Consider the case
q=2d+1
in (
16.3.3
) and the inequality
j\geq\max\{-k,0\}
.
We have an easy equivalence:

2d+1-2j-k\leq 2(d-2j-k)\Leftrightarrow 2j+k\leq-1
.
Assume that
k\leq 0
. Then
j+k\geq 0
.
There is no
(j,k)
satisfying the inequalities.
Hence
E_{1}^{-k,2d+1+k}=0
.
∎

Corollary 16.7
.

(1)

(16.7.1)

E_{\infty}^{0,2d}=E_{2}^{0,2d}={\rm Coker}(E_{1}^{-1,2d}\longrightarrow E_{1}^{0,2d}).

(2)

(16.7.2)

\displaystyle R^{2d}f_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}(E))=E_{2}^{0,2d}.

Proof.

These follow from (
16.6
).
∎

Now let the notations be as in §
14
.
Assume that
X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}
is of pure dimension
d\geq 0
.
Next we define a trace morphism

(16.7.3)

R^{2d}f_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}})_{\mathbb{Q}}\longrightarrow{\cal K}_{\overset{\circ}{T}}(-d).

Consider the following spectral sequence

:

(16.7.4)

\displaystyle E_{1}^{-k,q+k}=\bigoplus_{j\geq\max\{-k,0\}}

\displaystyle R^{q-2j-k}f_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k)}_{T_{1}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}({\cal O}_{{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k)}_{T_{1}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(2j+k)}_{\rm crys}(\overset{\circ}{X}_{T}/\overset{\circ}{T}))(-j-k)

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow R^{q}f_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}}).

for the case
E={\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
in
(
16.3.2
)
and consider

:

(16.7.5)

\displaystyle E_{1}^{0,2d}=

\displaystyle R^{2d}f_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(0)}_{T_{1}}/T*}({\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(0)}_{T_{1}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(0)}_{\rm crys}(\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{1}}/T)).

We have the trace morphism

(16.7.6)

\displaystyle{\rm Tr}_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(0)}_{T_{1}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\colon R^{2d}f_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(0)}_{T_{1}}/T*}({\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(0)}_{T_{1}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{{\rm crys}}(\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(0)}_{T_{1}}/\overset{\circ}{T}))_{\mathbb{Q}}\longrightarrow{\cal K}_{\overset{\circ}{T}}(-d).

by the proof of
[
O1
, (3.12)]
.
This morphism is surjective since
the trace morphism

\displaystyle{\rm Tr}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda,T_{1}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\colon R^{2d}f_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda,T_{1}}/T*}({\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{{\lambda},T_{1}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{{\rm crys}}(\overset{\circ}{X}_{{\lambda},T_{1}}/\overset{\circ}{T}))_{\mathbb{Q}}\longrightarrow{\cal K}_{\overset{\circ}{T}}(-d)

is surjective.

We prove that
{\rm Tr}_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(0)}_{T_{1}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}

induces a morphism
(E_{2}^{0,2d})_{\mathbb{Q}}\longrightarrow{\cal K}_{\overset{\circ}{T}}(-d)
,
which we denote by

(16.7.7)

{\rm Tr}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}}\colon(E_{2}^{0,2d})_{\mathbb{Q}}\longrightarrow{\cal K}_{\overset{\circ}{T}}(-d)

again. To prove this, let us recall the following theorem of Berthelot (
[
B2
, p. 567]
):

Proposition 16.8

(
[
B2
, p. 567]
).

Let
\kappa
be a perfect field of characteristic
p>0
.
Let
{\cal W}_{n}
be the Witt ring of
\kappa
of length
n>0
.
Let
Y
be a proper smooth scheme over
\kappa

of pure dimension
d
with structural morphism
