g_{Y}\colon Y\longrightarrow\kappa
.
Let
E
be a smooth closed subscheme of
Y

with pure codimension
e
with structural morphism

g_{E}\colon E\longrightarrow\kappa
.
Let
\iota\colon E\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}Y
be the closed immersion.
Then the following diagram is commutative
:

(16.8.1)

\begin{CD}R\Gamma(E/{\cal W}_{n})\otimes^{L}_{{\cal W}_{n}}R\Gamma(Y/{\cal W}_{n})[-2e]@>{\cup\iota^{*}}>{}>R\Gamma(E/{\cal W}_{n})[-2e]@>{{\rm Tr}_{g_{E}}}>{}>{\cal W}_{n}[-2d]\\
@V{G_{E/Y}\otimes{\rm id}}V{}V@V{}V{G_{E/Y}}V\Big\|\\
R\Gamma(Y/{\cal W}_{n})\otimes^{L}_{{\cal W}_{n}}R\Gamma(Y/{\cal W}_{n})@>{\cup}>{}>R\Gamma(Y/{\cal W}_{n})@>{{\rm Tr}_{g_{Y}}}>{}>{\cal W}_{n}[-2d].\end{CD}

Corollary 16.9
.

By abuse of notation, let

{\rm Tr}_{g_{E}}\colon H^{2(d-e)}_{\rm crys}(E/{\cal W}_{n})\longrightarrow{\cal W}_{n}

({\rm resp}.~{\rm Tr}_{g_{Y}}\colon H^{2d}_{\rm crys}(Y/{\cal W}_{n})\longrightarrow{\cal W}_{n})

be also the trace morphism.
Let
x
be an element of
H^{2(d-e)}_{\rm crys}(Y/{\cal W})
.
Let
c_{e}(E)\in H^{2e}_{\rm crys}(Y/{\cal W})
be
the cycle class of
E
.
Then
{\rm Tr}_{g_{E}}(\iota^{*}(x))={\rm Tr}_{g_{Y}}(c_{e}(E)\cdot x)
.

Proof.

By (
16.8.1
) we have a formula

{\rm Tr}_{g_{E}}(y\cdot\iota^{*}(y^{\prime}))={\rm Tr}_{g_{Y}}(G_{E/Y}(y)\cdot y^{\prime})

for
y\in H^{r}_{\rm crys}(Y/W)
and
y\in H^{2e-r}_{\rm crys}(Y/W)

(r\in{\mathbb{Z}})
. We have only to set
y=1\in H^{0}_{\rm crys}(Y/W)

and
y^{\prime}=x
.
∎

By the existence of the trace morphism
as a convergent crystal for a projective smooth morphism due to Ogus
[
O1
, (3.12)]
,
(
16.8
) and (
16.9
) are generalized for the relative case:

Theorem 16.10
.

Let
Y
be a projective smooth scheme over
\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}

of pure relative dimension
d
with structural morphism

g_{Y}\colon Y\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{T}
.
Let
E
be a relative smooth closed subscheme of
Y

with pure relative codimension
e
with structural morphism

g_{E}\colon E\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}
.
Let
\iota\colon E\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}Y
be the closed immersion.
Let
r
be a nonnegative integer.
Then the following diagram is commutative
:

(16.10.1)

\begin{CD}R^{r}g_{E/\overset{\circ}{T}}({\cal O}_{E/\overset{\circ}{T}})\otimes_{K}R^{2(d-e)-r}g_{Y/\overset{\circ}{T}}({\cal O}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{T}})_{K}@>{\cup\iota^{*}}>{}>R^{2(d-e)}g_{E/\overset{\circ}{T}}({\cal O}_{E/\overset{\circ}{T}})_{K}@>{{\rm Tr}_{g_{E/\overset{\circ}{T}}}}>{}>{\cal K}_{T}\\
@V{G_{E/Y}\otimes{\rm id}}V{}V@V{}V{G_{E/Y}}V\Big\|\\
R^{r+2e}g_{Y/\overset{\circ}{T}}({\cal O}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{T}})_{K}\otimes_{K}R^{2(d-e)-r}g_{Y/\overset{\circ}{T}}({\cal O}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{T}})_{K}@>{\cup}>{}>R^{2d}g_{Y/\overset{\circ}{T}}({\cal O}_{E/\overset{\circ}{T}})_{K}@>{{\rm Tr}_{g_{Y/\overset{\circ}{T}}}}>{}>{\cal K}_{T}.\end{CD}

Corollary 16.11
.

Let
x
be an element of
R^{2(d-e)}g_{Y/\overset{\circ}{T}}(Y/\overset{\circ}{T})_{K}
.
Let
c_{e}(E)\in R^{2e}g_{Y/\overset{\circ}{T}}(Y/\overset{\circ}{T})_{K}
be
the cycle class of
E
.
Then
{\rm Tr}_{g_{E/\overset{\circ}{T}}}(\iota^{*}(x))={\rm Tr}_{g_{Y/\overset{\circ}{T}}}(c_{e}(E)\cdot x)
.

Proposition 16.12
.

The trace morphism
(
16.7.6
)

induces a morphism
(E_{2}^{0,2d})_{\mathbb{Q}}\longrightarrow{\cal K}_{T}(-d)
,
which we denote by

(16.12.1)

{\rm Tr}^{A}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}}\colon(E_{2}^{0,2d})_{\mathbb{Q}}\longrightarrow{\cal K}_{T}(-d).

Proof.

For
\lambda_{0}<\lambda_{1}
,
let

\displaystyle G_{\lambda_{i}}\colon

\displaystyle R^{2d-2}f_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\{\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}\},T_{1}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}({\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\{\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}\},T_{1}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\rm crys}(\overset{\circ}{X}_{\{\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}\},T_{1}}/\overset{\circ}{T}))_{\mathbb{Q}}(-1)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow R^{2d}f_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(0)}_{T_{1}}/T*}({\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(0)}_{\lambda_{i},T_{1}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(0)}_{\rm crys}(\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda_{i},T_{1}}/T))_{\mathbb{Q}}

be the crystalline Gysin morphism for the closed immersion

X_{\{\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}\}}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}X_{\lambda_{i}}
.
By
[
Nak4
, (1.5.21)]
(cf.
[
Nak1
,
(10.1.2;\star)
]
)
the boundary morphism

\displaystyle E_{1}^{-1,2d}=

\displaystyle R^{2d}f_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(1)}_{T_{1}}/T*}({\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(0)}_{T_{1}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(0)}_{\rm crys}(\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{1}}/T))_{\mathbb{Q}}(-1)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow E_{1}^{0,2d}=R^{2d}f_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(0)}_{T_{1}}/T*}({\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(0)}_{T_{1}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(0)}_{\rm crys}(\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{1}}/T))_{\mathbb{Q}}=(E_{1}^{0,2d})_{\mathbb{Q}}

is given by the following Čech-Gysin morphism

(16.12.2)

\displaystyle G:=\sum_{\lambda_{0}<\lambda_{1}}(G_{\lambda_{0}}-G_{\lambda_{1}})\colon

\displaystyle R^{2d-2}f_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(1)}_{T_{1}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}({\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(1)}_{T_{1}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(1)}_{\rm crys}(\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{1}}/\overset{\circ}{T}))_{\mathbb{Q}}(-1)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow R^{2d}f_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(0)}_{T_{1}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}({\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(0)}_{T_{1}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(0)}_{\rm crys}(\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{1}}/\overset{\circ}{T}))_{\mathbb{Q}}.

Hence (
16.12
) follows from the commutative square (
16.10.1
).
∎

Definition 16.13
.

We call
{\rm Tr}^{A}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}}

the
trace morphism
of
X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}

with respect to
A_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural})_{\mathbb{Q}}
.

Henceforth, let the notations and the assumptions be as in §
14
.

Proposition 16.14
.

The trace morphism
{\rm Tr}^{A}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}}
extends to
the following morphism

\displaystyle{\rm Tr}^{A}_{X/K}\colon E_{2}^{0,2d}(X/K)\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{X/K}(-d)

on
{\rm Enl}^{\square}(S/{\cal V})
.
Here
E_{2}^{0,2d}(X/K)
is a log convergent
F
-isocrystal
on
{\rm Enl}^{\square}(S/{\cal V})
obtained by
E_{2}^{0,2d}

in
(
16.7.4
)
.

Proof.

Because the Gysin morphism extends to a morphism of convergent isocrystals
(
[
O1
, (3.13)]
),
we obtain (
16.14
) by (
8.11
).
∎

Proposition 16.15
.

If each fiber
X_{t}
of
X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}
for any exact closed point

t\in S(T)^{\natural}
is geometrically connected, then

{\rm Tr}^{A}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}}
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

By the description of the boundary morphism
E_{1}^{ij}\longrightarrow E_{1}^{i+1,j}

(
[
Nak4
, (1.5.21)]
),

(E_{2}^{0,2d})(d)_{\mathbb{Q}}
is the dual of
E^{00}_{2}
, which is equal to
{\cal K}_{T}
.
Hence
(E_{2}^{0,2d})_{\mathbb{Q}}\simeq{\cal K}_{T}(-d)
.
Since
{\rm Tr}^{A}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}}
is a surjective morphism of
the free module
{\cal K}_{T}
-modules of the same rank,

{\rm Tr}^{A}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}}
is an isomorphism.
∎

Corollary 16.16
.

Let us denote the composite morphism of the cup product and

{\rm Tr}^{A}_{X/K}
by
\langle?.?\rangle:

(16.16.1)

\displaystyle\langle?.?\rangle\colon

\displaystyle R^{q}f_{*}({\cal O}_{X/K})^{\natural,\square}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{X/K}}R^{2d-q}f_{*}({\cal O}_{X/K})^{\natural,\square}\overset{\cup}{\longrightarrow}R^{2d}f_{*}({\cal O}_{X/K})^{\natural,\square}\overset{{\rm Tr}^{A}_{X/K}}{\longrightarrow}{\cal O}_{X/K}(-d).

Then
\langle?.?\rangle
induces the following isomorphism

(16.16.2)

\displaystyle R^{q}f_{*}({\cal O}_{X/K})^{\natural,\square}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}{\cal H}{\it om}_{{\cal K}_{T}}(R^{2d-q}f_{*}({\cal O}_{X/K})^{\natural,\square},{\cal O}_{X/K}(-d)).

Proof.

Let
T
be an object of
{\rm Enl}_{p}^{\square}(S/{\cal V})
.
By the theory of convergent isocrystals, we may assume that

\overset{\circ}{T}
is a point. In fact, we may assume that

\overset{\circ}{T}
is the formal spectrum of the Witt ring of

\Gamma(\overset{\circ}{T}_{1,{\rm red}},{\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1,{\rm red}}})
.
Let

\displaystyle{\rm Tr}^{T}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}}\colon R^{2d}f_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}})\longrightarrow{\cal K}_{T}(-d)

be Tsuji’s trace morphism.
Tsuji’s Poincaré duality gives us an isomorphism

(16.16.3)

\displaystyle R^{q}f_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}})_{\mathbb{Q}}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}{\cal H}{\it om}_{{\cal K}_{T}}(R^{2d-q}f_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}})_{\mathbb{Q}},{\cal K}_{T}(-d)).

First assume that
\overset{\circ}{X}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}
is geometrically connected.
Then, consider the following composite
{\cal K}_{T}
-linear isomorphism

\displaystyle{\cal K}_{T}(-d)\overset{({\rm Tr}^{A}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}})^{-1}}{\longrightarrow}(E_{2}^{0,2d})_{\mathbb{Q}}=R^{2d}f_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}})_{\mathbb{Q}}\overset{{\rm Tr}^{T}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}}}{\longrightarrow}{\cal K}_{T}(-d).

This is obtained by a global section of
{\cal K}_{T}^{*}
.
Hence the morphisms (
16.16.2
) and (
16.16.3
) are the same up to

{\cal K}_{T}^{*}
.
If
\overset{\circ}{X}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}
is not geometrically connected, then
we have only to take an extension

\Gamma(\overset{\circ}{T}_{1,{\rm red}},{\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1,{\rm red}}})
.
∎

17
Coincidence of the weight filtrations I

Let the notations be as in §
6
and the beginning of the previous section.
In this section we construct a filtered morphism

(17.0.1)

\displaystyle\psi\colon(A_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E),P)\longrightarrow(H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E),P)

such that the underlying morphism

A_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E)\longrightarrow H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E)

is an isomorphism.
This is a log crystalline analogue of the filtered morphism constructed in
[
F2
]
.
To construct the morphism (
17.0.1
),
we have to solve problems about signs arising from the signs of
the boundary morphisms of
A_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E)

and
H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E)
,
which most mathematicians do not want to think of them as possible.
It is convenient to another
(A_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E),P)

whose boundary morphism
is different from that of
(A_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E),P)
.
Let us define it.

In this section we also consider the following boundary morphisms
of double complexes:

(17.0.2)

\begin{CD}A_{\rm zar}({\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural},{\cal E}^{\bullet})^{i,j+1}\\
@A{d\log t\wedge}A{}A\\
A_{\rm zar}({\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural},{\cal E}^{\bullet})^{ij}@>{\nabla}>{}>A_{\rm zar}({\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural},{\cal E}^{\bullet})^{i+1,j},\\
\end{CD}

which are different from (
16.0.2
).
This convention of signs is the same as
the original Steenbrink complex in
[
St1
]
.
Using these boundary morphisms, we have the double complex

A^{\prime}_{\rm zar}({\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural},{\cal E}^{\bullet})^{\bullet\bullet}:=(A_{\rm zar}({\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural},{\cal E}^{\bullet})^{ij})_{i,j\in{\mathbb{Z}}}
.
Let

A^{\prime}_{\rm zar}({\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural},{\cal E}^{\bullet})

be the single complex of

A^{\prime}_{\rm zar}({\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural},{\cal E}^{\bullet})^{\bullet\bullet}
.

The double complex

A^{\prime}_{\rm zar}({\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural},{\cal E}^{\bullet})^{\bullet\bullet}

has a filtration
P=\{P_{k}\}_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}

defined by the following formula:

(17.0.3)

P_{k}A^{\prime}_{\rm zar}({\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural},{\cal E}^{\bullet})^{\bullet\bullet}:=(\cdots P_{2j+k+1}A^{\prime}_{\rm zar}({\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural},{\cal E}^{\bullet})^{ij}\cdots).

Let
(A^{\prime}_{\rm zar}({\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural},{\cal E}^{\bullet}),P)

be the filtered single complex of the filtered double complex

(A^{\prime}_{\rm zar}({\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural},{\cal E}^{\bullet})^{\bullet\bullet},P)
.
Then the isomorphism

A^{\prime}_{\rm zar}({\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural},{\cal E}^{\bullet})^{ij}\owns\omega\longmapsto(-1)^{i}\omega\in A_{\rm zar}({\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural},{\cal E}^{\bullet})^{ij}

induces the following filtered isomorphism

(A^{\prime}_{\rm zar}({\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural},{\cal E}^{\bullet})^{\bullet\bullet},P)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}(A_{\rm zar}({\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural},{\cal E}^{\bullet})^{\bullet\bullet},P).

Set

(A^{\prime}_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E),P):=R\pi_{{\rm zar}*}((A^{\prime}_{\rm zar}({\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural},{\cal E}^{\bullet}),P)).

Obviously we obtain the following isomorphism:

\displaystyle(A^{\prime}_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E),P)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}(A_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E),P).

Proposition 17.1
.

The morphism

\displaystyle(-1)^{i}d\log t\wedge\colon{\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{i}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural}}\longrightarrow({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{i+1}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}})/P_{j}({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{i+1}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}})\quad(i\in{\mathbb{N}}).

induces the following isomorphism

(17.1.1)

(-1)^{\bullet}\theta\wedge:=(-1)^{\bullet}\theta_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}\wedge\colon Ru_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}(E))\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}A^{\prime}_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E).

Here
(-1)^{\bullet}
is only a notation and there is no other meaning.

Proof.

The proof of this proposition is the same as that of
[
Nak4
, (1.4.3)]
.
∎

We also have to change signs of the boundary morphisms of

H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E)
as follows.

Recall that the boundary morphism of

H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E)

is induced by the following boundary morphism

(17.1.2)

\small{\begin{CD}{\cal O}_{T}u^{[k]}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{T}}{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(m-1)}}}\Omega^{q+1}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(m-1)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\\
@A{(-1)^{m-1}\nabla}A{}A\\
{\cal O}_{T}u^{[k]}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{T}}{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(m-1)}}}\Omega^{q}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(m-1)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}@>{\underset{\lambda_{0}<\cdots<\lambda_{m-1}}{\sum}\sum_{j=0}^{m}(-1)^{j}\iota^{*}_{\underline{\lambda}_{j},\underline{\lambda}}}>{}>{\cal O}_{T}u^{[k]}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{T}}{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(m)}}}\Omega^{q}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(m)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\\
@V{(-1)^{m-1}((?)^{\prime}d\log t\wedge)}V{}V\\
{\cal O}_{T}u^{[k-1]}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{T}}{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(m-1)}}}\Omega^{q+1}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(m-1)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\\
\end{CD}}

for
\underline{\lambda}=\{\lambda_{0},\ldots,\lambda_{m-1}\}\in P(\Lambda)
.
Let

H^{\prime}_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E)
be the analogous complex
to
H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E)

with the following boundary morphism

(17.1.3)

\tiny{\begin{CD}{\cal O}_{T}u^{[k]}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{T}}{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(m-1)}}}\Omega^{q+1}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(m-1)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\\
@A{\nabla}A{}A\\
{\cal O}_{T}u^{[k]}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{T}}{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(m-1)}}}\Omega^{q}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(m-1)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}@>{(-1)^{q+m}\underset{\lambda_{0}<\cdots<\lambda_{m-1}}{\sum}\sum_{j=0}^{m}(-1)^{j}\iota^{\underline{\lambda}_{j}*}_{\underline{\lambda}}}>{}>{\cal O}_{T}u^{[k]}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{T}}{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(m)}}}\Omega^{q}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(m)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\\
@V{((?)^{\prime}d\log t\wedge)}V{}V\\
{\cal O}_{T}u^{[k-1]}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{T}}{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(m-1)}}}\Omega^{q+1}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(m-1)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}.\\
\end{CD}}

Let

\displaystyle\epsilon\colon{\mathbb{N}}\times{\mathbb{N}}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{Z}}/2

be a map satisfying the following equations

\displaystyle\epsilon(m-1,q+1)=\epsilon(m-1,q)+m-1

and

\displaystyle\epsilon(m,q)=\epsilon(m-1,q)+q+m.

Indeed, this function exists because

\displaystyle\epsilon(m+1,q+1)=\epsilon(m,q+1)+m+1+q+1=\epsilon(m,q)+m+m+q=\epsilon(m,q)+q

and

\displaystyle\epsilon(m+1,q+1)=\epsilon(m+1,q)+m+1=\epsilon(m,q)+m+1+q+m+1=\epsilon(m,q)+q.

Then the morphism

\displaystyle{\cal O}_{T}u^{[k]}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{T}}{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(m-1)}}}\Omega^{q}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(m-1)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\owns\omega\longmapsto(-1)^{\epsilon(m-1,q)}\omega\in{\cal O}_{T}u^{[k]}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{T}}{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(m-1)}}}\Omega^{q}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(m-1)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}

induces a filtered isomorphism

(17.1.4)

\displaystyle(H^{\prime}_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E),P)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E),P).

To fix the idea, set
\epsilon(0,0)=0
.
Then
\epsilon(0,q)=1
for odd
q
and
\epsilon(0,q)=0
for even
q
.
The morphism

\displaystyle{\cal O}_{T}u^{[k]}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{T}}{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}}}\Omega^{q}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\owns\omega\longmapsto(-1)^{q}\omega\in{\cal O}_{T}u^{[k]}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{T}}{\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(0)}}}\Omega^{q}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex},(0)}/\overset{\circ}{T}}

induces the following isomorphism

(17.1.5)

\displaystyle\widetilde{R}u_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(E)\langle u\rangle)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}H^{\prime}_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E)

by (
17.1.4
) and (
6.15.2
).

The following is a crystalline version of
[
F2
, (5.25), (5.29)]
modulo
the difference of signs:

Theorem 17.2

(
cf.
[
F2
, (5.25), (5.29)]
).

There exists the following filtered morphism

(17.2.1)

\displaystyle\psi^{\prime}\colon(A^{\prime}_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E),P)\longrightarrow(H^{\prime}_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E),P)

such that the underlying morphism

(17.2.2)

\displaystyle\psi^{\prime}\colon A^{\prime}_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E)\longrightarrow H^{\prime}_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E)

is an isomorphism fitting into the following commutative diagram

(17.2.3)

\begin{CD}A^{\prime}_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E)@>{\psi^{\prime},\sim}>{}>H^{\prime}_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E)\\
@A{(-1)^{\bullet}\theta\wedge}A{}A@A{}A{\simeq}A\\
Ru_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}(E))\widetilde{R}u_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(E)\langle u\rangle)\\
\Big\|@V{}V{\simeq}V\\
Ru_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}(E))=Ru_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}(E)).\end{CD}

Proof.

Fix a total order on
\Lambda
once and for all as before
and fix an isomorphism from orientation sheaves with
{\mathbb{Z}}
.
Note that

\displaystyle A^{\prime}_{\rm zar}({\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural},{\cal E}^{\bullet})=(\cdots\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{j=0}^{q}({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{q+1}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}})/P_{j}({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{q+1}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}})\longrightarrow\cdots)

with boundary morphism

\nabla+d\log t\wedge
.
Let

\displaystyle{\rm Res}^{\prime}_{{\cal P}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}}}\colon{\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\longrightarrow{\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet-(m+1)}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}

be the morphism defined by the following:

(17.2.4)

\displaystyle\sigma\otimes\omega d\log x_{\lambda_{i_{0}},y}\cdots d\log x_{\lambda_{i_{m}},y}\longmapsto\sigma\otimes b^{*}_{\lambda_{i_{0}}\cdots\lambda_{i_{m}}}(\omega)

(\sigma\in{\cal E}_{\bullet},\omega\in P_{0}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}/\overset{\circ}{T}},i_{0}<\cdots<i_{m})

(cf.
[
D1
, (3.1.5)]
).
Here
y
is a point of
\overset{\circ}{\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet}
,

r
is a nonnegative integer such that

M_{X,y}/{\cal O}_{X,y}^{*}\simeq{\mathbb{N}}^{r}
,

m
is a nonnegative integer less than
r
,

\underline{\lambda}=\{\lambda_{i_{0}},\ldots,\lambda_{i_{m}}\}

and
\{x_{\lambda_{0},y},\ldots,x_{\lambda_{r-1},y}\}
is a basis of

M_{X,y}/{\cal O}_{X,y}^{*}
.
Note that the morphism (
17.2.4
) is different from the morphism (
5.9.1
)
about the places of logarithmic forms in the source of the morphism.
We define the following morphism

\displaystyle\psi^{\prime}_{\rm loc}\colon\bigoplus_{j,q}({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{q+1}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}})/P_{j}({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{q+1}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}})\longrightarrow\underset{q,\underline{\lambda}}{\bigoplus}{\cal O}_{T}\langle u\rangle\otimes_{{\cal O}_{T}}{\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{q+1-\#\underline{\lambda}}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}/\overset{\circ}{T}}}

by

(17.2.5)

\displaystyle\psi^{\prime}_{\rm loc}:=\sum_{j\geq 0}\sum_{\{\underline{\lambda}\,|\,\#\underline{\lambda}\,\geq\,j+1\}}u^{[\#\underline{\lambda}-(j+1))]}\otimes{\rm Res}^{\prime}_{{\cal P}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}}}=\sum_{\underline{\lambda}}\sum_{j=0}^{\#\underline{\lambda}-1}u^{[\#\underline{\lambda}-(j+1))]}\otimes{\rm Res}^{\prime}_{{\cal P}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}}}

(cf.
[
F2
, p. 172]
).
Note that the restriction of
\psi^{\prime}_{\rm loc}
to

P_{j}({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{q+1}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}})
indeed vanishes.
It is clear that

\displaystyle{\rm Res}^{\prime}_{{\cal P}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}}}\circ\nabla=\nabla\circ{\rm Res}^{\prime}_{{\cal P}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}}}.

Let
\lambda_{0}<\cdots<\lambda_{m}
be the different elements of
\underline{\lambda}
.
Set
\underline{\lambda}_{i}:=\underline{\lambda}\setminus\{\lambda_{i}\}
.
We claim that

\displaystyle{\rm Res}^{\prime}_{{\cal P}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}}}\circ(d\log t\wedge)=(d\log t\wedge)\circ{\rm Res}^{\prime}_{{\cal P}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}}}+\sum_{i=0}^{m}(-1)^{i+q-m}{\rm Res}^{\prime}_{{\cal P}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}_{i}}}.

Indeed, express

\omega\in{\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{q+1}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}

by the following form:

\omega=\eta d\log x_{\lambda_{0}}\wedge\cdots\wedge d\log x_{\lambda_{m}}+\sum_{i=0}^{m}\eta_{i}d\log x_{\lambda_{0}}\wedge\cdots\wedge d\log x_{\lambda_{i-1}}\wedge d\log x_{\lambda_{i+1}}\wedge\cdots\wedge d\log x_{\lambda_{m}}+\eta^{\prime\prime},

where
\eta
,
\eta_{i}
have no log differential forms

d\log x_{\lambda_{0}},\ldots,d\log x_{\lambda_{m}}
and
\eta^{\prime\prime}

has no log differential forms
d\log x_{\lambda_{0}}\wedge\cdots\wedge d\log x_{\lambda_{i-1}}

for
0\leq i\leq m
.
Then

(17.2.6)

\displaystyle{\rm Res}^{\prime}_{{\cal P}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}}}(d\log t\wedge\omega)

\displaystyle={\rm Res}^{\prime}_{{\cal P}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}}}(d\log t\wedge\eta\wedge d\log x_{\lambda_{0}}\wedge\cdots\wedge d\log x_{\lambda_{m}})

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=0}^{m}{\rm Res}^{\prime}_{{\cal P}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}}}(d\log t\wedge\eta_{i}\wedge d\log x_{\lambda_{0}}\wedge\cdots\wedge d\log x_{\lambda_{i-1}}\wedge d\log x_{\lambda_{i+1}}\wedge\cdots\wedge d\log x_{\lambda_{m}})

\displaystyle=d\log t\wedge\eta+\sum_{i=0}^{m}(-1)^{(q+1-m)+i-1}\eta_{i}

\displaystyle=d\log t\wedge{\rm Res}^{\prime}_{{\cal P}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}}}(\omega)+\sum_{i=0}^{m}(-1)^{i+q-m}{\rm Res}^{\prime}_{{\cal P}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}_{i}}}(\omega).

Let

p_{\underline{\mu}}\colon\underset{q,\underline{\lambda}}{\bigoplus}{\cal O}_{T}\langle u\rangle\otimes_{{\cal O}_{T}}{\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{q+1-\#\underline{\lambda}}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}/\overset{\circ}{T}}}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{T}\langle u\rangle\otimes_{{\cal O}_{T}}{\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{q+1-\#\underline{\mu}}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet,\underline{\mu}/\overset{\circ}{T}}}

be the projection for
\underline{\mu}
.
Since

d\log t\wedge\colon{\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{q+1}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}/P_{j}({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{q+1}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}})\longrightarrow{\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{q+1}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}/P_{j+1}({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{q+1}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}),

we obtain the following formula by (
17.2.6
) for
j
and
\underline{\mu}
such that

\#\underline{\mu}\geq j+1
:

(17.2.7)

\displaystyle p_{\underline{\mu}}\circ\psi^{\prime}_{\rm loc}\circ(\nabla+d\log t\wedge)

\displaystyle=p_{\underline{\mu}}\circ\psi^{\prime}_{\rm loc}\circ\nabla+p_{\underline{\mu}}\circ\psi^{\prime}_{\rm loc}\circ d\log t\wedge

\displaystyle=u^{[\#\underline{\mu}-(j+1)]}\otimes{\rm Res}^{\prime}_{{\cal P}_{\bullet,\underline{\mu}}}\circ\nabla+u^{[\#\underline{\mu}-(j+2)]}\otimes(d\log t\wedge)\circ{\rm Res}^{\prime}_{{\cal P}_{\bullet,\underline{\mu}}}

\displaystyle+u^{[\#\underline{\mu}-(j+2))]}\otimes\sum_{i=0}^{m}(-1)^{i+q+m}\iota^{*}_{\underline{\mu}_{i},\underline{\mu}}{\rm Res}^{\prime}_{{\cal P}_{\bullet,\underline{\mu}_{i}}}

\displaystyle=u^{[\#\underline{\mu}-(j+1)]}\otimes{\rm Res}^{\prime}_{{\cal P}_{\bullet,\underline{\mu}}}\circ\nabla+u^{[\#\underline{\mu}-(j+2)]}\otimes(d\log t\wedge)\circ{\rm Res}^{\prime}_{{\cal P}_{\bullet,\underline{\mu}}}

\displaystyle+u^{[\#\underline{\mu}-1-(j+1)]}\otimes\sum_{i=0}^{m}(-1)^{i+q+m}\iota^{*}_{\underline{\mu}_{i},\underline{\mu}}{\rm Res}^{\prime}_{{\cal P}_{\bullet,\underline{\mu}_{i}}}

\displaystyle=p_{\underline{\mu}}\circ(\nabla+(?)^{\prime}d\log t\wedge+\sum_{\underline{\lambda}}\sum_{i=0}^{m}(-1)^{i+q+m}\iota^{*}_{\underline{\lambda}_{i},\underline{\lambda}})\circ\psi^{\prime}_{\rm loc}.

Here we define
u^{[-1]}
as
0
.
Let
\psi^{\prime}\colon A^{\prime}_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E)\longrightarrow H^{\prime}_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E)
be the induced morphism by
\psi^{\prime}_{\rm loc}
.
By (
17.2.7
) we see that
\psi^{\prime}
is a morphism of complexes.

Next we check that
\psi^{\prime}
is a filtered morphism.
The subsheaf

P_{2j+k+1}({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{q+1}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}})
of

{\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{q+1}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}

is mapped to

\displaystyle\sum_{\underline{\lambda}}\sum_{j=0}^{\#\underline{\lambda}-1}P_{2(\#\underline{\lambda}-(j+1))+((2j+k+1-\#\underline{\lambda})-(\#\underline{\lambda}-1))}({\cal O}_{T}\langle u\rangle\otimes_{{\cal O}_{T}}{\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\underline{\lambda}/\overset{\circ}{T}}})

\displaystyle=P_{k}\sum_{\underline{\lambda}}\sum_{j=0}^{\#\underline{\lambda}-1}({\cal O}_{T}\langle u\rangle\otimes_{{\cal O}_{T}}{\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\underline{\lambda}/\overset{\circ}{T}}}).

Hence
\psi^{\prime}_{\rm loc}
is a filtered morphism.
Consequently
\psi^{\prime}
is a filtered morphism.

As usual, we can see that
\psi^{\prime}
is independent of the choice of
an affine open covering of

X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}
and the simplicial immersion

X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0},\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal P}_{\bullet}
over
S(T)^{\natural}
.
Indeed, assume that we are given another similar immersion

X^{\prime}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0},\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal P}{}^{\prime}_{\bullet}
.
Then we can prove the independence as in the proof of (
6.16
).

Let
\omega
be a local section of

{\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{q}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
.
Then

\displaystyle\psi^{\prime}_{\rm loc}((-1)^{q}d\log t\wedge\omega)=(-1)^{q}\sum_{\underline{\lambda}}u^{[0]}\otimes{\rm Res}^{\prime}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}}(\omega)=(-1)^{\epsilon(0,q)}\sum_{\underline{\lambda}}{\rm Res}^{\prime}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}}(\omega).

Hence the diagram (
17.2.3
) is commutative.
By (
17.1.1
), (
17.1.5
) and (
6.12.1
)
we see that (
17.2.2
) is an isomorphism.
∎

Corollary 17.3
.

There exists the following filtered morphism

(17.3.1)

\displaystyle\psi\colon(A_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E),P)\longrightarrow(H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E),P)

such that the underlying morphism

(17.3.2)

\displaystyle\psi\colon A_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E)\longrightarrow H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E)

is an isomorphism fitting into the following commutative diagram

(17.3.3)

\begin{CD}A_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E)@>{\psi,\sim}>{}>H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E)\\
@A{\theta\wedge}A{\simeq}A@A{}A{\simeq}A\\
Ru_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}(E))\widetilde{R}u_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(E)\langle u\rangle)\\
\Big\|@V{}V{\simeq}V\\
Ru_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}(E))=Ru_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}(E)).\end{CD}

Definition 17.4
.

We call the filtered morphism (
17.3.1
) is the
crystalline Fujisawa morphism
.

Let the notations be as in §
13
.
Then we can prove the following:

Theorem 17.5

(
Comparison theorem of weight filtrations).

Let
P^{\prime}
be the induced filtration on

R^{q}f_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}})

(q\in{\mathbb{N}})
by the filtered complex

(H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}),P)
.
Then
P^{\prime}\otimes_{\cal V}K=P\otimes_{\cal V}K
.

Proof.

(cf.
[
Nak4
, (5.4.6), (5.4.7)]
)
Because
P\otimes_{\cal V}K
and
P^{\prime}\otimes_{\cal V}K

extend to a convergent
F
-isocrystal on
\overset{\circ}{T}/{\cal V}
,
we may assume that
\overset{\circ}{T}
is the formal spectrum of a finite extension of

{\cal V}
.
By the deformation invariance of
P\otimes_{\cal V}K
and
P^{\prime}\otimes_{\cal V}K
,
we may assume that
\overset{\circ}{T}
is the formal spectrum of the Witt ring of a
finite extension of
\kappa
. By a standard technique, we may assume that

\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}
is a scheme of finite type over a finite field.
By the convergence of

P\otimes_{\cal V}K
and
P^{\prime}\otimes_{\cal V}K
again,
we may assume that
\overset{\circ}{T}
is the formal spectrum of the Witt ring of a
finite field.
In this case the weight spectral sequences for

(H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}),P)
and

(A_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}),P)
and the purity of the
weight proved in
[
NY
, (6.11) or Appendix]
and
[
Nak1
]

(cf.
[
KM
]
,
[
CL
]
)) tell us that

P\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}=P^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}
.
∎

Corollary 17.6
.

The composite morphism
(
16.16.1
)

induces the following morphism

(17.6.1)

\displaystyle P_{k}R^{q}f_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}})_{\mathbb{Q}}\otimes_{{\cal K}_{T}}P_{k^{\prime}}R^{2d-q}f_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}})_{\mathbb{Q}}\longrightarrow

\displaystyle\longrightarrow P_{k+k^{\prime}}{\cal K}_{T}(-d),

which gives the following isomorphism

(17.6.2)

\displaystyle P_{k}R^{q}f_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}})_{K}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}P_{k}{\cal H}{\it om}_{{\cal K}_{S}}(R^{2d-q}f_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}})_{K},{\cal K}_{T}(-d))

for any
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
.

Proof.

By (
11.6.3
)
we have the following morphism

(17.6.3)

\displaystyle\cup\colon

\displaystyle P_{k}R^{h}f_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}})\otimes_{{\cal O}_{T}}P_{k^{\prime}}R^{2d-h}f_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow P_{k+k^{\prime}}R^{2d}f_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}).

Because
R^{2d}f_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}})

and
{\cal K}_{T}(-d)
are pure of weight
2d
,
the trace morphism

{\rm Tr}^{A}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}
is
a filtered morphism. Hence we have the morphism (
17.6.1
).

By (
16.16
) and by the same proof as that of (
17.5
),
we obtain the isomorphism (
17.6.2
).
∎

18
Semi-cosimplicial
p
-adic Steenbrink complexes and
coincidence of the weight filtrations II

In this section we define the semi-cosimplicial version of

(A_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E),P)

to give another proof of the comparison theorem of
weight filtrations (
17.5
).
This method is sketched in
[
KH
, p. 1270]

for the case of log de Rham-Witt complexes for the trivial coefficient
when the base field is finite.

Let the notations be as in §
16
.
Take an index subset
\underline{\lambda}=\{\lambda_{0},\ldots,\lambda_{m-1}\}
(
\lambda_{i}<\lambda_{j}
for
i<j
).
Set

(18.0.1)

\displaystyle A_{\rm zar}({\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\underline{\lambda}}/S(T)^{\natural},{\cal E})^{ij}

\displaystyle:=({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{i+j+1}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\underline{\lambda}}/\overset{\circ}{T}})/P_{j}

\displaystyle:=({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}{\Omega}^{i+j+1}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\underline{\lambda}}/\overset{\circ}{T}})/P_{j}({\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}{\Omega}^{i+j+1}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\underline{\lambda}}/\overset{\circ}{T}})\quad(i,j\in{\mathbb{N}}).

As in §
16
, we have the filtered double complex

(A_{\rm zar}({\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\underline{\lambda}}/S(T)^{\natural},{\cal E})^{\bullet\bullet},P)
and
the filtered single complex

(A_{\rm zar}({\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\underline{\lambda}}/S(T)^{\natural},{\cal E}),P):=s((A_{\rm zar}({\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\underline{\lambda}}/S(T)^{\natural},{\cal E})^{\bullet\bullet},P))
.
Let

\pi_{\underline{\lambda},{\rm zar}}\colon(X_{\underline{\lambda},\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet})_{\rm zar}\longrightarrow(X_{\underline{\lambda},\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}})_{\rm zar}

be the natural morphism of ringed topoi.
Let
f_{\underline{\lambda}}\colon X_{\underline{\lambda},\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{T}

and
f_{\underline{\lambda}\bullet}\colon X_{\underline{\lambda},\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{T}

be the structural morphisms.
The first aim in this section is to prove that

R\pi_{\underline{\lambda},{\rm zar}*}((A_{\rm zar}({\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\underline{\lambda}}/S(T)^{\natural},{\cal E}),P))

is well-defined.
Let us recall the morphism

\pi_{\underline{\lambda},{\rm zar}}\colon((X_{\underline{\lambda},\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet})_{\rm zar},f^{-1}_{\bullet}({\cal O}_{T}))\longrightarrow((X_{\underline{\lambda},\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}})_{\rm zar},f^{-1}_{\underline{\lambda}}({\cal O}_{T}))

of ringed topoi ((
6.24.1
)) and the complex (
6.24.2
);

\displaystyle Ru_{X_{\underline{\lambda},\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\underline{\lambda},\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}(E_{\underline{\lambda}}))

\displaystyle=R\pi_{\underline{\lambda},{\rm zar}}(s({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}}/S(T)^{\natural}})).

Proposition 18.1
.

(1)
There exists the following canonical isomorphism
:

(18.1.1)

\theta\wedge:=\theta_{X_{\underline{\lambda},\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}\wedge\colon Ru_{X_{\underline{\lambda},\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\underline{\lambda},\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}(E_{\underline{\lambda}}))\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}R\pi_{\underline{\lambda},{\rm zar}*}(A_{\rm zar}({\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\underline{\lambda}}/S(T)^{\natural},{\cal E})).

(2)
There exists the following canonical isomorphism
:

(18.1.2)

\displaystyle{\rm gr}_{k}^{P}R\pi_{\underline{\lambda},{\rm zar}*}(A_{\rm zar}({\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\underline{\lambda}}/S(T)^{\natural},{\cal E}))\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\bigoplus_{j\geq\max\{-k,0\}}\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\mu}=2j+k+1}

\displaystyle a_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu},\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}*}(Ru_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu},T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(E_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu},T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\rm crys}^{(2j+k)}(\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\mu},T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T})))[-2j-k].

Proof.

(1): The following proof is the crystalline version of the proof of

[
GK
, Proposition 3.2]
.
The proof is slightly different from that of
(
16.3
) by the property of “
P_{0}
” for
X_{\underline{\lambda}}
.

Since
{\cal E}^{\bullet}_{\underline{\lambda}}:={\cal E}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}}}
is a flat

{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}}}
-module,
it suffices to prove that the natural morphism

\displaystyle d\log t\wedge

\displaystyle\colon{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}}}}{\Omega}^{i}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}}/S}\longrightarrow

\displaystyle\{({\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}}}}{\Omega}^{i+1}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}/P_{0}\overset{(-1)^{i}d\log t\wedge}{\longrightarrow}{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\underline{\bullet,\lambda}}}}{\Omega}^{i+2}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}/P_{1}

\displaystyle\overset{(-1)^{i}d\log t\wedge}{\longrightarrow}\cdots)\}

is a quasi-isomorphism.

As in
[
M
, 3.15]

(cf.
[
Nak1
, (6.28) (9), (6.29) (1)]
),
it suffices to prove that the sequence

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow{\rm gr}_{0}^{P}({\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}_{n,\underline{\lambda}}}{\otimes}_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{n,\underline{\lambda}}}}{{\Omega}}^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{n,\underline{\lambda}}/\overset{\circ}{T}})\overset{\theta_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{n,\underline{\lambda}}}\wedge}{\longrightarrow}{\rm gr}_{1}^{P}({\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}_{n,\underline{\lambda}}}{\otimes}_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{n,\underline{\lambda}}}}{{\Omega}}^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{n,\underline{\lambda}}/\overset{\circ}{T}})[1]

\displaystyle\overset{\theta_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{n,\underline{\lambda}}}\wedge}{\longrightarrow}{\rm gr}_{2}^{P}({\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}_{n,\underline{\lambda}}}{\otimes}_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}_{n,\underline{\lambda}}^{\rm ex}}}{{\Omega}}^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{n,\underline{\lambda}}/\overset{\circ}{T}})[2]\overset{\theta_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{n,\underline{\lambda}}}\wedge}{\longrightarrow}\cdots

is exact.
By (
5.9.2
) we have only to prove that
the following sequence

(18.1.3)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}_{n,\underline{\lambda}}}{\otimes}_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{n,\underline{\lambda}}}}{\Omega}^{i}_{{\overset{\circ}{\cal P}}{}^{\rm ex}_{n,\underline{\lambda}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\mu}=1}({\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}_{n,\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}}}{\otimes}_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{n,\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}}}}b_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}*}\Omega^{i}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{n,\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{{\rm zar},\underline{\mu}}({\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{n}/S))

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\mu}=2}({\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}_{n,\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}}}{\otimes}_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{n,\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}}}}b_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}*}\Omega^{i}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{n,\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{{\rm zar},\underline{\mu}}({\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{n}/S))\longrightarrow\cdots

is exact for each nonnegative integer
i
.
Following the idea in the proof of
[
GK
, Proposition 3.2]
,
set
S_{j}:=\{\underline{\nu}~|\#(\underline{\nu}\setminus\underline{\lambda})=j\}

(j\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0})
.
Consider the following double complex

\Omega^{jk}_{i}:=\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\nu}=j+k,\underline{\nu}\in S_{j}}{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}_{n,\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\nu}}}{\otimes}_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{n,\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\nu}}}}b_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\nu}*}\Omega^{i}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{n,\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\nu}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{{\rm zar},\underline{\nu}}({\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{n}/\overset{\circ}{T})\quad(j,k\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0})

such that the horizontal boundary morphisms
and the vertical boundary morphisms are given by the Čech
morphism of the pull-backs of the restriction morphisms.
Then the complex (
18.1.3
) is equal to
\Omega^{\bullet\bullet}_{i}
.
Then
\Omega^{j\bullet}_{i}
is exact since the boundary morphisms
of
\Omega^{j\bullet}_{i}
is given identities with signs.
Hence the complex (
18.1.3
) is exact.

In a routine way
we can prove that the isomorphism (
18.1.1
) is independent of
the choice of the disjoint union
of an affine simplicial open covering of
X
and
a simplicial immersion

X_{\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal P}_{\bullet}
over
\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}
.

(2): As in the proof of
[
Nak4
, (1.4.4)]
, we obtain (
18.1.2
) by using (
5.9.2
).
∎

Corollary 18.2
.

The filtered complex

R\pi_{\underline{\lambda},{\rm zar}*}((A_{\rm zar}({\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}}/S(T)^{\natural},{\cal E}^{\bullet}),P))

is independent of the choice of the disjoint union
of an affine simplicial open covering of
X
and
a simplicial immersion

X_{\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal P}_{\bullet}
over
\overline{S(T)^{\natural}}
.

Proof.

This follows from (
18.1
).
∎

Definition 18.3
.

Set

(A_{\rm zar}(X_{\underline{\lambda},\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E_{\underline{\lambda}}),P):=R\pi_{\underline{\lambda},{\rm zar}*}((A_{\rm zar}({\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}}/S(T)^{\natural},{\cal E}^{\bullet}),P)).

and we call

(A_{\rm zar}(X_{\underline{\lambda},\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E_{\underline{\lambda}})),P)\in{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(f^{-1}({\cal O}_{T}))

the
p
-adic zariskian filtered Steenbrink complex
of

E_{\underline{\lambda}}
on
X_{\underline{\lambda},\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/(S(T)^{\natural},{\cal J},\delta)
.

We state the following for our memory:

Proposition 18.4
.

There exists the following canonical isomorphism
:

(18.4.1)

\displaystyle{\rm gr}^{P}_{k}A_{\rm zar}(X_{\underline{\lambda},\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E_{\underline{\lambda}})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{j\geq\max\{-k,0\}}\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\mu}=2j+k+1}a_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu},\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}*}(Ru_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu},T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(E_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu},T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}

\displaystyle\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\rm crys}^{(2j+k)}(\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\mu},T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T})))[-2j-k].

Definition 18.5
.

Set

\displaystyle(A_{\rm zar}(X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}}/S(T)^{\natural},E^{(\star)}),P):=(s(\cdots\longrightarrow a^{(m)}_{*}(\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\lambda}=m+1}A_{\rm zar}(X_{\underline{\lambda},\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E_{\underline{\lambda}}))\longrightarrow\cdots),\delta(L,P)).

We call
(A_{\rm zar}(X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}}/S(T)^{\natural},E^{(\star)}),P)

the
semi-cosimplicial
p
-adic Steenbrink complex
of
E
.

We have the following natural filtered morphism

\displaystyle(A_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E),P)\longrightarrow(A_{\rm zar}(X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}}/S(T)^{\natural},E^{(\star)}),P).

Proposition 18.6
.

The following diagram is commutative
:

(18.6.1)

\begin{CD}Ru_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}(E))@>{\theta\wedge,\sim}>{}>A_{\rm zar}(X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E^{(\star)})\\
@A{\simeq}A{}A@A{}A{}A\\
Ru_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}(E))@>{\theta\wedge,\sim}>{}>A_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E).\end{CD}

Here the right vertical morphism is an underlying morphism of
the filtered morphism

(18.6.2)

(A_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E),P)\longrightarrow(A_{\rm zar}(X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E^{(\star)}),P).

Proof.

Obviously the diagram (
18.6.1
) is commutative.
(
18.6
) follows from (
6.11.1
), (
16.3
) and
(
18.1
).
∎

Corollary 18.7
.

The morphism

(18.7.1)

A_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E)\longrightarrow A_{\rm zar}(X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E^{(\star)})

is a quasi-isomorphism.

Proof.

This follows from (
18.6
).
∎

Proposition 18.8

(
cf.
[
KH
, p. 1270]
).

There exists a filtered morphism

(18.8.1)

\displaystyle(H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E),P)\longrightarrow(A_{\rm zar}(X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}}/S(T)^{\natural},E^{(\star)}),P)

fitting into the following commutative diagram
:

(18.8.2)

\begin{CD}\widetilde{R}u_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(E^{(\star)})\langle u\rangle)=H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E)\\
@V{\simeq}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
Ru_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}(E^{(\star)}))@>{\sim}>{}>A_{\rm zar}(X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}}/S(T)^{\natural},E^{(\star)})\\
@A{\simeq}A{}A@A{}A{\simeq}A\\
Ru_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}(E))@>{\sim}>{}>A_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}}/S(T)^{\natural},E).\end{CD}

Consequently the morphism

(18.8.3)

\displaystyle H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E)\longrightarrow A_{\rm zar}(X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}}/S(T)^{\natural},E^{(\star)})

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

By (
6.14.1
) and (
18.6.1
)
we have the following commutative diagram

(18.8.4)

\begin{CD}\widetilde{R}u_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(E^{(\star)})\langle u\rangle)@>{\sim}>{}>Ru_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X^{(\star)}_{T_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}(E^{(\star)}))\\
@A{\simeq}A{}A@A{\simeq}A{}A\\
\widetilde{R}u_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(E)\langle u\rangle)@>{\sim}>{}>Ru_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}(E))\end{CD}

\begin{CD}@>{\theta\wedge,\sim}>{}>A_{\rm zar}(X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E^{(\star)})\\
@A{\simeq}A{}A\\
@>{\theta\wedge,\sim}>{}>A_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E).\end{CD}

It is almost trivial to check that the upper composite morphism in
(
18.8.4
) induces the following filtered morphism

(18.8.5)

(\widetilde{R}u_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(E^{(\star)})\langle u\rangle),P)\longrightarrow(A_{\rm zar}(X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E),P).

Indeed, since
d\log t\in P_{1}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}
,
the following composite morphism

\displaystyle{\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle

\displaystyle\longrightarrow{\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural}}

\displaystyle\overset{d\log t\wedge}{\longrightarrow}{\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}/P_{0}

induces the following morphism

\displaystyle P_{k}({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow(P_{k+1}+P_{0})({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}})/P_{0}

for each
i\in{\mathbb{N}}
.
Hence we obtain the following morphism of complexes:

\displaystyle P_{k}({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow(\cdots\longrightarrow(P_{2j+k+1}+P_{j})({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex},(\bullet)}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}})/P_{j}\longrightarrow\cdots).

Hence we obtain (
18.8
).
∎

Corollary 18.9
.

There exists the following diagram

(18.9.1)

\displaystyle(H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E),P)\longrightarrow(A_{\rm zar}(X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}}/S(T)^{\natural},E^{(\star)}),P)\longleftarrow(A_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E),P)

which induces the following isomorphisms

(18.9.2)

\displaystyle H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}A_{\rm zar}(X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}}/S(T)^{\natural},E^{(\star)})\overset{\sim}{\longleftarrow}A_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E).

For an integer
k
,
we can calculate the complex

{\rm gr}^{P}_{k}A_{\rm zar}(X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E)

as follows
:

\displaystyle{\rm gr}^{P}_{k}A_{\rm zar}(X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E)={\rm gr}^{\delta(L,P)}_{k}s(\cdots\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\lambda}=m+1}A_{\rm zar}(X_{\underline{\lambda},\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E_{\underline{\lambda}})\longrightarrow\cdots)

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{m\geq 0}\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\lambda}=m+1}\bigoplus_{j\geq\max\{-(k+m),0\}}\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\mu}=2j+k+m+1}a_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}*}Ru_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu},T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(E_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu},T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{{\rm crys},\underline{\mu}}(\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}))

\displaystyle[-k-2m-2j](-j-k-m).

Hence we obtain the following spectral sequence
:

(18.9.3)

\displaystyle E_{1}^{-k,q+k}=\bigoplus_{m\geq 0}\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\lambda}=m+1}\bigoplus_{j\geq\max\{-(k+m),0\}}\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\mu}=2j+k+m+1}R^{q-2j-k-2m}f_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu},T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}

\displaystyle(E_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu},T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(2j+k+m)}_{\underline{\mu},{\rm crys}}(\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}))(-j-k-m;v)\Longrightarrow R^{q}f_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}(E))\quad(q\in{\mathbb{Z}}).

Theorem 18.10

(
E_{2}
-
degeneration).

Let the notations be as in
§
14
.
Then the spectral sequence
(
18.9.3
)

degenerates at
E_{2}
modulo torsion.

Proof.

As in the proofs in §
13
,
the proof of this theorem is the same as that of
[
Nak4
, (5.4.3)]
.
∎

Now we can give another proof of (
17.5
) without using
the crystalline Fujisawa’s morphism (
17.4
) in the previous section.

Theorem 18.11

(
Comparison theorem of weight filtrations).

Let the notations be as in
§
14
.
Let
{}_{1}P
,
{}_{2}P
,
{}_{3}P
be the induced filtrations on

R^{q}f_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}})

by the filtered complexes

(A_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}),P)
,

(A_{\rm zar}(X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}),P)

and
(H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}),P)
, respectively.
Let
{}_{i}P_{K}
be the induced filtration on

R^{q}f_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}})\otimes_{\cal V}K
by
{}_{i}P
.
Then

\displaystyle{}_{1}P_{K}={}_{2}P_{K}={}_{3}P_{K}.

Proof.

The filtration
{}_{1}P_{K}

extends to a convergent
F
-isocrystal
on
\overset{\circ}{T}/{\cal V}
locally on
\overset{\circ}{T}
.
Similarly we can prove that
{}_{2}P_{K}
and
{}_{3}P_{K}
also
extend to a convergent
F
-isocrystal
on
\overset{\circ}{T}/{\cal V}
locally on
\overset{\circ}{T}
.
By using these results, a fundamental result in
[
O1
, (3.17)]
,
we may assume that
S
is the log point of a perfect field of characteristic
p>0
.
By the standard technique, we may assume that
\overset{\circ}{S}
is an affine
smooth scheme over a finite field
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
and then we may assume that

\overset{\circ}{S}={\rm Spec}({\mathbb{F}}_{q})
. In this case

{}_{1}P_{K}={}_{2}P_{K}={}_{3}P_{K}
by the purity of the Frobenius weight
for the crystalline cohomology of a proper smooth scheme over
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
.
∎

Remark 18.12
.

Let the notations be as in
[
F2
, (5.29)]
.
By using arguments in this section (but without using
the morphism in
[
F2
, (5.26)]
),
we can prove that there exists an isomorphism

\displaystyle H^{q}(Y,A)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}H^{q}(Y,K)

of mixed Hodge structures.
This is a much simpler proof than his proof in [loc. cit.].

We conclude this section by establishing
a relation between the crystalline Fujisawa’s morphism
and the morphisms in (
18.9.1
).
The following proposition is quite surprising to me
because I first thought that
the following diagram is not commutative at all.
It also tells us why we do not need
the crystalline Fujisawa’s morphism to prove (
17.5
)
in this section.

Proposition 18.13
.

The following diagram is commutative:

(18.13.1)

\begin{CD}A_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E)@>{\psi}>{}>H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E)\\
@V{\simeq}V{}V@V{}V{\simeq}V\\
A_{\rm zar}(X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E^{(\star)})=A_{\rm zar}(X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E^{(\star)}).\end{CD}

Proof.

This immediately follows from the commutativity of
the diagrams (
17.3.3
)
and (
18.8.2
).
∎

In fact, we can also obtain the following:

Theorem 18.14
.

There exists a filtered morphism

(18.14.1)

\psi^{(\star)}\colon(A_{\rm zar}(X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E),P)\longrightarrow(H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E),P)

such that
the composite morphism

(18.14.2)

\displaystyle(A_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E),P)\longrightarrow(A_{\rm zar}(X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E),P)\overset{\psi^{(\star)}}{\longrightarrow}(H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E),P)

is equal to
\psi
.
It fits into the following commutative diagram
:

(18.14.3)

\begin{CD}\widetilde{R}u_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}(E^{(\star)})\langle u\rangle)=H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E)\\
@V{\simeq}V{}V@A{}A{}A\\
Ru_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}(E^{(\star)}))@>{\sim}>{}>A_{\rm zar}(X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}}/S(T)^{\natural},E^{(\star)})\\
@A{\simeq}A{}A@A{}A{\simeq}A\\
Ru_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}(E))@>{\sim}>{}>A_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}}/S(T)^{\natural},E).\end{CD}

Proof.

We obtain the filtered complex
(A^{\prime}_{\rm zar}(X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E),P)

as in the previous section.
Then we obtain the following morphism

(A^{\prime}_{\rm zar}(X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E),P)\longrightarrow(H^{\prime}_{\rm zar}(X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E),P)

by the following morphism

\displaystyle\psi^{(\star),{\rm loc}}{}^{\prime}\colon\bigoplus_{\underline{\lambda}}\bigoplus_{j,q}({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{q+1}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}}/\overset{\circ}{T}})/P_{j}({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{q+1}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}}/\overset{\circ}{T}})\longrightarrow\underset{q,\underline{\lambda}}{\bigoplus}{\cal O}_{T}\langle u\rangle\otimes_{{\cal O}_{T}}{\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{q+1-\#\underline{\lambda}}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}/\overset{\circ}{T}}}

defined by

(18.14.4)

\displaystyle\psi^{(\star),{\rm loc}}{}^{\prime}:=\sum_{\underline{\lambda}}\sum_{j\geq 0}\sum_{\{\underline{\mu}\,|\,\#\underline{\mu}\,\geq\,j+1\}}u^{[\#\underline{\mu}-(j+1)]}\otimes{\rm Res}^{\prime}{}^{\underline{\lambda}}_{{\cal P}_{\bullet\underline{\mu}}}

(cf. (
17.2.5
)) as in the previous section.
Here
{\rm Res}^{\prime}{}^{\underline{\lambda}}_{{\cal P}_{\bullet\underline{\mu}}}
is the following residue morphism

{\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}/P_{j}({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}}/\overset{\circ}{T}})\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{T}\langle u\rangle\otimes_{{\cal O}_{T}}{\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet-\#\underline{\mu}}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}/\overset{\circ}{T}}}

with respect to
\underline{\mu}
.
We leave the rest of the proof to the reader because
the proof is the same as that of (
17.2
).
∎

19
p
-adic variational filtered log hard Lefschetz conjecture

In this section we prove the
p
-adic variational filtered log hard Lefschetz conjecture
(
1.8
) with respect to the weight filtration
when there exists a fiber obtained by a projective semistable family
over a complete discrete valuation ring in any characteristic.

First let us recall the first log crystalline Chern class defined in
[
Nak4
, (1.8.16)]
,
which is the log version of the first log crystalline Chern class defined in
[
BO2
, (3.1)]
.

Let
(T,{\cal J},\delta)
be a fine log PD-scheme on which
p
is locally nilpotent.
Let
T_{0}
be the exact closed log subscheme defined by
{\cal J}
.
Let
Y
be a fine simplicial log scheme over
T_{0}
.
Let
M_{Y/T}
be a sheaf on
{\rm Crys}(Y/T)
such that,
for an object
T^{\prime}
of
{\rm Crys}(Y/T)
,

\Gamma(T^{\prime},M_{Y/T}):=\Gamma(T^{\prime},M_{T^{\prime}})
.
Let
i\colon Y_{\rm zar}\longrightarrow(Y/T)_{\rm crys}
be the canonical morphism
defined by
\Gamma(U,i^{*}E):=E((U,U,0))
, where
U
is an open log subscheme of
Y
and
let
{\cal J}_{Y/T}
be a sheaf on
{\rm Crys}(Y/T)
defined by

\Gamma((U^{\prime},T^{\prime},\delta^{\prime}),{\cal J}_{Y/T}):=\Gamma(T^{\prime},{\rm Ker}({\cal O}_{T^{\prime}}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{U^{\prime}}))
.
Then we have the following exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow 1+{\cal J}_{Y/T}\longrightarrow M_{Y/T}\longrightarrow i_{*}(M_{Y})\longrightarrow 0.

(This is the log version of the exact sequence in
[
BO2
, (3.1.2)]
.)
Since
M_{Y}
and
M_{Y/T}
are sheaves of integral monoids,
the following sequence is exact:

(19.0.1)

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow 1+{\cal J}_{Y/T}\longrightarrow M^{\rm gp}_{Y/T}\longrightarrow i_{*}(M^{\rm gp}_{Y})\longrightarrow 0.

Hence we have the following composite morphism

(19.0.2)

\displaystyle c_{1,{\rm crys}}\colon M_{Y}^{\rm gp}\longrightarrow Ru_{Y/T*}(1+{\cal J}_{Y/T})[1]\overset{\log}{\longrightarrow}Ru_{Y/T*}({\cal J}_{Y/T})[1]\longrightarrow Ru_{Y/T*}({\cal O}_{Y/T})[1].

In
[
Nak4
]
we have called the morphism (
19.0.2
)
the first log crystalline Chern class map of
Y/T
.

Let
Y_{\bullet}
be the Čech diagram of an open covering of
Y
and
let
Y_{\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal Y}_{\bullet}
be an immersion
into a log smooth simplicial log scheme over
T
.
Let
{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}

be the log PD-envelope of this immersion over
(T,{\cal J},\delta)
.
Consider the following complex

\displaystyle M^{\rm gp}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\overset{d\log}{\longrightarrow}{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Y}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{1}_{{\cal Y}_{\bullet}/T}\overset{d}{\longrightarrow}{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Y}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{2}_{{\cal Y}_{\bullet}/T}\overset{d}{\longrightarrow}\cdots.

Denote this complex by

{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\times}_{{\cal Y}_{\bullet}/T}
.
Set

{\cal K}^{\times}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}/T}:={\rm Ker}({\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Y}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\times}_{{\cal Y}_{\bullet}/T}\longrightarrow M^{\rm gp}_{Y_{\bullet}}).

Set also

{\cal J}^{\bullet}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}:={\rm Ker}({\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Y}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Y}_{\bullet}/T}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{Y_{\bullet}}).

By using (
19.0.1
), we obtain the following logarithm

\log\colon{\cal K}^{\times}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}/T}\longrightarrow{\cal J}^{\bullet}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}

as in
[
BO2
, (3.2)]
.
Because the following sequence

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow{\cal K}^{\times}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}/T}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Y}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\times}_{{\cal Y}_{\bullet}/T}\longrightarrow M^{\rm gp}_{Y_{\bullet}}\longrightarrow 0

is exact,
we obtain the following morphism

\displaystyle M^{\rm gp}_{Y}=R\pi_{{\rm zar}*}(M^{\rm gp}_{Y_{\bullet}})\longrightarrow R\pi_{{\rm zar}*}({\cal K}^{\times}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}/T})[1].

Hence we obtain the following composite morphism

(19.0.3)

\displaystyle M^{\rm gp}_{Y}\longrightarrow

\displaystyle R\pi_{{\rm zar}*}({\cal K}^{\times}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}/T})[1]\overset{\log}{\longrightarrow}R\pi_{{\rm zar}*}({\cal J}^{\bullet}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}})[1]\longrightarrow R\pi_{{\rm zar}*}({\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Y}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Y}_{\bullet}/T})[1]

\displaystyle=Ru_{Y/T*}({\cal O}_{Y/T})[1].

In
[
Nak4
, (1.8.17)]
we have proved the following:

Proposition 19.1

(
[
Nak4
, (1.8.17)]
).

The first log crystalline Chern class map

c_{1,{\rm crys}}
in
(
19.0.2
)
is equal to
the morphism
(
19.0.3
)
.

Let the notations be as in §
6
.
Next we define the first log crystalline Chern class map in

\widetilde{R}u_{Y/\overset{\circ}{T}*}({\cal O}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{T}})[1]
by replacing
T
with
\overset{\circ}{T}
.
Namely,
consider the following complex:

\displaystyle{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\times}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}:=(M^{\rm gp}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\overset{d\log}{\longrightarrow}{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{1}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\overset{d}{\longrightarrow}{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{2}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\overset{d}{\longrightarrow}\cdots).

Set

\displaystyle{\cal K}^{\times}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}:={\rm Ker}({\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\times}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\longrightarrow M^{\rm gp}_{Y_{\bullet}})

and

{\cal J}^{\bullet}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}:={\rm Ker}({\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{Y_{\bullet}}).

Then we have the following logarithm morphism

\displaystyle\log\colon{\cal K}^{\times}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\longrightarrow{\cal J}^{\bullet}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}.

and the following morphism as above:

\displaystyle M^{\rm gp}_{Y}\longrightarrow R\pi_{{\rm zar}*}({\cal K}^{\times}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}})[1].

Hence we obtain the following composite morphism

(19.1.1)

\displaystyle M^{\rm gp}_{Y}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow R\pi_{{\rm zar}*}({\cal K}^{\times}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}})[1]\overset{\log}{\longrightarrow}R\pi_{{\rm zar}*}({\cal J}^{\bullet}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}})[1]

\displaystyle\longrightarrow R\pi_{{\rm zar}*}({\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}})[1]=\widetilde{R}u_{Y/\overset{\circ}{T}}({\cal O}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{T}})[1].

We denote this morphism by
\widetilde{c}_{1,{\rm crys}}
.

Finally we define the “Hirsch extension” of
\widetilde{c}_{1,{\rm crys}}
.

Let

\langle{\cal J}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}},U_{S(T)^{\natural}}\rangle

be the PD-ideal of

\Gamma_{{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}}({\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{T}}U_{S(T)^{\natural}})

generated by

{\cal J}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}
and

\Gamma_{{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}},i}({\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{T}}U_{S(T)^{\natural}})

(i\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1})
.
This is indeed a PD-ideal sheaf of

\Gamma_{{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}}({\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{T}}U_{S(T)^{\natural}})
.
Note that any local section of

\langle{\cal J}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}},U_{S(T)^{\natural}}\rangle

is locally nilpotent.
Consider the subgroup

1+\langle{\cal J}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}},U_{S(T)^{\natural}}\rangle
in

\Gamma_{{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}}({\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{T}}U_{S(T)^{\natural}})^{*}
.
Let

\alpha\colon M_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}

be the strucutral morphism.
Identify
{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}^{*}
with the image of it by
\alpha
.
Set

\displaystyle M_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\langle u\rangle:=M_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\oplus_{1+{\cal J}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}}(1+\langle{\cal J}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}},U_{S(T)^{\natural}}\rangle)

and
M^{\rm gp}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\langle u\rangle:=(M_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\langle u\rangle)^{\rm gp}
.
We obtain the following natural morphism

\displaystyle M^{\rm gp}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\langle u\rangle\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{1}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle

by using the morphism

d\log\colon M^{\rm gp}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\longrightarrow\Omega^{1}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}

and the local section
d\log t
.
Consider the following complex:

\displaystyle M^{\rm gp}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\langle u\rangle\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{1}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{2}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle\longrightarrow\cdots.

Denote this complex by
{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\times}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle
.
Set

\displaystyle{\cal K}^{\times}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle:={\rm Ker}({\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\times}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle\longrightarrow M^{\rm gp}_{Y_{\bullet}}).

Set also

{\cal J}^{\bullet}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\langle u\rangle:={\rm Ker}({\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{Y_{\bullet}}).

Then we have the following logarithm morphism

\displaystyle\log\colon{\cal K}^{\times}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle\longrightarrow{\cal J}^{\bullet}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\langle u\rangle.

Because the following exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow{\cal K}^{\times}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\times}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle\longrightarrow M^{\rm gp}_{Y_{\bullet}}\longrightarrow 0

is exact,
we obtain the following morphism

\displaystyle M^{\rm gp}_{Y}\longrightarrow R\pi_{{\rm zar}*}({\cal K}^{\times}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle)[1].

Hence we obtain the following composite morphism

(19.1.2)

\displaystyle M^{\rm gp}_{Y}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow R\pi_{{\rm zar}*}({\cal K}^{\times}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle)[1]\overset{\log}{\longrightarrow}R\pi_{{\rm zar}*}({\cal J}^{\bullet}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\langle u\rangle)[1]

\displaystyle\longrightarrow R\pi_{{\rm zar}*}({\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle)[1]=\widetilde{R}u_{Y/\overset{\circ}{T}}({\cal O}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle)[1].

Proposition-Definition 19.2
.

The morphism (
19.1.2
) is independent of the choices of
an open covering of
s
and
the simplicial immersion
Y_{\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal Q}_{\bullet}

into a log smooth scheme over
S(T)^{\natural}
.
We call the morphism (
19.1.2
) the
Hirsch extension
of

\widetilde{c}_{1,{\rm crys}}
.
We denote it by
\widetilde{c}_{1,{\rm crys}}\langle u\rangle
.

Proof.

To show the independence of the choices is a routine work,
we leave the proof to the reader.
∎

Proposition 19.3
.

The following diagram

(19.3.1)

\begin{CD}M_{Y}^{\rm gp}@>{\widetilde{c}_{1,{\rm crys}}\langle u\rangle}>{}>\widetilde{R}u_{Y/\overset{\circ}{T}}({\cal O}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle)[1]\\
\Big\|@V{}V{}V\\
M_{Y}^{\rm gp}@>{\widetilde{c}_{1,{\rm crys}}}>{}>\widetilde{R}u_{Y/\overset{\circ}{T}}({\cal O}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{T}})[1]\\
\Big\|@V{}V{}V\\
M_{Y}^{\rm gp}@>{c_{1,{\rm crys}}}>{}>Ru_{Y/S(T)^{\natural}}({\cal O}_{Y/S(T)^{\natural}})[1]\end{CD}

is commutative.

Proof.

This follows from the following commutative diagram of exact sequences
and the following two commutative diagrams:

\begin{CD}0@>{}>{}>{\cal K}^{\times}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle @>{}>{}>{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\times}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle @>{}>{}>M^{\rm gp}_{Y_{\bullet}}@>{}>{}>0\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\Big\|\\
0@>{}>{}>{\cal K}^{\times}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}@>{}>{}>{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\times}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}@>{}>{}>M^{\rm gp}_{Y_{\bullet}}@>{}>{}>0\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\Big\|\\
0@>{}>{}>{\cal K}^{\times}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural}}@>{}>{}>{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\times}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural}}@>{}>{}>M^{\rm gp}_{Y_{\bullet}}@>{}>{}>0,\end{CD}

\begin{CD}{\cal K}^{\times}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle @>{\log}>{}>{\cal J}^{\bullet}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\langle u\rangle\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
{\cal K}^{\times}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}@>{\log}>{}>{\cal J}^{\bullet}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\\
@V{}V{}V\Big\|\\
{\cal K}^{\times}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural}}@>{\log}>{}>{\cal J}^{\bullet}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\end{CD}

and

\begin{CD}{\cal J}^{\bullet}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\langle u\rangle @>{\subset}>{}>{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
{\cal J}^{\bullet}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}@>{\subset}>{}>{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\\
\Big\|@V{}V{}V\\
{\cal J}^{\bullet}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}@>{\subset}>{}>{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural}}.\end{CD}

∎

Proposition 19.4
.

Let
Y/S
and
T/S
be as in
§
6
.
The following composite morphism

(19.4.1)

\displaystyle{\cal O}_{Y}^{*}\subset M_{Y}^{\rm gp}\overset{c_{1,{\rm crys}}}{\longrightarrow}Ru_{Y/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{Y/S(T)^{\natural}})[1]\overset{N_{\rm zar}[1]}{\longrightarrow}Ru_{Y/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{Y/S(T)^{\natural}})[1]

is zero.
Consequently
N_{\rm zar}(\lambda)=0
in

R^{2}g_{Y/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{Y/S(T)^{\natural}})

for a local section of
\lambda\in R^{1}g_{Y/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{Y}^{*})
.

Proof.

Let the notations be as in §
6
.
Consider the following complex

\displaystyle{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\times}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural}}:={\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}^{*}\overset{d\log}{\longrightarrow}{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{1}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural}}\overset{d}{\longrightarrow}{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{2}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural}}\overset{d}{\longrightarrow}\cdots.

Set
{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}}}{\cal K}^{\times}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural}}:={\rm Ker}({\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\times}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural}}\longrightarrow{\cal O}^{*}_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}})
.
Then we have the following exact sequence:

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}}}{\cal K}^{\times}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural}}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\times}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural}}\longrightarrow{\cal O}^{*}_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}}\longrightarrow 0.

Let
c_{1,{\rm dR}}\colon{\cal O}^{*}_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural}}[1]
be
the following composite morphism

(19.4.2)

\displaystyle{\cal O}^{*}_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}}}{\cal K}^{\times}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural}}[1]\overset{\log}{\longrightarrow}{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural}}[1].

By the log version of the argument of
[
BO2
, (3.3)]
,
the following diagram is commutative:

\begin{CD}{\cal O}_{Y}^{*}@>{c_{1,{\rm crys}}}>{}>Ru_{Y/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{Y/S(T)^{\natural}})[1]\\
\Big\|\Big\|\\
{\cal O}_{Y}^{*}@>{c_{1,{\rm dR}}}>{}>R\pi_{{\rm zar}*}({\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural}})[1].\end{CD}

Consider also the following complex

\displaystyle{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\times}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}:={\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}^{*}\overset{d\log}{\longrightarrow}{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{1}_{{\cal Q}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\overset{d}{\longrightarrow}{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{2}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\overset{d}{\longrightarrow}\cdots.

Let
c^{\prime}_{1,{\rm dR}}\colon{\cal O}^{*}_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}[1]
be
the following composite morphism
