\displaystyle{\cal O}^{*}_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}}}{\cal K}^{\times}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}[1]\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}[1].

Obviously we have the following commutative diagram

\begin{CD}{\cal O}^{*}_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}}@>{c_{1,{\rm dR}}}>{}>{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}/S(T)^{\natural}}[1]={\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}/S(T)^{\natural}}[1]\\
\Big\|@A{}A{}A@A{}A{}A\\
{\cal O}^{*}_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}}@>{c^{\prime}_{1,{\rm dR}}}>{}>{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}[1]@>{\subset}>{}>{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle[1].\end{CD}

Hence we have the following commutative diagram

\begin{CD}{\cal O}^{*}_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}}@>{c_{1,{\rm dR}}}>{}>{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural}}[1]\\
\Big\|@A{}A{}A\\
{\cal O}^{*}_{Y_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}}@>{}>{}>{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}}}P_{0}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle[1].\end{CD}

By this commutative diagram
we obtain the following commutative diagram

(19.4.3)

\begin{CD}{\cal O}_{Y}^{*}@>{c_{1,{\rm crys}}}>{}>Ru_{Y/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{Y/S(T)^{\natural}})[1]\\
\Big\|@A{}A{}A\\
{\cal O}_{Y}^{*}@>{}>{}>P_{0}\widetilde{R}u_{Y/\overset{\circ}{T}*}({\cal O}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle)[1].\end{CD}

By (
10.3.1
) and (
10.7.1
) we obtain the following commutative diagram:

(19.4.4)

\begin{CD}Ru_{Y/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{{\cal Q}/S(T)^{\natural}})[1]@>{N_{\rm zar}[1]}>{}>Ru_{Y/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{{\cal Q}/S(T)^{\natural}})[1]\\
@A{}A{}A@A{}A{}A\\
P_{0}\widetilde{R}u_{Y/\overset{\circ}{T}*}({\cal O}_{{\cal Q}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle)[1]@>{N_{\rm zar}[1]}>{}>P_{-2}\widetilde{R}u_{Y/\overset{\circ}{T}*}({\cal O}_{{\cal Q}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle)[1]=0.\end{CD}

By (
19.4.3
) and (
19.4.4
) we see that
the composite morphism (
19.4.1
) is zero.
∎

Let
X/S
be an SNCL scheme.
By (
19.2
)
we have the following morphism

\displaystyle c_{1,{\rm crys}}\langle u\rangle\colon M_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}}^{\rm gp}\longrightarrow\widetilde{R}u_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle)[1].

Hence the natural morphism

\displaystyle\widetilde{R}u_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle)\longrightarrow Ru_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}({\cal O}_{X^{(\star)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle)=H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}}/S(T)^{\natural})

induces the following morphism

\displaystyle c_{1,{\rm crys}}\langle u\rangle\colon M_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}}^{\rm gp}\longrightarrow H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}}/S(T)^{\natural})[1].

It is obvious that the following diagram is commutative:

\begin{CD}M_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}}^{\rm gp}=M_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}}^{\rm gp}=M_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}}^{\rm gp}\\
@V{c_{1,{\rm crys}}}V{}V@V{c_{1,{\rm crys}}\langle u\rangle}V{}V@V{c_{1,{\rm crys}}\langle u\rangle}V{}V\\
Ru_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}})[1]@<{\simeq}<{}<\widetilde{R}u_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\langle u\rangle)[1]@>{\simeq}>{}>H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural})[1].\end{CD}

Proposition 19.5
.

Let
\lambda
be a a line bundle on
X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}
.
Then the morphism

\displaystyle c_{1,{\rm crys}}\langle u\rangle(\lambda)\cup\colon H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E)\longrightarrow H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E)

is the underlying morphism
of the following filtered morphism

(19.5.1)

\displaystyle c_{1,{\rm crys}}\langle u\rangle(\lambda)\cup\colon(H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E),P)\longrightarrow(H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E),P).

Proof.

By the proof of (
19.4
)
we see that
c_{1,{\rm crys}}\langle u\rangle

factors through

P_{0}H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural})
.
Hence
c_{1,{\rm crys}}\langle u\rangle(\lambda)\cup
is a filtered morphism

(H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}),P)\longrightarrow(H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}),P)
by (
11.2.8
).
∎

Next we construct a morphism

(19.5.2)

\displaystyle{\cal O}_{X}^{*}[-1]\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}}(A_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E),P)\longrightarrow(A_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}),P)

Here we endow
{\cal O}_{X}^{*}[-1]
with the trivial filtration.
To define this morphism, we prove the following:

Proposition 19.6
.

Assume that
\overset{\circ}{X}
is quasi-compact.
Endow
P_{0}\widetilde{R}u_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}})
with the trivial filtration.
Then there exists the following canonical morphism

(19.6.1)

\displaystyle P_{0}\widetilde{R}u_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}})\otimes^{L}_{f^{-1}({\cal O}_{T})}(A_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E),P)\longrightarrow(A_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E),P).

Proof.

Let
X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal P}_{\bullet}
be the simplicial immersion
in §
6
.
Then we have the following wedge product

\displaystyle\wedge\colon P_{0}({\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{q^{\prime}}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}})\otimes_{f^{-1}_{\bullet}({\cal O}_{T})}({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{q+1}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}})\longrightarrow{\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{q^{\prime}+q+1}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}.

This morphism induces the following morphism

\displaystyle\wedge\colon

\displaystyle P_{0}({\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{q^{\prime}}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}})\otimes_{f^{-1}_{\bullet}({\cal O}_{T})}({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{q+1}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}/P_{j})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow{\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{q^{\prime}+q+1}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}/P_{j}\quad(j\in{\mathbb{N}})

and

\displaystyle\wedge\colon P_{0}({\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{q^{\prime}}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}})\otimes_{f^{-1}_{\bullet}({\cal O}_{T})}P_{2j+k+1}({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{q+1}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow P_{2j+k+1}({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{q^{\prime}+q+1}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}})\quad(j,k\in{\mathbb{N}}).

Hence we have the following morphism

\displaystyle P_{0}\widetilde{R}u_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}})\otimes^{L}_{f^{-1}({\cal O}_{T})}(A_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E),P)\longrightarrow

\displaystyle R\pi_{{\rm zar}*}(P_{0}({\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}}))\otimes_{f^{-1}({\cal O}_{T})}R\pi_{{\rm zar}*}((A_{\rm zar}({\cal P}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural},{\cal E}^{\bullet}),P))\longrightarrow

\displaystyle R\pi_{{\rm zar}*}(P_{0}({\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}})\otimes_{f^{-1}_{\bullet}({\cal O}_{T})}(A_{\rm zar}({\cal P}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural},{\cal E}^{\bullet}),P))\overset{\wedge}{\longrightarrow}

\displaystyle R\pi_{{\rm zar}*}((A_{\rm zar}({\cal P}_{\bullet}/S(T)^{\natural},{\cal E}^{\bullet}),P))=(A_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E),P).

∎

Proposition 19.7
.

There exists the canonical morphism
(
19.5.2
)
.

Proof.

By the proof of (
19.4
),

\widetilde{c}_{1,{\rm crys}}\colon{\cal O}_{X}^{*}[-1]\longrightarrow\widetilde{R}u_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}})

):

Proposition 19.8
.

The induced morphism

\displaystyle\underset{j\geq\max\{-k,0\}}{\bigoplus}{\cal O}_{X}^{*}[-1]\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}}a^{(2j+k)}_{T_{0}*}(Ru_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k)}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(E_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k)}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\rm crys}^{(2j+k)}(\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T})))[-2j-k]\longrightarrow

\displaystyle\underset{j\geq\max\{-k,0\}}{\bigoplus}a^{(2j+k)}_{T_{0}*}(Ru_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k)}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(E_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k)}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\rm crys}^{(2j+k)}(\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T})))[-2j-k]

by the morphism
(
19.5.2
)
using the identification of

{\rm gr}^{P}_{k}(A_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural},E))

with

\underset{j\geq\max\{-k,0\}}{\bigoplus}a^{(2j+k)}_{T_{0}*}(Ru_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k)}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}*}(E_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k)}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\rm crys}^{(2j+k)}(\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T})))[-2j-k]

induces the first crystalline Chern class map of
\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k)}_{T_{0}}

on each direct factor.

Proof.

).
∎

Definition 19.9

(
cf.
[
Nak2
, (9.8)]
).

Let
\lambda:=c_{1,\star}({\cal L})\in R^{2}f_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}})

be the log cohomology
class of an invertible sheaf
{\cal L}
on
\overset{\circ}{X}
.
We say that
\lambda
is compatible with the spectral sequence (
16.3.2
)
if the induced morphism of the left cup product of
\lambda
on the

E_{1}
-terms of (
16.3.2
) is equal
to the induced morphism of the restriction of
{\cal L}

to various
{X}{}^{(k)}
’s
(k\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0})
.

The following has an application for
the weight spectral sequence (
16.3.3
) for the trivial coefficient,
which has not been proved in
[
Nak2
]
(but which has been expected)
(see
[
Nak2
]
and
[
A
]
for the application of this theorem):

Corollary 19.10
.

The cohomology class
\lambda
is compatible with the spectral sequence

(
16.3.2
)
.

Proposition 19.11
.

The following diagram

(19.11.1)

\begin{CD}{\cal O}_{X}^{*}[-1]\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}}(A_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}),P)@>{}>{}>(A_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}),P)\\
@V{c_{1,{\rm crys}}|_{{\cal O}_{X}^{*}[-1]}\otimes^{L}\psi}V{}V@V{}V{\psi}V\\
(H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}),P)\otimes^{L}_{f^{-1}({\cal O}_{T})}(H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}),P)@>{\cup}>{}>(H_{\rm zar}(X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{S}),P)\end{CD}

is commutative.

Proof.

Because the morphism
c_{1,{\rm crys}}|_{{\cal O}_{X}^{*}[-1]}
factors through

R\pi_{{\rm zar}*}(P_{0}({\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}/\overset{\circ}{T}})
,
there is no local residue of
c_{1,{\rm crys}}(\lambda)
for a line bundle
\lambda

on
\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}
.
By the local description of
\psi^{\rm loc}
(
17.2.5
),
we obtain the commutativity of (
19.11.1
).
∎

Let
L
be a relatively ample line bundle on

\overset{\circ}{X}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}
;
L
defines
the cohomology class in
H^{1}(Y,{\cal O}_{Y}^{*})
, which we denote by
L
again.
Consider the following composite morphism

(19.11.2)

\displaystyle{\cal O}_{Y}^{*}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}M_{Y}^{\rm gp}\overset{c_{1,{\rm crys}}}{\longrightarrow}Ru_{Y/T*}({\cal O}_{Y/S(T)^{\natural}})[1],

which we denote by
c_{1,{\rm crys}}
again.
Then we obtain the log cohomology class

\eta=c_{1,{\rm crys}}(L)
of
L

in
R^{2}f_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}})
.

Let the notations be as in §
14
.
In particular,
\overset{\circ}{T}
is assumed to be a
p
-adic formal
{\cal V}
-scheme.
Assume that the relative dimension of

\overset{\circ}{X}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}\longrightarrow T_{1}
is of pure dimension
d
.
In
[
Nak4
]
we have conjectured the following:

Conjecture 19.12

(
p
-adic variational filtered log hard Lefschetz conjecture).

(1)
The following cup product

(19.12.1)

\eta^{i}\colon R^{d-i}f_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}\longrightarrow(R^{d+i}f_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}})(i)

is an isomorphism.

(2)

In fact,
\eta^{i}
is the following isomorphism of filtered sheaves:

(19.12.2)

\eta^{i}\colon(R^{d-i}f_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}},P)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}((R^{d+i}f_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}})(i),P).

In the rest of this section
we would like to prove that (
19.12
) (1) implies (
19.12
) (2).

Theorem 19.13
.

Assume that
\overset{\circ}{T}
is the formal spectrum of
the Witt ring of a perfect field
\kappa
.
Then
(
19.12
) (1)
in this case implies
(
19.12
) (2)

in this case.

Proof.

Let
{\cal A}_{0}
be a smooth
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-algebra contained in
\kappa

such that
X
has a proper SNCL model
{\cal X}
over

{\cal S}_{0}:=({\rm Spec}({\cal A}_{0}),({\mathbb{N}}\oplus{\cal A}^{*}_{0}\longrightarrow{\cal A}_{0}))

(cf. the argument before
[
Nak1
, (3.1)]
)
and
L
comes from a line bundle
{\cal L}
on
{\cal X}
:

X={\cal X}\times_{{\cal S}_{0}}s
and
L={\cal L}\times_{{\cal S}_{0}}s
.
Let
{\cal A}
be a
p
-adically formally smooth algebra over
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}

which is a lift of
{\cal A}_{0}
. Endow
{\rm Spf}({\cal A})

with a hollow log structure
{\mathbb{N}}\oplus{\cal A}^{*}\longrightarrow{\cal A}
and
let
{\cal S}
be the resulting log formal scheme over

{\rm Spf}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})=({\rm Spf}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}),{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{*})
.
The log formal scheme
{\cal S}
has a PD-ideal
p{\cal O}_{{\cal S}}
,
which defines an exact closed immersion
{\cal S}_{0}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal S}
.
Let
\{{\cal P}^{q}_{k}\}_{k=0}^{\infty}

be the weight filtration on

R^{q}f_{{\cal X}/{\cal A}*}({\cal O}_{{\cal X}/{\cal A}})_{\mathbb{Q}}
.
We claim that

(19.13.1)

\displaystyle{\cal P}^{d+i}_{k}\cap{\rm Im}(c_{1,{\rm crys}}({\cal L})^{i}\cup)=c_{1,{\rm crys}}({\cal L})^{i}\cup({\cal P}^{d-i}_{k-2i}).

Indeed, by (
19.5
),

c_{1,{\rm crys}}({\cal L})\in P_{2}R^{2}f_{{\cal X}/{\cal A}*}({\cal O}_{{\cal X}/{\cal A}})_{\mathbb{Q}}
.
Hence, by (
11.6.3
), the right hand side on
(
19.13.1
) is contained in the left hand side of (
19.13.1
).
By (
14.9
),

{\cal P}^{q}_{k}
and
{\cal P}^{q}_{\infty}

extend to objects
{\cal P}^{q,{\rm conv}}_{k}
and

{\cal P}^{q,{\rm conv}}_{\infty}

of
F{\textrm{-}}{\rm Isoc}({\rm Spec}({\cal A}_{0})/{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
, respectively.
Because the category
F{\textrm{-}}{\rm Isoc}({\rm Spec}({\cal A}_{0})/{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})

is an abelian category (
[
O1
, p. 795]
),
both hand sides on (
19.13.1
) extend to objects of

F{\textrm{-}}{\rm Isoc}({\rm Spec}({\cal A}_{0})/{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
.
We may assume that
{\rm Spec}({\cal A}_{0})
is connected.
By
[
O1
, (4.1)]
, for a closed point
\overset{\circ}{s}
of
{\rm Spec}({\cal A}_{0})
,
the pull-back functor
\overset{\circ}{s}{}^{*}\colon{\rm Isoc}({\rm Spec}({\cal A}_{0})/{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\longrightarrow{\rm Isoc}(\overset{\circ}{s}/{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
is faithful.
Hence it suffices to prove that

(19.13.2)

\displaystyle\overset{\circ}{s}{}^{*}({\cal P}^{d+i,{\rm conv}}_{k})\cap{\rm Im}(\overset{\circ}{s}{}^{*}(c_{1,{\rm crys}}({\cal L})^{i}\cup))=\overset{\circ}{s}{}^{*}c_{1,{\rm crys}}({\cal L})^{i}\cup({\cal P}^{d-i,{\rm conv}}_{k-2i}).

Consider the
p
-adic enlargement

{\cal W}(\overset{\circ}{s})

of
{\rm Spec}({\cal A}_{0})/{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
.
Because
{\rm Spf}({\cal A})
is formally smooth over
{\rm Spf}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
,
there exists a morphism
{\cal W}(\overset{\circ}{s})\longrightarrow{\rm Spf}({\cal A})

fitting into the following commutative diagram

\begin{CD}\overset{\circ}{s}@>{\subset}>{}>{\cal W}(\overset{\circ}{s})\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
{\rm Spec}({\cal A}_{0})@>{}>{}>{\rm Spf}({\cal A})\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
{\rm Spec}({\mathbb{F}}_{p})@>{}>{}>{\rm Spf}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}).\end{CD}

Then the log structure of
{\cal W}(s)
is
the inverse image of the log structure of

({\rm Spf}({\cal A}),({\mathbb{N}}\oplus{\cal A}^{*}\longrightarrow{\cal A}))
.
By the proof of
[
Nak4
, (5.1.30)]
, we see that

(19.13.3)

\displaystyle(\overset{\circ}{s}{}^{*}({\cal P}{}^{\rm conv}_{\infty}))_{{\cal W}(\overset{\circ}{s})}=R^{q}f_{{\cal X}_{s}/{\cal W}(s)*}({\cal O}_{{\cal X}_{s}/{\cal W}(s)})_{\mathbb{Q}}

and

(19.13.4)

\displaystyle(\overset{\circ}{s}{}^{*}({\cal P}{}^{\rm conv}_{k}))_{{\cal W}(\overset{\circ}{s})}=P_{k}R^{q}f_{{\cal Z}_{s}/{\cal W}(s)*}({\cal O}_{{\cal Z}_{s}/{\cal W}(s)})_{\mathbb{Q}}.

Since the residue field
k(\overset{\circ}{s})
is a finite field,
the equality of (
19.13.2
) at the value
{\cal W}(\overset{\circ}{s})
holds by
the existence of the weight spectral sequence (
16.3.2
)
and by the purity of the weight as in the proof of
[
NS
, (2.18.2)]

and the action of the pull-back of the absolute Frobenius endomorphism
of
{\cal X}
on the line bundle is the multiplication by
p
.
This shows the equality (
19.13.2
)
because
{\cal W}(\overset{\circ}{s})
is a formally smooth lift
of
\overset{\circ}{s}
over
{\rm Spf}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
.
∎

Theorem 19.14
.

(
19.12
) (1)
implies
(
19.12
) (2)
.

Proof.

By using (
19.13
),
one can obtain (
19.14
) as in (
15.2
)
whose proof is the same as that of
[
Nak4
, (5.4.7)]
.
∎

Corollary 19.15
.

Let the notations be as in
(
19.12
)
if
there exists an exact closed point
t

of each connected component of
S(T)^{\natural}
such that
the fiber
{\cal X}_{t}
of
{\cal X}
over
t
is the log special fiber of
a projective strict semistable family over a complete discrete valuation ring of
equal characteristic or mixed characteristics.

Proof.

In
[
Nak4
, (5.5.19)]
(resp.
[
Nak4
, (5.5.16)]
) we have proved that the conjecture
(
19.12
) (1) is true if
there exists an exact closed point
t

of each connected component of
S(T)^{\natural}
such that
the fiber
{\cal X}_{t}
of
{\cal X}
over
t
is the log special fiber of
a projective strict semistable family over a complete discrete valuation ring of
mixed characteristics (resp. equal characteristic).
Now (
19.15
) follows from (
19.14
).
∎

The following is essentially the same as
(
1.11
):

Corollary 19.16
.

Let the assumptions be as in
(
19.15
)
.
Let
k
be a nonnegative integer and let
i
be a nonnegative integer.
Set

(19.16.1)

\displaystyle(P_{k}R^{d-i}f_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}})_{0}

\displaystyle:={\rm Ker}(\eta^{i+1}\colon P_{k}R^{d-i}f_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow P_{k}\{R^{d+i+2}f_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}})(i+2)\}).

Then

\displaystyle P_{k}R^{q}f_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}=

\displaystyle(P_{k}R^{q}f_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}})_{0}

\displaystyle\oplus\eta(P_{k-2}R^{q-2}f_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}})_{0}

\displaystyle\oplus\eta(P_{k-4}R^{q-4}f_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}})_{0}\oplus\cdots.

Moreover, assume that
\overset{\circ}{T}
is connected.
Let
b_{k}^{q}
be the rank of locally free sheaf

P_{k}R^{q}f_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}

on
\overset{\circ}{T}.

Then

b_{k}^{0}\leq b_{k+2}^{2}\leq b_{k+4}^{4}\leq\cdots

up to
b_{*}^{d}
and

b_{k}^{1}\leq b_{k+2}^{3}\leq b_{k+4}^{5}\leq\cdots

up to
b_{*}^{d}
.
In particular,

b_{\infty}^{0}\leq b_{\infty}^{2}\leq b_{\infty}^{4}\leq\cdots

up to
b_{\infty}^{d}
and

b_{\infty}^{1}\leq b_{\infty}^{3}\leq b_{\infty}^{5}\leq\cdots

up to
b_{\infty}^{d}
.

Proof.

The argument is well-known and easy.
Indeed, (
19.16
) immediately
follows from the following obvious commutative diagram

\begin{CD}R^{d-i-2}f_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}@>{\eta^{i+2},\sim}>{}>R^{d+i+2}f_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}\\
@V{\eta}V{}V@A{}A{\eta}A\\
R^{d-i}f_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}@>{\eta^{i},\sim}>{}>R^{d+i}f_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}\end{CD}

and the strict compatibility of
\eta
with respect to the weight filtration ((
19.12.2
)).
∎

20
p
-adic polarizations on log crystalline cohomology sheaves of projective SNCL schemes

In this section we give results for the
p
-adic variational monodromy-conjecture
and the
p
-adic variational filtered log hard Lefschetz conjecture for
log crystalline cohomologcal sheaves of SNCL schemes in certain cases
if the standard conjecture due to Grothendieck is true.
See also
[
A
]
and see
[
Sa2
]
for the
l
-adic case.
Let the notations be as in §
14
.
Consider the following cup product

(20.0.1)

\displaystyle(?,?)\colon

\displaystyle R^{q}f_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}})\otimes_{{\cal O}_{T}}R^{q^{\prime}}f_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow R^{q+q^{\prime}}f_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}})\quad(q,q^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{N}}).

Proposition 20.1
.

Let the notations be as before
(
19.4
)
.
For simplicity of notation, let us denote
N_{\rm zar}
simply by
N
.
Then the following hold
:

(1)

Let
L
be a line bundle on
X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}
. Set

\lambda=c_{1,{\rm crys}}(L)
and

l:=\lambda\cup\colon R^{q}f_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}})\longrightarrow R^{q+2}f_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}}).

Then

(l(x),y)=(x,l(y))=l((x,y))
.

(2)

N\circ l=l\circ N
.

Proof.

(1): (1) follows from (
11.6.1
).

(2): By (
11.1.1
),

(N\lambda)\cup x+l(Nx)=N(l(x))
for a local section
x
of

R^{q}f_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}})
.
By (
19.4
),
N\lambda=0
. Hence

l(Nx)=N(l(x))
. This means that
l\circ N=N\circ l
.
∎

Now assume that

\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}
is projective and of pure relative dimension
d
.
Let the notations be as in §
14
.
Then we have the following pairing:

(20.1.1)

\displaystyle\langle?,?\rangle\colon

\displaystyle R^{q}f_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}})_{\mathbb{Q}}\otimes_{{\cal K}_{T}}R^{2d-q}f_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}})_{\mathbb{Q}}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow R^{2d}f_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}})_{\mathbb{Q}}\overset{{\rm Tr}^{A}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}}}{\longrightarrow}{\cal K}_{T}(-d).

Here
{\rm Tr}^{A}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}}

is the trace morphism defined in (
16.13
).

Proposition 20.2
.

The following formulas hold
:

(20.2.1)

\displaystyle\langle N(x),y\rangle+\langle x,N(y)\rangle=0

for local sections

x\in R^{q}f_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}})_{\mathbb{Q}}

and

y\in R^{2d-q}f_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}})_{\mathbb{Q}}

and

(20.2.2)

\displaystyle\langle l(x),y\rangle=\langle x,l(y)\rangle

for local sections

x\in R^{q-2}f_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}})_{\mathbb{Q}}

and

y\in R^{2d-q}f_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}})_{\mathbb{Q}}
.

Proof.

By (
11.1.1
),

(N(x),y)+(x,N(y))=N((x,y))
.
By (
16.7.2
),

\displaystyle R^{2d}f_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}})=E^{0,2d}_{2}.

By (
16.6
) (1),
E^{-k,2d+k}_{1}=0
for
k\not=0
.
Because
N
induces a morphism

N\colon E^{0,2d}_{2}\longrightarrow E^{2,2d-2}_{2}
, this is the zero morphism.
Hence (
20.2.1
) follows.
The formula (
20.2.2
) follows from (
20.1
) (1).
∎

In the following let the notations be as in (
19.12
).
Let us recall the following:

Theorem 20.3

(
Hard Lefschetz theorem for crystalline cohomologies).

Let
Y
be a projective smooth scheme over
\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}

of pure dimension
e
. Let
L
be a relative ample line bundle on
Y/\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}
.
Let
\eta=c_{1,{\rm crys}}(L)
be the crystalline Chern class of
L

in
R^{2}f_{Y/\overset{\circ}{T}*}({\cal O}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{T}})

(
[
BO2
, §3]
)
.
Then the following cup product

(20.3.1)

\eta^{i}\colon R^{e-i}f_{Y/\overset{\circ}{T}*}({\cal O}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{T}})_{\mathbb{Q}}\longrightarrow R^{e+i}f_{Y/\overset{\circ}{T}*}({\cal O}_{Y/\overset{\circ}{T}})_{\mathbb{Q}}(i)

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

As in the proof of (
19.13
),
this follows from the usual hard Lefschetz theorem for crystalline cohomologies
of a projective smooth scheme over a finite field in
[
KM
]
.
(However there is a gap in the proof of weak Lefschetz theorem due to Berthelot
as already mentioned in the proof of (
13.1
).)
∎

Assume that
\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(0)}_{T_{1}}
is projective over
\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}
.
Then
\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k)}_{T_{1}}

(k\in{\mathbb{N}})

is also projective over
\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}
.
Following
[
GN
, (2.8)]
,
set

H^{-k,q,j}:=\begin{cases}R^{q+d-2j-k}f_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k)}_{T_{1}}/\overset{\circ}{T}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}(-j-k)&(j\geq\max\{0,-k\}),\\
0&{\rm otherwise}.\end{cases}

(This is a picking out of the direct factor of the
E_{1}
-term of
the weight spectral sequence (
16.3.3
)
twisted by
d
for the case of the trivial coefficient.)
We define the following morphisms

d^{\prime}\colon H^{-k,q,j}\longrightarrow H^{-k+1,q+1,j+1},

d^{\prime\prime}\colon H^{-k,q,j}\longrightarrow H^{-k+1,q+1,j},

\nu\colon H^{-k,q,j}\longrightarrow H^{-k+2,q,j+1}(-1)

by the following formula:

d^{\prime}(x)=\rho(x),\quad d^{\prime\prime}(x)=G(x),\quad\nu(x):={\rm id}

for a local section
x
of

R^{q+d-2j-k}f_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{1}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k)}_{T_{1}}/\overset{\circ}{T}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}(-j-k)
.
Here the morphism
d^{\prime\prime}
is different from
the morphism
d^{\prime\prime}
in
[
GN
, p. 145]
about signs.
Because one can easily check that

H^{k+2,q,j+k+1}=\begin{cases}0&(j=0),\\
H^{-k,q,j}&(j\geq 1),\end{cases}

(20.3.2)

{\rm Ker}(\nu^{k+1}\colon H^{-k,q,j}\longrightarrow H^{k+2,q,j+k+1}(-k-1)):=\begin{cases}H^{-k,q,0}&(j=0),\\
0&(j\geq 1).\end{cases}

Let
E^{\bullet\bullet}_{1}
be the
E_{1}
-term of the spectral sequence (
16.3.3
)
for the case of the trivial coefficient.
Set

\displaystyle H^{-k,q}

\displaystyle:=\bigoplus_{j\geq\max\{0,-k\}}H^{-k,q,j}=E_{1}^{-k,q+d+k}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}\quad(k,q\in{\mathbb{Z}}).

Set
H:=\bigoplus_{k,q\in{\mathbb{Z}}}H^{-k,q}
,

l_{1}:=\nu\colon H^{-k,q}\longrightarrow H^{-k+2,q}(-1)
and
l_{2}:=l
.
By the construction of
\nu
,
l_{1}^{k}={\rm id}\colon H^{-k,q}\longrightarrow H^{k,q}

is an isomorphism for
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
.
By (
20.3
)
l_{2}^{q}\colon H^{k,-q}\longrightarrow H^{k,q}
is an isomorphism for
q\in{\mathbb{N}}
..
Obviously
l_{1}\circ l_{2}=l_{2}\circ l_{1}
.
Hence
(H,l_{1},l_{2})
satisfies the axiom of the bigraded Lefschetz module in

[
GN
, (4.1)]
(see also the notion of the (bi)graded polarized Hodge structure in

[
Sa1
, (4.1.1), (4.2.1)]
).
Set

\displaystyle H^{-k,-q}_{0}:=H^{-k,-q}\cap{\rm Ker}(l_{1}^{k+1})\cap{\rm Ker}(l_{2}^{q+1}).

Then we have the following Lefschetz decomposition

\displaystyle H^{kq}=\bigoplus_{r,s\in{\mathbb{N}}}l_{1}^{r}l_{2}^{s}H^{k-2r,q-2s}_{0}.

By (
20.3.2
)

\displaystyle H^{-k,-q}_{0}:=H^{-k,-q,0}\cap{\rm Ker}(l_{2}^{q+1})

\displaystyle={\rm Ker}(l_{2}^{q+1}\colon R^{d-k-q}f_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k)}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}(-k)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow R^{d-k+q+2}f_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k)}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}(-k+q+1)

\displaystyle=(R^{d-k-q}f_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k)}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}})_{0}(-k).

Here
0
means the primitive part.
Let

\displaystyle\langle?,?\rangle\colon H\otimes_{{\cal K}_{T}}H\longrightarrow{\cal K}_{T}

be the morphism obtained by
the Poincaré duality morphism for crystalline cohomologies (
[
O1
, (3.12)]
).

Set

(20.3.3)

d:=G+\rho\colon H^{-k,q}\longrightarrow H^{-k+1,q}

as in (
16.5
).
Then
d^{2}=0
.
We also have the following relation

dl_{i}=l_{i}d\quad(i=1,2),

Set

\displaystyle\epsilon(a):=(-1)^{\frac{a(a-1)}{2}}\quad(a\in{\mathbb{Z}}).

For local sections
x
of

H^{-k,q,j}=R^{q+d-2j-k}f_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k)}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}})(-j-k)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}

and
y
of

H^{k,-q,j+k}=R^{-q+d-2j-k}f_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k)}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}})(-j)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}},

set

\psi(x,y):=\epsilon(-k-q-d)\langle x,y\rangle:=\epsilon(-k-q-d)\langle x,y\rangle_{(j,k)}\in{\cal K}_{T}(-d),

where
\langle x,y\rangle:=\langle?,?\rangle_{(j,k)}

means the Poincare duality morphism for

\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k)}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}
:

\displaystyle R^{q+d-2j-k}f_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k)}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}})(-j-k)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}\otimes_{{\cal K}_{T}}

\displaystyle R^{-q+d-2j-k}f_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k)}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}})(-j)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}\overset{\cup}{\longrightarrow}

\displaystyle R^{2(d-2j-k)}f_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k)}_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/S(T)^{\natural}*}({\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k)}_{T_{0}}/\overset{\circ}{T}})(-2j-k)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}\overset{\rm Tr(-2j-k)}{\longrightarrow}{\cal K}_{T}(-d).

The following is a
p
-adic analogue of
[
GN
, (3.5)]
:

Proposition 20.4

(
cf.
[
GN
, (3.5)]
).

The following relations hold
:

(1)

\psi(y,x)=(-1)^{d}\psi(x,y)
.

(2)

\psi(l_{1}y,x)+\psi(x,l_{1}y)=0
.

(3)

\psi(l_{2}y,x)+\psi(x,l_{2}y)=0
.

(4)

\psi(d^{\prime}y,x)+\psi(x,d^{\prime\prime}y)=0
.

(5)

\psi(d^{\prime\prime}y,x)+\psi(x,dy)=0
.

Proof.

The proof is the same as that of
[
GN
, (3.5)]
as follows.

(1): (1) follows from

\langle x,y\rangle=(-1)^{(d-k)^{2}-q^{2}}\langle y,x\rangle=(-1)^{d+k+q}\langle y,x\rangle
and

\displaystyle\dfrac{(-k-q-d)^{2}-(-k-q-d)+(k+q-d)^{2}-(k+q-d)}{2}

\displaystyle=k^{2}+q^{2}+d^{2}+2kq+d

\displaystyle=k+q\mod 2.

(2): For local sections
x
of
H^{-(k-2),q,j}

and
y
of
H^{k,-q,j+k}
,

\psi(l_{1}y,x)=\epsilon(-k-q-d)\langle x,y\rangle
and

\psi(x,l_{1}y)=\epsilon(-(k-2)-q-d)\langle x,y\rangle
.
It is easy to see that
\epsilon(a+2)\epsilon(a)=-1
.

(3): We can easily prove that (3) holds as in (2).

(4): For local sections
x
of

H^{-(k-1),q-1,j}

and
y
of

H^{k,-q,j+k}

\psi(d^{\prime}x,y)=\epsilon(-k-q-d)\langle d^{\prime}x,y\rangle

\psi(x,d^{\prime\prime}y)=\epsilon(-(k-1)-(q-1)-d)\langle x,d^{\prime\prime}y\rangle=\epsilon(-k-q+2-d)\langle x,d^{\prime\prime}y\rangle=-\epsilon(-k-q-d)\langle x,d^{\prime\prime}y\rangle
.

(5): (5) follows from (1) and (4).
∎

Theorem 20.5
.

Assume that
\overset{\circ}{T}
is a
p
-adic formal
{\cal V}
-scheme.
For an exact closed point
t
of
S(T)^{\natural}
, let
K_{0}(t)
be the fraction field
of the Witt ring
{\cal W}(\kappa_{t})
, where
\kappa_{t}
is the residue field of
t
.
Assume that there exists an exact closed point
t
of
each connected component of
S(T)^{\natural}
such that
for any
\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k)}

(k\in{\mathbb{N}})
and for any
i\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
the following Chern class morphism

\displaystyle{\rm CH}^{i}(\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k)}_{t})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}K_{0}(t)\longrightarrow H^{2i}_{\rm crys}(\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k)}_{t}/{\cal W}(\kappa_{t}))\otimes_{{\cal W}(\kappa_{t})}K_{0}(t)

is surjective
and

H^{2i+1}_{\rm crys}(\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k)}_{t}/{\cal W}(\kappa_{t}))\otimes_{{\cal W}(\kappa_{t})}K_{0}(t)=0

and the Hodge standard conjecture by Grothendieck is true for

\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k)}_{t}
. Then the variational
p
-adic monodromy-weight conjecture and
the variational
p
-adic filtered log hard Lefschetz conjecture are true.

Proof.

By the assumptions we can define a argument about positivity due to
Steenbrink-Satio (
[
Sa1
]
) for

H^{2i}_{\rm crys}(\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k)}_{t}/{\cal W}(\kappa_{t}))\otimes_{{\cal W}(\kappa_{t})}K_{0}(t)
as in the case where the base field is
{\mathbb{C}}

(cf.
[
It
]
,
[
RX
]
).
Hence the
p
-adic monodromy-weight conjecture and the
p
-adic log hard Lefschetz conjecture are true
for
X_{t}
(cf. [loc. cit.]).
Consequently (
20.5
) follows as in the proof of (
19.13
).
∎

21
Comparison theorems between our filtered complex (resp.
our filtered dga) with Kim-Hain’s filtered complex (resp. their filtered dga)

In this section we give the right construction of

(s(C(W\widetilde{\omega}[u])),P)
in
[
KH
]
.
The method of our construction is quite different from the construction
of
(s(C(W\widetilde{\omega}[u])),P)
in [loc. cit.] because we use results in
[
Nak4
]
and
the previous sections and because
the local log smooth scheme for our construction of

(s(C(W\widetilde{\omega}[u])),P)
is not necessarily a log smooth lift of a given log smooth scheme.
Our method does not need the admissible lift defined in
[
M
]
nor
the local calculation of the log blow up of the product of two admissible lifts in

[
Hy
]
(and
[
M
]
), and simplifies arguments about the construction and
the properties of several log de Rham-Witt complexes.
The main results in this section are comparison theorems between
(s(C(W\widetilde{\omega}[u])),P)

and
(s_{\rm TW}(C(W\widetilde{\omega}[u])\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}),P)

with our filtered complex
(H_{\rm zar},P)

and our filtered dga
(H_{{\rm zar},{\rm TW}},P)
in previous sections,
respectively, in the case where
the base log scheme is the log point of a perfect field of characteristic
p>0
.
As a result we see that
(H_{\rm zar},P)
and
(H_{{\rm zar},{\rm TW}},P)

are generalizations of
(s(C(W\widetilde{\omega}[u])),P)
and

(s_{\rm TW}(C(W\widetilde{\omega}[u])\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}),P)
, respectively,
to the case where the base log scheme is more general.

Let the notations be as in §
4
.
In this section we assume that
S
is the log point
s
of a perfect field

\kappa
of characteristic
p>0
. Let
{\cal W}_{n}

(n\geq 1)
be the Witt ring of

\kappa
and let
{\cal W}_{n}(s)
be the canonical lift of
s
over
{\cal W}_{n}
.
Let
Y
be a log smooth scheme of Cartier type over
s
.
Let
{\cal W}_{n}\Omega^{\bullet}_{Y}
and
{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{Y}
be the
log de Rham-Witt complex of
Y/s
defined in
[
Hy
]
,
[
M
]
and
[
Nak1
]
.
Let
\theta_{n}=
“
[d\log t]
”
\in{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{1}_{Y}
be
the canonical closed one form defined in

[
M
]
.
Let
{\cal W}_{n}(Y)
be the canonical lift of
Y
over
{\cal W}_{n}
.
Let
F_{{\cal W}_{n}(Y)}\colon{\cal W}_{n}(Y)\longrightarrow{\cal W}_{n}(Y)
be the Frobenius endomorphism of

{\cal W}_{n}(Y)
.
The sheaf
{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{i}_{Y}

(i\in{\mathbb{N}})
defined in
[
M
]
and
[
Nak4
]

is a
{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{Y})
-module,
where
{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{Y})
is the “reverse” Witt sheaf of
Y

in the sense of
[
Nak1
]
, that is,

{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{Y})={\cal H}^{0}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal Y}_{n}/{\cal W}_{n}(s)})
locally,
where
{\cal Y}_{n}
is a local log smooth lift of
Y
over
{\cal W}_{n}(s)
.
Let
{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{Y})^{\prime}
be
the “obverse” Witt sheaf in the sense of
[
Nak1
, §7]
,
that is,
{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{Y})^{\prime}
is the structure sheaf of
{\cal W}_{n}(Y)
.
Let

\displaystyle C^{-1}\colon{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{Y})^{\prime}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{Y})

be the inverse Cartier isomorphism in
[
Nak1
, (7.5)]
.
Let
F_{n}
be the evaluation of the crystal
F
of
{\cal O}_{Y/{\cal W}_{n}(s)}
-module
at the object
{\cal W}_{n}(Y)
of
the crystalline site of
Y
over
({\cal W}_{n}(s),p{\cal W}_{n},[~])
.
By using this isomorphism,

F_{n}
becomes a
{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{Y})
-module and each

F_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{Y})}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{i}_{Y}

is a
{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{Y})
-module.
Consequently
F_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{Y})}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{i}_{Y}

is a
{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{Y})^{\prime}
-module and we obtain
the complex
F_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{Y})}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{Y}

of
{\cal W}_{n}
-modules.
Let
F_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{Y})}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{Y}\langle u\rangle
be the Hirsch extension
of
F_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{Y})}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{Y}
with respect to
the
g^{-1}({\cal W}_{n})
-linear morphism

\varphi\colon{\cal W}_{n}u\owns u\longmapsto\theta_{n}\in{\rm Ker}({\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{1}_{Y}\longrightarrow{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{2}_{Y})
.
Here, to define the
g^{-1}({\cal W}_{n})
-linearity, we use the following composite morphism

\displaystyle{\cal W}_{n}\longrightarrow{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{Y})^{\prime}\overset{C^{-1},\sim}{\longrightarrow}{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{Y}).

Let us recall the following:

Proposition 21.1

(
[
Nak4
, (2.2.7)]
).

Let
R\colon{\cal W}_{n+1}\widetilde{\Omega}^{1}_{Y}\longrightarrow{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{Y}
be the projection defined in

[
Nak4
, (2.2.6)]
.
Then
R(\theta_{n+1})=\theta_{n}
.

In the following we always assume that
F is a flat locally nilpotent quasi-coherent
{\cal O}_{Y/{\cal W}_{n}(s)}
-module.

The following is a generalization of Kim-Hain’s result
in
[
KH
, Lemma 6]
(however see (
21.3
) below):

Theorem 21.2
.

The natural morphism

\displaystyle F_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{Y})}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{Y}\langle u\rangle\longrightarrow F_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{Y})}{\cal W}_{n}\Omega^{\bullet}_{Y}

is a quasi-isomorphism.

Proof.

As in

[
Hy
, (1.4.3)]

we have the following exact sequence

(21.2.1)

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow F_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{Y})}{\cal W}_{n}\Omega^{\bullet}_{Y}[-1]\overset{\theta_{n}\wedge}{\longrightarrow}F_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{Y})}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{Y}\longrightarrow F_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{Y})}{\cal W}_{n}\Omega^{\bullet}_{Y}\longrightarrow 0.

We can also derive this exact sequence immediately from
(
4.8.4
) by using the local definitions of

{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{q}_{Y}
and
{\cal W}_{n}\Omega^{q}_{Y}

(q\in{\mathbb{N}})
.
(In the following argument we do not use the injectivity of

\theta_{n}\wedge
in [loc. cit.]; instead we use the injectivity in a more general situation,
that is, we use the injectivity of
d\log t
in (
4.8.4
).)
We claim that the following sequence

(21.2.2)

\small{0\longrightarrow{\cal H}^{q-1}(F_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{Y})}{\cal W}_{n}\Omega^{\bullet}_{Y})\overset{\theta_{n}\wedge}{\longrightarrow}{\cal H}^{q}(F_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{Y})}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\bullet}_{Y})\longrightarrow{\cal H}^{q}(F_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{Y})}{\cal W}_{n}\Omega^{\bullet}_{Y})\longrightarrow 0\hskip 9.24994pt(q\in{\mathbb{N}})}

obtained by (
21.2.1
) is exact.
Indeed, this is a local problem. We may assume that there exists an
immersion
Y\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal Q}
into a log smooth scheme over
{\cal W}_{n}(s)
.
Let
\overline{\mathfrak{E}}
be the log PD-envelope of this immersion over

({\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s}),p{\cal W}_{n},[~])
.
Let
(\overline{\cal F},\overline{\nabla})
be the corresponding
{\cal O}_{\overline{\mathfrak{E}}}
-module
with integrable connection.
Set
({\cal F},\nabla):=(\overline{\cal F},\overline{\nabla})\otimes_{{\mathfrak{D}}(\overline{{\cal W}_{n}(s)})}{\cal W}_{n}

and
{\cal Q}:=\overline{\cal Q}\times_{\overline{{\cal W}_{n}(s)}}{\cal W}_{n}(s)
.
By the log Poincaré lemma and
[
Nak4
, (2.1.12.1), (2.2.14.1)]
,
we have the following equalities:

\displaystyle{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\cal Q}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}/{\cal W}_{n}(s)}=Ru_{Y/{\cal W}_{n}(s)*}(\epsilon^{*}_{Y/{\cal W}_{n}(s)/{\cal W}_{n}}(F))=F_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{Y})}{\cal W}_{n}\Omega^{\bullet}_{Y}

and

\displaystyle{\cal F}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\cal Q}}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\cal Q}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})}=\widetilde{R}u_{Y/{\cal W}_{n}*}(F)=F_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{Y})}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{Y}.

Hence the sequence (
21.2.2
) is equal to the exact sequence
(
4.8.4
) in the special case.
Consequently the sequence (
21.2.2
) is exact.
The rest of the proof is the same as that of (
4.8
).
∎

Remark 21.3
.

As pointed out in
[
EY
, (3.36) (2)]
,
there is a gap in the proof of
[
KH
, Lemma 6]
.
(
21.2
) fills this gap. See also (
4.4
) and (
4.12
) (1).

Set

\displaystyle{\cal W}_{n}\langle\langle u\rangle\rangle:=\prod_{m\in{\mathbb{N}}}{\cal W}_{n}u^{[m]}.

and

F_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{Y})}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{Y}\langle\langle u\rangle\rangle:={\cal W}_{n}\langle\langle u\rangle\rangle\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}}F_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{Y})}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{Y},

where the differential of

F_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{Y})}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{Y}\langle\langle u\rangle\rangle

is defined as in (
4.1.7
).

Theorem 21.4
.

The natural morphism

\displaystyle F_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{Y})}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{Y}\langle\langle u\rangle\rangle\longrightarrow F_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{Y})}{\cal W}_{n}\Omega^{\bullet}_{Y}

is a quasi-isomorphism.

Proof.

By (
21.2.1
) we see that the following sequence

(21.4.1)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\longrightarrow F_{n}\overset{\theta_{n}\wedge}{\longrightarrow}F_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{Y})}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{1}_{Y}\overset{d\log t\wedge}{\longrightarrow}\cdots\overset{\theta_{n}\wedge}{\longrightarrow}F_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{Y})}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{j-2}_{Y}

\displaystyle\overset{d\log t\wedge}{\longrightarrow}F_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{Y})}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{j-1}_{Y}\overset{\theta_{n}\wedge}{\longrightarrow}F_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{Y})}{\cal W}_{n}\Omega^{j}_{Y}\longrightarrow 0.

is exact for
j\in{\mathbb{N}}
.
Because
{\cal W}_{n}\Omega^{i}_{Y}
and
{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{i}_{Y}

(i\in{\mathbb{N}})

are quasi-coherent
{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{Y})
-modules by the local definitions of

{\cal W}_{n}\Omega^{i}_{Y}
and
{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{i}_{Y}
, the following sequence

(21.4.2)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\Gamma(V,F_{n})\overset{\theta_{n}\wedge}{\longrightarrow}\Gamma(V,F_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{Y})}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{1}_{Y})\overset{\theta_{n}\wedge}{\longrightarrow}\cdots\overset{\theta_{n}\wedge}{\longrightarrow}\Gamma(V,F_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{Y})}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{j-2}_{Y})

\displaystyle\overset{\theta_{n}\wedge}{\longrightarrow}\Gamma(V,F_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{Y})}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{j-1}_{Y})\overset{\theta_{n}\wedge}{\longrightarrow}\Gamma(V,F_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{Y})}{\cal W}_{n}\Omega^{j}_{Y})\longrightarrow 0

are exact for any log open affine subscheme of
Y
.
The rest of the proof is the same as that of (
4.3
).
∎

Corollary 21.5
.

The natural morphism

(21.5.1)

\displaystyle F_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{Y})}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{Y}\langle u\rangle\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}F_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{Y})}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{Y}\langle\langle u\rangle\rangle

is a quasi-isomorphism.

Theorem 21.6
.

There exists a canonical isomorphism

(21.6.1)

\displaystyle\widetilde{R}u_{Y/{\cal W}_{n}*}(F\langle u\rangle)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}F_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{Y})}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{Y}\langle u\rangle.

This isomorphism is compatible with the pull-back of a morphism

Y\longrightarrow Y^{\prime}
over
s\longrightarrow s^{\prime}
, where
Y^{\prime}/s^{\prime}
is an analogous object to

Y/s;
it is also compatible with the projections

\widetilde{R}u_{Y/{\cal W}_{n}*}(F\langle u\rangle)\longrightarrow\widetilde{R}u_{Y/{\cal W}_{n-1}*}(F\langle u\rangle)

and

F_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{Y})}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{Y}\langle u\rangle\longrightarrow F_{n-1}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n-1}({\cal O}_{Y})}{\cal W}_{n-1}\widetilde{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{Y}\langle u\rangle
.

Proof.

We leave the detailed proof to the reader because the proof of this theorem
is the same as that of
[
Nak4
, (2.2.13)]
if one uses
(
3.13
).
∎

Let the notations be as in §
6
.
We define the filtered complex

(s(C(W\widetilde{\omega}[u])),P)
defined in
[
KH
, Lemma 12]

from our point of view as follows.
To define it, we develop theory of log de Rham-Witt complexes

{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\bullet}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}
and

{\cal W}_{n}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}
for an element
\underline{\lambda}
of
P(\Lambda)
.
(In this section we assume that
\underline{\lambda}\not=\emptyset
.)
Here note that though
X_{\underline{\lambda}}
is not log smooth over
s
,
it is ideally log smooth over
s
.

First let us recall the filtered complex

(\widetilde{R}u_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})}({\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})})),P)

(a special case of (
6.21
)).
Set

(21.6.2)

\displaystyle{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}:={\cal H}^{i}(\widetilde{R}u_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})}({\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})})))\quad(i\in{\mathbb{N}})

and

(21.6.3)

\displaystyle P_{k}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}:={\cal H}^{i}(P_{k}\widetilde{R}u_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})}({\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})})))\quad(i\in{\mathbb{N}},k\in{\mathbb{Z}})

as abelian sheaves on
\overset{\circ}{X}
.
Set
P_{\infty}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}:={\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}
.
(I have not yet claimed that

P_{k}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}
is a subsheaf of

{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}
.)

Proposition 21.7
.

There exists a well-defined product structure

(21.6.1)

\displaystyle\wedge\colon{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}\times{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{j}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}\longrightarrow{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{i+j}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}

which induces a morphism

(21.6.2)

\displaystyle\wedge\colon P_{k}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}\times P_{k^{\prime}}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{j}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}\longrightarrow P_{k+k^{\prime}}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{i+j}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}.

Proof.

Consider a local immersion
X\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal P}
into
a log smooth scheme over
{\cal W}_{n}(s)
.
Let
{\mathfrak{D}}
be the log PD-envelope of this immersion over

({\cal W}_{n}(s),p{\cal W}_{n},[~])
.
Then, by the definition (
21.6.2
),

\displaystyle{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}:={\cal H}^{i}({\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})}).

Let
\omega_{1}\in{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})}

and

\omega_{2}\in{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{j}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})}

be cocycles.
Let
[\omega_{1}]\in{\cal H}^{i}({\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})})
and

[\omega_{2}]\in{\cal H}^{j}({\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})})

be the cohomology classes of
\omega_{1}
and
\omega_{2}
, respectively.
Set

(21.6.3)

\displaystyle[\omega_{1}]\wedge[\omega_{2}]=[\omega_{1}\wedge\omega_{2}].

If one of
[\omega_{1}]
and
[\omega_{2}]
is a coboundary, then

[\omega_{1}\wedge\omega_{2}]
is coboundary since
[\omega_{1}]
and
[\omega_{2}]
are cocycles.
Hence the product (
21.6.3
) is well-defined.
This product structure is independent of the choice of the immersion

X\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal P}
. Indeed, let
X\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal P}^{\prime}
be another immersion
into a log smooth scheme over
{\cal W}_{n}(s)
.
Then, by considering the product
{\cal P}\times_{{\cal W}_{n}(s)}{\cal P}^{\prime}
,
we may assume that there exists the following commutative diagram

\begin{CD}X@>{}>{}>{\cal P}\\
\Big\|@V{}V{}V\\
X@>{}>{}>{\cal P}^{\prime}\end{CD}

over
{\cal W}_{n}(s)
.
Let
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\prime}
be the log PD-envelope of the immersion

X\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal P}^{\prime}
over
({\cal W}_{n}(s),p{\cal W}_{n},[~])
.
Then we have the following commutative diagrams:

\begin{CD}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}={\cal H}^{i}({\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})})\\
\Big\|@A{}A{}A\\
{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}={\cal H}^{i}({\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}^{\prime}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\prime}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\prime}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})}),\end{CD}

\begin{CD}{\cal H}^{i}({\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})})\times{\cal H}^{j}({\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})})@>{\wedge}>{}>{\cal H}^{i+j}({\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})})\\
@A{}A{}A@A{}A{}A\\
{\cal H}^{i}({\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}^{\prime}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\prime}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\prime}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})})\times{\cal H}^{j}({\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}^{\prime}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\prime}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\prime}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})})@>{\wedge}>{}>{\cal H}^{i+j}({\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}^{\prime}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\prime}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\prime}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})}).\end{CD}

The last commutative diagram tells us that the product structure
(
21.6.3
) is independent of the choice of the immersion.
It is clear that the product structure
(
21.6.3
) induces the morphism (
21.6.2
).
∎

Proposition 21.8
.

Set
{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}):={\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{0}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}
.
There exists a well-defined

{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}})
-module structure
on
P_{k}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}

(k\in{\mathbb{Z}}\cup\{{\infty}\})

constructed in the proof of this proposition.
This
{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}})
-module structure induces
a well-defined
{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}})^{\prime}
-module structure
on
P_{k}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}

(k\in{\mathbb{Z}}\cup\{{\infty}\})
.

Proof.

By (
21.6.2
) we have the following morphism

(21.8.1)

\displaystyle\wedge\colon{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}})\times P_{k}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}\longrightarrow P_{k}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}\quad(k\in{\mathbb{Z}}\cup\{{\infty}\}).

This defines a
{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}})
-module structure
on
P_{k}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}
.
Now we have only to prove that there exists
the following well-defined morphism

(21.8.2)

\displaystyle{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}})^{\prime}\longrightarrow{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}).

Let
a_{i}

(i=0,\ldots,n-1)
be
a local section of
{\cal O}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}
.
Set
{\mathfrak{D}}_{\underline{\lambda}}:={\mathfrak{D}}\times_{{\cal P}}{\cal P}_{\underline{\lambda}}
.
Let
\widetilde{a}_{i}\in{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}_{\underline{\lambda}}}

be a lift of
a_{i}
.
As in
[
HK
, (4.9)]
and
[
Nak1
, (7.1.1)]
,
consider the following morphism

(21.8.3)

s_{n}(0,0)\colon{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}})^{\prime}\owns(a_{0},\ldots,a_{n-1})\longmapsto\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}p^{i}\widetilde{a}_{i}^{p^{n-i}}\in{\cal H}^{0}({\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})})={\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}).

As in
[
HK
, (4.9)]
, we can easily check that this morphism
is independent of the choice of the lift
\widetilde{a}_{i}
.
As in the proof of (
21.7
), we can check that this

{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}})^{\prime}
-module structure on

{\cal H}^{0}({\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})})={\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}})
is independent of the choice of
the immersion
X\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal P}
.
∎

Corollary 21.9
.

The wedge products
(
21.6.1
)
and
(
21.6.2
)

are
{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}})^{\prime}
-bilinear and

{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}})
-bilinear.

Remark 21.10
.

I do not check whether the morphism (
21.8.2
) is an isomorphism
in the case
\underline{\lambda}\not=\emptyset
.
More generally, let
(W_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}^{i})^{\prime}
be an abelian sheaf on

\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}}
which is a quotient of

(21.10.1)

\{W_{n}({\cal O}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\bigwedge^{i}(M^{\rm gp}/f^{-1}({\mathbb{Z}}))\}\oplus\{W_{n}({\cal O}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\bigwedge^{i-1}(M^{\rm gp}/f^{-1}({\mathbb{Z}}))\}

divided by a
{\mathbb{Z}}
-submodule
{\cal F}_{n}

generated by the images of
the local sections of the following type

(21.10.2)

(V^{j}(\alpha(a_{1}))\otimes(a_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge a_{i}),0)-p^{j}(0,V^{j}(\alpha(a_{1}))\otimes(a_{2}\wedge\cdots\wedge a_{i}))

(a_{1},\ldots,a_{i}\in M,~0\leq j<n),

where
V^{j}(b):=({\underset{j~{\rm times}}{\underbrace{0,\cdots,0}}},b,0,\ldots,0)
for a
local section
b\in{\cal O}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}

(
[
HK
, (4.6)]
) and a

{\mathbb{Z}}
-submodule

{\cal G}_{n}
, where

{\cal G}_{n}
is a
{\mathbb{Z}}
-submodule generated
by the images of the local sections of
the following type

(21.10.3)

(0,V^{j}(\alpha(a_{2}))\otimes(a_{2}\wedge\cdots\wedge a_{i}))\quad(a_{2},\ldots,a_{i}\in M,~1\leq j<n)

as an abelian sheaf on

X_{{\underline{\lambda}}}
.

It is not difficult to check that
the analogous morphism to
s_{n}
in
[
HK
, (4.9)]

factors through a morphism

(21.10.4)

s_{n}\colon(W_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}})^{\prime}\longrightarrow W_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}\quad(n\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}).

I have not checked whether there exists an isomorphism

\displaystyle({\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}})^{\prime}\longrightarrow{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}

as abelian sheaves because we do not need this property in this book.

Next we define the derivative

d\colon{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}\longrightarrow{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{i+1}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}
as follows.
This derivative has not been defined in
[
KH
]
.

Let
X\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal P}
be a local immersion into
a log smooth scheme over
{\cal W}_{2n}(s)
.
Let
{\mathfrak{D}}
be the log PD-envelope of this immersion over

({\cal W}_{2n}(\overset{\circ}{s}),p{\cal W}_{2n},[~])
.
Set
{\cal P}_{\underline{\lambda},n}:={\cal P}_{\underline{\lambda}}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{2n}}{\cal W}_{n}
.
By the local description of

{\cal P}^{\rm ex}
,
{\mathfrak{D}}
and
{\cal P}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}}

((
2.11
)), we see that

{\cal O}_{\mathfrak{D}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{i}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}}/{\cal W}_{2n}(\overset{\circ}{s})}

(i\in{\mathbb{N}})

is a flat
{\cal W}_{2n}
-module.
Consequently the following sequence is exact:

(21.10.5)

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{\mathfrak{D}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda},n}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})}\overset{p^{n}}{\longrightarrow}{\cal O}_{\mathfrak{D}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}}/{\cal W}_{2n}(\overset{\circ}{s})}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{\mathfrak{D}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda},n}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})}\longrightarrow 0.

The derivative

d\colon{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}\longrightarrow{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{i+1}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}

is, by definition, the boundary morphism

{\cal H}^{i}({\cal O}_{\mathfrak{D}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda},n}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})})\longrightarrow{\cal H}^{i+1}({\cal O}_{\mathfrak{D}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda},n}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})})

obtained by (
21.10.5
).
It is easy to see that this derivative is independent of the choice of
the immersion
X\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal P}
.
Here the reader should note that this independence immediately follows
since we allow not only the lift but also the immersion.
If one allows only the lift, a more complicated proof is necessary
since the product of a lift is not necessarily a lift (cf.
[
M
]
).

Proposition 21.11
.

There exists the following exact sequence
:

(21.11.1)

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow P_{k-1}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}\longrightarrow P_{k}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\mu}=k}a_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}*}({\cal W}_{n}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}}}[-k]\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{{\rm zar},\underline{\mu}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{s}))\longrightarrow 0.

Proof.

Let the notations be as in the proof of (
21.7
).
By the same proof as those of
[
M
, Lemme 1.2]
and
[
KH
, p. 1268]
, the following sequence is locally split:

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\longrightarrow P_{k-1}({\cal O}_{\mathfrak{D}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})})\longrightarrow P_{k}({\cal O}_{\mathfrak{D}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow{\rm gr}_{k}^{P}({\cal O}_{\mathfrak{D}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})})\longrightarrow 0.

Hence the following morphism

\displaystyle{\cal H}^{i}(P_{k-1}({\cal O}_{\mathfrak{D}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})}))\longrightarrow{\cal H}^{i}(P_{k}({\cal O}_{\mathfrak{D}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})}))

is injective.
This implies that the following morphism

\displaystyle P_{k-1}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}\longrightarrow P_{k}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}

is injective.

Because

\displaystyle{\rm gr}_{k}^{P}({\cal O}_{\mathfrak{D}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})})=

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\mu}=k}{\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{\mathfrak{D}}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}}}{\otimes}_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}}}}b_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}*}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})})[-k]\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{{\rm zar},\underline{\mu}}({\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{n}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})))

((
5.9.2
)),
we obtain the following equalities:

\displaystyle{\cal H}^{i}({\rm gr}_{k}^{P}({\cal O}_{\mathfrak{D}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})}))

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\mu}=k}{\cal H}^{i}({\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{\mathfrak{D}}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}}}{\otimes}_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}}}}b_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}*}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})}[-k]\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{{\rm zar},\underline{\mu}}({\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{n}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})))

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\mu}=k}a_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}*}({\cal W}_{n}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}}}[-k]\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{{\rm zar},\underline{\mu}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{s})).

Hence we obtain the exact sequence (
21.11.1
).
∎

Set

\displaystyle({\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\bullet}_{X^{(m)}},P):=\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\lambda}=m+1}({\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\bullet}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}},P)\quad(m\in{\mathbb{N}}).

Then

(21.11.2)

\displaystyle{\rm gr}^{P}_{k}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\bullet}_{X^{(m)}}=\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\lambda}=m+1}\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\mu}=k}a_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}*}({\cal W}_{n}\Omega^{\bullet-k}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{{\rm zar},\underline{\mu}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{s})).

The filtered complexes
({\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\bullet}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}},P)

and
({\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\bullet}_{X^{(m)}},P)
have well-defined product structures
which are compatible with the filtrations
P
’s.
Let
\theta_{\underline{\lambda}}\in{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{1}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}

be the cohomology class “
[d\log t]
” in

{\cal H}^{1}({\cal O}_{\mathfrak{D}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})})
.
It is easy to see that
\theta_{\underline{\lambda}}
is well-defined.
Now consider the Hirsch extension

{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\bullet}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}\langle u\rangle

of
{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\bullet}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}
by the morphism

{\cal W}_{n}u\owns u\longmapsto\theta_{\underline{\lambda}}\in{\rm Ker}({\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{1}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}\longrightarrow{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{2}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}})

and the filtration
P
on

{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\bullet}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}\langle u\rangle
:

(21.11.3)

\displaystyle P_{k}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\bullet}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}\langle u\rangle:=\bigoplus_{j=0}^{\infty}{\cal W}_{n}u^{[j]}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}}P_{k-2j}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\bullet}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}

We also consider the Hirsch extension

{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\bullet}_{X^{(m)}}\langle u\rangle

of
{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\bullet}_{X^{(m)}}
by the morphism

{\cal W}_{n}u\owns u\longmapsto\theta:=\oplus_{|\underline{\lambda}|=m+1}\theta_{\underline{\lambda}}\in{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{1}_{X^{(m)}}
.
Then we obtain the single complex

s({\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\bullet}_{X^{(\bullet)}}\langle u\rangle)

of the double complex
{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\bullet}_{X^{(\bullet)}}\langle u\rangle
,
where we consider the signs of differential morphisms of
the double complex
{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\bullet}_{X^{(\bullet)}}\langle u\rangle

as in the case of the Hirsch PD-filtered complex
H_{\rm zar}
stated in previous sections.
This is nothing but the cosimplicial dga

s(C(W\widetilde{\omega}[u]))
defined in
[
KH
, Lemma 12]

(modulo the ambiguity of the signs of the boundary morphisms of the double complex
in [loc. cit.]).

We define the diagonal filtration
\delta(L,P)
on

s({\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\bullet}_{X^{(\bullet)}}\langle u\rangle)
as usual:

(21.11.4)

\displaystyle\delta(L,P)_{k}s({\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\bullet}_{X^{(\bullet)}}\langle u\rangle)

\displaystyle:=\bigoplus_{m=0}^{\infty}P_{k+m}({\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\bullet}_{X^{(m)}}\langle u\rangle)

\displaystyle:=\bigoplus_{m=0}^{\infty}\bigoplus_{j=0}^{\infty}{\cal W}_{n}u^{[j]}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}}P_{k+m-2j}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\bullet}_{X^{(m)}}.

In the following we denote
\delta(L,P)
simply by
P
by abuse of notation.
In this convention

(21.11.5)

\displaystyle{\rm gr}_{k}^{P}s({\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\bullet}_{X^{(\bullet)}}\langle u\rangle)=\bigoplus_{m=0}^{\infty}\bigoplus_{j=0}^{\infty}{\cal W}_{n}u^{[j]}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}}{\rm gr}^{P}_{k+m-2j}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\bullet}_{X^{(m)}}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{m=0}^{\infty}\bigoplus_{j=0}^{\infty}\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\lambda}=m}\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\mu}=k+m-2j}{\cal W}_{n}u^{[j]}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}}a_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}*}({\cal W}_{n}\Omega^{\bullet-k}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{{\rm zar},\underline{\mu}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{s})).

Next we define the “projection”

(21.11.6)

\displaystyle R\colon{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\bullet}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}\longrightarrow{\cal W}_{n-1}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\bullet}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}\quad(n\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1})

by using the methods in
[
Hy
]
and
[
Nak4
]
with additional arguments.
This operator has not been defined in
[
KH
]
either.

Let
n
be a positive integer and let
i
be a nonnegative integer.
To define the morphism (
21.11.6
), we first assume that there exists an immersion

X\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal P}
into a formally log smooth scheme over
{\cal W}(s)

such that
{\cal P}
has a lift
\varphi\colon{\cal P}\longrightarrow{\cal P}

of the Frobenius endomorphism of
{\cal P}\times_{{\cal W}(s)}s
.
Let
{\cal P}^{\rm ex}
be the exactification of the immersion

X\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal P}
. Then
\varphi\colon{\cal P}\longrightarrow{\cal P}

induces the morphism
\varphi^{\rm ex}\colon{\cal P}^{\rm ex}\longrightarrow{\cal P}^{\rm ex}
.
Let
{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\lambda}
be a formally smooth component of
{\cal P}^{\rm ex}

which corresponds to a smooth component
X_{\lambda}
of
X
.
Let
e_{\lambda}\in M_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}
be a local section corresponding to
{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\lambda}
.
Then
\varphi^{*}(e_{\lambda})=e^{p}_{\lambda}u
for some
u\in{\cal O}^{*}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}
.
Hence
\varphi^{\rm ex}
induces a morphism

{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\lambda}\longrightarrow{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\lambda}
.
Consequently, for a subset
\underline{\lambda}
of
\Lambda
,

\varphi^{\rm ex}
induces a morphism

{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\underline{\lambda}}\longrightarrow{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\underline{\lambda}}
.
Let
{\mathfrak{D}}
be the log PD-envelope of the immersion

X\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal P}^{\rm ex}
over

({\cal W}(s),p{\cal W},[~])
.
Set
{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{n}:={\cal P}^{\rm ex}\times_{{\cal W}(s)}{\cal W}_{n}(s)
,

{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\underline{\lambda},n}:={\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\underline{\lambda}}\times_{{\cal W}(s)}{\cal W}_{n}(s)

and

{\mathfrak{D}}_{n}:={\mathfrak{D}}\times_{{\cal W}(s)}{\cal W}_{n}(s)
.
Consider the following morphism

\displaystyle\varphi^{{\rm ex}*}\colon

\displaystyle{\cal O}_{\mathfrak{D}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}{\Omega}{}^{i}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\underline{\lambda}}/{\cal W}(\overset{\circ}{s})}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}{\Omega}{}^{i}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\underline{\lambda}}/{\cal W}(\overset{\circ}{s})}\quad(i\in{\mathbb{N}}).

Because
{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{i}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\underline{\lambda}}/{\cal W}(\overset{\circ}{s})}
is a sheaf of flat

{\cal O}_{{\cal W}(s)}
-modules by the proof of
[
Nak4
, (1.3.5), (1.7.24)]

and because
\varphi^{{\rm ex}*}
is divisible by
p^{i}

(since
\varphi
is a lift of the Frobenius endomorphism),
the morphism

\displaystyle p^{-(i-1)}\varphi^{{\rm ex}*}\colon

\displaystyle{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}{\Omega}{}^{j}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\underline{\lambda}}/{\cal W}(\overset{\circ}{s})}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}{\Omega}{}^{j}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\underline{\lambda}}/{\cal W}(\overset{\circ}{s})}\quad(j\geq i-1)

is well-defined.
Because the image of the morphism above is contained in

p{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}}}{\Omega}{}^{j}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\underline{\lambda}}/{\cal W}(\overset{\circ}{s})}
for the case
j=i
,
we have the following well-defined morphism (cf.
[
Hy
, Editorial comment (5)]
)

(21.11.7)

\displaystyle p^{-(i-1)}\varphi^{{\rm ex}*}

\displaystyle\colon{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}={\cal H}^{i}({\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}_{n}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{n}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\underline{\lambda},n}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})})\longrightarrow

\displaystyle{\cal H}^{i}({\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}_{n+1}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}_{n+1}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{n+1,\underline{\lambda}}/{\cal W}_{n+1}(\overset{\circ}{s})})={\cal W}_{n+1}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}\quad(i\in{\mathbb{N}}).

Proposition 21.12
.

(1)
The morphism
p^{-(i-1)}\varphi^{{\rm ex}*}
in
(
21.11.7
)

is independent of the choice of
the immersion
X\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal P}
and the lift
\varphi\colon{\cal P}\longrightarrow{\cal P}

of the Frobenius endomorphism of

{\cal P}\times_{{\cal W}(s)}s
.
We set
{\bf p}:=p^{-(i-1)}\varphi^{*}\colon{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}\longrightarrow{\cal W}_{n+1}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}
.

(2)

{\rm Ker}({\bf p}\colon{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}\longrightarrow{\cal W}_{n+1}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}})=0
.

(3)

{\rm Im}({\bf p}\colon{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}\longrightarrow{\cal W}_{n+1}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}})={\rm Im}(p\colon{\cal W}_{n+1}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}\longrightarrow{\cal W}_{n+1}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}})
.

Proof.

(1): Let
X\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal P}^{\prime}
be another immersion and another
lift
\varphi^{\prime}\colon{\cal P}^{\prime}\longrightarrow{\cal P}^{\prime}

of the Frobenius endomorphism of
{\cal P}^{\prime}\times_{{\cal W}(\overset{\circ}{s})}\overset{\circ}{s}
.
Then, by considering the products

({\cal P}^{\rm ex}\times_{{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})}{\cal P}^{\prime}{}^{\rm ex})^{\rm ex}

and
\varphi\times\varphi^{\prime}
, we may assume that
there exists the following commutative diagram

\begin{CD}X@>{\subset}>{}>{\cal P}^{\rm ex}@>{\varphi}>{}>{\cal P}^{\rm ex}\\
\Big\|@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
X@>{\subset}>{}>{\cal P}^{\prime}{}^{\rm ex}@>{\varphi^{\prime}}>{}>{\cal P}^{\prime}{}^{\rm ex}.\end{CD}

Hence we have the following commutative diagram

\begin{CD}{\cal H}^{i}({\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}_{n}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{n}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{n}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})})@>{p^{-(i-1)}\varphi^{{\rm ex}*}}>{}>{\cal H}^{i}({\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}_{n+1}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{n+1}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{n+1}/{\cal W}_{n+1}(\overset{\circ}{s})})\\
@A{\simeq}A{}A@A{}A{\simeq}A\\
{\cal H}^{i}({\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}^{\prime}_{n}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\prime}{}^{\rm ex}_{n}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\prime}{}^{\rm ex}_{n}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})})@>{p^{-(i-1)}\varphi^{\prime}{}^{{\rm ex}*}}>{}>{\cal H}^{i}({\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}^{\prime}_{n+1}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\prime}{}^{\rm ex}_{n+1}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\prime}{}^{\rm ex}_{n+1}/{\cal W}_{n+1}(\overset{\circ}{s})}),\end{CD}

which shows the desired independence.

(2): We may assume that
{\cal P}
is a
formal lift
{\cal X}/{\cal W}(s)
of
X/s
.
Then

(\Omega^{\bullet},\phi):=(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal X}_{\underline{\lambda}}/{\cal W}(\overset{\circ}{s})},\varphi^{*})

satisfies the conditions
(6.0.1)\sim(6.0.5)
in
[
Nak1
]

(these conditions are only the abstract versions of
necessary conditions for
[
BO1
, (8.8)]
).
That is, the following hold:

(21.12.1)
\Omega^{i}=0
for
i<0
.

(21.12.2)
\Omega^{i}

(\forall i\in{\mathbb{N}})
are sheaves of
p
-torsion-free,
p
-adically
complete
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-modules.

(21.12.3)
\phi(\Omega^{i})\subset\{\omega\in p^{i}\Omega^{i}~|~d\omega\in p^{i+1}\Omega^{i+1}\}

(\forall i\in{\mathbb{N}})
.

(21.12.4) Set
\Omega_{1}^{\bullet}:=\Omega^{\bullet}/p\Omega^{\bullet}
.
Then there exists an
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-linear isomorphism

C^{-1}\colon\Omega^{i}_{1}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}{\cal H}^{i}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{1})\quad(\forall i\in{\mathbb{N}}).

(21.12.5) A composite morphism
(
{\rm mod}~p)\circ p^{-i}\phi\colon\Omega^{i}\longrightarrow\Omega^{i}\longrightarrow\Omega^{i}_{1}
factors
through
{\rm Ker}(d\colon\Omega^{i}_{1}\longrightarrow\Omega^{i+1}_{1})

and the following diagram is commutative:

\begin{CD}\Omega^{i}@>{\mod p}>{}>\Omega^{i}_{1}\\
@V{p^{-i}\phi}V{}V@V{}V{C^{-1}}V\\
\Omega^{i}@>{\mod p}>{}>{\cal H}^{i}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{1}).\end{CD}

The only nontrivial property is the existence of the inverse Cartier isomorphism

C^{-1}
in (21.12.4). In the proof of
[
KH
, Lemma 12]

Now set

(21.12.6)

Z^{i}_{n}:=\{\omega\in\Omega^{i}|~d\omega\in p^{n}\Omega^{i+1}\},\quad B^{i}_{n}:=p^{n}\Omega^{i}+d\Omega^{i-1},\quad{\mathfrak{W}}_{n}{\Omega}^{i}=Z^{i}_{n}/B^{i}_{n}.

Then
{\cal W}_{n}{\Omega}^{i}_{X}={\mathfrak{W}}_{n}{\Omega}^{i}
and
the morphism

{\bf p}\colon{\cal W}_{n}{\Omega}^{i}_{X}\longrightarrow{\cal W}_{n+1}{\Omega}^{i}_{X}

is equal to
{\bf p}\colon{\mathfrak{W}}_{n}{\Omega}^{i}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{W}}_{n+1}{\Omega}^{i}
in
[
Nak1
, p. 546]
.
Because the latter morphism is injective (
[
Nak1
, (6.8)]
), we obtain (2).

(3): By
[
Nak1
, p. 546]
,

{\rm Im}({\bf p}\colon{\mathfrak{W}}_{n}{\Omega}^{i}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{W}}_{n+1}{\Omega}^{i})={\rm Im}(p\colon{\mathfrak{W}}_{n+1}{\Omega}^{i}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{W}}_{n+1}{\Omega}^{i})
.
Hence we obtain (3).
∎

Definition 21.13

(
cf.
[
Hy
, (1.3.2)]
).
