(1) We call the morphism

{\bf p}\colon{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}\longrightarrow{\cal W}_{n+1}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}

Hyodo’s multiplication by

p
on

{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}
.

(2) The morphism
R\colon{\cal W}_{n+1}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}\longrightarrow{\cal W}_{n}{\Omega}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}

is, by definition, the unique morphism fitting into the following commutative diagram:

\begin{CD}{\cal W}_{n+1}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}@>{R}>{}>{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}\\
@V{p}V{}V@V{}V{\bf p}V\\
{\cal W}_{n+1}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}={\cal W}_{n+1}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}},\end{CD}

whose existence is assured by (
21.12
) (2), (3).

By the proof of (
21.12
) and
[
Nak1
, (6.27)]
,
the morphism
R
is equal to the following composite morphism
in the case where
X/s
has a local lift
{\cal X}/{\cal W}(s)

(cf.
[
HK
, (4.2)]
):

(21.13.1)

\displaystyle{\cal W}_{n+1}\widetilde{\Omega}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}=Z^{i}_{n+1}/B^{i}_{n+1}\overset{\underset{\sim}{p^{i}}}{\longrightarrow}p^{i}Z^{i}_{n+1}/p^{i}B^{i}_{n+1}

\displaystyle\overset{{\rm proj}.}{\longrightarrow}p^{i}Z^{i}_{n+1}/(p^{i+n}Z^{i}_{1}+p^{i-1}dZ^{i-1}_{1})

\displaystyle\overset{\underset{\sim}{\phi}}{\longleftarrow}Z^{i}_{n}/B^{i}_{n}={\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}.

By
[
Nak1
, (6.5)]
,
the morphism
R
is equal to the
following composite morphism
:

(21.13.2)

\displaystyle{\cal W}_{n+1}\widetilde{\Omega}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}=Z^{i}_{n+1}/B^{i}_{n+1}\overset{{\rm proj}.}{\longrightarrow}Z^{i}_{n+1}/(p^{n}Z^{i}_{1}+d\Omega^{i-1})

\displaystyle\overset{(p^{-i}\phi)^{-1}}{\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}}Z^{i}_{n}/(p^{n}\Omega^{i}+pd\Omega^{i-1})\overset{{\rm proj}.}{\longrightarrow}Z^{i}_{n}/B^{i}_{n}={\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}.

In particular,
R
is surjective. By the proof of (
21.12
),

(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal X}_{\underline{\lambda}}/{\cal W}(s)},\varphi^{*})
satisfies the axioms of
formal de Rham-Witt complexes defined in
[
Nak1
, (6.1)]
.
Hence the following diagram is commutative by

[
Nak1
, (6.8) (4)]
:

\begin{CD}{\cal W}_{n+1}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}@>{d}>{}>{\cal W}_{n+1}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{i+1}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}\\
@V{R}V{}V@V{}V{R}V\\
{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}@>{d}>{}>{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{i+1}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}.\end{CD}

Set

(21.13.3)

\displaystyle{\cal W}\widetilde{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}:=\varprojlim_{n}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}.

Here the projective system is taken by the projection

R\colon{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}\longrightarrow{\cal W}_{n-1}\widetilde{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}
.
In a standard way, we also have the operators

\displaystyle F\colon{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}\longrightarrow{\cal W}_{n-1}\widetilde{\Omega}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}

and

\displaystyle V\colon{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}\longrightarrow{\cal W}_{n+1}\widetilde{\Omega}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}.

By
[
Nak1
, (6.8) (4)]

Fd=dF

and
Vd=dV
.
We also see that

{\cal W}\widetilde{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}

becomes a Cartan-Dieudonné-Raynaud algebra over

\Gamma(s,{\cal O}_{s})
.

Remark 21.14
.

In the last paragraph in
[
KH
, p. 1264]
and
[
KH
, Lemma 12]
the sheaf

{\cal W}\widetilde{\Omega}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}
is defined by
the following formula

(21.14.1)

\displaystyle{\cal W}\widetilde{\Omega}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}:=\varprojlim_{n}{\cal H}^{i}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal X}_{n}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\cal X}}{\cal O}_{{\cal X}_{\underline{\lambda}}}),

where
{\cal X}
is the log formal scheme in the proof of (
21.12
) (2).
The reader should note that
the
{\cal W}\widetilde{\Omega}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}
in (
21.13.3
)
is different from the
{\cal W}\widetilde{\Omega}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}

in (
21.14.1
): the limit

\varprojlim_{n}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal X}_{n}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})}
in (
21.14.1
)
induces the limit taken with respect to
the operators

F\colon{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}={\cal H}^{i}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal X}_{n}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})})\longrightarrow{\cal W}_{n-1}\widetilde{\Omega}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}={\cal H}^{i}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal X}_{n-1}/{\cal W}_{n-1}(\overset{\circ}{s})})
’s
and not taken with respect to the operators

R\colon{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}\longrightarrow{\cal W}_{n-1}\widetilde{\Omega}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}
’s;
the construction of
{\cal W}\widetilde{\Omega}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}
in [loc. cit.] seems to be wrong.

Proposition 21.15
.

The projection
R
defined in
(
21.13
) (2)

induces the following morphism

\displaystyle R\colon P_{k}{\cal W}_{n+1}\widetilde{\Omega}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}\longrightarrow P_{k}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}\quad(k\in{\mathbb{N}}).

Proof.

The proof of this proposition is the same as that of
[
Nak1
, (8.4) (2)]
.
Indeed, assume that (
21.15
) holds for a natural number
k
.
We proceed on descending induction on
k
.
It is easy to prove that
the morphism
{\bf p}\colon{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}\longrightarrow{\cal W}_{n+1}\widetilde{\Omega}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}
induces a morphism

{\bf p}\colon P_{k}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}\longrightarrow P_{k}{\cal W}_{n+1}\widetilde{\Omega}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}
and we see that
the following diagram is commutative as in
[
Nak1
, (8.4) (1)]
:

\begin{CD}0@>{}>{}>P_{k-1}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}@>{}>{}>P_{k}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}\\
@V{\bf p}V{}V@V{\bf p}V{}V\\
0@>{}>{}>P_{k-1}{\cal W}_{n+1}\widetilde{\Omega}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}@>{}>{}>P_{k}{\cal W}_{n+1}\widetilde{\Omega}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}\end{CD}

\begin{CD}@>{}>{}>\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\mu}=k}a_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}*}({\cal W}_{n}{\Omega}^{i-k}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{{\rm zar},\underline{\mu}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{s}))@>{}>{}>0\\
@V{\bf p}V{}V\\
@>{}>{}>\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\mu}=k}a_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}*}({\cal W}_{n+1}\Omega^{i-k}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{{\rm zar},\underline{\mu}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{s}))@>{}>{}>0.\end{CD}

Since the following diagram

\begin{CD}P_{k}{\cal W}_{n+1}\widetilde{\Omega}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}@>{}>{}>\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\mu}=k}a_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}*}({\cal W}_{n+1}\Omega^{i-k}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{{\rm zar},\underline{\mu}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{s}))\\
@V{p}V{}V@V{}V{p}V\\
P_{k}{\cal W}_{n+1}\widetilde{\Omega}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}@>{}>{}>\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\mu}=k}a_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}*}({\cal W}_{n+1}\Omega^{i-k}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{{\rm zar},\underline{\mu}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{s}))\end{CD}

is commutative, the following diagram

(21.15.1)

\begin{CD}P_{k}{\cal W}_{n+1}\widetilde{\Omega}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}@>{}>{}>\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\mu}=k}a_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}*}({\cal W}_{n+1}\Omega^{i-k}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{{\rm zar},\underline{\mu}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{s}))\\
@V{R}V{}V@V{}V{R}V\\
P_{k}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}@>{}>{}>\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\mu}=k}a_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}*}({\cal W}_{n}\Omega^{i-k}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{{\rm zar},\underline{\mu}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{s}))\end{CD}

is commutative.
By (
21.11.1
) and (
21.15.1
) we see that
the morphism

R\colon P_{k}{\cal W}_{n+1}\widetilde{\Omega}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}\longrightarrow P_{k}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}
induces
the morphism

R\colon P_{k-1}{\cal W}_{n+1}\widetilde{\Omega}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}\longrightarrow P_{k-1}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}
.
∎

Corollary 21.16
.

The projection

R\colon P_{k}{\cal W}_{n+1}\widetilde{\Omega}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}\longrightarrow P_{k}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}
is surjective.

Proof.

Since the projections

R\colon{\cal W}_{n+1}\widetilde{\Omega}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}\longrightarrow{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}

and

R\colon{\cal W}_{n+1}{\Omega}^{i-k}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{{\rm zar},\underline{\mu}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{s})\longrightarrow{\cal W}_{n}\Omega^{i-k}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{{\rm zar},\underline{\mu}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{s})

are surjective, descending induction tells us that
the morphism
R\colon P_{k}{\cal W}_{n+1}\widetilde{\Omega}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}\longrightarrow P_{k}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}
is surjective.
∎

Set

\displaystyle({\cal W}\widetilde{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}},P):=\varprojlim_{n}({\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}},P)

and

\displaystyle(s({\cal W}\widetilde{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{X^{(\star)}}),P):=\varprojlim_{n}(s({\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{X^{(\star)}}),P)\quad(m\in{\mathbb{N}}).

By (
21.11.1
) and (
21.15.1
) we obtain the following isomorphisms:

(21.16.1)

\displaystyle{\rm gr}_{k}^{P}{\cal W}\widetilde{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}=\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\mu}=k}a_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}*}({\cal W}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}}}[-k]\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{{\rm zar},\underline{\mu}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{s}))

and

(21.16.2)

\displaystyle{\rm gr}^{P}_{k}{\cal W}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\bullet}_{X^{(m)}}=\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\lambda}=m+1}\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\mu}=k}a_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}*}({\cal W}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}}}[-k]\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{{\rm zar},\underline{\mu}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{s})).

Let
E
be a quasi-coherent crystal of
{\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{n}/{\cal W}(\overset{\circ}{s})}
-modules.
Set
E_{n}:=E_{{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{X})}
.
Then we have the integrable connection

(21.16.3)

\displaystyle\nabla\colon E_{n}\longrightarrow E_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{X})}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{1}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}.

Indeed, we have first obtained the log de Rham complex

E_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{X})}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal W}_{n}(X)/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s}),[~]}

and then we have the following natural composite morphism

\displaystyle E_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{X})}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal W}_{n}(X)/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s}),[~]}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow E_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{X})}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal W}_{n}(X_{\underline{\lambda}})/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s}),[~]}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow E_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{X})}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}.

Here the last morphism is obtained by the following morphism

(21.16.4)

\displaystyle{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\cal W}_{n}(X_{\underline{\lambda}})/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s}),[~]}\longrightarrow{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}},

which is, by definition, the induced morphism by

s_{n}(0,0)
in (
21.8.3
) and the following two morphisms

(21.16.5)

s_{n}(1,0)^{\prime}\colon\Omega^{1}_{{\cal W}_{n}(X_{\underline{\lambda}})/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s}),[~]}\owns d(a_{0},\ldots,a_{n-1})\longmapsto\left[\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\widetilde{a}_{i}^{p^{n-i}-1}d\widetilde{a}_{i}\right]\in{\cal H}^{1}(\Omega^{*}_{{\mathfrak{D}}_{\underline{\lambda}}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s}),[~]}),

(21.16.6)

\iota\colon\Omega^{1}_{{\cal W}_{n}(X_{\underline{\lambda}})/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s}),[~]}\owns d\log b\longmapsto\left[d\log\widetilde{b}\right]\in{\cal H}^{1}(\Omega^{*}_{{\mathfrak{D}}_{\underline{\lambda}}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s}),[~]}),

where
{\mathfrak{D}}_{\underline{\lambda}}
is the log scheme over

({\cal W}_{n}(s),p{\cal W}_{n},[~])
defined in the proof of (
21.8
)
and

\widetilde{b}\in M_{{\mathfrak{D}}_{\underline{\lambda}}}
are lifts of

a_{i}\in{\cal O}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}
and

b\in M_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}\subset M_{{\cal W}_{n}(X_{\underline{\lambda}})}
, respectively.
By the same proof as that for the well-definedness of
s_{n}(0,0)
again,

s_{n}(1,0)^{\prime}
and
\iota
are well-defined (
[
HK
, (4.9)]
).
In fact, we obtain the log de Rham complex

E_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{X})}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\bullet}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}
as in
[
Nak4
, (2.2.10)]
.

Theorem 21.17
.

Assume that
E
is locally free.
Then the following hold
:

(1)
There exists a canonical filtered isomorphism

(21.17.1)

(\widetilde{R}u_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})}(E_{\underline{\lambda}})),P)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}(E_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{X})}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}},P).

The isomorphism
(\ref{eqn:nrlowln})
is compatible with the projections.

(2)
The isomorphism
(
21.17.1
)
is contravariantly functorial
with respect to a morphism satisfying the conditions
(8.1.6)
for the case
S=s
and
T={\cal W}_{n}(s)
.

Proof.

(1): Let
X_{\bullet}
be the Čech diagram obtained by an affine open covering of
X

such that there exists a simplicial immersion

X_{\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal P}_{\bullet}
into a log smooth
scheme over
\overline{{\cal W}_{n}(s)}
.
Let
\overline{\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet}
be the log PD-envelope of this immersion
over
({\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s}),p{\cal W}_{n},[~])
.
Since the immersion
X_{0}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal W}_{n}(X_{0})
is nilpotent
and since
\overline{\cal P}_{\bullet}
is log smooth over
\overline{{\cal W}_{n}(s)}
,
we have a morphism
{\cal W}_{n}(X_{0})\longrightarrow\overline{\cal P}_{0}
extending
the immersion
X_{0}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal P}_{0}
.
Hence we have a morphism
{\cal W}_{n}(X_{\bullet})\longrightarrow\overline{\cal P}_{\bullet}

extending the immersion
X_{\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal P}_{\bullet}
.
This morphism induces a morphism

{\cal W}_{n}(X_{\bullet})\longrightarrow\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}

extending the immersion
X_{\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}
.
Set
\overline{\cal E}{}^{\bullet}:=\epsilon^{*}_{X/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})}(E)_{\overline{\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet}}
.
Set
{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}:=\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}\times_{\overline{{\cal W}_{n}(s)}}{\cal W}_{n}(s)
.
Set
{\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet}:=\overline{\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet}\times_{\overline{{\cal W}_{n}(s)}}{\cal W}_{n}(s)
.
Set
{\cal E}{}^{\bullet}:=\overline{\cal E}{}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\overline{\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet}}}{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}_{\bullet}}
.
The morphism
{\cal W}_{n}(X_{\bullet})\longrightarrow\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}

induces the following morphism

(21.17.2)

\displaystyle{\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})}\longrightarrow{\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal W}_{n}(X_{\underline{\lambda},\bullet})/({\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})),[~]}.

Let

\pi\colon((X_{\bullet})_{\rm zar},f^{-1}_{\bullet}({\cal W}_{n}))\longrightarrow(X_{\rm zar},f^{-1}({\cal W}_{n}))

be the natural morphism of ringed topoi.
The cosimplicial version of the morphism (
21.16.4
) and
the morphism
{\cal W}_{n}(X_{\bullet})\longrightarrow\overline{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}

induces the following morphism

(21.17.3)

\displaystyle{\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal W}_{n}(X_{\underline{\lambda},\bullet})/({\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})),[~]}\longrightarrow E^{\bullet}_{n}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\pi^{-1}({\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\bullet}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}).

By (
21.17.2
) and (
21.17.3
)
we have the following composite morphism

(21.17.4)

\displaystyle{\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})}\longrightarrow{\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal W}_{n}(X_{\underline{\lambda},\bullet})/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s}),[~]}\longrightarrow E^{\bullet}_{n}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\pi^{-1}({\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\bullet}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}).

This composite morphism preserves
P
’s as in
[
Nak4
, (2.3.18.3)]
.
Hence we have the following morphism

(21.17.5)

\displaystyle({\cal E}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})},P)\longrightarrow(E^{\bullet}_{n}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}^{\rm ex}_{\bullet}}}\pi^{-1}({\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\bullet}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}),P).

By the cohomological descent for a bounded below complex,
we have the morphism (
21.17.1
).
It is easy to check that this morphism
is independent of the choice of
the simplicial immersion
X_{\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal P}_{\bullet}
.

We claim that the morphism (
21.17.1
) is a filtered isomorphism.
This is a local problem on
X_{\underline{\lambda},{\rm zar}}
. Hence we may assume that
the filtrations
P
’s on the sources and the targets of (
21.17.1
)
are finite. It suffices to prove that
the morphism

(21.17.6)

{\rm gr}_{k}^{P}(\widetilde{R}u_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})}(E_{\underline{\lambda}})),P)\longrightarrow{\rm gr}_{k}^{P}(E_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{X})}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}).

is an isomorphism.
The source of (
21.17.6
) is equal to

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\mu}=k}a_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}*}Ru_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})}(E_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{{\rm crys},\underline{\mu}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})))[-k].

by (
6.21.1
).
On the other hand the target of (
21.17.6
) is equal to

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\mu}=k}E_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{X})}a_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}*}({\cal W}_{n}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}}}[-k]\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{{\rm zar},\underline{\mu}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{s}))

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\mu}=k}a_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}*}(E_{n}|_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}}}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}}})}{\cal W}_{n}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}}}[-k]\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{{\rm zar},\underline{\mu}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{s}))

by (
21.11.1
).
Hence (
21.17.6
) is an isomorphism by

[
IR
, III (1.5)]
and Etesse’s comparison theorem

[
E
, II (2.1)]
.

The compatibility of the isomorphism (
21.17.1
)
with the projections follows from the proof of
[
Nak1
, (7.18)]
.

(2): Using the argument after (
8.1
), we can prove the functoriality
without difficulty. We leave the detail of the proof to the reader.

We finish the proof.
∎

Corollary 21.18
.

(1)

Let
s(E_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{X})}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{X^{(\bullet)}})
be the single complex of the double complex

(\cdots\longrightarrow E_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{X})}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{X^{(m)}}\longrightarrow\cdots)_{m\in{\mathbb{N}}}
,
where
E_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{X})}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{X^{(m)}}:=\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\lambda}=m+1}E_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{X})}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}
.
Then there exists a canonical filtered isomorphism

(21.18.1)

(\widetilde{R}u_{X^{(\star)}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X^{(\star)}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})}(E)),P)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}(s(E_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{X})}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{X^{(\bullet)}}),P).

The isomorphism is compatible with the projections.

(2)
The isomorphism
(
21.18.1
)
is contravariantly functorial
with respect to a morphism satisfying the conditions

(8.1.6)
for the case
S=s
and
T={\cal W}_{n}(s)
.

(3)
Let

\widetilde{R}u_{X/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})}(E))\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\widetilde{R}u_{X^{(\star)}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X^{(\star)}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})}(E^{(\star)}))

be the isomorphism
(
6.11.2
)
in the case
S=s
and
T={\cal W}_{n}(s)
.
Then the natural morphism

(21.18.2)

E_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{X})}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\bullet}_{X}\longrightarrow s(E_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{X})}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\bullet}_{X^{(\bullet)}})

in
C^{+}(f^{-1}({\cal W}_{n}))

fits into the following commutative diagram

(21.18.3)

\begin{CD}\widetilde{R}u_{X/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})}(E))@>{\sim}>{}>\widetilde{R}u_{X^{(\star)}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X^{(\star)}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})}(E^{(\star)}))\\
@V{\simeq}V{{\rm(\ref{eqn:nrlowln})}{\rm~for~the~case}~\underline{\lambda}=\emptyset}V@V{{\rm(\ref{eqn:nrlsln})}}V{\simeq}V\\
E_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{X})}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\bullet}_{X}@>{}>{}>s(E_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{X})}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\bullet}_{X^{(\bullet)}})\end{CD}

in
D^{+}(f^{-1}({\cal W}_{n})
.
Here the upper isomorphism in
(
21.18.3
)
is the isomorphism

(
6.11.2
)
in the special case.
Consequently the morphism
(
21.18.2
)
is a quasi-isomorphism.

Proof.

We can construct the morphism (
21.18.1
) as in the construction of
the isomorphism (
21.17.1
).
Now (
21.18
) immediately follows from (
21.17
).
∎

Let
R\colon{\cal W}_{n+1}\widetilde{\Omega}^{1}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}\longrightarrow{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{1}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}
be the projection.
By (
21.1
),
R(\theta_{\underline{\lambda},n+1})=\theta_{\underline{\lambda},n}
.
Set

\displaystyle\theta:=\varprojlim_{n}\theta_{n}\in{\rm Ker}({\cal W}\widetilde{\Omega}^{1}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}\longrightarrow{\cal W}\widetilde{\Omega}^{2}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}).

Then we can consider the following Hirsch extensions

\displaystyle({\cal W}\widetilde{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}\langle u\rangle,P):=\varprojlim_{n}({\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}\langle u\rangle,P)

and the following filtered complex

\displaystyle({\cal W}\widetilde{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{X^{(*)}}\langle u\rangle,P):=(s(a^{(\bullet)}_{*}({\cal W}\widetilde{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{X^{(\bullet)}}\langle u\rangle)),P):=\varprojlim_{n}(s(a^{(\bullet)}_{*}({\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\bullet}_{X^{(\bullet)}}\langle u\rangle)),P)\quad(m\in{\mathbb{N}}).

By (
21.16.1
) and (
21.16.2
),

(21.18.4)

\displaystyle{\rm gr}_{k}^{P}a_{\underline{\lambda}*}({\cal W}\widetilde{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}\langle u\rangle)=\bigoplus_{j=0}^{\infty}\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\mu}=k-2j}{\cal W}u^{[j]}\otimes_{\cal W}a_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}*}({\cal W}\Omega^{\bullet-k}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{{\rm zar},\underline{\mu}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{s}))

and

(21.18.5)

\displaystyle{\rm gr}^{P}_{k}{\cal W}\widetilde{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{X^{(*)}}\langle u\rangle=\bigoplus_{j=0}^{\infty}\bigoplus_{m=0}^{\infty}\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\lambda}=m}\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\mu}=k+m-2j}a_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}*}({\cal W}\Omega^{\bullet-k}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{{\rm zar},\underline{\mu}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{s})).

Definition 21.19
.

Set

\displaystyle({\cal W}_{n}H_{X}(E),P):=(s(E_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{X})}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\bullet}_{X^{(\bullet)}}\langle u\rangle),P)

and

\displaystyle({\cal W}H_{X}(E),P):=\varprojlim_{n}({\cal W}_{n}H_{X}(E),P).

We call
({\cal W}H_{X}(E),P)

Kim-Hain’s filtered complex
of
E
.
When
E
is trivial, we call

({\cal W}H_{X}(E),P)

Kim-Hain’s filtered complex
of
X/{\cal W}

and we denote it by
({\cal W}H_{X},P)
.

Next we define
{\cal W}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}

which has not been defined in
[
KH
]
.

Let
i
be a nonnegative integer and let
n
be a positive integer.
Consider a local immersion

X\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal P}
as in the proof of (
21.7
).
Let us recall the complex (
6.24.2
) in the case of the trivial coefficient and
set

\displaystyle{\cal W}_{n}{\Omega}{}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}:={\cal H}^{i}(Ru_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}/{\cal W}_{n}(s)*}({\cal O}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}/{\cal W}_{n}(s)})).

The wedge product of
\theta_{\underline{\lambda}}\in{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{1}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}

induces the following morphism

\displaystyle\theta_{\underline{\lambda}}\wedge\colon{\cal W}_{n}\Omega^{i-1}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}\longrightarrow{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}\quad(i\in{\mathbb{N}}).

Proposition 21.20
.

The abelian sheaf
{\cal W}_{n}\Omega^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}

(i\in{\mathbb{N}})

fits into the following exact sequence
:

(21.20.1)

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow{\cal W}_{n}\Omega^{i-1}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}\longrightarrow{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}\longrightarrow{\cal W}_{n}\Omega^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}\longrightarrow 0.

Proof.

The problem is local.
Consider the following locally split exact sequence as in (
4.8.1
):

(21.20.2)

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}/{\cal W}_{n}(s)}[-1]\overset{d\log t\wedge}{\longrightarrow}{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}/{\cal W}_{n}(s)}\longrightarrow 0

This exact sequence gives us the following sequence:

(21.20.3)

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}}/{\cal W}_{n}(s)}[-1]\overset{d\log t\wedge}{\longrightarrow}{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}}/{\cal W}_{n}(s)}\longrightarrow 0

By the same proof as that of (
4.8
), the following sequence
is exact:

(21.20.4)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\longrightarrow{\cal H}^{i-1}({\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}}/{\cal W}_{n}(s)})\overset{d\log t\wedge}{\longrightarrow}{\cal H}^{i}({\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow{\cal H}^{i}({\cal O}_{{\mathfrak{D}}}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}}/{\cal W}_{n}(s)})\longrightarrow 0

This means that the following sequence is exact:

(21.20.5)

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{0}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}\overset{\theta_{\underline{\lambda}}}{\longrightarrow}\cdots\overset{\theta_{\underline{\lambda}}}{\longrightarrow}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{i-1}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}\overset{\theta_{\underline{\lambda}}}{\longrightarrow}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}\longrightarrow{\cal W}_{n}\Omega^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}\longrightarrow 0.

Because we see that

{\cal W}_{n}\Omega^{i-1}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}={\rm Coker}(\theta_{\underline{\lambda}}\colon{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{i-2}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}{\longrightarrow}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{i-1}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}})

by the local calculation, we obtain the exact sequence (
21.20.1
).
∎

Corollary 21.21
.

There exists an inverse Cartier isomorphism

(21.21.1)

\displaystyle C^{-1}\colon\Omega^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}{\cal W}_{1}\Omega^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}.

Proof.

The inverse Cartier isomorphism

C^{-1}\colon\widetilde{\Omega}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}{\cal W}_{1}\widetilde{\Omega}^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}

(the proof of
[
KH
, Lemma 12]
)
induces the inverse Cartier isomorphism (
21.21.1
) by
(
21.20.5
) since
C^{-1}(d\log t)=d\log t
.
∎

Remark 21.22
.

Let the notations be as in the proof of (
21.20
).
By (
21.21
),
(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal X}_{\underline{\lambda}}},\phi^{*})
satisfies

(21.12.1)\sim(21.12.5)
.
Hence all the results in
[
Nak1
, §6]
for formal de Rham-Witt complexes
hold for
\{{\cal W}_{n}\Omega^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
.
For example,

(21.22.1)

\displaystyle{\rm Fil}^{r}{\cal W}_{n+1}\Omega^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}=V^{r}{\cal W}_{n+1-r}\Omega^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}+dV^{r}{\cal W}_{n+1-r}\Omega^{i-1}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}.

(21.22.2)

\displaystyle d^{-1}(p^{n}{\cal W}\Omega^{i+1}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}})=F^{n}{\cal W}\Omega^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}},

(21.22.3)

\displaystyle R_{n}\otimes^{L}_{R}{\cal W}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}={\cal W}_{n}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}},

(21.22.4)

{\rm Ker}(d\colon{\cal W}_{n}\Omega^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}\longrightarrow{\cal W}_{n}\Omega^{i+1}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}})=F^{n}({\cal W}_{2n}\Omega^{i}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}).

By using (
21.21
) and by the same proof as that of (
21.12
),
we obtain the Hyodo’s multiplication

{\bf p}\colon{\cal W}_{n}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}\longrightarrow{\cal W}_{n+1}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}

and the projection

R\colon\colon{\cal W}_{n+1}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}\longrightarrow{\cal W}_{n}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}

such that
{\bf p}R=p=R{\bf p}
.

Let
F
be a quasi-coherent crystal of
{\cal O}_{X/{\cal W}(s)}
-modules.
Set
F_{n}:=F_{{\cal W}_{n}(X)}
.
Then we have the following integrable connection

\displaystyle\nabla\colon F_{n}\longrightarrow F_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{X})}{\cal W}_{n}\Omega^{1}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}

as in (
21.16.3
)
and in fact, we obtain the log de Rham complex

F_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{X})}{\cal W}_{n}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}
as in
[
Nak4
]
.
To obtain it, we have first obtained the log de Rham complex

F_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}})}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal W}_{n}(X_{\underline{\lambda}})/{\cal W}_{n}(s),[~]}

and then we have used a natural morphism

(21.22.5)

\displaystyle{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{{\cal W}_{n}(X_{\underline{\lambda}})/{\cal W}_{n}(s),[~]}\longrightarrow{\cal W}_{n}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}.

We obtain this morphism by (
21.8.3
) and by replacing

{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})
with
{\cal W}_{n}(s)
in
(
21.16.5
) and (
21.16.6
).

Theorem 21.23
.

Let the notations be as in
(
21.17
)
.
Then the following hold
:

(1)
There exists a canonical isomorphism

(21.23.1)

Ru_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}/{\cal W}_{n}(s)*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X/{\cal W}_{n}(s)}(E))_{\underline{\lambda}})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}E_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{X})}{\cal W}_{n}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}.

This isomorphism is compatible with the projections.

(2)
The isomorphism
(
21.23.1
)
is
contravariantly functorial
with respect to a morphism satisfying the conditions

(8.1.6)
for the case
S=s
and
T={\cal W}_{n}(s)
.

Proof.

Let the notations be as in the proof of (
21.17
).
Then, by replacing
{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})
by
{\cal W}_{n}(s)
,
we have the morphism (
21.23.1
).
It is easy to check that the morphism (
21.23.1
)
is independent of the choice of
the simplicial immersion
X_{\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal P}_{\bullet}
.

Now the question is local on
X
;
we may assume that there exists an immersion

X\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal P}
into a log smooth scheme
over
{\cal W}_{n}(s)
.
By (
21.20.2
) we have the following exact sequence

(21.23.2)

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow{\cal E}_{n}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}}/{\cal W}_{n}(s)}[-1]\overset{d\log\tau\wedge}{\longrightarrow}{\cal E}_{n}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})}\longrightarrow{\cal E}_{n}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}{}^{\rm ex}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}{}^{{\rm ex}}_{\underline{\lambda}}/{\cal W}_{n}(s)}\longrightarrow 0.

By (
21.20.4
) we have the following exact sequence

(21.23.3)

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow E_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{X})}{\cal W}_{n}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}[-1]\overset{d\log\tau\wedge}{\longrightarrow}E_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{X})}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}\longrightarrow{\cal W}_{n}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}\longrightarrow 0.

The morphisms (
21.17.1
) and (
21.23.1
)
induce the morphism from (
21.23.2
) to (
21.23.3
).
Because the morphism (
21.17.1
) is an isomorphism,
we see that the morphism (
21.23.1
) is an isomorphism by
induction on the degree of the cohomological sheaves of the complexes

Ru_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}/{\cal W}_{n}(s)*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X/{\cal W}_{n}(s)}(E))_{\underline{\lambda}})

and

E_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{X})}{\cal W}_{n}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}
.

(2): Using the argument after (
8.1
), we can prove the functoriality
without difficulty. We leave the detail of the proof to the reader
(cf. the proof of (
21.17
) (2)).

We finish the proof.
∎

Set

\displaystyle{\cal W}_{n}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{X^{(m)}}:=\bigoplus_{\underline{\lambda}=m+1}{\cal W}_{n}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}.

and

\displaystyle{\cal W}_{n}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{X^{(*)}}:=s({\cal W}_{n}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{X^{(\bullet)}}):=\bigoplus_{\underline{\lambda}=m+1}{\cal W}_{n}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}.

Corollary 21.24
.

(1)

Let
E_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{X})}{\cal W}_{n}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{X^{(\star)}}
be the single complex of
the double complex

(\cdots\longrightarrow a^{(m)}_{*}(E_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{X})}{\cal W}_{n}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{X^{(m)}})\longrightarrow\cdots)_{m\in{\mathbb{N}}}
,
where
E_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{X})}{\cal W}_{n}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{X^{(m)}}:=\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\lambda}=m+1}E_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{X})}{\cal W}_{n}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}}
.
Then there exists a canonical filtered isomorphism

(21.24.1)

{R}u_{X^{(\star)}/{\cal W}_{n}(s)*}(E)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}E_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{X})}{\cal W}_{n}{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{X^{(\star)}}.

The isomorphism
(\ref{eqn:nsln})
is compatible with the projections.

(2)
The isomorphism
(
21.24.1
)
is contravariantly functorial
with respect to a morphism satisfying the conditions
(8.1.6)

for the case
S=s
and
T={\cal W}_{n}(s)
.

(3)
Let

{R}u_{X/{\cal W}_{n}(s)*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X/{\cal W}_{n}(s)}(E))\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}Ru_{X^{(\star)}/{\cal W}_{n}(s)*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X^{(\star)}/{\cal W}_{n}(s)}(E^{(\star)}))

be the isomorphism
(
6.11.1
)
in the case
S=s
and
T={\cal W}_{n}(s)
.
Then the natural morphism

(21.24.2)

E_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{X})}{\cal W}_{n}{\Omega}{}^{\bullet}_{X}\longrightarrow E_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{X})}{\cal W}_{n}{\Omega}{}^{\bullet}_{X^{(\star)}}

in
C^{+}(f^{-1}({\cal W}_{n}))

fits into the following commutative diagram

\begin{CD}{R}u_{X/{\cal W}_{n}(s)*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X/{\cal W}_{n}(s)}(E))@>{\sim}>{}>{R}u_{X^{(\star)}/{\cal W}_{n}(s)*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X^{(\star)}/{\cal W}_{n}(s)}(E^{(\star)}))\\
@V{{\rm(\ref{eqn:nruowln})}{\rm~for~the~case}~\underline{\lambda}=\emptyset}V{\simeq}V@V{{\rm(\ref{eqn:nsln})}}V{\simeq}V\\
E_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{X})}{\cal W}_{n}{\Omega}{}^{\bullet}_{X}@>{}>{}>E_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{X})}{\cal W}_{n}{\Omega}{}^{\bullet}_{X^{(\star)}}\end{CD}

in
D^{+}(f^{-1}({\cal W}_{n})
.
Here the upper isomorphism in the commutative diagram above is the isomorphism

(
21.24.2
)
in the special case.
Consequently the morphism
(
21.24.2
)
is a quasi-isomorphism.

Proof.

This immediately follows from (
21.17
).
∎

The following is a main result in this section

Theorem 21.25

(
Comparison theorem between Hirsch pre-weight-filtered complexes
and Kim-Hain’s filtered complexes).

Let the notations be as in
(
21.17
)
.
Then there exists a canonical filtered isomorphism

(21.25.1)

\displaystyle(H_{\rm zar}(X/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s}),E),P)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}({\cal W}_{n}H_{X}(E),P)

in
{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}(f^{-1}({\cal W}_{n}))

fitting into the following commutative diagram

(21.25.2)

\begin{CD}Ru_{X^{(\star)}/{\cal W}_{n}(s)*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X^{(\star)}/{\cal W}_{n}(s)}(E^{(\star)}))@>{\sim}>{}>E_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{X})}{\cal W}_{n}{\Omega}{}^{\bullet}_{X^{(\star)}}\\
@A{\simeq}A{}A@A{}A{\simeq}A\\
H_{\rm zar}(X/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s}),E)@>{\sim}>{}>{\cal W}_{n}H_{X}(E)\\
@A{\simeq}A{}A@A{}A{\simeq}A\\
\widetilde{R}u_{X/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})}(E)\langle u\rangle)@>{\sim}>{}>E_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{X})}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{X}\langle u\rangle\\
@V{\simeq}V{}V@V{}V{\simeq}V\\
Ru_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/{\cal W}_{n}(s)*}(\epsilon^{*}_{X/{\cal W}_{n}(s)}(E))@>{\sim}>{}>E_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{X})}{\cal W}_{n}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X}\end{CD}

in
D^{+}(f^{-1}({\cal W}_{n}))
.
The isomorphism
(
21.25.1
)
and the commutative diagram

(
21.25.2
)
are contravariantly functorial
for a morphism
g\colon X\longrightarrow Y
satisfying
the condition
(8.1.6)
for the case
S=s
and
T={\cal W}_{n}(s)

and a morphism
g_{\rm crys}^{*}(F)\longrightarrow E
of
{\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})}
-modules for an analogous sheaf
F
in
(\overset{\circ}{Y}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s}))_{\rm crys}
to
E
.
The isomorphism
(
21.25.1
)
and the commutative diagram

(
21.25.2
)
are compatible with respect to the projections.

Proof.

Let
X_{\bullet}
be the Čech diagram obtained by
an affine open covering of
X
and let
X_{\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal P}_{\bullet}

be the simplicial immersion into a log smooth
scheme over
\overline{{\cal W}_{n}(s)}
as before.
Since the immersion
X_{0}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal W}_{n}(X_{0})
is nilpotent
and since
\overline{\cal P}_{\bullet}
is log smooth over
\overline{{\cal W}_{n}(s)}
,
we have a morphism
{\cal W}_{n}(X_{0})\longrightarrow\overline{\cal P}_{\bullet}
extending
the immersion
X_{\bullet}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\cal P}_{\bullet}
.
Let

\pi\colon((X_{\bullet})_{\rm zar},f^{-1}_{\bullet}({\cal W}_{n}))\longrightarrow(X_{\rm zar},f^{-1}({\cal W}_{n}))

be the natural morphism of ringed topoi.
The filtered morphism (
21.17.5
) induces the following filtered morphism

(21.25.3)

\displaystyle({\cal E}^{\bullet}_{n}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}_{\bullet}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\cal P}_{\bullet\underline{\lambda}}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})}\langle u\rangle,P)\longrightarrow(E^{\bullet}_{n}\otimes_{{\cal O}_{{\cal P}_{\bullet}}}\pi^{-1}(W_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\bullet}_{X_{\underline{\lambda}}})\langle u\rangle,P).

This morphism induces the following filtered morphism

(21.25.4)

\displaystyle(H_{\rm zar}(X/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s}),E),P)\longrightarrow({\cal W}_{n}H_{X}(E),P).

The following induced morphism by this morphism

\displaystyle{\rm gr}^{P}_{k}H_{\rm zar}(X/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s}),E){\longrightarrow}{\rm gr}^{P}_{k}{\cal W}_{n}H_{X}(E).

is equal to the following morphism by (
8.7.1
) and (
21.11.5
):

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{m\geq 0}\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\lambda}=m+1}\bigoplus_{j\geq 0}\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\mu}=k+m-2j}a_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}*}Ru_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})*}(E_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{{\rm crys},\underline{\mu}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})))

\displaystyle\{-(k+m-2j)\}[-m](-(k+m-j);v)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{m=0}^{\infty}\bigoplus_{j=0}^{\infty}\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\lambda}=m}\bigoplus_{\#\underline{\mu}=k+m-2j}{\cal W}_{n}u^{[j]}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}}a_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}*}(E_{n}\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{X})}{\cal W}_{n}\Omega^{\bullet-k}_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}\cup\underline{\mu}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{{\rm zar},\underline{\mu}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{s})).

By
[
IR
, III (1.5)]
and Etesse’s theorem
[
E
, I Theorem 2.1]
this is an isomorphism.

We leave the proofs of the commutativity of the diagram
(
21.25.2
) and the
contravariant functoriality and the compatibility
with the projections to the reader.
∎

Remark 21.26
.

Let
({\cal W}_{n}A_{X}(E),P)
be the filtered complex defined in
[
Nak4
, (2.3.21)]
.
Then we can construct a filtered morphism
(which we should call the
log de Rham-Witt Fujisawa’s morphism
):

\displaystyle\psi^{\rm dRW}\colon({\cal W}_{n}A_{X}(E),P)\longrightarrow({\cal W}_{n}H_{X}(E),P)

whose underlying morphism

\displaystyle\psi^{\rm dRW}\colon{\cal W}_{n}A_{X}(E)\longrightarrow{\cal W}_{n}H_{X}(E)

is an isomorphism fitting into the following commutative diagram

(21.26.1)

\begin{CD}{\cal W}_{n}A_{X}(E)@>{\psi^{\rm dRW},\sim}>{}>{\cal W}_{n}H_{X}(E)\\
@A{\theta\wedge}A{}A@A{}A{\simeq}A\\
E\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{X})}{\cal W}_{n}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X}E\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{X})}{\cal W}_{n}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\bullet}_{X}\langle u\rangle\\
\Big\|@V{}V{\simeq}V\\
E\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{X})}{\cal W}_{n}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X}=E\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}({\cal O}_{X})}{\cal W}_{n}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X}.\end{CD}

The filtered morphism
\psi^{\rm dRW}
is compatible with the projections.

As in §
18
, we can also construct the “semi-cosimplicial version”

({\cal W}_{n}A_{X^{(\star)}}(E),P)
of
this filtered complex
{\cal W}_{n}A_{X}(E)
and we can prove that
the following diagram is commutative
:

(21.26.2)

\begin{CD}(H_{\rm zar}(X/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s}),E),P)@>{\sim}>{}>({\cal W}_{n}H_{X}(E),P)\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
(A_{\rm zar}(X^{(\star)}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s}),E),P)@>{\sim}>{}>({\cal W}_{n}A_{X^{(\star)}}(E),P)\\
@A{}A{}A@A{}A{}A\\
(A_{\rm zar}(X/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s}),E),P)@>{\sim}>{}>({\cal W}_{n}A_{X}(E),P).\end{CD}

This commutative diagram is compatible with the projections.

By the same argument for the definition of the product (
11.2.8
),
we obtain the following morphism

(21.26.3)

\displaystyle\cup\colon({\cal W}_{n}H_{X}(E),P)\otimes_{{\cal W}_{n}}({\cal W}_{n}H_{X}(E^{\prime}),P)\longrightarrow({\cal W}_{n}H_{X}(E\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/{\cal W}_{n}}}E^{\prime}),P),

where
E^{\prime}
is an analogous crystal of

{\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})}
-modules to
E
.
By the local calculation, we obtain the following:

Proposition 21.27
.

Assume that
\overset{\circ}{X}
is quasi-compact.
Then the following diagram is commutative
:

(21.27.1)

\begin{CD}(H_{\rm zar}(X/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s}),E),P)\otimes^{L}_{f^{-1}({\cal W}_{n})}(H_{\rm zar}(X/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s}),E^{\prime}),P)@>{\cup}>{}>(H_{\rm zar}(X/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s}),E\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})}}E^{\prime},P)\\
@V{\simeq}V{}V@V{}V{\simeq}V\\
({\cal W}_{n}H_{X}(E),P)\otimes_{f^{-1}({\cal W}_{n})}({\cal W}_{n}H_{X}(E^{\prime}),P)@>{\cup}>{}>({\cal W}_{n}H_{X}(E\otimes_{{\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})}}E^{\prime}),P).\end{CD}

The commutative diagram
(
21.27.1
)
is contravariantly functorial
for a morphism
g\colon X\longrightarrow Y
satisfying
the condition
(8.1.6)
for the case
S=s
and
T={\cal W}_{n}(s)

and morphisms
g_{\rm crys}^{*}(F)\longrightarrow E

and
g_{\rm crys}^{*}(F^{\prime})\longrightarrow E^{\prime}

of
{\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{X}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s})}
-modules
for analogous sheaves
F
and
F^{\prime}
in
(\overset{\circ}{Y}/{\cal W}_{n}(\overset{\circ}{s}))
to
E

and
E^{\prime}
, respectively.
The commutative diagram
(
21.27.1
)
is
compatible with respect to the projections.

If one consider the case where
E
and
E^{\prime}
are trivial, then we obtain
the commutative diagram (
1.7.2
).

Definition 21.28
.

Set

\displaystyle({\cal W}H_{X,{\mathbb{Q}}},P):=s_{\rm TW}(a^{(\bullet)}_{*}(({\cal W}\widetilde{\Omega}{}^{\bullet}_{X^{(\bullet)}}\langle u\rangle_{\mathbb{Q}}),P)).

We call
({\cal W}H_{X,{\mathbb{Q}}},P)

Kim-Hain’s filtered dga
of
X/{\cal W}
.

We conclude this book by giving the following:

Theorem 21.29
.

Set
{\cal W}{\Omega}{}^{\bullet}_{X^{(\star)},{\mathbb{Q}}}:=s_{\rm TW}(a^{(\bullet)}_{*}({\cal W}{\Omega}{}^{\bullet}_{X^{(\bullet)},{\mathbb{Q}}}))
.
There exists a canonical filtered isomorphism

(21.29.1)

\displaystyle(H_{{\rm zar},{\rm TW}}(X/{\cal W}(\overset{\circ}{s}))_{\mathbb{Q}},P)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}({\cal W}H_{X,{\mathbb{Q}}},P)

in
D({\rm A}^{\geq 0}{\rm F}(f^{-1}({\cal W})))

fitting into the following commutative diagram

(21.29.2)

\begin{CD}R_{\rm TW}u_{X^{(\star)}/{\cal W}(s)*}({\cal O}_{X^{(\star)}/{\cal W}(s)})_{\mathbb{Q}}@>{\sim}>{}>{\cal W}{\Omega}{}^{\bullet}_{X^{(\star),{\mathbb{Q}}}}\\
@A{\simeq}A{}A@A{}A{\simeq}A\\
H_{{\rm zar},{\rm TW}}(X/{\cal W}(\overset{\circ}{s}))_{\mathbb{Q}}@>{\sim}>{}>{\cal W}H_{X,{\mathbb{Q}}}\\
@A{\simeq}A{}A@A{}A{\simeq}A\\
\widetilde{R}_{\rm TW}u_{X/{\cal W}(\overset{\circ}{s})*}({\cal O}_{X_{\overset{\circ}{T}_{0}}/{\cal W}(\overset{\circ}{s})}\langle u\rangle)_{\mathbb{Q}}@>{\sim}>{}>({\cal W}\widetilde{\Omega}^{\bullet}_{X}\langle u\rangle)_{\mathbb{Q}}\\
@V{\simeq}V{}V@V{}V{\simeq}V\\
R_{\rm TW}u_{X/{\cal W}(s)*}({\cal O}_{X/{\cal W}(s)})_{\mathbb{Q}}@>{\sim}>{}>{\cal W}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X,{\mathbb{Q}}}.\end{CD}

The isomorphism
(
21.29.1
)
and the commutative diagram

(
21.29.2
)
are contravariantly functorial
for a morphism
g\colon X\longrightarrow Y
satisfying
the condition
(8.1.6)
for the case
S=s
and
T={\cal W}(s)
.
The isomorphism
(
21.29.1
)
and the commutative diagram

(
21.29.2
)
are compatible with respect to the projections.

Proof.

The morphism (
21.17.4
)
in the case where
E
is trivial is a filtered morphism of dga’s.
Hence we have the filtered morphism (
21.29.1
).

We leave the proofs of the commutativity of the diagram
(
21.29.2
) and the
contravariant functoriality and the compatibility
with projections to the reader.
∎
